## Detailed product requirements — preserved source

The §4 module catalogue lives in `Modules.md`. All other detailed sections and confirmed decisions are retained below. Read `Project-entry.md` first for context, but do not replace binding detail with a summary.

## 1. Executive overview

Madeora is a **modular lifestyle and household management application**: one place to organize personal life and coordinate a Home with other people. It is for individuals, couples, families, and shared households, not website creation, business analytics, or a generic corporate project-management tool.

Users build a dashboard from widgets and modules that fit their lives. They can manage schedules, tasks, chores, shopping, food, routines, pets, goals, rewards, and memories. Related features should cooperate rather than act like unrelated mini-apps. **Ora** is a friendly, concise, permission-aware organizational assistant that helps users understand and plan their day without silently changing their data.

Madeora should feel calm, approachable, and flexible: powerful beneath the surface, simple during everyday use.

## 2. Product goals and principles

- Reduce friction in day-to-day planning, personal organization, and Home coordination.
- Support both personal and shared information, with clear ownership, visibility, and permissions.
- Be useful with native Madeora functionality even when no third-party service is connected.
- Let users choose which modules and widgets they want; do not force a one-size-fits-all dashboard.
- Make cross-module workflows genuinely work end to end.
- Keep assistant actions reviewable, understandable, and recoverable.
- Support desktop, responsive devices, and a dedicated household wall-display experience.

**Non-goals:** website builder functionality; decorative dashboards with fake metrics; integrations that are necessary for basic functionality; claiming an MVP is complete when core flows are placeholders.

## 3. Product language and structure

- **Madeora:** product and overarching brand.
- **Home:** shared household context. A user may have personal information and participate in shared Home information.
- **Personal context:** information belonging to an individual, subject to their chosen visibility.
- **Home context:** shared household data, governed by membership and permissions.
- **Module:** a feature area (e.g. Meals or Pets).
- **Widget:** a dashboard component exposing a useful slice of module data/actions.
- **Ora:** assistant within Madeora. Its visual identity is fixed and recognizable, while the wider UI can be personalized.

Always show which context the user is working in. Remember the user's selected context where appropriate.

## 5. Ora — assistant behavior

Ora is a **friendly, concise, text-first organizational assistant**, not an autonomous agent with unlimited authority.

- Provide summaries, planning help, reminders, and task-oriented suggestions using information the user is authorized to access.
- Respect personal/Home context and granular permissions.
- Present proposed changes for **user review and confirmation before applying**; proposals must be editable.
- Explain relevant assumptions, avoid inventing facts, and acknowledge unavailable data.
- Provide safe undo/recovery for supported changes.
- If learning preferences or patterns, do so transparently and with understandable controls.
- When unable to perform an action, say so and offer a supported alternative.
- Keep its dashboard presence contextual and dismissible; use a contextual side panel where useful.
- Maintain a consistent Ora appearance rather than letting accent themes obscure its identity.

### Confirmed shared Home calendar authorization (12 October 2026)
- **Home permissions determine who may edit shared Home calendar events**, regardless of which Home member originally created the event. Being the creator alone does not override Home permission policy.
- **Editing and deleting are separate configurable capabilities.** A member may have calendar edit permission without calendar delete permission.
- Apply authorization on the server to all entry points: calendar screens, widgets, Ora actions, bulk actions, recurring-event changes, and Google Calendar synchronization initiated through Madeora.
- **Important boundary:** Home permissions do not grant access to an individual's unshared/private events, nor do they override the connected Google account's provider permissions. If a requested Google-side change is unauthorized or unsupported, Madeora must report it accurately and preserve data.
- For recurring Home events, check the relevant edit/delete capability for the selected scope (this event, this and following, all events).
- Deletion still requires the previously approved destination choice: remove from Madeora versus delete everywhere, with confirmation for consequential provider-side deletion.

## 6. Membership, privacy, and permissions

### Confirmed multi-Home model
- A single account can create and join **multiple Homes**.
- Membership, roles, permissions, and Home-owned data are separate for each Home.
- Switching Homes changes the active shared context; personal data remains distinct.

### Confirmed Home-independent adult account onboarding (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** An adult may register, verify their account and immediately use their **personal space and personal dashboard without creating or joining any Home**. Creating a Home, accepting an invitation or requesting to join one is an optional later action, not a registration prerequisite. Never silently create a Home or assign Home Owner status during account creation.
- After registration, show a useful, accessible personal-dashboard starting experience with eligible personal widgets and clear, non-blocking **Create a Home** and **Join a Home** actions. Allow users to skip Home setup indefinitely; avoid repeated intrusive prompts or treating zero Homes as an error. Personal features and records use personal ownership, quota, privacy and permissions, not a fictional default Home.
- The navigation/context switcher must offer a clearly identified **Personal** context even with zero Homes. If no Home memberships exist, omit or disable Home-specific views and actions with helpful explanations rather than empty, misleading Home dashboards. After a Home is created or approved membership begins, make that Home available in the existing explicit switcher without moving personal records, changing personal dashboard configuration or automatically adding Home widgets.
- Joining follows existing secure invitation, link/code request and manager-approval requirements. A pending, rejected, expired or revoked invitation does not grant Home access or prevent continued personal use. A person may create a Home and later join other Homes, subject to existing membership and ownership rules.
- Keep global adult display name, avatar, accent color, optional profile fields, sign-in/2FA, account sessions, notification defaults, exports, deactivation and deletion available independently of Home membership. Per-Home profile overrides, Home permissions, household activity and shared-display pairing only become available for a particular authorized Home. Personal data is never shared into a newly joined Home without explicit authorized action.
- Account recovery, removal from the last Home and voluntary departure must safely return an otherwise active adult to personal context. Distinguish **zero Home memberships** from a deactivated account or pending account deletion. If a Home is suspended/deleted, the personal account and dashboard remain usable unless separately restricted for an independently justified reason.
- Acceptance criteria: register by supported email/password or social provider without invitation; complete verification and open personal dashboard with zero Homes; create personal content; skip Home setup; start and abandon Home creation; pending/rejected join request; accept approved Home membership; create first Home and become its single Owner; switch between personal and Home contexts; lose last Home membership and retain personal content; ensure personal widgets never leak into Household Displays; accessible mobile/desktop empty states and account recovery.

### Confirmed explicit multi-Home switching for adult accounts (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A single adult account may create or join multiple independent Homes using one personal identity and sign-in. Each Home maintains separate membership, role, permissions, settings, dashboard configuration and Home-owned records; personal-space data remains separately owned. This reaffirms the existing multi-Home model and explicitly rejects both one-Home-per-account restrictions and an automatic combined multi-Home dashboard.
- Provide a prominent, accessible **active Home switcher** in the authenticated application shell, with the active Home clearly named and visually distinguishable. Show only Homes in which the adult has current authorized membership. Switching changes the active Home context, navigation, permissions, dashboard and Home-scoped filters; it must not merge or expose data across Homes. Provide a distinct, clearly identified personal-space context where applicable.
- Remember the last valid active Home as a convenience, but on sign-in, reload, deep link, new tab, offline recovery or membership change, verify access before displaying any Home data. If access is lost, select a safe authorized destination or personal context and explain the change without revealing revoked Home information. Deep links to a different authorized Home must clearly indicate the context change rather than silently performing cross-Home edits.
- Enforce Home scope and permissions server-side for every action, search, notification, widget, file, background operation, integration and audit entry. Pending forms, uploads, optimistic actions, offline queues and confirmation dialogs must retain and revalidate their **original Home identifier**; switching Homes must never redirect an unfinished operation to another Home. Warn or preserve drafts safely when switching would discard work.
- Roles may differ across Homes (e.g. manager in one and ordinary member in another). A role in one Home grants no permissions in another. Invitations and joining remain governed by the existing acceptance rules; merely belonging to multiple Homes does not authorize data sharing between them. Explicit cross-Home sharing follows separately approved sharing rules.
- Household Displays remain paired to exactly one Home and never expose the adult's Home switcher or inherit the approving manager's other memberships. Child profiles remain Home-scoped under their approved guardian model; do not automatically carry them into an adult's other Homes. Notifications and external links must label their Home without leaking content from inaccessible Homes.
- Acceptance criteria: one adult belongs to two Homes with different roles and dashboard layouts; switch in both directions; personal context remains separate; reload restores authorized Home; revoked membership while viewing; cross-Home deep link; pending edit and offline action across a switch; different Home timezones; no combined dashboard; Home-specific search and notification; shared display stays fixed to its paired Home; keyboard, mobile and screen-reader switching.

### Confirmed one child identity across multiple Homes (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A child may participate in more than one Home through **one canonical child identity**, without requiring an email address or creating duplicate independent child accounts. Each Home retains independent child membership, household profile presentation where appropriate, chores, rewards, history, settings, feature permissions and access boundaries. Do not combine Home dashboards or household activity histories by default.
- **Explicit authorized linking:** Creating a child in one Home does not discover, invite, link or enroll that child in any other Home automatically, including other Homes joined by either guardian. Linking an existing child identity to another Home requires a deliberate, reviewable request and approval by appropriately authorized guardians/primary guardians for the affected memberships, consistent with applicable child protection and legal requirements. Do not allow a single Home manager, shared display or someone merely knowing the child's name to claim an existing child identity. Resolve disputes, unavailable guardians and changes in custody through a safe hold/review process rather than silently overriding authority.
- **Per-Home guardianship:** Preserve the approved primary/optional secondary guardian structure **separately for each Home**. A guardian's authority in Home A grants no automatic ability to administer the child's Home B membership, permissions, credentials, household data or guardians. Child identity-level operations, such as credential recovery and transition to an independent account, require an explicitly defined authorized cross-Home process that does not let one household silently seize or lock out the child in another.
- **Authentication and dashboards:** Maintain one child's underlying identity with email-free credentials under the existing approved-device PIN and permitted password rules. On independent login, offer an explicit switcher among that child's currently authorized Homes and personal dashboard where enabled; enabling dashboard/login in one Home does not automatically enable it in another. Household Displays remain bound to one Home, present only that Home's eligible member identity and never offer cross-Home switching.
- **Privacy and data minimization:** Keep Home-owned records and sensitive profile fields isolated. Show only the minimum information necessary to verify a linking request; do not expose another Home's name, location, guardians, roster or activity to unauthorized people. Where an identity-level attribute must be shared, clearly distinguish it from Home-specific display names and settings and obtain appropriate guardian approval for consequential changes. Prevent cross-Home search, notifications, audit logs, exports and inferred disclosures.
- **Membership lifecycle:** Leaving or removing the child from one Home removes only that Home membership and associated access, not the canonical child identity or memberships in other Homes. Preserve applicable Home history, retention and deletion rights independently. Handle unlinked duplicates, mistaken links, guardian changes, lost credentials, revocation, concurrent requests and age-aware transition without silently merging or deleting histories.
- **Acceptance criteria:** same child in two Homes with different guardians and permissions; explicit two-sided authorized link; denied/expired link; ambiguous duplicate names; no automatic enrollment through adult multi-Home access; one Home removes child; child logs into two authorized Homes; separate dashboard enablement; Household Display fixed to one Home; privacy of other Home; contested guardian approval; correction of mistaken link; audit and accessible mobile flow.

### Confirmed identity-level guardians for multi-Home child credentials (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Separate authority over a child's **canonical identity and shared authentication credentials** from the primary/secondary guardian roles assigned independently inside each Home. A Home primary guardian is not automatically an identity guardian; being a Home manager, knowing the child's username, or managing a Home membership cannot grant access to global credential resets or other Homes.
- Explicitly assign and verify identity-level guardian authority through an authorized, auditable onboarding or cross-Home linking process. Show the identity guardians and their scope only to appropriately authorized people, without exposing other Home memberships. Establish an accountable identity-level primary guardian and, where permitted, an optional secondary/recovery guardian; changes require stronger verification, affected-party review/notification when safe, and a controlled recovery path for disputes or unavailable guardians. Never silently transfer authority after a Home membership change.
- Identity guardians may manage the child's global username, independent-login password, trusted login devices, credential recovery and identity-wide authentication lockout within approved policy. Home guardians retain authority only over that Home's dashboard enablement, feature permissions, profile presentation and Home-scoped approved-device PIN rules. A Home secondary guardian's routine PIN assistance never implies authority to reset global credentials or enable independent login across Homes.
- A global credential reset or revocation must invalidate affected sessions/devices consistently and explain its cross-Home sign-in impact before confirmation, without exposing private Home data. Changes to access within one Home must not disable or expand access in another; the child's effective access requires both valid identity authentication and current Home-specific authorization. For child transitions to independent status, transfer global credential and recovery authority only after the verified age-aware claim flow.
- Recovery must protect against takeover, lockout and coercion: require verified guardian identity and appropriate step-up authentication, rate limits, short-lived recovery challenges, auditable outcomes and safe handling of conflicting guardianship claims. If authority is contested, restrict consequential changes pending review rather than automatically approving whichever Home requests first. Do not expose credentials, secrets, recovery tokens or another Home's membership information in notices or logs.
- Acceptance criteria: child belongs to two Homes with different primary guardians; Home-only guardian denied global reset; assigned identity guardian resets forgotten password; global reset invalidates appropriate sessions but preserves Home records; secondary Home guardian resets only an allowed local PIN; disputed identity-guardian change; lost primary identity guardian; unlink one Home; offline stale session; transition to independent account; notification privacy and cross-Home audit isolation.

### Confirmed child profiles and shared household device direction (13 October 2026)
- **Approved product direction:** Every person participating in Madeora has a distinct identity/profile for attribution and permissions, but **a child must not need an email address, self-service registration or a conventional adult account**. An authorized parent/guardian creates a child profile directly inside a Home, comparable in ease of setup to creating a pet profile but representing a person with distinct privacy, rights and potentially a login. A child profile is not a pet record and must have age-appropriate identity and protection rules.
- A child profile can participate in appropriate Home features (including chores, completions, rewards and assigned activities) **without a personal dashboard**. At creation, parent/guardian chooses whether to enable a child personal dashboard; authorized guardians can enable it later without recreating the profile or losing existing history. Dashboard access and independent sign-in are separate choices, not synonyms.
- Children have **restricted rights by default**, not ordinary Member/Administrator privileges. A child profile is managed by **one or two explicitly assigned parents/guardians**, subject to authorized Home membership and verified relationship/guardian assignment rules to be decided. Guardians control protected profile details, access settings, permission changes and sensitive operations; a child may edit only explicitly allowed non-sensitive profile fields and perform age-appropriate actions. Guardian access does not imply access to unrelated private accounts or Homes.
- Support a future **email-free child sign-in method** so a child with permitted personal access can sign in independently without using a parent's credentials. The credential method is specified below: guardian-managed username with device-limited PIN or independent password; age restrictions and detailed guardian authority remain to be decided.
- A kitchen tablet or wall display is a **Home-scoped shared device/session**, not a persistent sign-in as whichever adult opened it. Household members should be able to record eligible chore completions and other permitted shared actions without logging out and back in for each person. The device must identify **who performed each action** for accurate attribution, points, approval and audit; selecting a name alone must not grant adult-level permissions or reveal personal data.
- Shared-device access must remain restricted to a deliberately approved set of low-risk actions and explicitly authorized public/Home display data. Privileged operations (account/profile management, parental controls, permissions, purchases, sensitive personal details and protected content) require stronger individual verification or handoff to a personal session. Provide quick recovery from wrong-member selection, device revocation, idle/privacy behavior and protection against unauthorized visitors; precise verification and device enrollment rules remain to be decided.
- Treat child creation, guardian permissions, child-specific editable fields, independent login, shared-device attribution, and shared-device action security as separate upcoming product decisions. Reconcile these choices with existing Home invitation, account deletion, role, privacy and wall-display policies; **do not force children through adult email invitations** or let shared-display mode bypass authorization.
- Acceptance criteria for subsequent design: child added without email; child participates without personal dashboard; dashboard enabled later with history intact; one or two guardians; child cannot edit protected information; authorized child edits safe fields; email-free sign-in; chore completion attributed to correct child/adult from kitchen display; guest cannot become Administrator by choosing a name; device revoked; guardian removed from Home; shared-screen private data remains hidden.

### Confirmed guardian-managed child sign-in (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** An authorized guardian creates and manages each child's email-free sign-in identity and unique username. The child never needs an email address or adult self-registration to participate in the Home, and creating a child profile does not automatically enable independent login or a personal dashboard.
- On explicitly **approved household devices**, guardians may enable a child-specific **PIN sign-in** for convenient age-appropriate access. On independent/unapproved devices, permitted children use a **password** with their username; guardians decide whether independent access is enabled at all. Do not treat a short PIN as a general internet-facing credential or allow PIN-only remote authentication.
- Device approval is Home-scoped, attributable to an authorized adult, reviewable and revocable. Require suitable safeguards against guessing, including rate limits, temporary lockouts and protected credential storage; prevent shared-display member selection from bypassing child authentication or granting adult privileges. Do not expose PINs or passwords in Home settings or audit logs.
- One or two assigned guardians may issue, reset, rotate, disable or revoke the child's credentials and approved-device access according to their verified authority. A child may change permitted credentials only through an age-appropriate, guardian-authorized flow; credential recovery never requires the child to own an email address. Changes and recovery events are auditable without logging secrets.
- The child signs into **their own restricted identity**, not a parent's account. Permissions, accessible dashboard sections, profile editing, Home data and actions are enforced server-side regardless of device or login method. Dashboard availability, ability to participate in chores, and independent sign-in are separate guardian-managed settings; enabling them later preserves prior attributed activity.
- Account and guardian lifecycle: support lost devices, forgotten PIN/password, locked-out child, guardian reassignment, loss of guardian Home membership, and removal or suspension of a child's access without orphaning their chore/history records. Never silently transfer guardian authority or expose the child's private data to another Home.
- Acceptance criteria: child created with no email; PIN works only on approved device; username/password works only if independent access enabled; unapproved device cannot use PIN; wrong-PIN lockout; guardian reset/revoke; two guardians with correct authority; child dashboard enabled later; shared kitchen display cannot use child profile selection to elevate privileges; accessibility and privacy-preserving errors.

### Confirmed shared-device chore attribution and optional PIN verification (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** On an explicitly enrolled Home-scoped kitchen tablet or other shared display, an eligible household member can select their own profile and complete an available chore without switching the device into their personal account. Home managers can require member-specific PIN verification and/or require verification for particular actions; the stricter applicable rule wins. A selected name alone never authenticates privileged operations.
- The display presents only permitted Home-visible chores and a clear **Who completed this?** member-selection step, with the selected identity and chore shown before confirmation. Check eligibility, assignment/claim rules, completion state, required checklist, approvals, point allocation, age-appropriate restrictions and current permissions on the server. Do not let a member choose an ineligible person or bypass the approved chore workflow.
- PIN verification is short-lived, action-scoped and bound to the selected member, enrolled device and Home. Do not expose PINs, permit a PIN to authorize general account access or silently retain a verified identity for later users. Child PINs follow the separately approved guardian-managed, approved-device-only rules. For adults, PIN enrollment/recovery and whether they opt in to PIN verification require a separate account decision; never infer or reveal adult credentials.
- If a chore requires stronger verification, show the reason and a PIN prompt or secure personal-device handoff. If a person lacks an enrolled PIN, offer a permitted alternative rather than silently skipping verification. Managers may configure stricter requirements but cannot disable mandatory verification for sensitive account, permission, guardian or privacy actions; those actions remain unavailable from ordinary shared-display mode.
- Record the selected attributed member, device identity, verification level, timestamp and actual operation in permission-aware audit history; distinguish **attributed completion** from cryptographically verified identity when PIN was optional. Preserve immediate Undo and authorized correction, avoiding double points or duplicate completion under rapid taps, concurrency, offline retries or network failures. Display concise success/failure feedback without exposing private details.
- Shared-device privacy and safety: no implicit access to personal dashboards, private notes, passwords, settings or other Homes; clear transient identity selection and PIN entry after action/timeout; lock or restrict the device when revoked, offline beyond its permitted scope or no longer enrolled. Provide accessible large touch targets, member name/avatar disambiguation, child-friendly flows, incorrect-member correction and rate-limited PIN failures.
- Acceptance criteria: adult and child complete eligible chores by member selection; per-member PIN required; action-specific PIN required; both policies overlap; no PIN enrolled; wrong PIN and lockout; unassigned/claimed/approval-required chores; fast repeated taps; offline replay; wrong-member Undo; device revoked; visitor selecting manager name cannot edit permissions; no lingering member context after completion.

### Confirmed primary and secondary child guardian authority (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each child profile has **one primary guardian** and may have **one secondary guardian**, both verified adult Home members with explicit guardian assignments. The primary guardian controls guardian assignments and sensitive child access decisions; the secondary guardian can manage routine child settings within a defined permission boundary. Guardian status is distinct from Home Owner/Administrator roles: being a Home manager alone does not grant parental access to a particular child's private profile.
- **Primary-only sensitive authority:** Assign, replace or remove the secondary guardian; initiate a controlled primary-guardian transfer; enable/disable independent child sign-in, add or revoke independently trusted devices, authorize or disable a child's personal dashboard and its sensitive access scope, and change child privacy/permission policy. Credential recovery or rotation must not let a secondary guardian bypass primary-only independent-access restrictions; distinguish routine assistance with a device PIN from granting broader access.
- **Secondary routine authority:** View the child's permitted profile and activity, manage ordinary profile details, help with approved-device PIN resets within existing policy, and adjust allowed safe customization and day-to-day settings. A secondary guardian may not promote themselves, remove the primary guardian, grant independent access, widen visibility, or change protected identity/security information unless a separately authorized primary-guardian action delegates a specifically permitted operation; no implicit blanket delegation.
- Both guardians can assist with authorized household participation such as chores and age-appropriate settings, subject to the underlying Home and feature permissions. Children may edit only guardian-permitted avatar, preferred display name and appearance settings; neither child nor secondary guardian can use those edits to change login identity or protected records. Show the child's guardian roles and effective permissions to authorized adults in clear language.
- **Continuity and safety:** A child cannot be left without an eligible primary guardian. When the primary guardian leaves a Home, becomes unavailable, or loses eligibility, require a secure, auditable transfer/recovery process with verification and appropriate Home-level oversight; do not automatically elevate the secondary guardian or any Home administrator. Handle conflicts, pending transfers, guardian invitation/acceptance, and revocation without orphaning the child's activity or exposing private information.
- Apply server-side checks to all guardian operations, including shared displays, offline actions, API access and background sync. Require fresh strong adult authentication for sensitive changes, present an impact preview and confirmation, record actor/time/old-new authority, and notify affected guardians when safe and appropriate. Child PIN, shared tablet profile selection or a normal Home session cannot authorize sensitive guardian changes.
- Acceptance criteria: one guardian; add second guardian; routine secondary edit; denied secondary attempt to enable remote child login; primary changes independent login; child safe customization; primary transfer; guardian leaves Home; conflicting edits; child dashboard enabled later; shared-display privilege escalation blocked; audit, privacy and recovery.

### Confirmed age-aware child-to-independent-account transition (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Offer a guided, age-aware transition from a guardian-managed child profile to the same person's independent Madeora account once the person meets the **applicable legal and product eligibility requirements** for independent account management. Do not hardcode a universal birthday or treat reaching a certain age as automatic consent, immediate promotion or loss of guardian protection; determine applicable requirements from the person's jurisdiction and current policy.
- Provide age-appropriate advance information to the young person and authorized primary guardian. When eligible, the young person can **claim their existing identity** through a secure guided flow: establish independently controlled credentials and verified recovery contact as required, review the profile, active Home memberships, personal dashboard, data visibility, permissions, guardian access and notification/privacy preferences, and expressly confirm the transition. Do not require the child to have had an email address before the transition; request an appropriate independent credential/recovery method when needed.
- Preserve the same person's identity and eligible historical chores, rewards, contributions, habits, goals, achievements and personal settings without recreating them or transferring them into a guardian's account. Distinguish Home-owned records, third-party contributions, and personal records: transition does not silently grant ownership of Home data, reveal other members' private information, or expand Home role privileges. Continue honoring retention, consent, export and deletion rules.
- Guardian authority and independent control must change only upon successful verified transition, never merely because of a birthday. Before final confirmation, show which guardian controls and existing shared-device PIN privileges will end, remain as explicitly authorized Home functions, or require re-enrollment. After transition, former guardians do not retain privileged access to private account data or credential recovery solely because they were guardians; independent Home permissions still apply.
- Support postponement where permitted, failed/abandoned verification, lack of a recovery channel, guardian disagreement, temporary loss of access, cross-Home memberships, account-name collisions and interrupted/retried transitions. Preserve the managed profile and safe access until transition completes; do not create duplicate accounts or orphan history. If law or policy requires a different consent/guardian procedure, present that requirement explicitly and block an ineligible transition.
- Audit initiation, eligibility assessment, consent/verification, credential creation and completion without exposing secrets or unnecessary sensitive personal information. Notify relevant parties appropriately, provide understandable privacy choices, accessible mobile/desktop flows and a secure support/recovery path for disputed or mistaken transitions.
- Acceptance criteria: younger child remains managed; eligible child claims same identity; history retained; dashboard enabled after years of chore-only use; independent login/recovery established; primary/secondary authority ceases appropriately; old child PIN revoked/re-enrolled; guardian cannot view newly private account data; Home role unchanged; multiple Homes; failed verification; duplicate retry; jurisdictional age rule; no automatic birthday promotion.

### Confirmed quick child profile creation (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** In **Home → Members → Add child**, allow an authorized adult to create a guardian-managed child identity quickly with a **name, an eligible primary guardian, and only information genuinely required by applicable law or product eligibility**. Do not require email, username, PIN, password, avatar, secondary guardian, personal dashboard, independent sign-in, or complete permission customization at creation.
- Make the primary guardian explicit and validate their eligibility and acceptance/authority before activating the child profile. Default the proposing authorized adult when eligible, but never silently assign a guardian without authorization. If a legally required detail is missing, explain why it is required, collect the minimum necessary, and protect its visibility and retention; do not invent universal mandatory birthdate requirements.
- Immediately after successful creation, show a clear child profile and optional next steps: avatar/display preferences, optional secondary guardian, allowed child customization, dashboard activation, restricted permissions, approved-device PIN, and independent username/password access. Each optional step can be skipped and configured later through authorized child settings, without duplicating identity or losing activity.
- By default, the new child has **restricted Home participation permissions**, no independent login, and no personal dashboard until a guardian enables them. A child can still be assigned and complete eligible chores through permitted shared-device workflows. Do not force an adult Home email invitation or treat a child as an ordinary unrestricted Member.
- Validate name length and safe text, disambiguate duplicate names without forcing legal names to be unique, and show confirmation of the created identity and assigned primary guardian. Guard against duplicate submissions, offline retries, conflicting guardian changes and partially failed creation; avoid orphan profiles and support an authorized retry/recovery path.
- Respect primary/secondary guardian authority, multi-Home isolation, child privacy, Home membership, auditing and secure deletion/retention rules. Show optional configuration status without pressuring guardians to enable a child's personal dashboard or sign-in; adding these later preserves attributed history.
- Acceptance criteria: create child without email or birthday unless legally required; select eligible primary guardian; reject ineligible guardian; skip all optional settings; assign a chore before dashboard/login exists; enable dashboard and PIN later; add secondary guardian later; two children with the same preferred name; retry after connection failure; unauthorized adult blocked; cross-Home data isolation; protected identity fields and accessible setup.

### Confirmed age-appropriate child permission presets (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Provide understandable guardian-selected permission presets for managed children, initially **Young Child**, **Child**, and **Teen**, rather than treating a child's age as an automatic grant of access. The primary guardian selects and changes the preset; use a restrictive default on quick creation. Describe the capabilities of each preset before applying it; do not imply a preset establishes legal age or maturity.
- Presets provide sensible starting permissions for safe participation in Home features (such as viewing assigned chores, recording permitted completions, viewing earned rewards, and accessing an enabled personal dashboard). Permit authorized guardians to adjust specifically designated **safe feature capabilities** per child, without requiring field-by-field configuration of every product permission. Show which choices come from the preset and which are overrides; allow restoring preset defaults with a preview.
- Keep protected capabilities **non-overridable by any child preset or safe-feature adjustment**: guardian assignment, account recovery and credential policy, independent-login authorization, protected identity and birthdate information, child privacy/security policy, Home administration, membership/role changes, financial or integration credentials, and access to other people's private data. Enforce these boundaries server-side across dashboards, shared tablets, notifications, APIs, integrations and Ora, never only by hiding controls.
- Respect the established guardian hierarchy: the primary guardian controls sensitive access and guardian assignments; a secondary guardian may manage routine settings and only safe overrides within their granted authority. Neither a child nor an ordinary Home member may escalate privileges by changing presets, switching profiles on a shared display, or changing their display name. Child profile appearance edits remain separately guardian-permitted.
- Preset changes and overrides require an understandable **before/after access preview**, explicit confirmation for meaningful expansion of access, and an audit record of actor, date, affected child and effective permissions. Apply updates consistently to active sessions and approved devices; revoke or refresh permissions promptly and protect against stale/offline actions and conflicting guardian edits. Existing historical activity remains attributed to the same child.
- Personal dashboard enablement, independent username/password login, and approved-device PIN remain **separate guardian-controlled choices**; selecting a Teen preset never silently enables any of them. Do not automatically promote presets on birthdays. Where jurisdictional rules require additional restrictions or consent, apply those rules regardless of preset.
- Acceptance criteria: new child starts restricted; compare three presets; change safe chore/reward visibility; deny protected setting override; primary versus secondary guardian authority; switch preset while retaining explicit overrides with a clear review; restore defaults; child on shared display cannot elevate privileges; two guardians edit concurrently; offline stale permission denied; child later gains dashboard/login without a role escalation; age milestone does not auto-promote; multi-Home privacy remains isolated.

### Confirmed dedicated household display identity and privacy lock screen (13 October 2026)
- **Approved customized option A with optional C privacy mode:** A household display has its **own Home-scoped device login/identity**, distinct from every adult or child profile. After an authorized Home manager enrolls the device and signs it in as a **Household Display**, it launches **only the Home dashboard in purpose-built display mode**; it does not open personal dashboards, account settings, Home administration, or an ordinary member session. No individual member must remain signed in for the screen to function.
- In normal mode, display the **full authorized shared Home dashboard** by default, including eligible household widgets and information intended for a communal screen. “Full” means all **shared-display-safe Home content**, not all data in the Home: private personal data, protected child details, secrets, sensitive notifications, and administration controls remain hidden or redacted even if a widget is otherwise available. Respect the existing wall-display visual, responsive and privacy specifications.
- Provide an easy-to-activate **minimal privacy/party lock screen** for visitors or gatherings. It replaces sensitive/shared household details with a deliberately minimal, privacy-safe presentation (for example clock/date and an optional non-sensitive welcome message); no chores, member lists, schedules, rewards, personal names, private previews, or interactive member actions leak through it. The manager can choose to activate it on the display via an authorized control or remotely through an authorized personal session. Leaving privacy mode requires manager-authorized verification; tapping the screen or selecting a member must not unlock it. Privacy mode must survive refresh, reconnection and device restart until explicitly disabled.
- The display identity uses **separate revocable credentials/enrollment** created by an authorized Home manager, without reusing a child's username/PIN or an adult's password. Support multiple individually named household displays per Home, enrollment review, lost-device revocation, credential rotation, and clear device status. Enrollment must not grant ordinary Home Member/Administrator permissions or permit switching to another Home without fresh authorization.
- In normal mode, allow the previously approved low-risk shared interactions such as chore completion: members select themselves and complete eligible chores with **optional per-member/per-action PIN verification**. Each mutation is attributed to the actual selected/verified person, never to the display identity; the display's identity is recorded separately as the device origin in audit. PIN verification does not establish a persistent adult session or unlock protected controls. Enforce permissions, child restrictions, approval requirements, undo and duplicate protection server-side.
- Provide a straightforward **Return to display** behavior after a short action, and automatically clear temporary member selections and sensitive input. Keep manager-only setup, configuration, exit-from-kiosk, and privacy-mode unlock behind strong verification; avoid exposing account switching or login secrets to passers-by. If the device loses connectivity, show a safe read-only or privacy-preserving fallback and do not falsely claim chore completion.
- Acceptance criteria: enroll display without adult session remaining active; dashboard-only device login; full shared Home view without private information; child and adult complete chores with correct attribution and optional PIN; enable party lock screen and hide all household details; lock survives refresh/restart; unauthorized visitor cannot unlock; revoke lost display; two displays in one Home; multi-Home isolation; offline safe behavior; screen reader/touch accessibility; Home manager cannot expose private member data by selecting a shared widget.

### Confirmed manager-approved pairing for household displays (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Register a Household Display using a **short-lived, single-use pairing code** shown on the unregistered tablet. An authorized Home manager signs into their **own independent account session** on a trusted device, enters or scans the code in Home → Household Displays, reviews the target device, names it, selects the intended Home, and explicitly approves pairing. Do not require or create a reusable device username/password, and never share the manager's credentials or session with the display.
- Before approval, the unregistered screen shows only pairing instructions and its temporary code, **no Home information or household controls**. Codes expire promptly, cannot be reused, are rate-limited against guessing, and can be regenerated or cancelled. Approval binds the code to the initiating display session, Home, device record and manager approval; defend against code substitution, cross-Home pairing, concurrent claims, replay and stale approvals. Show a clear confirmation on both devices and an understandable retry path for expired, incorrect, already used or interrupted codes.
- After approval, provision a **persistent, device-specific, securely stored and revocable display credential** scoped exclusively to the approved Home and Household Display role. On refresh, restart or normal reconnect, the tablet resumes its restricted dashboard-only session without requiring a household member to log in. A device credential never inherits the approving manager's account rights and cannot access personal dashboards, other Homes, child settings, administration or privileged APIs.
- Home managers can see each enrolled display's chosen name, pairing/last-seen status and enrollment metadata, rename it, revoke it immediately, and re-pair it after a reset or lost credential. Revocation or Home deletion invalidates access on the server, including stale sessions and offline attempts; a revoked or reset display returns to the unpaired screen without showing cached sensitive Home data. Rotate credentials securely as appropriate, and keep device-specific audit records for pairing, failed attempts, renaming and revocation.
- Retain the approved normal shared dashboard, manager-authorized privacy/party lock screen and member-attributed chore actions with optional PIN. Pairing alone never unlocks privacy mode, authorizes a member action, or changes child/adult permissions. Provide accessible setup and recovery feedback on small tablets, and do not claim successful pairing until the server confirms it.
- Acceptance criteria: fresh tablet shows only code; manager pairs into correct Home and names display; manager closes their personal session while display remains active; restart persists restricted session; expired/used/wrong code fails; two managers attempt same code; two displays enroll independently; attacker cannot reuse code or access another Home; lost device is revoked and cached content hidden; network interruption during approval; privacy lock survives reconnect; display cannot open Home settings or impersonate a manager.

### Confirmed independent layouts for each Household Display (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each paired Household Display has its **own manager-configurable dashboard layout, widget selection, widget arrangement, orientation and display preferences**, scoped to that particular device within its Home. The kitchen tablet can prioritize chores and meals while a living-room display prioritizes shared calendar and household overview. Do not automatically synchronize layout changes across devices or treat a personal dashboard layout as a display layout.
- On pairing, initialize a usable, accessible default shared Home dashboard layout. An authorized Home manager may edit each device's configuration through Home → Household Displays, preview the actual device's orientation and responsive layout, save or cancel changes, and restore that display's defaults. The paired display itself cannot open manager configuration without separate manager authorization. Renaming, reconnecting or restarting a display must not erase its configuration.
- Restrict widget choices and widget data to **shared-display-safe information** regardless of manager selection, respecting child and personal privacy, multi-Home isolation, accessibility, wall-display behavior and the previously approved display-only role. Never allow a per-device layout to bypass authorization, expose private details, or enable privileged actions. Display-level interaction controls remain limited to previously approved low-risk member-attributed actions with applicable PIN rules.
- Treat orientation as a per-device preference with sensible auto/adaptive behavior for portrait and landscape, avoiding clipping, overflow and inaccessible touch targets. Preserve user-selected arrangement where practical across sizes; provide a predictable responsive fallback for unsupported layouts. Appearance, density, clock and screen presentation settings can differ by display without changing the Home's or a member's personal settings.
- The manager-authorized **privacy/party lock screen is independently activatable on each display**; activating it on the kitchen tablet need not hide the living-room display. Privacy mode overrides that device's entire configured layout and persists across restart/reconnect until manager-authorized unlock. Device revocation immediately disables its dashboard and configuration access; re-pairing a reset device requires manager review before restoring any prior layout.
- Save configuration changes atomically, handle simultaneous manager edits with conflict detection rather than silent overwrites, and record device configuration changes in a permission-aware audit. If a widget becomes unavailable or loses authorization, hide it safely and offer managers a non-sensitive configuration warning; a temporarily offline display must not falsely claim that a remote layout update has applied.
- Acceptance criteria: pair two displays in one Home; assign different widgets and arrangements; change portrait versus landscape preferences; refresh and restart each independently; modify kitchen layout without affecting living room; test privacy mode on one display only; reject private widgets and protected controls; manager concurrent edits; lost/revoked display; unavailable widget; offline reconnection; mobile-size and wall-size accessibility.

### Confirmed Household Display member-only actions (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Only registered, currently authorized members of the display's paired Home may perform state-changing actions from a Household Display. Visitors, babysitters, grandparents and other non-members may view only the approved shared-display-safe dashboard in normal mode; viewing does not confer participation, editing or completion rights. Do not introduce temporary guest profiles or a generic Guest actor for display actions under this decision.
- For eligible actions such as completing or claiming chores, require selection of an actual Home member and apply the previously approved configurable per-member/per-action PIN policy. The selected member must be active and eligible for the specific action; merely tapping another member's name never grants their privileges, bypasses approvals or authorizes sensitive actions. Preserve attribution to the member and separately record the device origin; enforce checks server-side against stale, spoofed and cross-Home identities.
- Restrict available display interactions to previously approved low-risk Home actions. Member-only status does not make account administration, guardian controls, protected personal data, Home settings, or privacy-mode unlock accessible on the shared display. A Home manager must use an independently authorized session for protected operations.
- When privacy/party mode is enabled, show the minimal safe screen to everyone, including members, until a manager-authorized unlock; hide actions, names and private/shared household details. Respect per-device independent layouts and privacy state. If membership is revoked, the member leaves the Home, or permissions change, block further actions immediately even if the display has cached their profile; clear transient member selections and inputs after each action or inactivity.
- Provide understandable, non-revealing feedback for visitors and ineligible members, and never pretend offline actions succeeded. Handle accidental taps, duplicate submissions, audit history, accessibility and quick return to shared display without requiring a persistent personal sign-in.
- Acceptance criteria: visitor can view safe normal dashboard but cannot act; unregistered babysitter cannot complete chores; registered child completes permitted chore with required PIN if configured; registered adult action correctly attributed; selecting a manager cannot open administration; former member's cached selection rejected; cross-Home member rejected; privacy mode hides actions for all; two displays enforce same Home membership rules; offline or duplicate submission fails safely.

### Confirmed Home invitations (12 October 2026)
- Authorized Home members can invite people by **email invitation** or **shareable invite link**.
- Invite links must **expire**, be **revocable**, and require the recipient to **sign in** before joining.
- An invitation never grants membership merely because someone opens a link; joining requires an explicit acceptance step.
- Invitation issuance, acceptance, cancellation, and expiration must respect the inviter's Home permissions and must not disclose private Home data before acceptance.
- The interface must distinguish pending, accepted, expired, revoked, and invalid invitations, with helpful error and recovery states.
- **Confirmed invitation role policy (12 October 2026):** New invitations default to **Member**. An inviter with permission to invite Administrators may instead select **Administrator** when issuing either an email invitation or an invite link. Do not assume that every user who can invite Members may also invite Administrators; this elevated capability must be explicitly authorized.
- The recipient must see the **proposed Home role before accepting**. The accepted role must match the authorized invitation; it must not silently escalate or change between preview and acceptance. If the inviter loses the necessary permission or the invitation is revoked, acceptance must fail safely.
- Invitations cannot confer the **Owner** role; ownership changes use the separately approved explicit transfer process.
- **Confirmed invite-link expiration (12 October 2026):** Shareable Home invitation links expire after **7 days by default**. When creating a link, an authorized inviter may choose **24 hours**, **7 days**, or **30 days**. Show the selected expiry before sharing and the expiry status afterward. Links can be revoked earlier; expired or revoked links cannot be used to join. The expiration countdown starts when the link is issued.
- **Confirmed invite-link usage (12 October 2026):** New shareable links are **single-use by default**: the first successful acceptance consumes the link. An authorized inviter may instead enable **multi-use** and specify a **maximum number of successful member acceptances** when creating the link. Each accepted new membership counts toward the cap; merely opening the link, signing in, or attempting an unsuccessful acceptance does not. Once the cap is reached, the link is no longer valid. Expiration and revocation apply to both modes.
- Show the chosen usage mode, maximum acceptances, remaining uses, and expiration to the inviter. The recipient must see the Home and proposed role before explicitly accepting; acceptance must be atomic so simultaneous attempts cannot exceed the cap. Avoid revealing private Home information before acceptance.
- **Confirmed multi-use cap range (12 October 2026):** The inviter can configure a multi-use link for **2 through 10 successful acceptances**, inclusive. Validate the cap at creation and enforce it atomically at acceptance. Single-use remains the default.

### Confirmed manager invitations and approved adult join requests (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Adults can join a Home through (1) an invitation issued by a currently authorized Home manager, accepted by the invited adult, or (2) an adult-initiated **join request** using a valid Home join link or code, explicitly approved by a currently authorized Home manager. **Possessing, opening or sharing a link/code never grants membership on its own.** The adult must authenticate with an existing account or create and verify a new adult account as required; child profiles follow their separate guardian-managed enrollment process.
- Distinguish **invitation links** from **join-request links/codes** in UI, wording, and authorization. Existing approved invitation rules (role preview, seven-day default expiry, 24-hour/seven-day/30-day choices, single-/multi-use caps, recipient-bound email invitations, active-invitation limit, revocation and explicit acceptance) remain in force. For adult membership, any earlier invitation-acceptance rule implying immediate admission is superseded: acceptance creates a **pending approval request**, unless a separate already-approved membership exists. Do not count opening a link, sending a request or manager rejection as a successful invitation acceptance; consume any single-/multi-use invitation capacity only on successful approved admission, with atomic checks. Pending requests must not reserve admission indefinitely or bypass expiration, revocation or caps; if authorization expires before approval, require a new valid invitation/request.
- A join-request code/link must be generated and shared by an authorized manager, be unpredictable, time-limited, revocable and scoped to one Home. Show only a minimal, privacy-safe Home identifier and the requested default **Member** role before submission; an applicant cannot request or self-select Owner or Administrator privileges. Administrator admission requires a separately authorized manager invitation and approval consistent with existing elevated-role policy. Prevent Home enumeration, brute-force code attempts, repeated unsolicited requests, account discovery and spam through rate limits, generic errors and abuse controls.
- Managers with the appropriate membership-approval capability can review pending requests, see the applicant's minimally necessary verified account identity and request provenance, approve or decline, and optionally cancel/withdraw outstanding invitations. Applicant and managers see accurate Pending / Approved / Declined / Expired / Withdrawn states, with accessible feedback; do not expose unrelated household member data before approval. Notify relevant parties with privacy-safe messages and allow applicant withdrawal while pending.
- **Approval is the membership boundary:** revalidate the manager's current authority, applicant identity and eligibility, Home status, role, code/link validity, capacity, existing memberships and concurrent requests at the moment of approval. Create at most one membership per adult/Home, with an auditable approving actor, requested role and timestamp. An invitation's role cannot silently change; approvals cannot grant a role higher than authorized. If an applicant is already a member, show that status rather than duplicating membership. Rejection grants no access and does not leak protected reasons.
- Membership requests do not grant dashboard, files, notifications, searches, Home APIs or Household Display action access until approved and membership is active. Changes, expiry, manager removal, Home deletion, failed account verification, duplicate requests, offline retries and simultaneous manager approvals must fail safely and remain auditable. Rejoining after revoked membership requires a new authorized process, never silent reinstatement.
- Acceptance criteria: manager invites new/existing adult; recipient accepts but awaits manager approval; manager approves/declines; adult requests using valid code; expired/revoked code; applicant withdraws; non-manager attempts approval; role escalation attempt; single-use and multi-use invitation capacity consumed only by successful admissions; simultaneous approvals; duplicate existing membership; Home deleted during pending; no pre-approval information leak; accessible mobile flow.

### Confirmed active invitation management (12 October 2026)
- A Home may have **up to 25 active invitations in total**, combining outstanding email invitations and active shareable links. Enforce this limit at invitation creation, including simultaneous creation attempts.
- An invitation counts as active while it can still result in at least one successful new membership. **Expired, revoked, accepted single-use, and fully consumed multi-use invitations do not count** toward the limit. An email invitation accepted by its intended recipient no longer counts.
- Authorized members can **view pending invitations and their statuses, revoke invitations, and resend email invitations**. **Resend** and **Renew** are separate actions with the confirmed semantics below. Neither action may bypass the 25-invitation limit.
- Show a clear limit-reached state with safe actions to review/revoke pending invitations; avoid exposing recipient email addresses to members without relevant membership-management permission.
- Preserve existing expiration, role, email verification, sign-in, and per-link successful-acceptance limits. The 25 limit concerns **active invitation records**, not a cap on Home members or the combined acceptance capacity of multi-use links.

### Confirmed email invitation resend and renewal (12 October 2026)
- For an outstanding email invitation, authorized members can choose **Resend** or **Renew** as separate, clearly labeled actions.
- **Resend** sends the existing invitation email again with the **same link, recipient, proposed role, and original expiration**. It does not create a second invitation or extend validity.
- **Renew** explicitly replaces the previous email invitation with a new invitation link and a **fresh expiration period starting at renewal**. The old link becomes invalid immediately, including when someone tries to accept it later. Preserve the verified-email recipient restriction and proposed role; any role change requires separately authorized editing or a new invitation.
- The new expiration must be visible before confirming renewal; whether email invitations offer configurable expiration durations beyond their existing policy remains a separate product detail. Renewal should not be represented as successful until the replacement is valid and the old link invalidated.
- Resend and renewal must be permission-checked, auditable, rate-limited against abuse, and handle email delivery failures transparently. Renewal must not accidentally create two active invitations or bypass the Home limit of 25.
- An already accepted invitation cannot be resent or renewed as a pending invitation; expired or revoked invitations require an explicitly authorized new invitation or reissue flow, to be specified separately.

### Confirmed email invitation account matching (12 October 2026)
- **Email invitations are recipient-specific.** Acceptance is allowed only when the signed-in Madeora account has the exact invited email address verified on that account. Possessing or forwarding the email invitation link does not grant a different account permission to join.
- If the signed-in account does not match or the invited email is unverified, do not accept the invitation. Explain the mismatch and offer safe sign-in, verification, or a request for a new invitation without revealing Home-private data.
- **General shareable invitation links are account-independent:** any eligible signed-in account can explicitly accept within the link's approved expiration, revocation, role, and usage limits. A link must not silently bypass Home membership checks or create duplicate memberships.
- Validate these conditions at acceptance, not merely on preview. Treat the invited address and verification status as sensitive and do not expose them to unrelated users.

### Confirmed Home roles
- **Owner:** full Home control, membership, settings, and permissions.
- **Administrator:** manages members, shared content, integrations, and dashboards within Owner-granted limits.
- **Member:** accesses and edits only capabilities granted by Home permissions.
- **Configurable module/capability permissions are required in the MVP**, not a post-launch enhancement.

### Confirmed Home ownership transfer (12 October 2026)
- Each Home must have **exactly one current Owner**; a Home must never be left without an Owner.
- The current Owner can initiate transfer **only to an existing member of that Home**.
- The nominated member must **explicitly accept** before ownership changes. Until acceptance, the existing Owner retains ownership and responsibility.
- The last/current Owner **cannot leave the Home** while a transfer is pending or without completing an accepted transfer first.
- The transfer must be atomic: on acceptance, the recipient becomes Owner and the former Owner ceases to be Owner; avoid transient ownerless states.
- Provide clear pending, accepted, declined, canceled, expired/invalid, and failed states; notify the relevant people and preserve an audit record. Pending transfer cancellation and expiry details require supporting specification.
- Transfer does not automatically change ownership of a member's private data or disclose their private events. Other Home permissions and linked-provider authorization remain enforced.

### Confirmed exactly one Home Owner (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Every active Home has **exactly one Owner**, reaffirming the existing ownership rule. Multiple simultaneous Owners and automatic recovery-successor ownership are not supported.
- Other adults may be Administrators with explicitly delegated Home management capabilities, but no delegated capability silently confers Owner status or authority reserved to the Owner, including ownership transfer and Home deletion.
- Ownership can change only through the existing explicit transfer flow: the current Owner nominates an eligible existing adult Home member; that member explicitly accepts; permissions and membership are revalidated; the atomic transition leaves exactly one Owner at all times.
- A pending, declined, expired, withdrawn or failed transfer does not change ownership. The current Owner cannot leave an active Home without a completed transfer; the previously approved guided transfer-or-close and 30-day deletion-recovery rules remain in force.
- Enforce these invariants in every client, API, offline retry, automation, and concurrent operation; maintain audit and appropriate notifications. Never auto-promote an Administrator, child, guest or Household Display.
- Acceptance criteria: two adults sharing responsibilities; Administrator with broad delegated rights; attempted second Owner assignment; concurrent transfer attempts; recipient losing membership before acceptance; Owner departure; Home deletion and restoration; permissions and audit.

### Confirmed verified Home ownership recovery (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** If the sole Home Owner cannot access their account, prioritize the standard account recovery route. When account recovery is genuinely unavailable, provide a **separate, carefully verified Home ownership recovery** process rather than leaving the Home permanently unmanaged. This is an exceptional recovery path, not ordinary delegated ownership transfer or automatic Administrator promotion.
- Permit an eligible, verified adult Home member to request review; no request, existing Administrator role, device possession, shared Household Display, join code, child guardian status or claimed family relationship alone authorizes takeover. Require strong independent evidence of identity, current membership, loss of Owner access and legitimate authority, proportionate to risk. Do not rely solely on email access, knowledge of household details, a single code or unverifiable self-attestation.
- Show a clear pending recovery state and enforce a meaningful waiting/objection period before any transfer. Notify the existing Owner through available verified channels and alert relevant adult Home administrators/members with privacy-minimized details; support cancellation by a verified existing Owner, dispute submission, investigation, rejection and appeal. Never expose private recovery evidence or credentials to other members.
- If a dispute, suspected compromise, contradictory evidence or unsafe recovery condition exists, **pause transfer** pending secure review. Provide transparent status and escalation/recovery options without promising an automatic takeover or a guaranteed resolution timeframe. Protect children, personal spaces and other Homes from spillover access.
- Successful recovery requires explicit approval through the verified process and acceptance by the eligible recipient; revalidate membership, roles, evidence and current ownership at commit time. Transfer atomically from the existing Owner to the recipient, ensuring **exactly one Owner at every moment**. The former Owner's global account and memberships in other Homes are unaffected; revoke their former Home Owner capabilities and Home access only as authorized by the resolved case, without silently deleting shared history.
- If the original Owner regains account access during the process, suspend or cancel pending takeover and require reassessment. After completion, offer a protected dispute/appeal path without automatically reversing a verified transfer. Record restricted audit evidence, notifications, decision rationale and status transitions with appropriate retention, access controls and security monitoring.
- Acceptance criteria: Owner recovers own account; unreachable Owner; authorized adult initiates verified recovery; unverified stranger/child/display attempts takeover; owner objects during waiting period; competing claimants; identity mismatch; suspended dispute; Owner returns before commit; concurrent transfer/removal; successful atomic handover; failed approval; multi-Home and child privacy; revoked device/session; recovery audit and appeal.

### Confirmed global adult notification defaults with per-Home overrides (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Every adult account has global default notification preferences for optional categories and delivery channels, with independently configurable **per-Home overrides**. Overrides apply only to the named Home; changes to global defaults propagate to Homes still inheriting them, without overwriting explicit Home-specific choices. Show whether each setting is inherited or overridden, and allow resetting to the global default.
- Distinguish account-level security and recovery alerts from optional household activity notifications. Essential security messages about authentication, account recovery, suspicious sessions, 2FA, sensitive account changes and ownership recovery use verified, appropriate channels and cannot be disabled by muting a Home or opting out of optional categories; avoid promising delivery when contact channels are unavailable. Do not use marketing preferences to govern transactional/security alerts.
- Support configurable optional categories such as invitations/requests, chores, tasks, calendars, meals, shopping, pets, rewards and reminders where the corresponding feature actually generates notifications. Channel choices are limited to channels genuinely supported and verified for the recipient (for example in-app, push and opt-in email); do not imply that every feature supports every channel. A Home-specific preference cannot enable an account-wide channel that has not been configured or consented to.
- Apply account-level quiet hours, timezone and accessibility settings as defaults, with Home-specific overrides where supported; explain timezone behavior when Homes differ, and respect previously approved module-specific reminder controls, opt-ins, snoozes, silent-update rules and Ora preferences. A broad Home notification toggle must not silently override an explicit feature opt-out or turn previously silent events into broadcasts.
- Evaluate the effective preference at delivery time using recipient identity, originating Home, notification category, channel, applicable module-specific settings, quiet hours, current membership and visibility. A notification from one Home must never inherit another Home's overrides or disclose its private content. Notifications for selected Home widgets on the global personal dashboard retain the **source Home** and obey its effective notification settings, not the currently selected Home.
- Provide an understandable settings overview with global defaults, a per-Home list, channel verification/permission state, preview of effective choices and an accessible reset-to-default action. Only the adult manages their own delivery preferences; Home Owners and Administrators cannot read or change another adult's private notification settings. Home-wide operational rules may suppress events but cannot force a member to opt into optional delivery.
- Handle device push permissions, undeliverable email, revoked Home membership, deactivated accounts, disabled integrations, expired sessions, offline devices and simultaneous preference changes safely. Recheck authorization before sending, minimize sensitive information on lock screens and shared devices, and avoid duplicate deliveries across multiple Homes/devices through idempotent notification identifiers. Keep privacy-minimized audit/debug information without exposing another member's settings.
- Acceptance criteria: default inheritance across two Homes; override only one Home; global change with/without overrides; reset override; category/channel combinations; quiet hours in differing Home timezones; essential security alert despite Home mute; unverified email/push permission; existing module-specific opt-out; silent vacation updates; selected Home widget; revoked membership; account deactivation/reactivation; Household Display and child session separation; accessible mobile settings.

### Confirmed temporary adult account deactivation (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Adults may voluntarily **temporarily deactivate** their Madeora account and securely reactivate it later, without requesting permanent account deletion. Preserve their personal data, Home memberships, existing roles and authorized shared Home history subject to ordinary retention and changes made by authorized Home members. Do not impose an automatic deactivation expiry or unconfirmed deletion.
- Before confirmation, explain that deactivation suspends account access, personal dashboards, account sessions, personal notifications and account-operated integrations; distinguish it clearly from signing out, leaving a Home, Home closure and the separate 30-day permanent-account-deletion recovery process. Require recent authentication and explicit confirmation, with accessible warning about ongoing household responsibilities.
- On deactivation, revoke all adult account sessions, refresh tokens and active API/real-time access, disable personal background jobs and notifications, and suspend personal third-party sync without deleting external provider data. A deactivated identity cannot sign in normally, perform Home actions, receive new account sessions or silently resume through Apple/Google sign-in; route verified authentication to the dedicated secure reactivation flow. Maintain only strictly necessary security and recovery notifications.
- **Home continuity:** Keep the person's memberships and roles recorded but mark their account as inactive to other authorized Home members without disclosing private reasons. Do not automatically transfer ownership, promote Administrators, remove shared Home history or delete Home-owned content. Surface inactive assignees and outstanding work to authorized Home managers for manual reassignment under existing task rules; do not silently perform chores, approvals or other actions on their behalf.
- A sole Home Owner may not deactivate while doing so would leave an active Home without an **operationally available Owner**. First complete an accepted ownership transfer or initiate the established guided Home-close/deletion flow, including its 30-day recovery responsibilities; if pending deletion still requires the Owner's restoration authority, retain a tightly scoped, verified recovery route and make that consequence explicit. Apply this independently to every Home owned by the account. An Administrator's deactivation does not confer their delegated permissions on others.
- Reactivation requires strong proof of control of the same adult identity through an existing verified sign-in method, with the account's enabled 2FA and risk-based recovery checks; protect against takeover, abuse and reactivation by another Home member. Create fresh sessions only after successful reactivation, show any intervening membership/role changes, and never restore revoked Home access or expired invitations automatically.
- Personal integrations remain suspended until the user deliberately reconnects or reauthorizes each integration; do not silently reuse stale tokens or resume third-party synchronization. Respect existing data ownership, visibility, child guardianship and per-Home permissions. Deactivation does not remove identity-level guardianship or alter another guardian's authority; flag essential guardian duties that need a safe authorized handover, without exposing child-private information.
- Deactivation and reactivation are auditable account-security events with privacy-minimized records and account-holder security notices.
