Enforce inactive state server-side across widgets, Ora, APIs, Household Displays, offline queues and integrations; offline devices may show cached data temporarily but cannot sync or execute actions, and protected caches should be cleared on reconnect.
- Acceptance criteria: ordinary adult with one/multiple Homes; Member and Administrator; sole Owner blocked until transfer-or-close; Owner with several Homes; pending Home deletion and recovery; active chores and delegated permissions; children/guardian duties; Apple/Google and password login; optional TOTP; all sessions revoked; integrations paused and deliberately reconnected; Home removal during inactivity; secure reactivation; offline queued writes; audit and privacy-safe inactive status; distinction from permanent deletion.

### Confirmed secure multi-account switching on ordinary shared devices (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ordinary phones, tablets and computers may retain **multiple separately authenticated Madeora accounts** and offer explicit account switching without signing every account out. This is not a new Home role, a shared account, or the separately approved paired Household Display mode. Each remembered account retains its own stable identity, memberships, global settings and independent sessions.
- Present an accessible account chooser showing only the minimal user-approved local identification needed to distinguish remembered accounts (such as permitted name and avatar). Allow adding an account through its normal sign-in flow, selecting an account, removing one remembered account from this device, and viewing which account is active. Never disclose another account's Homes, notifications, activity, credentials or private profile content on the chooser; do not infer that users of the same device belong to the same Home.
- **Switching is not authentication:** Require appropriate individual reauthentication before exposing a different account's private data. An adult uses their established sign-in and enabled optional 2FA as required; an eligible child uses their approved guardian-managed credentials/PIN and device restrictions. A remembered account must not silently unlock merely because a different account authenticated. Support a clearly explained, narrowly scoped short-lived unlock only where security review permits it; use idle locking and explicit sign-out to prevent shoulder-surfing or unintended access.
- Keep account tokens, protected caches, offline queues, push-notification routing, personal dashboards, selected Home widgets, active Home context, search results and integrations **strictly isolated per account**. On switching, hide the previous account's private UI immediately, cancel or partition pending requests, clear transient forms and action context, and ensure delayed network responses or background updates cannot populate the new account's view. Enforce account and Home authorization server-side for every read/write, not only through UI filtering.
- Device/session management remains **per adult account**: remote revocation, sign-out everywhere, account deactivation, password/security changes and Home membership revocation must invalidate that account's local access without automatically revoking other remembered accounts. Removing a remembered account clears its local tokens and protected cached data without deleting its global Madeora account or changing Home membership. Explain the difference between switching, locking, signing out and forgetting an account.
- For offline use, allow only previously authorized, protected and explicitly supported cached views/actions within the active account; never unlock a different remembered account without its required local verification, and never replay one account's queued actions under another. Revoked sessions cannot sync, and stale offline data must be cleared as soon as revocation is learned. Minimize lock-screen notification previews and prevent another account's notifications appearing in the active account's interface.
- On a shared or untrusted device, clearly communicate the privacy risk of remembering an account and provide an easy **do not remember me** choice. Use secure device-backed storage where available, short-lived credentials and anti-session-fixation safeguards. Never store plaintext passwords, child PINs, TOTP seeds or recovery codes to enable fast switching. Household Display pairing remains a distinct restricted device identity; this feature must not grant its visitor mode access to personal accounts or vice versa.
- Acceptance criteria: two adults with distinct Homes; adult and eligible child on one tablet; switch with optional 2FA; wrong PIN and lockout; no account/Homes leaked in chooser; different active Home contexts; overlapping network requests and push messages; offline switch and queued writes; one account remotely revoked while another remains active; deactivated account; account removed locally versus deleted globally; Household Display separation; keyboard, screen reader and narrow-screen account chooser.

### Confirmed long-term adult account inactivity reminders and security review (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** For adult accounts that have not had a qualifying authenticated interaction for a defined extended period, send privacy-conscious inactivity reminders and apply a proportionate security review when the adult returns. **Inactivity alone must never automatically deactivate or delete the account, remove or change Home memberships or roles, transfer ownership, revoke guardian status, or erase personal/shared data.** This is distinct from voluntary account deactivation, routine session expiry and confirmed security compromise.
- Define a transparent, configurable-at-product-policy (not user-specific guessed) inactivity threshold and reminder cadence in supporting specifications, with a clear last-qualifying-activity definition. Count authenticated account use across any Home and the personal dashboard; do not treat background sync, automated integrations, notification delivery, Household Display use or another remembered account's activity as proof that the adult was active. Avoid publishing a rigid threshold until approved.
- Before and after the threshold, use only verified account contact channels and existing essential account-notification rules to send a limited, non-alarming reminder. Do not disclose Home names, family composition, children, sensitive account activity or precise inactivity dates in subject lines, push previews or shared-device notifications. Respect channel deliverability, bounce/suppression, rate limits and applicable notification/legal rules; do not flood an unreachable address or make reminder delivery a prerequisite for account recovery.
- On return after extended inactivity, require fresh authentication if the session has expired and apply a **risk-proportionate security review**: confirm access to the current verified email/sign-in method, prompt review of unfamiliar sessions and linked providers, honor enabled optional TOTP, and use step-up verification for sensitive changes or suspicious signals. Never force enrollment in optional 2FA, disclose private security risk indicators or treat elapsed time alone as proof of compromise. Provide accessible recovery routes when a former contact method is unavailable.
- Preserve all existing account-wide and per-Home permissions and identities until an independently authorized change occurs. Existing Home managers may still use their established authority to reassign tasks or remove members, but no automated inactivity policy may silently do so. A returning member sees accurate current permissions and Home membership, not a stale offline snapshot. The sole-Owner and guardian continuity safeguards remain intact.
- Protect privacy in the member directory and Household Displays: do not publicly label someone 'inactive for years' or reveal their login recency merely because an inactivity reminder was triggered. Show only existing, authorized status indicators, and distinguish account inactivity from explicit deactivation and absence/vacation settings. Do not use login inactivity to infer a person's health, relationships or whereabouts.
- Make reminder attempts, security-review prompts and completed security actions auditable using minimized security records, without exposing internal risk rules. Prevent stale reminders after a successful return, duplicate messages from concurrent checks, repeated challenges in an already verified session, and cross-account leakage on shared devices. Support offline return, lost email access, social sign-in, optional 2FA, expired/revoked sessions, and users with zero or multiple Homes.
- Acceptance criteria: first inactivity threshold; repeated reminders bounded by cadence; activity on any Home or personal dashboard resets inactivity; background jobs and Household Display activity do not; failed/bounced contact; no auto-deletion/deactivation/membership change; Owner and guardian preserved; secure return and suspicious-session review; lost contact recovery; social login and TOTP; shared-device and offline privacy; accessible reminder and review flows.

### Confirmed categorized private account activity history (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C:** Provide an account-level **Account → Activity history** view of significant changes affecting the signed-in person's account. Categories are **Security**, **Privacy**, **Profile**, **Integrations**, and **Home memberships**. This is a private account record, not a Home activity feed, staff audit console or searchable directory of other users. Retain the separately approved **Security activity** view and dedicated downloadable adult security report as focused surfaces; do not replace, weaken or silently extend their access rules.
- **Events and attribution:** Show meaningful completed, attempted or rejected changes as appropriate: sign-ins and security/recovery actions (from eligible existing security history); privacy preference changes and guided-review completion (not mere viewing); account and per-Home profile presentation edits; connection, permission and revocation of third-party integrations; Home invitations, membership approvals, role changes, departures and removals affecting this account. Label event type, outcome, timestamp in the viewer's chosen display timezone, relevant Home context when still authorized, and safe actor attribution where permitted. Avoid logging every routine view, keystroke, draft or background sync as a user-facing event.
- **Search and filters:** Offer keyword search over **authorized, redacted event labels and permitted metadata**, category and outcome filters, date range, and relevant Home filter where appropriate; newest first by default. Filters and search must not reveal hidden raw fields, other members' private identities, removed-Home details or restricted internal security signals. Clearly distinguish no events, no matching results, temporarily unavailable history and records beyond retention. Support pagination or progressive loading and accessible clear/reset filters.
- **Visibility boundaries:** Only the authenticated account holder may view their adult account history; Home Owners/Administrators do not acquire access to another adult's account timeline. Apply existing independent-child and guardian access/consent rules to child-related entries; do not assume a guardian can read unrelated adult or child account-security details. Household Displays, public profiles, Home-wide activity feeds and other remembered users on shared devices cannot access this timeline. Historical Home events remain intelligible after leaving a Home without exposing its current members, private data or revoked permissions.
- **Redaction and retention:** Show privacy-minimized descriptions; never expose passwords, recovery codes, TOTP seeds, tokens, complete IP addresses, private email/recovery addresses, confidential support notes, precise location, private third-party payloads or internal fraud/risk logic. Apply the existing security-audit retention and legally required preservation rules to security events; define and publish appropriate category-specific user-visible retention for non-security events in supporting specifications. Do not promise indefinite history, allow filtering to reconstruct deleted/expired records, or permit users to edit protected audit records. Account deletion, exports and privacy requests follow their already approved ownership and retention boundaries.
- **States and safeguards:** Record changes consistently across devices and Homes; handle offline edits once synchronized, retries and duplicate deliveries without creating misleading duplicate events. If an operation fails or is reversed, show accurate status rather than a false successful change. Require authenticated access, refresh permissions when loading results, protect cached search results on sign-out/account switch, and provide clear loading, empty, error, retry and expired-history states. Use existing strong reauthentication rules for any sensitive drill-down or report download, not for ordinary safe event browsing.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Search and filter across all five categories; overlapping filters and date boundaries; correct Home-context labels; changes from two devices; failed versus successful integration changes; invitation pending/approved/revoked; leaving or losing access to a Home; personal versus child/guardian views; cross-Home and shared-device isolation; no sensitive values in search results or previews; retention expiry and legal holds; offline synchronization; accessible keyboard, screen-reader and responsive interactions; consistency with Security activity and personal data export.

### Confirmed downloadable adult security activity report (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** In Account Settings > Security activity, adults can request a **dedicated downloadable report** of eligible account-security events. This supplements, rather than replaces, the existing on-screen security activity timeline and category-selective general personal-data export. It is a user-initiated report, not automatic periodic delivery or a complete internal forensic/audit dump.
- Include eligible chronological events within the documented user-visible retention window: successful and relevant blocked sign-ins, session creation and revocation, password and primary-email changes, Apple/Google sign-in links, TOTP setup/disablement and recovery-code regeneration (never the codes), recovery actions, suspicious-session reports and account deactivation/reactivation. Include relevant exceptional Home ownership recovery affecting this account without exposing another member's confidential evidence. Provide understandable event labels, outcome, timezone-aware timestamps and optional privacy-minimized device/browser context. Identify the covered date range and any omissions or aggregation of noisy failed attempts.
- Require **recent strong reauthentication** before requesting or retrieving the report, respecting the adult's enabled 2FA and account recovery protections. Require a new authorization check at download time; rate-limit requests, prevent enumeration and cross-account access, and never allow a Home Owner, Administrator, guardian, child or Household Display to request another adult's report. Shared-device account switching must not expose report previews or downloads to a different remembered account.
- Offer an accessible report-request screen with date-range selection within available history, a clear explanation of included fields and excluded restricted security metadata, and explicit confirmation. Prepare large reports asynchronously with accurate pending/ready/failed/expired states; use a readable, portable file format (such as CSV and/or structured JSON, with a human-readable explanation) and a clearly identified timezone. Document the final format in supporting specifications without implying that the report contains all backend logs.
- Deliver through a **short-lived, authenticated download** in the account, not an unprotected email attachment or public share link. Notify the verified account contact that the report is ready without including sensitive report content or a reusable bearer download link; keep lock-screen notifications minimal. Protect temporary files and links from caching, indexing, cross-account leakage and unauthorized forwarding, expire them automatically, and allow the requester to discard an unused report early. Align with the existing secure export infrastructure where applicable, but do not silently assume that the general export's seven-day ZIP window is appropriate for a more sensitive security report; establish an explicitly reviewed shorter expiry in the supporting specification.
- Redact raw IP addresses by default, precise locations, secrets, passwords, recovery codes, TOTP seeds, session tokens, internal fraud/risk indicators, confidential support notes and third-party personal data. Apply documented retention, legal access and investigation exceptions: downloading a report does not enable editing or deleting protected security audit records. Explain that unavailable/expired records cannot be reconstructed.
- Handle account deactivation, pending account deletion, revoked sessions, concurrent report requests, mid-generation security changes, email changes, canceled requests and service failures safely. Freeze the report's stated snapshot/cutoff time, check authorization before release, and avoid mixing data from another Home or identity. Record report requests/downloads as privacy-minimized security events without recursively including newly generated report activity in the same snapshot.
- Acceptance criteria: request with and without enabled TOTP; stale reauthentication; report date filtering and timezone; readable export and accurate snapshot; pending/failed/expired/canceled states; sign-in and recovery events; redaction of sensitive metadata; another adult or Home manager denied; shared-device switching; revoked session between preparation and download; email address changed; deactivated/deleting account; repeated requests/rate limits; accessible mobile request and download flow.

### Confirmed adult account security activity history (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Provide each adult with a chronological, account-wide **Security activity** view in Account Settings covering significant sign-in successes and relevant failed or blocked attempts, session creation and revocation, sign-out everywhere, password creation/change/reset, primary email change requests and confirmations, Apple/Google sign-in method linking/unlinking, optional TOTP enrollment/disablement/recovery-code regeneration, recovery events and suspicious-session reports. Include relevant account deactivation/reactivation and exceptional Home ownership recovery affecting the account, clearly distinguished from ordinary Home activity.
- Each event shows an understandable action label, approximate timestamp with timezone, outcome (completed, blocked, pending or reversed where applicable), and privacy-minimized device/browser or approximate location context only when useful and reliable. Mark the current session where applicable, but do not imply that a device label or approximate location establishes who acted. Never display raw IP addresses by default, secrets, tokens, password hints, TOTP seeds, recovery codes, full verification links or unnecessary identifying details.
- Only the authenticated adult can inspect their account-wide security history; require appropriate reauthentication for especially sensitive details or actions. Home Owners, Administrators, guardians, other remembered accounts on shared devices, Household Displays and other Homes cannot read another adult's account-security timeline by virtue of their role. Security events concerning Home management may also have a separately authorized, minimal Home audit entry without exposing account-wide information.
- Provide filtering by event category and date where useful, clear ordering and pagination, understandable empty/loading/error states, and an accessible way to report an unfamiliar event. From a suspicious event, link to relevant existing controls (revoke session, change password, review linked providers, inspect optional 2FA, recover account) without implying that every event proves compromise. Critical independent security alerts remain enabled regardless of optional notification settings; viewing history does not replace them.
- Record security events reliably and tamper-evidently with server-authoritative timestamps and correlation identifiers for investigation; do not expose internal risk signals or sensitive metadata to the user interface. Apply documented, proportionate retention and data minimization, protect stored records and access, and distinguish visible history from restricted security audit records retained for legitimate safety/legal needs. Do not allow ordinary users to alter or selectively erase security events; respect applicable access, export, deletion and retention rights without weakening abuse investigations.
- Handle failed sign-ins carefully: avoid exposing another person's attempted credentials or enabling account enumeration, suppress noisy duplicates/rate-limit abusive attempts, and present meaningful summaries when appropriate. Treat asynchronous changes, delayed event delivery and concurrent device activity consistently; never show events from another account during shared-device switching or from another Home through a personal dashboard widget.
- Acceptance criteria: successful and blocked sign-ins; routine password change retaining current session versus reset revoking all; individual and global session revocation; email verification pending/completed; Apple/Google link/unlink; TOTP enable/disable and recovery codes; suspicious-session report; account recovery; event filtering and timezone display; duplicate or delayed events; inaccessible audit backend; privacy and retention; two accounts on a shared tablet; no Home-manager access; responsive keyboard and screen-reader support.

### Confirmed adult device and session management (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Every adult can inspect their own active account sessions, revoke one selected session, or **sign out everywhere**. Provide a privacy-conscious session list with recognizable device/browser/platform, approximate last activity, creation time where useful, and a clearly marked current session; avoid precise location tracking or exposing full IP addresses by default. Device descriptions are best-effort and not proof of identity.
- Treat sessions as **account-wide across all Homes**, with each Home's permissions evaluated independently on every request. Do not confuse adult login sessions with independently paired Household Display credentials or guardian-managed child sessions; their revocation controls remain separate.
- Individual revocation must invalidate that session's server-side authorization and refresh credentials, block subsequent API and real-time access, and prevent revoked sessions from re-establishing access via cached tokens. Signing out everywhere invalidates all adult sessions, including the current one, and requires fresh authentication; explain the effect before confirmation. Never revoke another person's account sessions based solely on a Home role.
- Offer a **Report suspicious session** action with immediate revocation and guided account-protection steps (review sign-in methods, change password when applicable, check 2FA and recovery options, revoke other sessions). Notify the account holder through a verified channel of suspicious sign-in or sensitive session changes without including secrets or unnecessarily detailed device/location information. Reporting does not by itself prove compromise or expose private security information to Home managers.
- Require recent reauthentication for sensitive account-wide session controls where appropriate, but provide a secure path for an already authenticated user to revoke an obviously suspicious session promptly. Apply rate limiting, CSRF protection, secure cookie/token storage and rotation, short-lived access tokens, refresh-token revocation and replay detection, with documented expiry and idle-timeout behavior. Do not add trusted-device enrollment or user-configurable session lifetimes under this option.
- Handle offline devices honestly: revocation takes effect on the server immediately, while a disconnected device may temporarily display previously cached information; deny further synchronization/actions and purge protected caches at the next connection or app activation. Ensure a lost Home membership or reduced permissions also blocks Home-specific data even if the adult session itself remains valid.
- Record privacy-minimized security audit events for sign-in, session creation/revocation, global sign-out and suspicious-session reports; protect retention and visibility, and never log raw tokens, passwords, recovery codes or full session secrets.
- Acceptance criteria: phone/laptop/browser sessions; current-session label; approximate last activity; individual remote sign-out; sign out everywhere; session expired between list and revocation; concurrent sessions and refresh-token race; suspicious-session reporting; optional 2FA and linked Apple/Google sign-in; offline revoked device; removed Home membership; multi-Home isolation; child and Household Display separation; accessibility and mobile layouts.

### Confirmed optional adult two-factor authentication (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Two-factor authentication (2FA) is **optional for every adult account**, including Home Owners and Administrators. No Home role or Home policy may silently require 2FA under this decision. Encourage adoption for privileged accounts without blocking ordinary access solely because 2FA is disabled.
- Offer time-based one-time passwords (TOTP) through standard authenticator applications, with QR/manual secret enrollment, proof-of-possession confirmation, clear enabled/disabled status and accessible setup instructions. Protect TOTP seeds at rest and in transit; never display them again after setup, include them in exports, or expose them to other Home members or Household Displays.
- Issue single-use, securely generated recovery codes at enrollment; show/download them once, store only protected verifiers, support deliberate regeneration that invalidates old codes, and clearly explain safe offline storage. Rate-limit verification and recovery attempts, prevent replay, and avoid leaking whether an account exists.
- For password-based sign-in, require a second factor after successful password authentication when enabled; enforce it consistently across sessions, API clients and supported devices. For Apple/Google sign-in, rely on the provider's completed authentication as the external identity proof, and explicitly define whether a separate Madeora TOTP challenge applies to linked social sign-in so users cannot bypass their chosen Madeora protection by changing methods. Prefer enforcing Madeora 2FA consistently for every enabled adult account sign-in method, subject to an explicitly reviewed secure federation design.
- Require recent strong reauthentication and appropriate second-factor/recovery verification before disabling 2FA, changing sensitive credentials, linking/unlinking sign-in methods or generating replacement recovery codes. Provide a carefully verified lost-authenticator recovery process with delay, notifications and abuse safeguards; possession of a Home role, shared display, child's PIN or unverified phone number is not sufficient.
- Preserve one global adult account's 2FA setting across all Home memberships, with no per-Home leakage of authenticator secrets or recovery codes. Never apply adult 2FA requirements automatically to guardian-managed child login or paired Household Display credentials; protect those through their separately approved controls.
- Log security-relevant enrollment, disablement, recovery and failed attempts without recording secrets or full codes; notify the adult through verified channels of sensitive changes. Provide clear recovery and lockout UX without weakening protection through support shortcuts.
- Acceptance criteria: optional enrollment by Member/Admin/Owner; TOTP clock drift and invalid code; recovery code single use and regeneration; password and linked Apple/Google sign-in; disabled 2FA normal sign-in; 2FA cannot be bypassed by another sign-in method; stolen/replayed codes; lost authenticator; unauthorized disablement; rate limiting; multi-Home account; shared-display/child separation; accessibility and mobile responsiveness.

### Confirmed customizable Administrator permissions (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** The single Home Owner may grant and revoke **individual, explicit management capabilities** for each adult Administrator. Provide a sensible, least-privilege default permission set, with readable descriptions of what each permission permits and its consequences. This extends delegated Administrator authority without creating another Owner or changing the Home membership hierarchy.
- Model capabilities separately from the Administrator role (for example: managing ordinary members/invitations, chores and rewards, calendars and shared content, Home settings, integrations, and Household Display enrollment/settings). Final capability names, dependencies and scope require a dedicated permission matrix. Never assume that access to one module authorizes management of unrelated modules or personal data.
- **Owner-only powers remain non-delegable:** ownership transfer, exceptional ownership-recovery approval, Home deletion/cancellation where reserved to the Owner, assignment/removal of Administrators and modification of Administrator permissions, and any other explicitly Owner-only security action. An Administrator with member-management capability may remove ordinary Members only, never another Administrator or the Owner.
- The Owner can inspect each Administrator's effective permissions, preview changes, and grant or revoke capabilities with clear confirmation for sensitive changes. Explain dependent permissions and potential loss of access; reject invalid combinations rather than silently broadening authority. Permission changes are Home-specific and never propagate to other Homes or global adult identity settings.
- Enforce effective permissions server-side for every action and data read, including API requests, Ora, integrations, mobile clients, Household Displays and offline sync. Revalidate on commit and after membership/role changes; revoked rights must not survive through cached tokens, stale UI or queued offline actions. Apply minimum necessary privileges to all newly introduced features by default.
- Log who changed whose permissions, which capabilities changed, previous/new values, Home, timestamp and outcome in a restricted audit trail. Notify affected Administrators about meaningful permission changes and surface security-sensitive grants to the Owner. Do not leak private profile or recovery evidence in logs or notices.
- Acceptance criteria: new Administrator gets safe defaults; Owner grants and revokes one capability; two Administrators have different effective rights; authorized Member removal but blocked Administrator removal; attempted Owner-only operation; privilege escalation via API/Ora/offline replay; simultaneous permission revocation and action; Home switching; audit, notifications, accessibility and responsive permission editor.

### Confirmed Home member-removal authority (12 October 2026)
- The **Home Owner** may remove Members and Administrators, but cannot be removed by another Home member. An Owner wishing to leave must first complete the approved ownership-transfer process.
- An **Administrator with the membership-management permission** may remove **regular Members only**. Administrators may not remove the Owner or other Administrators, even if they have membership-management permission.
- Regular Members cannot remove other Home members.
- The interface must hide or disable unauthorized removal actions, and the server must reject unauthorized attempts, including through Ora, integrations, and direct requests.
- Removal requires an explicit confirmation explaining the consequences for access, Home-owned items, personal shares, and outstanding assigned tasks. Apply the approved departure/content-ownership and unassignment policies.
- Member-removal actions must be recorded in a permission-protected audit trail. Define retention and notification details in supporting specifications.

### Confirmed adult Home removal authority reaffirmation (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B (reaffirmed):** The Home Owner may remove adult Members and Administrators. An Administrator may remove **ordinary Members only** when explicitly granted the membership-management/removal capability by the Owner; Administrators may never remove the Owner or other Administrators. Regular Members cannot remove others. This confirms, rather than replaces, the existing 12 October Home member-removal authority rule.
- **Owner protection:** Nobody can remove the current Owner; ownership must first be transferred through the existing explicit acceptance workflow before the former Owner may leave. Do not treat an unaccepted transfer as authorization to remove the Owner.
- **Authorization at action time:** Validate actor role, specifically granted permission, target's current role, active membership and Home identity on both preview and final submission. If roles or permissions change during confirmation, fail safely and refresh; reject direct requests, automation, integrations, Household Display interactions and stale/offline retries that bypass the UI.
- **Consequences and confirmation:** Before removal, identify the affected adult and Home, show loss of access, impact on assignments, approvals, pending invitations, Home-owned records and personal shares, and require explicit confirmation. Apply the established data-retention, ownership and reassignment rules without silently deleting another person's personal data or unrelated Home memberships.
- **Immediate revocation:** End the removed adult's access to this Home across active sessions, notifications, APIs, integrations and Home-specific shares where applicable, without terminating their global account or memberships in other Homes. Preserve permission-filtered audit records and provide privacy-safe notifications; reinstatement requires a new approved membership process.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Owner removes Member/Administrator; authorized Administrator removes Member; unauthorized Administrator fails; Administrator attempts to remove Administrator or Owner; Owner tries to leave without transfer; concurrent role change during confirmation; active session and offline device access revoked; assigned work handled; multi-Home account retains other Homes; repeat removal idempotently rejected or reported as already removed.

### Confirmed privacy-minimized Home history after adult departure (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When an adult voluntarily leaves or is removed from a Home, preserve the minimum Home-owned shared history necessary for accurate completed chores, task activity, reward/points ledgers, shared records, accountability and aggregate statistics. Preserve limited historical attribution only where genuinely needed to understand the Home record, and minimize exposed personal identifiers. This refines the existing limited-attribution rule, not the distinct permanent-account-deletion anonymization rule.
- Keep an appropriate historical display name/snapshot when needed for attribution, but remove live profile links, current avatar/contact information, optional birthday/phone/preferences, identity/account identifiers and access to personal/private items. Do not let a Home Owner or Administrator access or edit the departed adult's global personal profile. Use a neutral former-member label where identity is unnecessary, and apply viewer-specific privacy permissions in histories, search, exports, reports, notifications, Ora and Household Displays.
- The departed adult loses Home access immediately across sessions, APIs, devices, cached views and integrations, but retains their global account, other Home memberships and personally owned data. Home-owned records do not become the departed member's private property, and the former member cannot erase other members' shared history by editing their own profile.
- Provide an authenticated personal-data review/export/deletion or correction request path for eligible data concerning the former member, subject to legal rights, justified Home record retention, other members' rights and security/audit requirements. Clearly distinguish changes to their own personal data from changes to Home-owned shared records; handle requests transparently without guaranteeing deletion of records that must lawfully or legitimately be retained. Do not expose the Home's current private data to a former member through this process.
- Preserve established Home-specific points balances and historical ledgers without allowing redemption while absent; keep completed activity totals stable, flag future assignments for authorized reassignment, and retain appropriate immutable audit evidence. Account deletion after its recovery period separately triggers the approved stronger Home-visible **“Former member”** anonymization, with necessary legal/security exceptions.
- Confirm departure consequences before voluntary exit or authorized removal, and record authorized removal and privacy-related changes with appropriate audit access. Protect against stale offline updates, race conditions, removal and rejoining, linked personal shares, multi-Home accounts and duplicate processing.
- Acceptance criteria: adult leaves versus manager removes; old completed chore and points ledger remain accurate; former display name appears only where needed; profile/email/phone not exposed; Home wall display privacy; personal-data request without rewriting another member's record; private shares revoked; historical exports; adult rejoins; account permanently deleted and anonymized; concurrent removal and edit; other Home memberships unaffected.

### Confirmed editable starter personal dashboard for new adult accounts (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Immediately after successful adult registration, provide a small, useful, **fully editable starter personal dashboard** without requiring the account to create or join a Home. The default layout is a starting point, not a mandatory onboarding wizard or a fixed product experience.
- Seed a modest, balanced set of **personal-only** widgets, initially including a personal calendar overview, personal tasks/to-dos and relevant personal quick actions where supported by the approved module specifications. Use empty states and helpful create/add actions when there is no data; never fabricate events, tasks, goals or sample household members as if they were real. Do not add a Home chore, shared calendar, member directory or other Home-dependent widget for a Home-less account.
- Arrange the starter widgets with clear hierarchy and responsive layouts for desktop, tablet and phone, consistent with Madeora's approved visual reference, light/dark appearance and accessibility rules. Keep the first view useful without excessive widgets, intrusive prompts or forced customization. A simple optional Create a Home / Join a Home affordance may remain visible outside the widget content without blocking personal use.
- The account holder can immediately **move, resize where supported, configure, add, hide or remove every starter widget**, including removing all widgets to create an intentionally empty dashboard. Provide an obvious Add widgets action, accessible edit controls, and a reversible reset-to-starter-layout action that previews its impact and does not delete underlying personal records. Distinguish restoring a layout from clearing user content.
- Persist the customized personal layout with the adult account across supported devices and Home switches, following the existing per-device/responsive layout rules. Never silently restore removed starter widgets on sign-in, device change, joining a Home, leaving a Home, reactivation or application updates. Existing adults retain their configured dashboards; any future starter-layout improvements must not overwrite them.
- After joining a Home, keep the personal starter widgets personal and private. The user may **explicitly** add individually labeled, authorized Home widgets under the approved selected-Home-widget policy; no automatic cross-Home data mixing, sharing or permission changes. An adult without Homes must still be able to use all eligible personal widgets and the widget catalog should clearly explain why Home-dependent widgets are unavailable.
- Handle loading, sync failure, offline creation, partially available modules, calendar integrations not connected, empty task lists, duplicate edits and deleted/unavailable widget types without breaking the dashboard. Apply current privacy and authorization checks to widget data, actions, search, Ora, notifications and caches; shared Household Displays have their own separate default layout and must not inherit this personal starter dashboard.
- Acceptance criteria: brand-new verified adult with zero Homes sees usable calendar/tasks/quick actions; empty-data prompts are truthful; skip Home creation; reorder, resize, remove all, add and reset widgets; refresh and sign in on another device; offline/failed widget; accessibility and responsive layouts; join first and second Home without automatic Home widgets; explicitly add labeled Home widget; leave all Homes; existing customized adult dashboard not overwritten; no Household Display inheritance.

### Confirmed selected Home widgets on the global personal dashboard (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each adult has one persistent personal dashboard across all their Home memberships. They may explicitly add supported widgets sourced from individually selected, currently authorized Homes. This is a deliberate, widget-level cross-Home view **within personal space**, not a combined multi-Home dashboard or a merger of Home-owned records. It refines the earlier prohibition on an automatic combined Home dashboard without changing the explicit active-Home switcher or Home isolation rules.
- Every Home-sourced widget must clearly and persistently identify its source Home (including compact/mobile layouts, accessibility labels and relevant notifications). Home widgets are opt-in individually, never automatically added when joining a Home. Personal widgets remain separate and are not automatically published back to Homes.
- Scope each widget's queries, actions, links, caches and permissions to its explicitly selected Home. Check current membership and item-level authorization when displaying and acting; do not rely on the currently active Home or allow one Home's data to bleed into another widget. Actions from a widget must clearly identify the affected Home and follow that Home's normal validation, confirmations, attribution and audit rules.
- If membership, a permission or source-widget availability is lost, promptly stop showing protected content, invalidate cached/offline copies, and show a neutral unavailable/remove-or-reconfigure state without disclosing inaccessible data. Preserve a user's unrelated personal layout and other Home widgets. If a Home is renamed, update its label without changing the binding.
- Respect the Home's child privacy, shared versus personal data boundaries, sensitive fields, display restrictions, notifications and cross-Home policies. A personal dashboard is not a Household Display: the latter remains permanently scoped to one paired Home and cannot show cross-Home widgets.
- Support adding, moving, resizing, configuring and removing eligible Home widgets in the existing personal dashboard editor, including multiple distinct Homes and repeated supported widget types where appropriate. Provide an accessible source-Home selector, source labels, empty/loading/error states, mobile layout behavior and a safe preview before adding.
- Acceptance criteria: adult belongs to two Homes with different roles; add one widget from each; source labels remain visible after switching active Home; act on a widget for non-active Home without misrouting; permission revoked; leave Home; Home renamed; offline cached content; child/private information excluded; Household Display cannot use this feature; remove a widget without deleting its source records; screen reader labels and narrow layouts.

### Confirmed global adult avatar and display name with per-Home overrides (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each adult identity has one global default **display name and avatar**, with optional, independently configured **display-name and avatar overrides per Home**. Overrides change only how the same adult is presented within that Home; they never create another account, alter credentials, change identity-level guardianship, or combine Home memberships and permissions.
- On joining a Home, inherit the global name/avatar unless the adult explicitly chooses Home-specific values. Allow changing, clearing and resetting either override separately to the global default; global updates affect only fields still inheriting the default. Clearly distinguish global and Home-specific settings, with a preview for each Home and its eligible shared surfaces.
- Adults control their own global and Home-specific presentation. Owners and Administrators cannot impersonate another adult by changing their avatar or preferred display name; any separately authorized Home-specific administrative labels must remain distinct and cannot modify account identity. Validate text, file type/size, image safety and accessibility alternatives; avoid exposing original image metadata or private source files.
- Render the effective Home-specific name/avatar consistently in that Home's member directory, chores, assignments, calendar participation, rewards, activity feeds and other Home-scoped interfaces. On the adult's global personal dashboard, use the global identity for account-level UI; each selected Home widget uses that source Home's effective presentation and visible Home label. A Household Display uses only its paired Home's effective, shared-safe profile presentation and privacy rules.
- Per-Home overrides do not override per-field privacy controls: private or unapproved profile data stays hidden, and a name/avatar visible to Home members is not automatically public to visitors, exports, integrations or a Household Display. Enforce visibility and authorization in APIs, caches, notifications, search, Ora and offline clients; revoke access promptly after leaving a Home or changing visibility.
- Preserve historical shared records and the established privacy-minimized former-member attribution when a member leaves; do not use an old avatar, current cross-Home alias or live profile link to re-identify a departed member. Account deletion follows the separately approved stronger anonymization rules. Keep audit identity stable and internal, not inferred from mutable display names.
- Acceptance criteria: global default in two Homes; distinct name/avatar in one Home; reset one override while retaining the other; global name/avatar change propagates only to inheriting Homes; duplicate display names do not confuse identity or action attribution; unauthorized manager edit denied; avatar removal; Household Display visibility; selected Home widget; leave Home; offline cache revocation; screen-reader identification and responsive editing.

### Confirmed simple Home member directory without custom groups (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Each Home has a straightforward, permission-filtered member directory presenting its adult and child members in one coherent list. Provide optional sorting and filters for adults, children and current Home roles, but **do not support custom member groups**, group names, group membership administration, or group-based bulk targeting. This choice is about directory organization and does not change existing role, guardian, household membership or chore-assignment rules.
- Display each member's effective Home-specific nickname/name, permitted avatar, personal identifying accent and role or age-appropriate member category as authorized. Duplicate names and accent colors remain allowed; use accessible additional labels or context when necessary, never color alone. Do not expose private account fields, guardianship details or other Home memberships simply to populate a directory.
- Offer a clear default ordering and optional alphabetical/name, member-type and role sorting where meaningful. Filters must be reversible, combinable where sensible, clearly indicate active criteria and results count, and provide useful empty/no-match states. Preserve a member's own view preferences without altering another person's directory or the Home's underlying data.
- Selecting a member opens only that viewer's authorized Home-scoped profile or actions. Managers see management controls only for capabilities actually granted to them; child and former-member privacy, role hierarchy and current membership checks apply throughout. A Household Display receives a restricted, shared-safe directory view with no privileged management actions.
- Existing multi-member actions, such as assigning a chore to several individually selected members, may use explicit member selection where separately authorized; this decision **does not introduce persistent groups**, group-based permissions, group shortcuts, automatic family relationships or bulk actions. Never infer a group from roles, surnames, shared addresses or guardianship.
- Handle pending invitations separately from active members; clearly distinguish inactive/deactivated or departed members only where their status is legitimately visible. Update lists and controls safely after join, removal, role changes, name/color changes, offline reconnection and permission revocation without exposing stale member data.
- Acceptance criteria: one-member and large Home; adult/child/role filters and reset; sorting and empty results; duplicate names/colors; per-Home nicknames; accessible member disambiguation; manager versus ordinary member actions; child privacy; paired Household Display; multi-Home isolation; no create/edit/use custom group feature; offline/stale membership changes; keyboard, screen reader and narrow-screen layouts.

### Confirmed optional effective-permissions overview after joining a Home (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Once Home membership is approved and activated, offer a short, **optional, skippable** explanation of what the joining member can currently view, do, manage and personalize. Do not gate Home access on completion or confuse the overview with invitation approval, permission granting or required training. Coordinate with the separately approved skippable welcome personalization so both steps form a concise, non-repetitive welcome experience and can be skipped independently.
- Base the overview on **effective permissions in the selected Home**, not just the role label: Owner-only authority, individually customized Administrator capabilities, ordinary Member rights, child/guardian permissions, feature availability and any relevant Home-specific restrictions. Group explanations in plain language such as **You can**, **You can manage**, and **Ask an authorized manager about**; avoid listing controls the user cannot access as actionable buttons.
- Show the current Home name, effective role and meaningful permitted capabilities, with contextual links to available features. Clearly distinguish account-wide abilities (such as personal profile and session settings) from Home-specific powers. An Administrator's customized grants must be reflected accurately; do not suggest all Administrators share the same permissions. For children, use age-appropriate language and only explain abilities available under their approved profile/device access, without exposing private guardian details.
- Keep an accessible **My permissions in this Home** view in Home Settings for later reference, including the last refreshed state and an option to revisit the explanation. On role changes, permission grants/revocations, child access changes, Home feature changes or membership removal, recalculate the displayed effective permissions and avoid stale claims. Where a change materially affects the member, provide an appropriate in-app explanation or notification according to their preferences, without unsolicited repeated onboarding.
- Do not reveal another member's private permissions, protected identity details, other Home memberships, security settings or hidden modules. Do not use the overview to bypass server-enforced authorization; backend checks remain authoritative for every action, including Ora, integrations, APIs and offline synchronization. Handle partial loading, offline sessions and revoked access with clear, safe states rather than displaying unverified permissions as current.
- Acceptance criteria: adult Owner, ordinary Member and two Administrators with different custom grants; approved new member skips and later revisits; welcome personalization skipped independently; child with and without profile access; permissions changed while overview is open; Home switch with distinct role; disabled module; offline/stale permissions; membership revoked; keyboard, screen-reader and mobile presentation.

### Confirmed optional welcome personalization for new Home members (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** After an adult successfully joins a Home through the existing invitation/request-and-approval process, offer a brief, **optional and skippable** welcome step to preview and personalize their effective Home nickname/display name, avatar and member-identifying accent color. Never make personalization a condition of access, imply it grants membership approval, or delay essential Home functionality; skipped fields inherit existing global/default Home values.
- Show the currently inherited name, avatar and accent clearly, and distinguish **Use global default**, **Set Home override** and **Reset to default**. Nickname is an optional preferred Home label, not a second competing identity. Provide a preview of how the member appears in the Home directory, chores/activity attribution and privacy-eligible shared surfaces, without revealing protected member data or suggesting that shared-display visibility is automatically granted.
- Explain that personal accent colors identify contributors across Home activities but **are not unique identifiers**. Warn non-blockingly about identical/visually similar member colors, suggest accessible alternatives and always retain names plus permitted avatars/icons or contextual labels. Do not compare or disclose colors or membership from other Homes.
- For children, offer equivalent age-appropriate personalization only when the child has approved profile access and the applicable profile-edit permissions allow it. Children may select/change their own accent by default when they have profile access, subject to guardian reset; child nickname/avatar editing remains governed by established guardian permissions. For children without profile access, guardians may configure eligible Home presentation without forcing child onboarding or enabling credentials.
- Respect per-Home privacy and identity boundaries: adults edit only their own presentation; no Owner/Administrator may use onboarding to impersonate an adult or bypass profile visibility. Home-specific changes must not overwrite global profile data or other Homes' overrides. Home managers cannot see or modify private global settings just because they invited someone.
- Save each field independently with validation and accessible controls; allow back, skip, retry and resume without creating duplicate overrides. Handle avatar upload failure, offline status, concurrent profile edits, expired invitations, withdrawn approval, removed membership and interrupted setup without exposing Home content or losing other successfully saved fields. Make the same controls available later in Home profile settings, and avoid automatically repeating the welcome flow after routine sign-in or Home switching.
- Acceptance criteria: approved adult join with inherited defaults; skip all; nickname/avatar/color independently overridden; preview and reset; duplicate-color warning does not block; two Homes remain isolated; unapproved join sees no Home data; child with/without profile access; guardian resets child color; avatar upload failure; revoked membership mid-setup; mobile/keyboard/screen-reader support; shared Household Display privacy.

### Confirmed optional per-Home nicknames and duplicate member names (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Allow each adult or child to have an optional **Home-specific nickname** used as their preferred member label in that Home. A nickname is a presentation override, not a new identity, credential or membership; duplicate or visually similar display names and nicknames are allowed and must not prevent saving. Existing global display names and per-Home display-name overrides remain supported: expose a single clear effective Home name, distinguishing a preferred nickname from an ordinary Home display-name override rather than introducing competing unexplained labels.
- Adults control their own nickname. Children may edit a nickname only when their established guardian-controlled, age-appropriate profile-edit permissions permit it; authorized guardians may manage protected child profile naming. Home managers cannot silently rename another adult or use an administrative label to impersonate them. Validate length, trimming, unsupported characters and misleading or abusive text; allow clearing a nickname to fall back to the effective Home display name.
- Render the effective permitted Home name/nickname consistently in member directories, chores, calendars, assignments, rewards, activity history, Ora interactions and Home-sourced widgets. Global account surfaces use the global display name; a paired Household Display uses only its Home's privacy-approved member presentation. Nicknames from one Home must never appear in another Home without an explicit independent choice.
- **Duplicate-name disambiguation:** Keep stable internal account/member IDs for all records and actions. When two members share a visible name, supplement their name with permitted avatar, member-specific icon or accessible contextual qualifier (such as a clearly labeled profile selector), never color alone. Screen readers and keyboard users must be able to distinguish options; Ora must request clarification before acting on ambiguous names rather than guessing. Do not reveal private names or other Home memberships to disambiguate.
- Historical records retain stable actor identity and follow the approved privacy-minimized attribution policy for former members; nickname changes do not rewrite audit actors or silently alter another person's records. Handle deleted avatars, identical colors, missing nicknames, offline edits, concurrent changes, Home departure and account deletion without mistaken attribution or stale private profile disclosure.
- Acceptance criteria: two members named Alex with identical colors; optional nicknames in one Home but not another; reset nickname and inherit Home/global name; adult versus guardian-controlled child editing; duplicate nicknames accepted; unambiguous chore attribution and accessible member selection; Ora clarification; cross-Home widget and Household Display privacy; member leaves Home; offline/concurrent nickname changes.

### Confirmed personal accent color with per-Home overrides (13 October 2026)
- **Approved extension to option B:** Each adult chooses a **global personal accent color** for their account and personal dashboard, with optional **per-Home accent-color overrides**. The global choice is the default for every Home unless the adult explicitly overrides it for that Home; these are appearance preferences, not identity, membership, role or access-control changes.
- Show inherited versus overridden color in account and Home appearance settings, with live accessible previews. Allow selecting a curated solid-color palette or a validated custom solid color, changing or removing individual Home overrides, and resetting a Home to inherit the global value. A global accent change updates only Homes that still inherit it; Home-specific overrides remain intact.
- Apply the global accent to account-level navigation and the adult's global personal dashboard. Within a selected Home context, use that adult's effective accent for the Home, including their Home dashboard and eligible member-facing surfaces. Home-sourced widgets embedded on the global personal dashboard must remain clearly labeled with their source Home; use consistent global dashboard chrome and avoid silently imposing another Home's accent on unrelated widgets. If a widget uses a source-Home accent as a visual cue, keep the Home label explicit and contrast-safe.
- Accent overrides are **personal presentation preferences**, not a way for an adult to change another member's UI or the shared Home brand/theme. Owners and Administrators cannot set another adult's personal accent merely because of their Home role. Household Displays keep their own device/Home appearance configuration and do not inherit the viewing adult's global or per-Home accent. Child accent selection follows the separately approved child-controlled default with guardian reset; other protected child profile settings remain guardian-managed.
- Maintain accessible contrast, visible keyboard focus and distinguishable semantic status colors in both light and dark mode; derive hover, pressed and focus states from safe tokens rather than using arbitrary user colors unmodified. Respect reduced-transparency and accessibility settings, and preserve the established ban on element-specific gradients. Fall back safely if a custom color fails validation or a device cannot render it.
- Store global and per-Home preferences separately and synchronize them only to authorized sessions; clear or stop applying an override after Home departure without exposing other Homes. Include accessible color selection beyond hue alone, a reset control and responsive previews. Test two Homes with different overrides, global changes, reset/inheritance, cross-Home personal widgets, shared Household Display, invalid/low-contrast colors, light/dark themes and offline sync.

### Confirmed shared Home theme and member-identifying accent colors (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B, with clarification:** Each Home has a shared default theme/accent managed by the Owner or an Administrator explicitly granted appearance-management permission. Each member's **personal accent color also identifies who did what** in Home activity, assignments, chores, calendars, rewards and other multi-person interfaces. This applies to **adults and children**, not just adult accounts. Distinguish the Home's background/interface theme from the member-specific identifying color; one must not overwrite the other.
- Adults retain the approved global personal accent and optional per-Home override. The effective member color in a Home is that member's per-Home override if set, otherwise their global personal accent. Child profiles also receive a stable identifying accent in each Home, using an accessible suggested default or a guardian-approved selection; allow child color customization by default when the child has authorized profile access, with guardian reset as specified below. A child linked across Homes may have different Home-specific colors without sharing private data across Homes.
- Use the **actor's effective member color**, not the viewer's own accent or the Home theme color, when representing who completed, created, assigned or participated in an action. Render attribution with the member's permitted display name/avatar or an appropriate privacy-safe label and icon/text; **never rely on color alone**. In multi-member views, provide distinguishable swatches and accessible legends. Color changes update current presentation without rewriting historical actor IDs, immutable audits, or the recorded source Home; use privacy-minimized attribution for departed members.
- Handle visually similar member colors through an accessible collision warning and suggested alternatives; do not silently change an adult's chosen personal accent. Use contrast-safe outlines/patterns/text where colors coincide or are inaccessible. Treat identity attribution as based on stable internal member IDs, never color, name or avatar alone.
- The shared Home theme is the default for common Home surfaces. An adult's personal Home accent override controls their own experience without changing the shared Home theme or another person's identifying color. Household Displays inherit the shared Home theme by default and may retain independently configured device-level theme/layout overrides; their member-specific activity colors still reflect the effective member colors for that Home, subject to shared-display privacy rules.
- Changes to shared Home appearance require the explicit Home appearance permission and an audit record. Home managers cannot arbitrarily modify another adult's personal color; child color resets follow the guardian hierarchy while child-initiated color changes are allowed by default with profile access. Enforce correct attribution, privacy and effective colors in personal cross-Home widgets, Home dashboards, shared displays, APIs, notifications, exports and offline views without leaking data from other Homes.
- Acceptance criteria: two adults and two children with distinct member colors; adult global accent inherited in one Home and overridden in another; child-selected color with guardian reset; different shared Home theme; one Household Display with device theme override; chore attribution remains accurate after changing colors; overlapping colors with accessible labels; per-Home permissions; departing member; selected cross-Home widgets; contrast and screen-reader checks.

### Confirmed optional duplicate member accent colors with accessibility warnings (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Adults and children may freely choose identical or visually similar personal identifying accent colors within the same Home. Color uniqueness is **not** required, and a collision must never block saving an otherwise valid accessible color. This applies to global accents and per-Home overrides, including child-selected colors; guardians retain the existing ability to reset a child's color, but duplicate colors alone do not automatically require a reset.
- In the color picker, compare the proposed effective color with other visible members' effective Home colors. Give a clear, non-blocking warning when a color is identical or difficult to distinguish, and offer accessible alternative swatches with previews. Members may keep their original choice; do not automatically reassign or silently alter another member's color. Do not disclose private profiles or membership in other Homes when checking collisions.
- In chores, calendars, assignments, rewards, activity history, member lists, Household Displays and cross-Home widgets, pair attribution colors with permitted names and/or distinct icons, labels or patterns. When multiple actors have matching colors, show separate identifiable entries and accessible legends; never rely on hue alone or imply that equal colors mean the same person. Use stable internal member IDs and source-Home context for all actions and historical attribution.
- Keep contrast validation and semantic status-color separation mandatory; a collision warning is optional advice, not a substitute for readable text, keyboard access or screen-reader labels. Reevaluate warnings when members join, leave or change their colors without changing any member's selection. Handle offline changes and synchronization conflicts without overwriting another member's preferences.
- Acceptance criteria: two adults choose identical colors; child chooses same color as guardian; near-identical custom colors trigger suggestions but can be saved; no warning for sufficiently distinguishable colors; guardian reset remains possible; accessible legends and screen readers distinguish actors; light/dark and shared-display rendering; Home membership changes; multiple Homes and privacy isolation; concurrent/offline color edits.

### Confirmed child-controlled accent colors with guardian reset (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C:** Children who have authorized access to their own profile can select and change their **personal identifying accent color by default**, without requesting guardian approval for each change. An authorized guardian can reset the color to a safe suggested default or an existing approved value. This specifically supersedes earlier language requiring guardian permission for each child appearance/color edit; it does **not** grant children authority to change protected identity, guardian assignments, Home roles or security settings.
- A child profile without independent login or profile-edit access retains a guardian-set or automatically suggested accessible color. Once the child can access permitted profile customization (including an approved household-device profile experience), color selection is enabled by default. Guardians may restrict profile access under established age-appropriate safeguards, but do not require separate color approval while that access is enabled.
- Support a default personal accent and optional per-Home identifying-color overrides for children linked to more than one Home, without mixing Home data or disclosing membership across Homes. Apply the same inheritance, explicit override/reset, accessible palette/custom-color validation and preview principles as adult accents, adapted to child-friendly controls. The child's change in one Home must not silently alter another Home's explicit override.
- An authorized guardian may reset the child's color, including when inappropriate, inaccessible or confusingly similar to another member's color. Explain resets in age-appropriate language; log guardian-initiated changes appropriately without exposing sensitive child information. Guardians' reset authority follows the approved per-Home guardian hierarchy; cross-Home/global changes require appropriate identity-level guardian authorization and must not confer powers over another Home.
- Use the child's effective color consistently for member attribution in chores, rewards, calendars, activities and Household Displays, alongside names/icons rather than relying on color alone. Preserve stable actor IDs and historical attribution when colors change. Do not let child-editable colors alter shared Home themes, another member's accent, access permissions or semantic status colors.
- Acceptance criteria: child with profile access changes color without prior guardian approval; child without profile access retains suggested/guardian color; authorized guardian resets; unauthorized adult cannot reset; two Homes with different child overrides; global inheritance; collision and contrast warnings; shared display attribution; offline conflict handling; screen reader and age-appropriate UI.

### Confirmed global identity with Home-specific profile presentation (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C:** Each adult retains **one canonical Madeora account identity** across Personal context and all joined Homes. Their global display name and avatar are defaults; the adult may independently set a **Home-specific display name and avatar** for each Home. A Home presentation override is not a second account, credential, identity, membership, or security principal. Stable internal identity and Home-member identifiers, not names or pictures, determine authorization and attribution.
- In **Account/Profile settings** provide global display name/avatar controls; in **Home → My profile** provide clear per-Home name/avatar controls, preview, change, remove and **Reset to global** actions. Display which values are inherited versus overridden. A global change propagates only to Homes inheriting that field; explicit Home overrides remain unchanged. Allow name and avatar to be overridden independently. Respect the previously approved optional Home nickname as the preferred label where set, with a single clearly explained effective name rather than competing labels.
- Adults control their own global identity presentation and their own per-Home overrides. Home Owners/Administrators must not silently edit another adult's global or Home-specific name/avatar. For children linked across Homes, use the existing guardian authority and age-appropriate profile-edit permissions; child Home-specific presentation stays isolated and identity-level protected changes require the appropriate guardian approval. Changing a name/avatar cannot grant roles, modify membership, impersonate another account in protected actions, or bypass parental controls.
- Member lists, Home-owned widgets, chores, calendars, rewards, activity attribution, search results, and Ora use the **effective permitted presentation for the source Home**. Personal/account settings use the global presentation. A paired Household Display uses only the paired Home's privacy-approved presentation, never another Home's override or an unapproved global/private field. Cross-Home experiences must label the source Home without disclosing its roster, profile overrides or private account details to unauthorized viewers.
- **Privacy by default:** Do not expose account email, recovery channels, phone, birthday, linked sign-in providers, account identifiers, other Home memberships, or another Home's display name/avatar merely because a person belongs to a Home. Existing explicit per-Home optional-field visibility controls still govern any eligible shared details. Joining a new Home begins with global presentation defaults and privacy-preserving visibility; never copy an override or visibility grant from another Home. Provide a preview of what other Home members and shared displays can see.
- Validate names and avatar uploads for supported types, size, safe content and accessibility; support removing an avatar, sensible initials fallback, offline and failed uploads, concurrent edits, and membership revocation while editing. Permit duplicate names but disambiguate with privacy-safe context and accessible non-color-only cues; Ora must clarify ambiguous references. Historical records keep stable actor IDs and established departed-member privacy rules rather than rewriting authorship on rename. Prevent stale cached images or cross-Home leakage after removal, override reset or permission changes.
- Acceptance criteria: adult with different names/avatars in two Homes and global Personal context; override only name or only avatar; global update affects inherited fields but not overrides; reset independently; existing nickname precedence; two members with identical names; guardian-managed child linked to two Homes; Home manager cannot rename another adult; Household Display uses paired-Home presentation; no private account-email/other-Home exposure; avatar upload failure; revoked membership; offline conflict; responsive and screen-reader-accessible settings.

### Confirmed central privacy dashboard and optional guided review (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C:** Provide **Account → Privacy** as a central, accessible overview of the signed-in person's privacy posture and relevant controls, alongside an **optional guided privacy review**. The dashboard is an entry point and summary, not a new authority model; existing Account, Security, Profile and Home settings remain authoritative and available directly. Never automatically change a preference, consent, membership, visibility rule or security control simply because the dashboard or review is opened or completed.
- **Dashboard contents:** Summarize current account/profile visibility, discoverability (accounts are **not searchable**), Home memberships and per-Home presentation/visibility, personal data-sharing and integration permissions where applicable, linked sign-in/recovery options, active sessions/trusted devices, security notifications, and existing data export/deletion controls. Show only controls relevant to the account's age, permissions and enabled features. Label settings as **account-wide**, **Home-specific**, **device-specific** or **guardian-managed**; avoid presenting an account-wide toggle for a Home-scoped rule. Link each summary to its existing authoritative setting and explain who can see or change it.
- **Guided review:** Offer a clearly optional, resumable sequence covering the person's profile visibility, Home-by-Home sharing, connected services/data permissions, account security/recovery, device sessions and data rights. Display the current value, scope, consequence and recommended consideration in plain language; allow **Keep as is**, **Change setting** (using the existing authorized control), **Back**, **Skip** and **Finish**. Review completion is informational, not evidence that settings were modified or consent was granted. Never preselect expanded sharing, weaken security defaults, or bundle unrelated permissions. If a change requires reauthentication, guardian approval or another confirmation, follow the existing workflow before saving it.
- **Permissions and privacy:** Adults see only their own account-level details and Homes they may access; Home managers see only Home controls permitted by their role, not another member's private account settings, recovery methods, other Homes or device history. Eligible children receive age-appropriate explanations and only the controls they are permitted to edit; restricted controls are clearly marked as guardian-managed, with the existing primary/secondary guardian boundaries unchanged. The review cannot override child safeguards, invite approval, privacy defaults or Household Display restrictions. Shared Household Displays must not expose or launch personal account privacy reviews.
- **State and resilience:** Indicate when a value is inherited, unavailable, loading, out of date or restricted. Use privacy-safe summaries (mask sensitive addresses/device identifiers), avoid leaking account existence or other Homes, and require fresh verification for sensitive actions as already specified. If settings are changed elsewhere during a review, refresh and revalidate before saving; on failed saves, show that the change did **not** take effect. Offline views may show clearly marked cached settings but cannot claim successful changes without server confirmation. Preserve review progress without retaining secrets or unnecessary copies of private settings; allow dismissal and later restart. Do not nag users into completion.
- **Accessibility and consistency:** Support keyboard/screen-reader navigation, clear progress indicators, responsive/mobile layouts, interface-language and regional preferences, understandable help text, and deep links back to the appropriate settings.
