Changes made from either entry point must appear consistently in the other after confirmation. The dashboard does not invent new collection, tracking, disclosure or retention permissions.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Adult with two Homes and distinct profile visibility rules; review without any changes; single-setting change and confirmation; skipped/restarted review; role-restricted Home controls; guardian-managed child and eligible child; Household Display denied personal privacy dashboard; sensitive security action requiring step-up; stale/offline settings and save failure; revoked Home membership during review; private information masked; no cross-Home disclosure; accessible keyboard and screen-reader completion; direct settings and dashboard remain synchronized.

### Confirmed time-limited, risk-based trusted devices (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C:** Eligible accounts may designate a personal sign-in device as **trusted for a limited period** after successful authentication and appropriate verification. Trust reduces unnecessary routine step-up challenges but **never substitutes for authentication**, grants no new permissions and never exempts sensitive changes, recovery, suspicious activity or required optional 2FA from proportionate verification. The device trust decision is independent of Home membership, account switching and Household Display pairing.
- **Enrollment and visibility:** Offer **Trust this device** only after successful sign-in on a suitable personal device, with clear explanation of scope, expiry and shared-device risk; do not preselect it. In **Account → Security → Devices and sessions**, show each trusted device with a privacy-safe label, last relevant activity, trust status and expiry, plus **Revoke trust** and appropriate **Sign out** actions. Users may decline trust and continue signing in normally. Define a bounded default trust lifetime in supporting product policy; do not treat trust as permanent or automatically renew it indefinitely.
- **Risk-based challenges:** Reassess trust when device signals change materially, a device is unfamiliar, suspicious sign-in/recovery activity occurs, credentials or recovery methods change, a session is revoked, or the trust period expires. Require fresh verification appropriate to risk and existing sign-in/2FA policy. Sensitive operations such as password/email/provider/2FA changes, recovery-code replacement, account deletion and high-impact guardian changes always require fresh step-up verification even on a trusted device. Avoid disclosing internal risk scores or allowing repeated retries to bypass challenges.
- **Revocation and lifecycle:** Permit an adult to revoke an individual device's trust remotely and revoke all trusted devices after suspected compromise, with confirmation and privacy-minimized security history/notifications. Revocation takes effect server-side immediately for future privileged access; if the user also chooses sign-out, invalidate that device's account session and prevent queued offline writes from bypassing revocation. Trust expires independently of ordinary session expiry and is cleared when required by consequential account security events; expired trust must not silently extend a session or preserve access after deactivation.
- **Shared devices and children:** Do not offer persistent adult trusted-device enrollment on paired Household Displays, which retain their separate restricted pairing identity. On ordinary multi-account/shared devices, default to **not trusted** and warn against trusting a device used by others; trust is always account-specific and cannot unlock another remembered account. For children, follow established eligibility and guardian hierarchy: independent child-device trust requires the primary guardian's existing authorization and may be revoked by the authorized guardian; a child's PIN or routine device access cannot independently expand trusted-device authority. Apply age-appropriate expiry, review and restrictions without exposing child-private activity to unrelated Home members.
- **Privacy, offline and errors:** Store device trust using secure, revocable device-bound mechanisms where available, not plaintext credentials or recovery secrets. Minimize fingerprinting and retention, avoid revealing exact location or hardware identifiers in routine UI, and keep device lists private to the account/authorized guardian. Offline cached views remain subject to existing account locks and permissions; offline status never creates, renews or validates trust after its expiry, and a revoked device cannot synchronize protected actions on reconnection. Provide clear paths for lost devices, inaccessible verification methods, expired trust, unsupported devices, mistaken revocation and concurrent sign-ins.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Enrollment offered only after verified sign-in and explicit consent; trusted device reduces routine prompts without skipping sensitive-operation checks; untrusted and expired devices require appropriate challenges; suspicious activity triggers verification; remote single-device and all-device revocation; session sign-out distinct from trust revocation; credentials/recovery changes invalidate trust as appropriate; multi-Home and multi-account isolation; no Household Display enrollment; guardian-controlled child trust; offline and stale-token handling; notification/audit consistency; accessible responsive device management.

### Confirmed risk-based account security notifications (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C:** Always issue account-level security alerts for **critical security changes** and **credible suspicious activity**; users cannot disable these alerts. Allow adults to configure routine notifications for ordinary successful sign-ins and recognized/new-device activity separately. Disabling routine notifications never suppresses mandatory alerts, in-product security history, required verification challenges or protective actions.
- **Mandatory events:** Notify for completed or attempted high-impact credential, primary/backup email, recovery-code, linked sign-in method, and optional 2FA changes; consequential account recovery, session-wide revocation, account deactivation/deletion requests, and security-relevant reversals. Notify for credible takeover indicators such as suspicious sign-ins, unusual recovery attempts, repeated abuse patterns or detected unauthorized changes, using risk assessment rather than indiscriminately alerting on every failed password entry. Keep existing event-specific verification and notification obligations authoritative.
- **Routine preferences:** In **Account → Security → Notifications**, explain mandatory versus optional categories, let adults choose routine new-device and successful sign-in alerts, and show current settings and recent notification activity. Default to protective but non-noisy routine settings; provide clear per-category controls, confirmation and accessible feedback. Preferences are account-wide across Homes, not Home role settings. A routine event elevated by risk becomes mandatory regardless of preference.
- **Delivery and safeguards:** Deliver mandatory alerts through available verified, independently controlled account contact channels, prioritizing established trusted channels and avoiding reliance solely on a potentially compromised new address or session. Provide in-app security activity when safely authenticated; never rely on push alone. Distinguish a queued/sent alert from confirmed receipt, handle bounce, unavailable channels, outages, retries, deduplication and delayed delivery, and offer safe account-recovery guidance. Do not disclose secrets, recovery codes, full addresses, precise location, device identifiers, Home data or child information in push previews or email content. Link to a trusted in-app review flow rather than embedding sensitive action tokens in alerts.
- **User action:** Explain the event, approximate time, safe device/session context and whether action is needed; offer **This was me**, **Secure my account**, session review/revocation and recovery where authorized. Reporting an event as suspicious must trigger proportionate safeguards and review without automatically deleting content or changing Home memberships. Prevent phishing, alert flooding and notification-driven account enumeration; rate-limit and group repetitive low-value events without suppressing distinct critical changes.
- **Children and shared devices:** Follow existing child eligibility, guardian authority and privacy rules: deliver appropriate security notices to authorized guardians or independently eligible child accounts as established by account-access policy, without exposing unrelated Home or guardian information. Never show private account-security alerts on paired Household Displays or to other remembered accounts on a shared device. Respect offline, signed-out, multi-device and multi-Home states; critical notices remain available after reauthentication.
- **Audit and acceptance criteria:** Record event, risk classification, channel/delivery state and user response in the previously approved private security activity history, with appropriate retention and privacy controls. Test routine sign-in alerts on/off; recognized versus new device; suspicious sign-in despite opt-out; primary email and backup recovery changes; lost/compromised mailbox; 2FA and linked-provider changes; simultaneous device sessions; repeated failed attempts without notification storms; offline delivery; child/guardian access; shared tablet and Household Display isolation; cross-Home privacy; accessible responsive settings and actionable messages.

### Confirmed private accounts and invitation-only discovery (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Madeora has **no searchable account directory**. Members cannot browse or search other Madeora accounts by name, email, avatar, phone number or identifier, and there is no opt-in public discoverability switch. Home membership is initiated only through existing authorized **recipient-specific email invitations**, **private shareable invitation links**, or the separately approved **manager-issued private Home join-request links/codes**. Do not introduce unsolicited connection requests, public Home search, contact-book matching or account recommendations as alternate discovery paths.
- **Email invitation:** An authorized inviter enters the intended recipient email without receiving an account-exists lookup result. Delivery and acceptance follow the already approved recipient-bound verified-email matching, sign-in, role preview, expiry, resend/renewal and manager-approval rules. The inviter sees invitation delivery/status information only to the extent needed for managing their own Home invitation, never a recipient's account profile or unrelated memberships. Avoid account enumeration through validation, timing, delivery errors and repeated attempts.
- **Private invitation link/code:** An authorized manager may share a private Home invitation link under the existing seven-day default (24-hour/7-day/30-day options), revocation, single-/multi-use and cap rules, or a separate time-limited Home join-request link/code. These are distinct flows with distinct UI labels. A code is not a global user-search identifier. A recipient must authenticate, review a privacy-safe Home/role preview, explicitly accept or request admission and obtain the separately approved manager approval before membership becomes active. Possession of a link/code never grants immediate access.
- **Privacy boundaries:** Before approved membership, show only the minimum Home identification and proposed role needed for informed consent; do not reveal Home member lists, private profiles, account email addresses, child information, dashboards, or whether a named person has a Madeora account. After joining, members may see only the Home-scoped presentation and fields authorized under the established per-Home visibility rules; account recovery details and other Home memberships remain private. Existing guardian and child enrollment safeguards continue to apply; do not make children publicly discoverable.
- **Safety and lifecycle:** Limit invitation creation and delivery abuse, link/code guessing, repeated requests, and automated enumeration with appropriate rate limits, anti-abuse controls and privacy-safe error messages. Support expired, revoked, exhausted, already-member, mismatched verified-email, declined, pending and cancelled states without leaking account existence. Existing active-invitation limits and atomic successful-admission usage accounting remain authoritative. Provide accessible copy/share, revoke, status and recovery flows, and do not silently import device contacts or send invitations without an authorized explicit action.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Searching by name/email yields no directory; email invite to an existing or new account does not reveal which; forwarded recipient-specific email invite fails for a different verified email; private shareable link reaches an eligible account without exposing directory data; join-request code requires manager approval; no pre-approval Home roster/profile exposure; child accounts cannot be enumerated; expired/revoked/exhausted link rejected; rate-limited abuse; duplicate membership prevented; cross-Home privacy; accessible mobile and desktop invitations.

### Confirmed extended optional adult profiles and per-Home visibility (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Adult accounts have a display name, avatar and account email, with optional birthday, phone number and personal preferences. Do not require optional fields to register, join a Home or use ordinary features. Treat account email as a credential/contact detail, **not automatically public profile information**.
- Distinguish identity-level profile fields and account/security data from Home-specific presentation and visibility. A member may independently choose which eligible optional profile fields are visible to members of each Home they belong to; newly joined Homes receive privacy-preserving defaults, never copied visibility grants from another Home. Show a preview of what each Home can see and provide simple per-field, per-Home controls. Personal-only information stays hidden from other members unless expressly shared.
- Adult users edit their own optional information and preferences; Home Owners and Administrators do not gain authority to change an adult's identity-level profile, verified email, birthday, phone or personal preferences by virtue of Home role. An authorized Home role may manage only explicitly defined Home-specific member labels or settings, without modifying global identity data.
- Validate optional birthday/date, international phone number and preference inputs, distinguish unverified optional phone numbers from verified recovery/contact methods, and avoid inferring age or eligibility from an optional or unverified birthday. Where birthday-based experiences are offered, require the necessary verified/consented information separately and explain its purpose. Allow fields to be cleared and visibility to be revoked.
- Personal preferences may include language, regional formatting, appearance and notification preferences; preserve existing separate Home/dashboard and Household Display configurations. Clarify whether a preference is global, per-Home or per-device before saving. Do not infer that a private profile field is shareable through a calendar event, chores, rewards, search, Ora, exports or notifications.
- Enforce profile visibility in member directories, dashboards, Household Displays, mobile views, API responses, integrations, search, notifications and audit interfaces. Household Displays show only fields expressly permitted for that shared surface, even if visible to Home members. Revoking visibility must take effect on subsequent reads and cached/offline surfaces as soon as feasible; avoid stale disclosure and notify clearly when a device is offline.
- Secure edits, account email changes and linked social-provider information with appropriate reauthentication, verification and audit safeguards; never silently overwrite a verified account email from a Google/Apple profile update. Follow existing retention/deletion/export and multi-Home isolation policies.
- Acceptance criteria: create adult with minimum profile; add/change/remove birthday, phone and preferences; distinct visibility in two Homes; new Home defaults; hide email; non-manager sees only allowed fields; manager cannot edit another adult's global profile; shared-display privacy; Apple Hide My Email; unverified phone not used for recovery; offline cache after revocation; accessibility and responsive profile editor.

### Confirmed routine adult password changes and session revocation (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** After a successful **routine password change initiated by an authenticated adult**, retain only the **current recently reauthenticated and verified session** and revoke all other sessions for that Madeora adult account across browsers, devices and Homes. Do not preserve other remembered sessions on the same shared device. The current session may receive rotated credentials and must remain subject to existing optional 2FA, expiry and risk checks; retaining it is not an exemption from reauthentication for sensitive actions.
- Require the current password or another sufficiently strong existing sign-in verification method, recent reauthentication, and enabled 2FA where applicable before changing a password. Validate the new password against applicable security requirements, prevent weak/compromised passwords where feasible, avoid revealing password contents, and never log passwords. Social-only adults must securely establish a password rather than pretend to change one they do not have; changing a Madeora password must not alter Apple/Google provider credentials or unlink their identities.
- **Stricter recovery path:** Password resets using recovery links/codes, suspected account compromise, suspicious-session reports with elevated risk, and security-initiated credential recovery must revoke **all existing sessions including the initiating session** and require a fresh sign-in (and optional 2FA if enabled). Do not let a reset flow masquerade as a routine change to preserve a potentially compromised session. Apply rate limits, one-time expiring reset tokens and risk-based verification; avoid account-enumeration leaks.
- Enforce revocation server-side for access/refresh credentials, realtime connections, APIs, background sync and queued offline writes. A disconnected revoked device may temporarily display protected cached content, but cannot perform authorized sync/actions and must clear inaccessible caches when it learns of revocation. Other accounts remembered on a shared device and paired Household Display credentials are unaffected unless independently compromised or revoked.
- Provide clear confirmation before submission explaining which sessions will remain or be signed out, then show a success state with the retained current session (routine change) or a fresh sign-in requirement (reset). Send independent security notices through verified account channels, provide a direct suspicious-change reporting route, and record privacy-minimized security audit events without secrets. If session revocation partially fails, fail closed or block further use until the account-wide revocation requirement is satisfied; avoid misleading success messaging.
- Acceptance criteria: routine password change with current phone and other laptop/tablet sessions; several Homes; multiple remembered accounts on one tablet; current-session token rotation; wrong current password; optional TOTP; linked Google/Apple and social-only account; expired/used reset token; password reset from signed-out browser; suspicious activity; offline revoked device and queued actions; concurrent refresh-token race; notifications and audit; accessible responsive confirmation and error states.

### Confirmed secure self-service primary account email changes (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** An authenticated adult may change their account's **primary email address** through self-service Account Settings. This changes a verified account contact/sign-in identifier, **not** the stable Madeora account identity, existing Home memberships, roles, child guardian relationships, personal dashboard, content, history or permissions. A Home Owner or Administrator cannot change another adult's account email through their Home role.
- Require recent reauthentication using an existing trusted sign-in method, with an additional challenge when risk warrants it and the account's enabled optional 2FA respected. Verify ownership of the **new email** through a single-use, short-lived confirmation link or code; do not activate the new address before verification. Send an independent security notification to the **previous verified email**, including a safe route to report an unauthorized change. Do not make continued access to the old mailbox an unconditional requirement, because people may have lost it; high-risk cases follow secure account recovery.
- Show the proposed change, current masked address and pending-verification state. Permit cancel/retry, expire pending requests safely, rate-limit attempts and prevent concurrent changes from overwriting one another. Until confirmation, the old verified primary address remains authoritative for sign-in and security notifications; after confirmation, switch the primary email atomically and invalidate superseded verification tokens. Notify both old and new addresses of completion where deliverable, without exposing secrets or Home data.
- Enforce unique verified primary-email ownership and privacy-preserving responses when an address is already in use; never silently merge accounts. Handle email normalization carefully, case/Unicode/provider alias rules without assuming different addresses identify the same person. An unverified new address must not be usable for account recovery, invitations as an authenticated identity, or security-sensitive notices.
- For Google/Apple sign-in, distinguish the Madeora primary email from the external provider's email, including Apple Hide My Email. Changing Madeora's primary email **must not silently link, unlink or change** a Google/Apple identity, replace an existing verified sign-in method, or bypass linked-provider authentication and optional 2FA. If email/password sign-in is enabled, update its email identifier after verification while retaining the existing password unless separately changed. Preserve at least one working recovery/sign-in route.
- Protect ongoing sessions and account recovery: audit the request, verification, completion and cancellation without storing tokens or full email addresses in broadly visible logs; send security alerts independently of household notification preferences; apply risk-based session reauthentication/revocation for suspicious changes. Ensure old-email links no longer authorize new changes after completion, and provide secure support/recovery handling for disputed changes or inaccessible inboxes.
- Account email remains private by default and does not become visible to Home members, child profiles, Household Displays, cross-Home widgets, Ora or integrations because it changed. Propagate verified contact changes only to services explicitly authorized to receive them, with clear errors/retries and no cross-Home leakage.
- Acceptance criteria: password account email change; Google-only and Apple Hide My Email accounts; pending verification, expiration, cancellation and resend; old-email alert; email already associated with another account; concurrent requests; lost old inbox; invalid/stolen verification link; optional 2FA and risk reauthentication; session and recovery handling; no account or Home data loss; privacy and accessible mobile flows.

### Confirmed self-service Google and Apple sign-in method management (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Adults can securely **link or unlink one Google identity and one Apple identity** to their existing stable Madeora account through Account Settings > Sign-in methods. This is self-service after appropriate verification; it does not create a second account, merge accounts, change the primary Madeora email, or alter Homes, guardian relationships, dashboards, permissions, content or audit history. Multiple identities from the same provider are outside this approved scope.
- Show the available methods and their states (connected, disconnected, verification required, temporarily unavailable) with provider identity hints minimized and visible only to the account holder. Clearly distinguish Madeora's verified primary email, an email/password credential, Google identity and Apple identity, including Apple Hide My Email. Provider email changes must not silently change the Madeora primary email or infer that two identities belong to the same person.
- **Link flow:** Require an authenticated adult session, recent strong reauthentication and enabled optional TOTP when applicable, then explicit consent and successful fresh provider authentication proving control of the selected Google/Apple identity. Present the account to which the identity will be linked and confirm before committing. Never link automatically from a matching email, a provider callback, an invitation or a shared-device account chooser. If the external identity is already linked to another Madeora account, reject safely without revealing that account's details; offer an appropriate separately verified recovery route, not automatic merging or transfer.
- **Unlink flow:** Show the exact provider to disconnect, explain effects on future sign-in, and require recent reauthentication and confirmation. Prevent unlinking the **last usable sign-in/recovery method**; first guide the adult to establish and verify another method, such as an email/password credential or another accessible linked provider. Consider actual method availability and recovery access, not merely the count of listed methods. An unverified email, inactive provider session or lost mailbox is not sufficient fallback.
- Linking or unlinking must not bypass existing optional 2FA, password reset/session revocation policy, suspicious-activity safeguards, or account security alerts. For routine verified changes, use proportionate session protections; for suspected compromise, revoke affected sessions or require stronger recovery. Record privacy-minimized security activity events and notify through verified account channels independently of optional Home notifications. Never store provider access tokens, credentials or private relay addresses in broad activity logs or reports.
- Handle interrupted OAuth consent, denied permissions, expired/replayed state, mismatched callback account, provider outage, changed/revoked provider credentials, account deletion/deactivation, concurrent linking/unlinking, offline attempts, and device/account switching. Use anti-CSRF state, one-time flows and atomic idempotent updates; never leave a method half-linked or permit a callback initiated under one Madeora account to affect another. Provide accessible actionable errors without leaking account existence.
- Changes apply account-wide across all Homes, but Home Owners/Administrators and Household Displays cannot manage or inspect another adult's sign-in providers. Keep linked identities out of Home directories, widgets, search, Ora, exports and integrations except where explicitly authorized by existing account privacy/export rules. Existing sessions and recovery rules remain authoritative.
- Acceptance criteria: password-only adult links Google then Apple; Google-only adult establishes password then unlinks Google; Apple Hide My Email; provider email differs from Madeora primary email; duplicate provider already linked elsewhere; denied/expired/replayed callback; concurrent operations; optional TOTP; attempted last-method unlink; lost provider; shared device account switch during callback; offline and revoked sessions; security notices/audit; unchanged Home memberships and stable identity; accessible responsive settings.

### Confirmed optional backup account recovery methods (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Offer adults **optional verified backup email** and **dedicated account recovery codes** as additional account-level recovery methods. Neither is required for registration, Home membership, ordinary sign-in or privileged Home roles. They supplement, rather than replace, primary-email recovery, linked Google/Apple sign-in and optional authenticator-based 2FA recovery. Do not introduce a trusted-person recovery contact under this decision.
- In Account Settings > Security > Recovery methods, explain each method's purpose, verification state, last update and limitations without exposing secrets. The backup email is distinct from the primary Madeora email and must be independently verified before it can receive sensitive recovery challenges. Never automatically copy a Google/Apple provider email, an unverified optional profile phone number or another Home member's contact into recovery settings. Enforce safe address uniqueness/collision rules without account enumeration; changing the primary email does not silently promote, replace or verify the backup email.
- **Enroll or change backup email:** Require an active adult session, recent strong reauthentication, enabled Madeora TOTP when applicable, explicit confirmation and a single-use expiring verification challenge sent to the proposed address. Keep the prior verified method unchanged until replacement verification completes; alert existing verified contact channels of additions, changes and removals. Provide cancellation, resend with limits, and a secure path for lost access or suspected takeover. Never expose the backup address to Home members, guardians by role, Household Displays, search, Ora or cross-Home widgets.
- **Dedicated account recovery codes:** Generate a limited set of high-entropy, single-use codes separately from TOTP recovery codes; explain the distinction and what recovery each code can authorize. Show/download once during strongly reauthenticated setup, advise secure offline storage, store only protected verifiers, rate-limit and audit redemption, and reject replay. Regenerating codes invalidates the prior set only after verified confirmation; never display existing codes again, send them by email, include them in account exports/security reports or cache them for shared-device switching.
- **Recovery flow:** Treat possession of a backup mailbox or a single recovery code as a recovery factor, **not automatic proof of account ownership sufficient to bypass all other protections**. Use risk-proportionate additional checks, notifications to remaining verified channels, waiting/objection periods for high-risk loss-of-access cases, and restricted manual review when evidence conflicts. Protect enabled TOTP without creating a shortcut through a weaker backup method; document the precise allowed combinations of primary/backup email, codes and linked providers during implementation-level security review. Do not allow Home ownership, Administrator rights, child guardianship or a paired display to serve as proof of adult account identity.
- After a successful high-risk recovery, rotate/revoke relevant sessions, reset compromised recovery material, review linked sign-in methods and require fresh authentication; preserve the same adult account, Homes, data and permissions unless a separately authorized change occurs. Send privacy-minimized security notifications and record events in the private security activity history. Respect account deactivation/deletion recovery boundaries and do not resurrect revoked memberships or bypass the one-Owner-per-Home rule.
- Handle unverified/expired backup email, mailbox compromise, bounced messages, duplicate addresses, lost/stolen/reused codes, concurrent regeneration/recovery, rate limiting, interrupted flows, provider outages, multi-account shared devices, offline clients and account takeover disputes with clear safe errors. Recovery methods are account-wide, never per-Home; no secrets in logs, exports, push previews or integrations.
- Acceptance criteria: adult enrolls/removes/changes verified backup email; new email verification pending/expired; primary and backup emails differ; Apple Hide My Email; optional 2FA enabled/disabled; dedicated recovery code used once and replay blocked; regenerated codes invalidate originals; lost primary email and provider; suspected mailbox compromise; last usable sign-in method protection; recovery from shared tablet; account recovery revokes sessions; no cross-Home or child/display disclosure; accessible mobile/desktop flows and protected audit history.

### Confirmed individual interface language across Homes (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Every adult and child with authorized profile/interface access can select their **own Madeora interface language**, used consistently in their personal dashboard and in every Home they can access. This is an account/profile-level preference, **not a per-Home language override**; changing Homes must not silently change the member's language. Children without independent profile access receive a guardian-selected/default appropriate language until they can manage the setting under their existing profile permissions.
- Each paired **Household Display** has an independent interface language setting, configurable only by authorized Home/device managers. Its shared interface and Ora follow that display's language, not the language of the last member who interacted with it. Switching selected member for a chore or action must not change the shared display language or reveal private language/profile settings.
- Localize navigation, widgets, system-generated labels, forms, validation, error and empty states, onboarding, permission explanations, notifications where applicable, accessibility labels, and Ora according to the **current viewer's** language. Home managers cannot force another adult's language or infer private account preferences from Home management permissions. Children may choose their language when their approved access permits, subject to guardian-controlled profile restrictions.
- **Do not automatically translate user-created content**, including event titles, task and chore descriptions, recipe text, comments, notes, Home names or nicknames. Show the original author-entered text to authorized viewers, even when their interface language differs. Explicitly distinguish localized system labels from original shared content; do not silently rewrite stored records when a language preference changes.
- Apply the established locale/timezone and regional-formatting rules separately from interface translation: translated interface text must not alter event times, week starts, numerical quantities, date semantics, permissions or underlying record identity. Use a clear documented fallback for unsupported/missing translations and prevent misleading mixed-language action labels where possible. A member must be able to preview or undo a language change without losing navigation or entered form data.
- For **shared or cross-Home widgets**, use the viewing member's interface language for controls while preserving the source Home label, privacy boundaries and original authored content. Shared Household Displays remain restricted to paired-Home data. Ora continues to use the current member's interface language or the Household Display's configured language, with no separate Ora language preference and no automatic language detection from typed input.
- Provide accessible language selection in personal/child profile settings, correctly identify language for screen readers and text direction where supported, and ensure responsive layouts accommodate longer translations. Account-security messages and essential recovery flows must remain understandable if the chosen language becomes unavailable, using an explicit supported-language fallback.
- Acceptance criteria: Dutch-speaking adult and English-speaking adult use the same Home simultaneously; child changes language with authorized profile access; guardian configures language for child without profile access; switching Homes does not change language; Household Display retains its own language across member selection; Ora follows viewer/display language; user-authored records remain unchanged; localized notifications and security/recovery flow; fallback language, screen-reader pronunciation, long strings, offline caches, and cross-Home widget isolation.

### Confirmed automatic regional defaults with independent overrides (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C:** At first setup, Madeora proposes sensible regional formatting defaults from available device/locale signals, with an understandable fallback when detection is unavailable. Adults and children eligible to manage their own profile settings can independently override **date display, 12/24-hour time display, first day of the week, number separators and measurement units**. These are account/profile preferences, independent of interface language, effective timezone and Home membership; eligible child access remains subject to existing guardian permissions.
- Provide a clearly labeled **Automatic / Custom** choice for regional formatting, with a live, accessible preview of representative dates, times, numbers and units before saving. Allow individual overrides where supported rather than requiring every regional setting to change together. Returning a setting to Automatic restores the current detected default for that setting; do not silently reset explicit custom preferences when devices, Homes or languages change.
- Apply the viewing member's preferences to their own UI and personal views, including localized system text, widgets, input hints and Ora's display of formatted values. Each Household Display uses its own Home/display regional formatting configuration controlled by authorized Home/device managers; do not borrow the last interacting member's private preferences. For Home-shared schedules and date-based rules, the **Home scheduling timezone and authoritative Home calendar boundaries remain unchanged**; a personal week-start display preference cannot redefine a Home recurrence, deadline or shared reporting period.
- **Formatting must never change meaning or stored data.** Store dates, instants, quantities and units in canonical typed representations; convert for display and validated input without silently altering scheduled instants, recurrence definitions, recipe quantities, monetary values, history or source text. Do not translate or rewrite user-authored text. When an input such as `03/04/2026` is ambiguous, make the expected format clear and validate or clarify before a consequential action. Preserve precision, dimensional compatibility and clear unit labels when displaying conversions; do not treat unit preferences as permission to edit stored measurements.
- Distinguish personal display preferences from explicit formats mandated by integrations, exported machine-readable data, legal/financial documents or external providers. Use stable, documented exchange formats and show the applicable format or units when ambiguity matters. A change of regional preference should re-render views without rewriting existing records, modifying other members' preferences, or changing notification timing and quiet-hour rules.
- Handle missing locale data, unsupported formats, device disagreement, offline use, preference sync conflicts, accessibility, right-to-left supported languages, and safe fallback behavior. Keep formatting understandable in validation, error messages, notifications, export previews and account recovery, and ensure Home-specific settings do not disclose personal preferences to other members.
- Acceptance criteria: English interface with Belgian date/24-hour clock and metric units; Dutch interface with different date/number preferences; automatic initialization; custom override survives device and Home switching; reset one setting to Automatic; eligible child versus guardian-managed child; separate Household Display formatting; two members view same Home event differently without rescheduling it; Monday/Sunday week-start display versus Home recurrence boundary; ambiguous date input; unit conversion and rounding; offline fallback and synchronization; accessible previews and error states.

### Confirmed personal timezone detection and Home scheduling timezone (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C:** For adults and eligible children with personal interface access, Madeora detects the current device timezone by default and uses it as the member's **effective personal timezone**. Members may select a supported timezone manually; that override persists across devices and Homes until they explicitly return to automatic detection or choose another timezone. Do not silently discard a manual override when the device travels or changes.
- A Home has a **separately configured scheduling timezone**, managed only by authorized Home managers. Home-owned calendars, chores, routines, vacations, date boundaries, recurring schedules, and other Home-anchored rules continue to use the Home timezone regardless of which member is viewing them, where a device is located, or which interface language is selected. A member changing their personal timezone must never reschedule Home records or alter their stored intended instants.
- Personal views may present time-specific Home events in the viewer's effective timezone where appropriate, but must preserve Home-local calendar-day meaning for date-only items, recurrence periods, due dates and Home schedule grids. Clearly label the applicable timezone whenever different interpretations could affect action or understanding; offer a way to see the Home-local time. Household Displays follow their paired Home's scheduling timezone for shared scheduling, independently of the display's interface language and any temporary member selection.
- **Automatic detection is a presentation and interpretation preference, not a silent scheduling migration.** On device timezone change, refresh the effective timezone and time formatting without rewriting stored events, recurring rules, reminders, history or Home boundaries. Where different signed-in devices report different zones in automatic mode, each device uses its own detected zone for that member's current session; a manual override is consistent across sessions. If detection is unavailable, use the last known valid personal timezone or a clearly disclosed safe fallback, and allow correction.
- For an Ora request with an unspecified timezone, the previously approved **member-configured-timezone rule** now means the member's **effective personal timezone** (detected in automatic mode or manually selected), not automatically the Home timezone. Show the resolved timezone in timed-action previews and require clarification for missing/invalid zones and DST ambiguities under the existing Ora rules. Explicit timezone instructions and module-specific Home scheduling boundaries remain authoritative.
- Timezone settings are separate from interface language, locale, calendar week-start settings and notification preferences. Handle daylight-saving gaps/overlaps, travel, offline sessions, sync conflicts, recurring events, integrations and quiet hours without duplicate or shifted executions. Revalidate the timezone and scheduling impact before consequential writes, especially if a device zone changed after the preview.
- Acceptance criteria: adult travels with automatic detection enabled; manual override persists across Homes and devices; return to automatic mode; two devices in different zones; child with authorized settings access; shared Home viewed by members in different zones; Household Display keeps Home scheduling timezone; Home manager changes Home timezone with an impact preview; date-only chore remains on its Home-local date; timed event displays a converted instant without rescheduling; Ora uses effective personal timezone; DST gap/overlap; timezone detection denied/unavailable; offline reconnect; integration and reminder stability; accessible timezone labels and previews.

### Confirmed adult email/password and Apple/Google sign-in (13 October 2026)
- **Approved customized option A plus social sign-in:** Adult Madeora accounts support verified **email address and password** and optional **Sign in with Google** and **Sign in with Apple**. **Passkeys are not included in the approved initial scope**. This supersedes the earlier A/B/C alternatives rather than treating Apple/Google as requiring passkeys.
- Adults may create an account with email/password or an available Apple/Google identity, and may link or unlink supported methods from authenticated account settings. One person retains one Madeora identity, personal data, Home memberships and permissions regardless of chosen sign-in method; never create or merge accounts solely because provider email addresses match.
- Require verified control of credentials and explicit confirmation before linking existing accounts. Handle Apple private relay/hidden email, provider email changes, already-linked providers, revoked provider consent, disabled provider accounts and failed sign-in without leaking account existence. Prevent account takeover through unverified addresses or automatic social-account linking.
- Password sign-in includes secure reset, email verification, brute-force/rate-limit protections, session management and reauthentication for sensitive account actions. Offer a safe fallback/recovery route if a linked social provider is unavailable; prevent removing the last usable sign-in/recovery method. Password creation is optional for a social-only account but can be added after secure verification.
- Adult authentication is distinct from guardian-managed child username/PIN/password sign-in and manager-paired Household Display identities. Apple/Google sign-in does not grant additional Home rights or bypass child/guardian or shared-device restrictions.
- Acceptance criteria: create with email/password, Apple, Google; log in with each; link/unlink a provider without duplicating a Home membership; provider email differs from account email; Apple Hide My Email; duplicate provider identity; unverified email collision; password reset; social-provider outage; lost provider access; last-method removal; multi-Home switching; revoked session and privacy-safe errors.

### Confirmed category-based deletion of adult personal data (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authenticated adults can permanently delete **eligible personally owned data by category** from Account Settings without deleting their Madeora account. Supported categories must reflect actual module ownership and include, where eligible, personal tasks, calendar entries, recipes, habits/goals, personal media and other personal records. Show unavailable categories and limitations honestly; never present Home-owned or another member's records as individually deletable merely because the adult can view or contributed to them.
- Provide a **review and impact preview** before deletion: selected categories, approximate eligible record counts, examples or scope, linked widgets and automations, integration effects, irreversible consequences, shared references, and any records excluded because of shared ownership, another person's rights, retention duties or ongoing security/audit needs. Distinguish deletion from archiving, hiding, leaving a Home, revoking a share, and permanent account deletion. Offer a personal data export before destructive confirmation without making export mandatory.
- Require recent reauthentication, a clear category-specific explicit confirmation and stronger safeguards for unusually broad or sensitive requests. Never delete from an ambiguous bulk-selection action. Display pending, processing, completed, partially completed and failed states, with honest per-category results and safe retry handling. Prevent accidental duplicate execution and concurrent updates from silently restoring deleted data.
- **Shared Home boundaries:** Personal-category deletion does not erase Home-owned calendars, chore completions, rewards, approvals, shared histories, records authored jointly or another person's information. Where an adult owns a personal item shared into a Home, clearly preview whether deletion removes their source item and authorized shared projections; respect other members' rights and preserve necessary minimized historical attribution under the approved former-member/history policy. Never use this workflow to bypass separate Home deletion or Home data administration permissions. Child data remains subject to guardian, consent and child-specific rules.
- Apply category deletion to the source of truth and eligible search indexes, dashboards, widgets, reminders, derived statistics, caches and authorized integrations, with defined eventual propagation and explicit offline-device limitations. Cancel pending personal reminders/actions where their underlying records were deleted; reject stale offline writes that would recreate erased content. Coordinate with connected external services only within granted authority and clearly distinguish local deletion from third-party deletion that cannot be guaranteed.
- Follow existing module-specific deletion and recovery periods **where applicable**; label the point at which a deletion becomes irreversible, and do not promise immediate physical erasure of legally retained records, protected security logs or backups. Minimize or de-identify residual data where required and describe backup expiration/retention transparently. Preserve separate account security activity retention and justified audit records. Protect other members' information and prevent cross-Home disclosure in previews, notifications and audit trails.
- Invalidate or regenerate pending/ready personal exports containing deleted data, and ensure any new export reflects the post-deletion state. Deletion never changes the adult's account identity, login methods, other categories, Home memberships or Home permissions unless an explicitly explained dependency requires it.
- Acceptance criteria: delete one and multiple categories; empty category; export before deletion; reauthentication failure; cancel before confirmation; shared personal item and Home-owned contribution; multi-Home separation; child/guardian restrictions; legally retained and security records; recovery-window distinctions; linked widgets and reminders; external integration errors; offline stale edits; in-progress export invalidation; partial failure/retry; accessible mobile, keyboard and screen-reader confirmations.

### Confirmed comprehensive, category-selective personal data exports (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C:** From Account Settings, adults can request either a **full eligible personal account export** or a **category-selective export**. Categories include account/profile/preferences, personally owned calendar entries, tasks, recipes, habits/goals, eligible personal media and **eligible personal contributions associated with selected Homes**. Display only supported categories and describe their scope, expected formats, exclusions and availability; a selection is not permission to extract all records the user can view.
- **Preserve the previously approved distinction between personal and Home-owned exports:** Category selection in the account export does **not** grant the separate Home data export capability, bypass Home Owner-configured export permissions, or turn a personal export into a whole-Home archive. An adult may export their own eligible data and permitted personal contributions from a Home, but Home-owned records, other members' personal data, child-sensitive data and shared content requiring separate authorization remain excluded or minimized. Whole-Home exports still require the explicit per-Home export permission and its own workflow.
- Offer **All eligible data** and **Choose categories** with a clear review screen, category descriptions and an optional per-Home scope selector for eligible contributions. The Home selector shows only currently authorized memberships and never exposes membership in other Homes to another member. Provide a meaningful empty selection error, a preview of what will and will not be included, and clear explanations when a category contains no eligible records. Account-level categories remain available without any Home membership.
- Apply source-of-truth permissions and privacy at record and field level throughout export preparation and again at download. Membership loss, permission revocation, account deactivation, Home suspension/deletion, data deletion, guardian restrictions and newly private shared records must block or invalidate affected exports. Never serve a stale archive after access changes; request a fresh export if eligibility changed. A former member's eligible personally owned information may remain exportable where applicable, without resurrecting revoked access to Home-owned history.
- Keep the existing **asynchronous job**, **single organized ZIP**, per-module JSON/CSV and eligible attachment formats, manifest with scope and exclusions, in-app plus email ready notification, secure sign-in/reauthentication, **seven-day expiry from generation**, failure/retry states and audit safeguards. Record selected categories and selected Home scopes in the private manifest without leaking other members' information; distinguish complete, empty and partial results honestly. Never send an export archive or direct public download URL by email.
- **Children and guardians:** Independent export by an eligible child is allowed only where the established age, consent, authentication and guardian rules authorize it. A guardian may request a child-data export only within their existing authority and the child’s privacy rights; apply record-level filtering to exclude other guardians’ or members’ private information and child-sensitive fields outside that authority. Never treat Home management as blanket authority to export a child’s personal account. Explain any unavailable categories and provide a protected, age-appropriate review and delivery flow.
- **Preparation and expiry safeguards:** Show queued, preparing, ready, failed, canceled and expired states, with safe retry/cancel options and clear notices. Require fresh authorization at download, bind the archive to its requester, use non-public short-lived authenticated access, and automatically remove the prepared archive after its existing seven-day window (or sooner on invalidation). Warn before expiry when appropriate without sending the archive or sensitive details in notifications. Avoid leaking data through filenames, previews, errors, logs or shared displays.
- Account export is available only to the authenticated adult for their own eligible data. A Home Owner or Administrator cannot export another adult's account by virtue of their role; Home export permissions remain independent. Minimize third-party integration data and exclude credentials, security secrets, access tokens and protected audit internals. Treat security-activity data and legally retained records according to their own access, export and retention restrictions.
- Acceptance criteria: full export; single category; multiple categories; eligible child and guardian-authorized child export; unauthorized guardian or Home manager denied; queued/canceled/failed/expired preparation; no Home account; two Homes with distinct permissions; selected eligible Home contributions but no Home-owned bulk export; unauthorized category/field excluded; shared child/private record excluded; Home departure or revocation while preparing and before download; stale ZIP invalidated; account deactivation; manifest and empty categories; large media; seven-day expiry; export-before-deletion choice; accessible mobile/keyboard/screen-reader selection and progress states.

### Confirmed personal data export (12 October 2026)
- Users can **request and download an export of their personally owned Madeora data at any time** from **Account Settings**, independently of the account-deletion process.
- Provide portable, documented **JSON and CSV** formats as appropriate for structured data, and include eligible uploaded files/media in their original or otherwise usable formats when permitted.
- The export must cover personal data across supported Madeora modules (e.g. personal calendar data, tasks, recipes, habits, memories, preferences) without silently omitting eligible categories. Clearly describe the scope and any excluded information.
- Respect ownership and privacy boundaries: **do not include other members' private information or Home-owned records merely because the requester can view them**. Any access or export of shared/Home-owned data needs separately specified authorization and safeguards.
- Require appropriate authentication/reauthentication for sensitive exports; protect generated files, provide clear preparation/download/expiration/failure states, and prevent unauthorized access through predictable or shared links.
- Offer export access before account deletion; during the 30-day account recovery period, normal account access remains suspended, so any additional export route must be separately designed and authorized rather than assumed.
- Supporting specifications must define exact field coverage, large exports, file availability, export preparation limits and privacy/security acceptance tests; the seven-day generated-file lifetime is confirmed.

### Confirmed export preparation and ready notifications (12 October 2026)
- **Personal and Home data exports are prepared asynchronously in the background**, so users do not need to keep the export screen open. Show a clear export job status (requested, preparing, ready, failed, expired) and provide retry/recovery guidance for failures.
- When an export is ready, notify the requesting user through **both an in-app notification and an email**. The email must not attach the archive or expose an unauthenticated direct download URL; it should direct the user to sign in to Madeora.
- **Sign-in is required to download the ZIP archive securely**, with the previously confirmed permission and underlying data-access checks performed on every download. Sensitive downloads may require additional verification according to the security specification.
- The seven-day availability period begins when the export is successfully generated, not when the user opens the notification. Clearly display the expiry date/time in the export screen.
- If export preparation fails, show a meaningful failure state without claiming success; specify safe retries and notification handling in supporting requirements. Notification delivery failure must not alter the export's ready state.
- Export jobs must honor permission revocation, account/Home suspension or deletion, and privacy boundaries throughout generation and delivery; no export should become downloadable after the requester loses authorization.

### Confirmed export package structure (12 October 2026)
- **Every personal and Home data export is delivered as a single organized ZIP archive**, not a collection of separate module downloads.
- Inside the ZIP, organize eligible content into **clearly named module folders**. Include **JSON and/or CSV** representations appropriate to each data type, and eligible uploaded attachments/media in accessible file formats. Preserve relationships between records and attachments using stable references.
- Include a **manifest** describing the export type (personal or specific Home), generation date, included modules, file formats, attachment references, record counts where meaningful, schema/version information, and clearly stated exclusions or failures. Do not place sensitive secrets, access tokens, or other members' private information in the manifest.
- Use predictable, portable filenames and character encodings, and ensure the archive can be opened using common ZIP tools. Clearly report partial export failures rather than presenting an incomplete archive as complete.
- The previously approved **7-day download expiration** and **permission rechecks on every download** apply to the ZIP archive. Export authorization and privacy boundaries remain unchanged.
- Supporting specifications must define exact schema and attachment organization, large-export handling, and safe ZIP packaging; these implementation details do not change the approved single-archive experience.

### Confirmed generated export expiration (12 October 2026)
- **Personal and Home data export files expire 7 days after successful generation.** Clearly show the expiry date/time and expired state; remove or render generated artifacts inaccessible after expiry, subject only to legally required retention outside normal download access.
- **Recheck authentication, export permission, Home membership, and underlying record-access rights on every download**, not merely when the export was requested or generated. Revoked access must immediately block download, even within the seven-day window.
- Never serve a stale export containing information the requester is no longer entitled to access. If authorization or underlying visibility has changed, deny download and require a fresh authorized export rather than attempting to rely on the old file.
- Protect download links from public sharing or unauthorized reuse; provide clear messages for expired, revoked, unavailable, and failed downloads. This policy applies equally to personal and Home exports.

### Confirmed Home data export permissions (12 October 2026)
- **Home-owned data export is a separately configurable Home permission**, available from MVP. The **Owner** can export Home data and may grant or revoke the export capability for selected **Administrators or Members**.
- An authorized requester can export **only Home-owned records and fields they are otherwise permitted to access**. Export permission does not override module visibility, record-level access, privacy settings, or restrictions on other members’ personally owned content shared into the Home.
- Keep Home export distinct from the existing personal data export in Account Settings. Use portable structured formats (JSON/CSV as appropriate) and eligible files, with clear scope and exclusions.
- Enforce both export permission and underlying data access at generation and download time. Recheck permissions before download; revoked or departed members must not retain access to generated Home exports. Protect export files, record audit events, and provide clear processing, failure, and expiration states.
- Home deletion/recovery and account suspension must block unauthorized exports. Supporting specifications must define large-export handling, download lifetime, and exact per-module data coverage.

### Confirmed export choice before account deletion (12 October 2026)
- Before the user can confirm deletion of their Madeora account, require an **explicit, non-preselected choice** between **“Export my data first”** and **“Continue without exporting.”** Do not let the user accidentally bypass this decision.
- **Export my data first:** Start the already approved background personal-data export flow, with secure sign-in download, in-app and email ready notifications, and a seven-day ZIP availability period. Keep the account usable and do not initiate account deletion until the user has had an opportunity to obtain the export and explicitly proceeds with deletion. The user must still complete the normal deletion confirmation and any required Home ownership transfers.
- **Continue without exporting:** Permit the user to proceed to the normal account-deletion confirmation without generating an export, while clearly explaining the eventual permanent loss of personal data after the 30-day recovery period.
- Export is **optional**, not a condition for account deletion. Do not assume the user downloaded a generated archive merely because preparation succeeded; show accurate status.
- This explicit export choice is separate from the already confirmed ability to request personal exports at any time in Account Settings.

### Confirmed Madeora account deletion and recovery (12 October 2026)
- Users may request deletion of their entire Madeora account, subject to an explicit, consequential confirmation and clear explanation of the recovery deadline and data consequences.
- **Before account deletion can begin, the user must complete an accepted ownership transfer for every Home they own.** A pending invitation or transfer request is insufficient. Account deletion must not leave any Home ownerless.
- Account deletion starts a **30-day recovery period**. During this period the account is **inaccessible for normal use**; provide a secure, narrowly scoped recovery path allowing the account holder to cancel deletion and restore their account before the deadline.
- After the 30-day period expires without recovery, **permanently delete the account and its personally owned data**, subject to applicable legal retention requirements. Explain any mandatory retention clearly and avoid presenting retained records as available for normal use.
- Account deletion must not erase **Home-owned content** or another member's personally owned content. Remove the deleting user's memberships and personally owned sharing relationships consistently with the approved Home departure/content ownership policies. For Home-owned tasks assigned to the user, clear future assignments and flag them for reassignment while preserving relevant Home history.
- Suspend the deleting user's account-level sessions, personal integrations, notifications, and access during recovery. Do not delete external Google Calendar events solely because the Madeora account is being deleted; revoke/remove personal integration access upon final deletion without impacting other users' accounts.
- **Confirmed integration behavior on account restoration (12 October 2026, option B):** Preserve the user's personal integration **preferences and configuration** throughout the 30-day recovery period, but keep all personal third-party synchronization **disabled** after account restoration until the user **explicitly reconnects and authorizes each service again**. Do not silently resume Google Calendar or other integrations based on an old token, even if technically still valid.
- Show each formerly connected integration as **Reconnection required** and explain that no synchronization is occurring. Only resume after successful user-initiated authorization, checking current provider access and permissions. Reconcile intervening changes with the confirmed conflict-resolution policy; do not overwrite either side silently.
- Account restoration does not reactivate integrations attached to a Home where the user no longer has authorized membership, and does not affect other members' or Homes' independent integrations. Original third-party data remains untouched solely because of account deletion/restoration.
- **Confirmed account-deletion notification privacy (12 October 2026, option C):** Do **not** send notifications to other Home members when a user requests or completes Madeora account deletion. Do **not** send them notifications about membership changes or task reassignment resulting from that account deletion, whether in-app, email, push, or via Ora-generated alerts.
- Necessary Home state changes still take effect: affected membership/access and Home task assignments must be updated correctly, and authorized members may see the current membership list, unassigned tasks, and reassignment flags when they view the Home. **No proactive alerts** to other members about these account-deletion-related changes.
- Keep the reason for departure (account deletion) private; do not expose it in Home activity feeds, audit views available to other members, notification digests, or other member-facing surfaces. This does not override legally required security records or notifications to the account holder.
- This choice is specific to **account deletion and its resulting membership/task changes**. It does not revoke the separately approved notifications to all members when an entire **Home** is deleted or restored.

- **Confirmed membership recovery policy (12 October 2026, option B):** If the account is restored within its 30-day recovery period, restore **only Home memberships that remain authorized and have not been revoked** by authorized Home members. A removed or revoked membership is not reinstated merely because the account was recovered; the user needs a new invitation to rejoin. Do not restore access to a permanently deleted Home or an inaccessible Home pending deletion.
- Any **Home ownership transferred before account deletion stays transferred** after account recovery. The returning user cannot automatically reclaim Owner status; they return only with whatever role and permissions remain authorized.
- During the recovery period, authorized Home members must be able to revoke the suspended user's membership so the revocation is respected at restoration. Apply authorization checks before restoring Home visibility, shared items, permissions, and integrations; never expose Home data based on stale cached memberships.
- Supporting specifications must define the timing of membership suspension versus removal, email/account verification for recovery, treatment of queued actions, notification behavior, data export, third-party tokens, backups, and legally required retention.
- The 30-day account recovery window is separate from any Home deletion recovery period and does not permit bypassing ownership-transfer requirements.

### Confirmed Home deletion and recovery (12 October 2026)
- Only the **Home Owner** may initiate deletion of a Home, after a deliberate confirmation explaining consequences and the recovery deadline.
- Deletion starts a **30-day recovery period**, rather than immediately permanently erasing the Home. The Home becomes **inaccessible to all members** during this period, including through dashboards, widgets, Ora, notifications, integrations, wall displays, and direct links.
- The Owner may **restore the Home within those 30 days**; restoration must recover Home-owned content, membership, roles, and Home settings consistently, subject to appropriate security checks.
- **Confirmed invitation invalidation (12 October 2026):** As soon as Home deletion is initiated, **all pending email invitations and shareable invitation links for that Home are permanently invalidated**, regardless of their previous expiration date or remaining uses. They must **not** become valid again if the Home is restored. Authorized members must issue fresh invitations after restoration. Existing accepted memberships are not invitations and follow the separately approved Home restoration policy.
- Reject acceptance attempts against invalidated invitations, including race conditions and stale links, without exposing Home data; show a helpful invalid-invitation message.
- If not restored before the deadline, **permanently delete the Home and its Home-owned data** according to the approved retention and deletion policy. Do not delete members' personally owned source items; revoke their sharing relationship to the deleted Home.
- Clearly show the scheduled permanent-deletion date and restoration action to the authorized Owner, without exposing archived Home data to others.
- **Confirmed Home deletion/restoration notifications (12 October 2026):** When Home deletion begins, **every current Home member (including the Owner)** receives both an **in-app notification and an email** stating that the Home is inaccessible and showing its scheduled permanent-deletion date. When the Home is restored, **all those members** receive both an in-app notification and an email confirming restoration. Notification delivery failures must not silently cancel or reverse the deletion/restoration operation; track and report delivery state appropriately.
- **Only the Owner can restore** the Home during its 30-day recovery window. Notifications to other members are informational and do not confer restoration or access rights while the Home is pending deletion.
- **Confirmed Home Google Calendar lifecycle (12 October 2026, option B):** On initiation of Home deletion, immediately **suspend all Home-specific Google Calendar connections and synchronization**, including inbound updates, outbound writes, background jobs, and Home-visible cached calendar data. No Home member can access those connections during the 30-day recovery period.
- If the Owner restores the Home within 30 days, **restore eligible Home-specific connections** only where provider authorization, account ownership, and permissions remain valid. Clearly identify any connections that require reauthorization; resume synchronization safely with conflict detection rather than silently overwriting changes made during suspension.
- If the Home reaches permanent deletion, permanently remove its Home-specific connection configurations, tokens/authorizations held for that Home, and associated Home-side sync state, subject to mandatory legal/security retention.
- **Never delete original Google Calendar events as a consequence of Home deletion**, including events that originated in Madeora and were synchronized to Google. Home deletion is not equivalent to the separately confirmed explicit “Delete everywhere” event action.
- Personal Google Calendar connections belonging to individual members and their events remain unaffected by deletion of one Home, except that Home-specific calendar visibility/sharing is suspended and ultimately removed if the Home is permanently deleted. If a connection serves multiple Homes, do not interrupt unaffected Homes.
- Define reconnection eligibility, queued writes, webhook events, and conflict recovery in the supporting integration specification; never claim synchronization succeeded when it did not.


- Supporting specifications must define precise deadline/timezone handling, notification delivery/retry details, third-party integrations, backups/legal retention, queued actions and restoration conflict handling; invitation invalidation on deletion is confirmed. Do not treat those details as already approved.

### Confirmed voluntary Home departure (12 October 2026)
- **Members and Administrators may leave a Home at any time without approval** from the Owner or another member.
- Before departure, show an explicit confirmation explaining the immediate loss of Home access, the removal of their personally owned shares from that Home, retention of Home-owned content, and unassignment of their outstanding Home tasks. Do not force the user to obtain permission to leave.
- After confirmation, revoke Home access and apply the approved content-ownership and task-unassignment policies consistently across views, Ora, integrations, notifications, and wall displays.
- **The Owner cannot leave until an existing Home member has accepted ownership transfer**; a pending or declined transfer is not sufficient. After transfer completes, the former Owner may leave as a non-Owner member.
- No automatic grace period or undo is implied by this decision; returning to a Home follows the established invitation process unless a separate recovery rule is approved.

### Confirmed guided transfer-or-close for departing Home Owner (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A Home Owner who wants to leave must complete a guided **transfer ownership or close Home** flow; the Home must never become active without an Owner. This clarifies the existing voluntary-departure rule rather than allowing an unapproved ownerless state.
- **Transfer path:** Show eligible adult Home members, select a proposed successor, explain the consequences and send an explicit ownership-transfer invitation. Ownership changes only after the eligible recipient accepts and authorization is revalidated. Pending, declined, expired or withdrawn invitations do not permit the current Owner to leave. After accepted transfer, the former Owner can confirm ordinary voluntary departure under the existing content, access-revocation and reassignment rules. Do not auto-promote children, Household Displays, guests or nonmembers.
- **Close path:** When no eligible/accepting successor exists, the Owner may initiate the already-approved **Home deletion** workflow, including explicit destructive-action confirmation, notice to current members, immediate Home access/synchronization suspension, and the established **30-day recoverable deletion period**. Only the Owner authorized under that deletion policy can restore during the recovery window; closing does not create a successor or transfer Home-owned data to the departing person's personal space.
- Explain clearly that **closing is not the same as leaving a still-active Home**: the Owner retains the limited restoration authority needed for the deletion recovery window, without continued normal Home access. The Owner's personal account and other Home memberships remain intact. Do not claim the Owner has fully severed all Home-related recovery responsibility until permanent deletion or a separate authorized recovery resolution.
- Preview shared Home-owned content, personally owned shares, unfinished chores/tasks, affected child memberships/guardianships, Household Display pairings, integrations, and access implications without disclosing data beyond the Owner's permissions. Reuse existing retention, notification, account-privacy and historical-attribution policies; never silently delete personal originals or third-party calendar events.
- Protect against concurrent ownership changes, transfer acceptance races, stale membership roles, duplicate close requests, offline retries and in-progress deletion/restoration. All steps must be permission-checked and auditable; errors must leave the Home in a consistent owned or explicitly pending-deletion state.
- Acceptance criteria: Owner with eligible accepting successor; no adult successor; transfer declined or pending; transfer acceptance while membership changes; Home with children and paired displays; deletion and restoration within 30 days; deletion expiry; owner belonging to multiple Homes; queued actions and synchronization shutdown; no ownerless active Home.

### Confirmed member departure and removal: content ownership (12 October 2026)
- When a member voluntarily leaves a Home or is removed by an authorized member, **Home-owned content stays in that Home**, including Home-owned tasks, events, recipes, shopping items, and memories.
- **Personally owned content shared into that Home by the departing member is unshared from that Home** and becomes private to its owner with respect to that Home. The departing member retains their personal item; the Home and its remaining members lose access to it.
- If the same personal item is shared with other Homes, those other sharing relationships remain unchanged. Never delete the underlying personal item or revoke unrelated Homes' access.
- Other members' personal and shared content is unaffected by the departure.
- Apply these rules to all views and access paths, including widgets, search, Ora, notifications, integrations, cached/offline data, and wall displays. Do not leave accessible stale copies of newly private information.
