- Explain the consequences before a voluntary departure or authorized removal, without revealing private details to the remover. Enforce permissions and ownership on the server, not only in UI.
- Distinguish **ownership** from **creator/contributor**: an item created by a member on behalf of the Home remains Home-owned. Do not assume everything the member created is personally owned.
- Existing single-Owner safeguards still apply: the Owner must complete an accepted ownership transfer before leaving.
- **Confirmed task reassignment rule (12 October 2026, option B):** When a member leaves or is removed, any active Home-owned tasks assigned to them remain in the Home but become **unassigned** and are **clearly flagged for reassignment**. Do not automatically assign them to the Owner or require reassignment before departure/removal.
- Apply this to outstanding chores, recurring task assignments and other Home-owned task types, preserving task content, schedules, and completion history. For recurring tasks, future assignments to the departed member must be cleared; do not erase past completed occurrences or attribution.
- Members with appropriate Home task-management permissions can reassign the flagged tasks. Show a useful reassignment queue/state without exposing the departing person's private data.
- Supporting specifications must address attribution/history, third-party calendar events, queued offline actions, and retention/audit behavior without violating the confirmed ownership rule.

### Confirmed origin metadata for independent Home-owned copies (12 October 2026)
- **Home-owned copies created through personal-to-Home ownership transfer show a subtle, non-intrusive origin indicator**, such as **“Copied from personal”**, in relevant item details or contextual UI. Include the **date of copying** in an optional details section.
- Clearly distinguish the independent copy from live synchronized sharing: **do not imply ongoing synchronization, a live source link, or automatic updates**. Editing or deleting a copy does not change the personal original or other Home copies.
- **Do not reveal private source content or personal account/profile information** through origin metadata, item previews, tooltips, search, notifications, exports, Ora responses, or audit views. Do not expose other destination Homes or copies to users lacking access to them.
- Keep the indicator accurate if the personal original is later deleted or its account permanently deleted; do not retain a navigable link to inaccessible or deleted source content. Apply the confirmed account-deletion anonymization rules to any identity-bearing provenance.
- Define responsive placement, accessibility text, and empty/unknown provenance states in screen specifications without making the origin label visually dominant.

### Confirmed MVP personal and Home storage allowances (12 October 2026)
- **MVP must enforce separate, configurable storage allowances for each personal account and each Home.** Personal-owned uploads and attachments count against the owner's personal allowance; independently Home-owned content and attachments count against the destination Home's allowance, including copies created by ownership transfer.
- Display storage **used, available, and total allowance** in appropriate personal and Home settings, visible only to authorized viewers. Explain which storage pool an upload or transfer will use and show actionable over-limit errors.
- **Block uploads and ownership transfers that would exceed the relevant allowance**, including transfers that require copying media. Validate capacity before committing and recheck at execution to prevent concurrent operations from bypassing limits. Never claim a transfer succeeded if required attachments could not be stored; leave source content intact and give clear retry guidance.
- Storage quotas are **configurable product policy**, not hard-coded assumptions. Exact personal/Home limits, tiers, pricing implications, and any paid expansion remain **open until pricing is decided**. Do not invent amounts or imply unlimited storage.
- Supporting specifications must address usage calculation, file-size constraints, quotas for multi-Home transfers, deletion/recovery storage accounting, and graceful handling of quota changes without silently deleting existing content.

### Confirmed full-storage behavior (12 October 2026)
- **When a personal or Home storage allowance is exhausted, restrict only new file/media storage and operations requiring additional file/media capacity.** Do not block creation or editing of ordinary text-based records, tasks, calendar events, shopping lists, or other non-file features merely because the storage pool is full.
- Block new uploads and permanent ownership transfers whose required attachment copies would exceed the applicable personal/Home allowance. A transfer with no additional file/media storage requirement may proceed if otherwise authorized; do not unnecessarily block it based solely on exhausted file storage.
- Continue allowing users to view, edit metadata for, download where authorized, and delete existing files; deleting content to free space must respect recovery/retention rules. Do not silently delete or degrade existing content.
- Clearly identify the affected storage pool and explain how to free capacity or obtain more when available. Apply capacity checks at execution as well as in the UI, including multi-Home operations.
- This is a **file/media storage quota**, not a blanket restriction on all Madeora data or account functionality. Exact allowances remain undecided pending pricing.

### Confirmed deleted-file storage accounting (12 October 2026)
- **Deleted files and media continue counting toward their original personal or Home storage allowance while recoverable.** Storage is released only after the underlying file/media is **permanently deleted** and no longer retained as recoverable content.
- Show recoverable deleted-file usage in the storage breakdown where authorized, so users understand why deleting an item has not yet freed capacity. Do not promise immediate storage recovery for soft deletion.
- On restoration, recoverable media retain their original quota allocation; restoration must not double-count the file. Once permanently removed, update usage and applicable 80%/95% warning states.
- Apply this rule consistently to deleted attachments, personal items, Home-owned copies, and any applicable account/Home recovery periods. Respect privacy and ownership boundaries; deleting one independent copy does not release storage occupied by another.
- The **7-day individual-file recovery period** is confirmed below; the permanent-deletion workflow must honor the separately approved 30-day account and Home recovery periods.

### Confirmed deleted-file recovery period (12 October 2026)
- **Individually deleted files and attachments remain recoverable for 7 days after deletion**, then are permanently deleted and their occupied personal or Home storage allowance is released.
- During the seven-day window, authorized users can restore recoverable files from the appropriate personal or Home recovery area. Show the deletion time, scheduled permanent-removal time, and remaining recovery window; do not promise recovery after expiry.
- While recoverable, files continue counting toward their original storage pool, consistent with the confirmed deleted-file storage accounting policy. Restoring a file does not count its size twice.
- After the seven-day window, remove access to the file and its recoverable copies; respect legally required retention outside normal user-accessible recovery without promising immediate physical erasure of backups.
- **Scope:** This is the standard recovery period for individually deleted files/attachments, **not** the separately approved 30-day account or whole-Home recovery period. A file contained in an account/Home undergoing whole-entity recovery must remain restorable with that entity during its applicable 30-day recovery window; deletion of the entire account/Home must not be shortened to seven days by this rule. Define interaction and retention precedence in the supporting specifications.
- Define the behavior for files attached to deleted parent records, simultaneous restores, permission revocations, and failures in permanent-removal processing; never expose files to users who have lost access.

### Confirmed recovery period for individually deleted items (12 October 2026)
- **Individually deleted Madeora items across modules** (including tasks, recipes, memories, shopping lists, and comparable records) **remain recoverable for seven days** after deletion, then are permanently removed from normal recovery access. This matches the confirmed seven-day recovery period for individual files and attachments.
- Restore the deleted item and its exclusively attached, still-recoverable media together, preserving relationships, ownership and authorized access. Files referenced by other active items remain available to those items.
- Provide an appropriate personal or Home trash/recovery view with deletion and expiry dates; require current item-level and Home permissions to view or restore. Once the seven-day window ends, do not promise restoration.
- **Entire account and Home deletions retain their separate 30-day recovery periods.** The seven-day item/file policy must not cause content required for whole-account or whole-Home recovery to expire prematurely. Legal retention requirements remain applicable.
- Specify consistent handling of irreversible early deletion, recurring calendar items, externally synchronized records, restoration conflicts and partial failures in module-level specifications. Deleting a Madeora item must not implicitly delete external provider data contrary to the approved integration rules.

### Confirmed meaningful conflict handling on item restoration (12 October 2026)
- **Restore individually deleted Madeora items automatically when restoration is safe**, preserving the original record identity, ownership, relationships, and eligible attachments under the confirmed seven-day recovery policy.
- **Detect genuine restoration conflicts and ask an authorized user to resolve them before applying a conflicting change.** Examples may include a unique slot or identifier already occupied, invalidated parent relationships, conflicting recurrence/integration state, or permission changes that prevent restoration to the former destination. Never silently overwrite active data.
- **Matching names or titles alone are not conflicts**: two tasks or recipes may legitimately have the same name, and restoration should not force deduplication on that basis.
- Provide clear, context-specific options only where safe and permitted (such as restoring as a separate item, selecting a valid destination, or cancelling), showing the consequences. Do not assume a generic overwrite/merge is always valid. Recheck permissions and the seven-day recovery deadline before committing.
- Handle concurrent changes, expired trash items, unavailable attachments and external calendar synchronization without losing active data or implying restoration succeeded when it did not. Supporting module specifications must identify actual uniqueness and relationship constraints and corresponding acceptance tests.

### Confirmed individual and selected-item bulk restoration (12 October 2026)
- **Personal and Home trash support both restoring one item and restoring a user-selected set of recoverable items in a single action.** Bulk restore is not an implicit restore-all operation; the user chooses the items.
- For each selected item, **recheck current authorization, ownership, destination validity, and the seven-day recovery deadline**. Restore safe items automatically, including their recoverable exclusively attached files and relationships.
- **Treat each item independently:** meaningful conflicts, expired items, missing attachments, insufficient permissions, and processing failures must be reported per item, without blocking restoration of unrelated safe items or silently overwriting active data.
- Show an accurate bulk result summary (restored, needs resolution, failed, no longer eligible) and provide a way to resolve eligible conflicts individually. Do not describe partially successful bulk actions as fully completed.
- Respect module-specific integration/recurrence rules and the separately confirmed 30-day whole-account/Home recovery policies. Repeated restore requests must not create duplicate records or attachments.

### Confirmed personal Ora hide and disable control (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each member can independently **hide and disable Ora** through personal settings and can re-enable it at any time. This choice affects only that member, not other members or their Home's shared records, permissions or assistant preferences.
- When disabled, hide Ora's persistent entry point, expandable panel, contextual suggestions, proactive reminders and celebrations, and Ora-specific interaction prompts across personal and Home screens. Do not initiate Ora-driven background suggestions or display Ora content on that member's personal surfaces while disabled. Core Madeora modules, their ordinary controls, and essential non-Ora system notifications remain available.
- Disabling Ora is **not the same as deleting data**: preserve the member's configured Ora preferences and controlled long-term memories unless they separately delete or change them. Existing seven-day rolling interaction history continues to expire on its normal schedule; disabled status must not extend retention or create new interactions. Provide distinct controls for clearing history and managing memories.
- Re-enabling restores Ora's eligible entry points and the member's previous frequency/category settings, subject to current permissions and Home context. Do not repeat first-use onboarding automatically, resurrect expired history or automatically execute previously pending requests. Any abandoned guided workflow requires fresh preview and confirmation.
- Keep wall-display Ora settings and display-only behavior separately scoped: disabling Ora for one member must not suppress a shared wall display for everyone. No Home-wide disable authority is introduced by this personal preference.
- Acceptance criteria: hide/disable from personal settings, persistence across sessions/devices, no Ora surfaces or proactive activity while disabled, unaffected core functions and other members, unchanged independent Home wall display, re-enable with preserved preferences, privacy and history expiry, accessibility and no unexpected queued actions.

### Confirmed Ora graceful unavailability fallback (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When Ora is unavailable because of a service outage, timeout, or connectivity failure, keep its entry point and panel visible with a calm, clearly labeled **temporarily unavailable** state. Do not hide Ora or imply that a request succeeded.
- Offer a user-triggered **Retry** control and direct navigation to relevant Madeora screens or actions that the member can use without Ora. Never make access to core Madeora features dependent on Ora availability; distinguish an Ora-only failure from a broader app or network outage where core functionality may also be affected.
- Preserve already confirmed changes and authoritative module data. For an interrupted guided workflow, show only verified outcomes, never automatically replay unconfirmed or potentially duplicate writes, and require a fresh permission/record-state check and appropriate preview/confirmation before retrying a consequential action.
- Keep recent interaction history and controlled long-term memories separate from availability status. Do not invent responses, expose hidden errors or sensitive data, or promise that a failed request was saved; make recovery and retry status understandable.
- Respect accessible status announcements, keyboard navigation, mobile layouts, reduced-motion preferences and the wall-display display-only restriction. Wall displays may omit unavailable suggestions but must not expose interactive retry or personal history.
- Acceptance criteria: Ora-only outage, timeout, offline state, retry success/failure, partial workflow completion, stale context or permissions, no duplicate actions, accessible error state and uninterrupted operation of otherwise available Madeora modules.

### Confirmed Ora lightweight first-use introduction (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** On first use, Ora offers a **short, skippable, interactive introduction** within its existing expandable panel. It never blocks access to Madeora, takes over the dashboard, or creates a standalone AI onboarding experience.
- Introduce Ora as a **focused Madeora helper, not a general-purpose AI assistant**. Present three or four concrete capabilities grounded in enabled modules and current permissions: for example, checking today's schedule, finding relevant chores, helping with shopping or meals, and showing household reminders. Do not promise unavailable capabilities.
- Offer one optional **Try an example** action using a safe, read-only Madeora request by default. If an example would change data, show the existing preview and require explicit confirmation; never seed real records or perform writes merely to demonstrate Ora.
- Provide obvious **Skip** and **Done** controls. Once completed or skipped, do not automatically repeat the introduction on subsequent visits or devices for that member; allow voluntary rediscovery of a concise “What Ora can do” help view in the panel without creating chat-management features.
- Adapt examples to personal versus Home context and to module availability, membership and permissions; avoid exposing private Home information in onboarding examples. Keep content concise, keyboard/screen-reader accessible, responsive and compatible with reduced-motion preferences.
- Acceptance criteria: first-time display, skip/done persistence, optional example with safe confirmation, returning-member behavior, no blocking overlay, permission-aware examples, Home switching, accessibility and unavailable-module fallbacks.

### Confirmed Ora text-only interaction (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Ora is **text-only** throughout Madeora. Members interact using simple Madeora-scoped typed requests, contextual suggestions, and action buttons. Do not implement microphone controls, speech recognition, text-to-speech, voice playback, spoken reminders, or voice-first modes.
- Preserve Ora's lightweight helper scope and limited guided workflows: text entry does not turn Ora into a general-purpose chatbot or autonomous agent. Changes still require the existing preview, explicit confirmation, authorization, and approval safeguards.
- The previously specified abstract **“listening” animation state must not imply audio capture**. In the text-only product, interpret it as attentive/ready-for-input or focused typing state, and label it accordingly in the visual specification; never display a microphone/listening indicator or request microphone permission.
- All messages, suggestions, status changes, errors, confirmations, and celebrations must be accessible through readable text and keyboard/screen-reader-compatible controls, with reduced-motion alternatives.
- Acceptance criteria: users can complete supported Ora journeys without audio permissions or audio hardware; no voice controls or spoken output appear on desktop, mobile, or wall displays; Ora's states accurately reflect text interactions.

### Confirmed Ora limited guided cross-module workflows (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora can coordinate a bounded catalog of **predefined Madeora-specific multi-step workflows** across authorized modules (for example, looking up a planned meal and its recipe, then proposing ingredients to add to a selected shopping list). This is guided product assistance, **not** unrestricted autonomous planning, external agent execution, or a general-purpose AI chatbot.
- Before any changes, present a concise **impact preview** identifying the source records, target personal space/Home, proposed changes, and any important assumptions or duplicates. Obtain **explicit user confirmation** before applying the workflow; never interpret an initial natural-language request as final confirmation.
- Respect per-module permissions, ownership, sharing boundaries, approval requirements, existing validations and audit trails at **every step**, including after Home/context switches. Never use Ora to bypass approval workflows or silently expose personal data in Home destinations.
- If information is missing or ambiguous (for example, multiple dinners, recipes, or shopping lists), ask a focused clarification or offer safe choices. Do not invent records or assume cross-Home targets. Support cancellation before confirmation.
- On execution, report actual per-step outcomes accurately. Prevent duplicate additions and repeated submissions; handle concurrent edits, stale previews, partial failures and retries without claiming the whole workflow succeeded. Where a step cannot be safely completed, stop or offer a clearly explained recovery path instead of silently proceeding.
- Keep the workflow catalog intentionally limited and visible in Ora's contextual suggestions and action buttons; unsupported requests should redirect to the appropriate Madeora screen. No separate chat threads, folders, search, or general-purpose agent capabilities are introduced.
- Acceptance criteria: a supported cross-module workflow previews and confirms before writing; ambiguous targets are clarified; permission changes prevent unauthorized writes; partial failures and duplicate requests are handled safely; cancelled and unsupported requests do not change data.

### Confirmed Ora contextual suggestions, actions, and limited natural-language input (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora combines contextual suggestions, actionable buttons, and a simple natural-language text field in its existing expandable panel. The text field accepts ordinary Madeora-related requests (for example, “What is on my schedule today?” or “Show my overdue chores?”) without requiring rigid commands.
- **Strict product scope:** Ora helps members understand and operate authorized Madeora modules, records, routines, and Home workflows. It is **not** a general-purpose AI assistant, unrestricted web researcher, content-generation studio, or standalone chat product. Do not add separate conversations, chat folders, chat search, or broad conversational capabilities.
- Responses should be concise, relevant, grounded in currently accessible Madeora information, and paired with clear action buttons when useful. For out-of-scope requests, politely explain Ora's Madeora-focused capabilities rather than fabricating an answer or pretending to have external access.
- Resolve ambiguous requests with a short clarification; never invent missing records or claim that a task was completed before its action succeeds. Any consequential write, deletion, sharing, purchase-like redemption, or permission-sensitive action must follow existing authorization, approval, and confirmation rules.
- Preserve privacy boundaries between personal space and each Home; on context switches, re-evaluate access and do not carry over private retrieved details or action targets. Respect member preferences, quiet behavior, memory consent, and accessibility (including keyboard entry, screen readers, and reduced motion).
- Acceptance criteria: Madeora-focused free-text questions work alongside suggestion chips and action buttons; ambiguous/out-of-scope requests are handled safely; permission changes and Home switches cannot expose information; failed actions do not report success; no full-chat management features appear.

### Confirmed Ora seven-day rolling interaction history (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora retains a lightweight, bounded rolling history of interactions for **seven days**, including across app closes and subsequent sign-ins. Individual interaction entries expire automatically seven days after their creation; opening the panel or sending a new message does not extend older entries' retention.
- Keep a single simple interaction stream in Ora's expandable panel, **not** separate conversations, folders, titles, pins, or search. The history is for short-term continuity only; long-term controlled preference memories are stored and managed separately and are not deleted merely because an interaction expires.
- Scope and protect history by the relevant individual and personal/Home context. Switching context, leaving a Home, revoked access, or signing out must not expose previously accessible private/Home content; recheck permissions before showing any retained interaction or action. Never treat history as permission to perform a write without fresh confirmation.
- Provide a straightforward **Clear recent interactions** control that removes the user's accessible stream from active display and future Ora context; show confirmation of the effect, without deleting separately saved long-term memories. Expiration/clearing must not erase underlying tasks, rewards, approval audits, or other authoritative records.
- Keep the panel responsive with concise messages and useful empty/expired states; do not imply that seven-day history is a full transcript archive. Apply applicable retention, account/Home deletion and data-export policies, without promising immediate physical erasure from legally retained backups.
- Acceptance criteria: continuity after reopening within seven days; automatic expiration of entries older than seven days; clear-history behavior; separate long-term memory retention; Home switches and membership/permission changes; sign-out/session isolation; expired-action revalidation; no multi-chat management features.

### Confirmed Ora lightweight helper scope and simple interaction history (12 October 2026)
- **User clarification overrides any interpretation of Ora as a full general-purpose AI/chat product.** Ora is a lightweight, context-aware Madeora helper for relevant reminders, celebrations, guidance, and limited permission-aware actions; not a standalone chatbot or full AI workspace.
- **No separate chat threads, new-chat management, folders, conversation renaming, pinning, or chat search.** Do not design a ChatGPT-style conversation library or dedicated chat-history management screens.
- Keep one simple, bounded recent interaction stream within the existing expandable Ora panel so users can follow an ongoing exchange and return after minimizing it. Older interaction retention should be minimal, privacy-aware, and explicitly specified; avoid promising indefinite full transcripts.
- Keep the previously approved controlled preference memory separate from this lightweight interaction stream. Members can review/correct/delete remembered preferences without needing chat threads or a full chat archive. The assistant's contextual suggestions and user controls remain in scope.
- **Reconcile earlier 'conversation history' wording** in the persistent-panel and memory requirements as lightweight recent interaction continuity only, not multi-conversation chat management. Scope, simplicity, and low UI overhead take precedence.
- Acceptance criteria: Ora panel preserves recent context on minimize/navigation; no thread/folder/search UI exists; personal/Home context boundaries prevent leaks; memory controls remain usable; interaction data follows applicable privacy and deletion rules.

### Confirmed transparent smart creation of Ora memories (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may automatically save useful, non-sensitive **personal** preferences and recurring context as long-term memory, without requesting approval for each ordinary preference. The user must be clearly informed what was saved, where it is stored, and offered an immediate **Undo** action.
- **Explicit informed confirmation is required before saving sensitive information or any shared Home memory.** Do not infer consent to save or share from conversation context; authorization and Home scope must be checked at the time of confirmation. No cross-Home or personal-to-Home propagation occurs automatically.
- Automatic memory creation must be selective, relevant, and non-intrusive: avoid speculative inferences, incidental remarks, unnecessary duplicates, and treating tentative statements as durable facts. Provide a setting to disable automatic personal memory creation without disabling Ora entirely.
- Undo must reliably remove the newly saved memory from active use and retrieval, with clear feedback; if an existing memory was updated, undo restores the previous version when safe. Users can subsequently review, edit, and delete memories in the existing memory-management interface.
- On contradictory preferences or ambiguous scope, ask rather than silently overwrite; current explicit instructions and authoritative Home records take precedence over remembered context. Apply privacy, export, retention, deletion, and permission rules from the confirmed controlled long-term Ora memory section.
- Acceptance criteria include: non-sensitive personal memory with disclosure and undo; sensitive-memory consent refusal; shared Home-memory permission and consent; wrong-Home prevention; duplicate/conflict handling; disabled automatic memory; undo after update; and no retrieval of deleted memories.

### Confirmed controlled long-term Ora memory (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may retain useful long-term preferences and recurring context across conversations to improve assistance, rather than relying solely on conversation history. Memory is controlled and transparent, not an unrestricted automatic profile.
- Members can **review, correct, and delete** remembered information in an accessible memory-management interface; changes take effect in future assistance and are reflected consistently in Ora's retrieval behavior. Clearly distinguish long-term memories from chat history and underlying Home records.
- **Personal memories remain private to their owner. Home memories are separately scoped to a specific Home** and available only to members with the applicable permissions. Never automatically promote personal preferences, conversation contents, or memories into shared Home memory, or propagate one Home's memories to another.
- Before saving a shared Home memory, make the sharing scope clear and require appropriate authorization; never infer consent to share private information merely from chatting in a Home context. Honor deletion, member departure, Home/account deletion, and revoked permissions without exposing retained context through summaries, suggestions, or historical chats.
- Provide clear visibility when Ora is using remembered context and a practical way to correct it. Avoid storing sensitive personal information by default; require explicit, informed consent where appropriate. Memories must not override current source-of-truth records or explicit user instructions.
- Specify memory creation and update rules, retention, user controls, conflict resolution, data export/deletion, and permission-based acceptance tests in the Ora supporting specification. Do not promise instant deletion from technical backups where retention obligations apply.

### Confirmed Ora subtle animated achievement celebrations (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora acknowledges genuine achievements with **brief, subtle animations and concise celebratory text**, either near the completed action in its relevant module or inside Ora's existing panel. Avoid intrusive overlays, full-screen effects, confetti, focus-stealing pop-ups, or a separate celebration center.
- Trigger only after the underlying module confirms a successful eligible completion, milestone, or reward outcome; never imply that pending approval, a failed operation, or an unfulfilled reward is already completed. Existing chore, reward, and leaderboard rules determine eligibility; celebrations do not award extra points.
- Respect each member's celebration-category toggle, Quiet/Balanced/Active settings, quiet hours, disabled Ora state, dismissal/suppression behavior, and accessibility preferences. When motion is reduced, provide a comparable unobtrusive static acknowledgment; do not rely on motion, sound, or color alone. No sound or voice is introduced.
- Prevent duplicate celebrations across the module and Ora panel, repeated refreshes, device sessions, and retried events. Keep celebrations short, dismissible, and non-blocking; never interrupt editing, confirmations, or keyboard focus.
- Personal achievements remain private. Shared Home and wall-display celebrations are limited to information explicitly permitted for that audience; honor leaderboard opt-outs and revoked access, and avoid revealing personal balances or sensitive details.
- Specify presentation, trigger eligibility, success-versus-pending states, accessibility, privacy, preference behavior, deduplication, and error-state acceptance criteria in the Ora visual and functional supporting specifications.

### Confirmed Ora partial overviews with source availability disclosure (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When one or more relevant data sources are unavailable, delayed, or known to be stale, Ora still provides a useful **partial daily or weekly overview** from currently accessible and sufficiently reliable records. Never block the entire answer merely because one integration or module is unavailable, and never describe a partial overview as complete.
- Display a concise, clearly visible notice identifying which relevant source or category is missing or outdated (for example, Google Calendar synchronization), with its last-known refresh time where reliably available. Distinguish live accessible records from stale or unverified information; do not silently substitute old cached records as current facts or fabricate missing commitments.
- If the unavailable source could materially affect counts, busy-day highlights, conflict assessments, or an apparent “nothing planned” conclusion, qualify or omit those conclusions. An empty available subset must not be described as a completely free day. Keep the optional supporting-record expansion limited to actual permitted records and label incomplete coverage.
- Respect the active personal/Home context, member permissions, provider visibility, and module reminder rules. Do not reveal inaccessible records or infer their contents from synchronization errors. Never create independent early warnings, extra notifications, or automatic schedule changes because a source is unavailable.
- Where useful, offer a simple manual refresh/retry or navigation to the affected integration/module; explain when data remains unavailable. If a follow-up arrives after sources recover, rebuild the answer using freshly authorized records rather than silently relying on the prior partial result.
- Acceptance criteria: Google Calendar offline with tasks/chores available; stale integration with known/unknown last-sync time; no available records; partial week with a missing day/source; duplicate prevention after recovery; incomplete busy-day assessment; source-details expansion; permission revocation; cross-Home boundaries; mobile and screen-reader announcement of incomplete coverage.

### Confirmed Ora expandable overview sources (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora keeps daily and weekly cross-module summaries and busy-day observations concise by default, while offering an optional **“View related items”** expansion containing the specific accessible calendar events, tasks, chores, and reminders that support the answer.
- Show each supporting item with a clear source-module label, relevant date/time or status, and a link to its original record where navigation is permitted. Group or deduplicate linked/synced representations so the same underlying commitment is not misleadingly counted twice. The expansion is read-only; editing follows existing module or confirmed Ora action flows.
- Resolve the expansion only within the member's currently active personal/Home context and current permissions. Recheck access and source freshness when opening or following links, including after Home switching, revoked access, deletion, or sync changes. Never expose even the existence or metadata of private or unauthorized records.
- If a source becomes inaccessible, unavailable, or stale, omit restricted details and clearly indicate that the supporting breakdown may be incomplete. Never fabricate links or imply that a factual claim has complete support when the available records do not establish it.
- The disclosure control must be keyboard- and screen-reader-accessible, usable on mobile, and not force a verbose record list into every answer. It does not create a new reporting module, notifications, or additional data retention.
- Acceptance criteria: compact default answer; expand/collapse; direct links; deduplicated synced events; busy-day count correspondence; private/shared mixed records; Home switching; revoked/deleted records; stale/offline source; empty support; mobile and assistive technology.

### Confirmed Ora visible busy-day highlights (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** In an explicitly requested daily or weekly overview, Ora may briefly point out days with **visibly more scheduled commitments** than other days in the requested period, based solely on current records accessible in the active personal space or Home. This is a modest factual observation, not an assessment of the member's stress, capacity, health, or ability to cope.
- Base a highlight on an evident concentration of accessible appointments, tasks, and chores, considering timed versus untimed entries and avoiding double-counting synced or repeated representations. Use restrained wording such as “Wednesday looks busier, with several scheduled items”; avoid hard-coded universal workload thresholds or claims that a day is objectively overloaded.
- Do not reveal the existence, timing, or busy status of private or unshared events, including when summarizing a shared Home. Where permissions or source freshness make the overview incomplete, avoid definitive comparative claims and disclose the limitation where relevant.
- Show at most a short contextual observation alongside the requested overview; do not add separate proactive alerts, background workload monitoring, extra deadline reminders, or automatic rescheduling. Members can navigate to the source records; any later edits require the already approved preview, permission checks, and explicit confirmation.
- Acceptance criteria: one conspicuously dense day; evenly distributed week; only untimed items; mixed durations; duplicate synced events; canceled/completed items; hidden private calendars; incomplete sync; switching Homes; no inferred stress or automatic changes; concise accessible mobile output.

### Confirmed Ora on-demand daily and weekly overviews (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora supports concise, read-only unified overviews for **a specific calendar day or a calendar week**, requested explicitly by a member. Combine relevant accessible calendar events, tasks, chores, and existing reminders in the currently active personal space or Home; do not expand this into monthly/custom-range analysis or independent deadline monitoring.
- Resolve expressions such as “this week” and “next week” against the member's applicable timezone and Madeora's established week-start locale/settings; display the exact date range and active context. For an ambiguous week reference, ask for clarification rather than silently choosing a different period.
- Weekly responses group commitments by day in date order, summarize the most relevant items concisely, distinguish timed/all-day/untimed items, and provide links to source modules for complete detail. Preserve the existing daily overview behavior when a single day is requested.
- Apply the same per-request permissions, Home isolation, linked-calendar deduplication, completed/canceled state handling, source freshness and partial-data disclosures as the approved daily overview. Never expose inaccessible events or silently combine data from different Homes.
- Weekly overviews are **on demand only**; they do not create a weekly greeting, notification, reminder schedule, recurring digest, or additional proactive panel indicator. The separately approved optional once-per-local-day first-visit overview remains daily only. Any proposed data changes still require an editable preview and explicit confirmation.
- Acceptance criteria: today/tomorrow; current/next/specified calendar week; week crossing month/year and daylight-saving boundaries; locale week start; mixed timed/untimed entries; duplicate synced events; privacy and Home switching; partial/unavailable data; empty and dense weeks; no unsolicited weekly delivery; and accessible responsive presentation.

### Confirmed Ora on-demand unified daily overview (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** In response to a member's explicit request such as “What do I have planned for tomorrow?”, Ora may assemble a **concise unified daily overview** from relevant accessible calendar events, tasks, chores, and existing module reminders. This is a read-only cross-module view, not a new planner, independent reminder service, or comprehensive behavioral analysis.
- Resolve the requested date in the member's applicable timezone, display the date and active personal-space/Home context, and gather only records authorized in that context. Do not silently mix Homes, private records from other members, or inaccessible linked-calendar details. Clearly label categories or sources where helpful and distinguish scheduled times from all-day or unscheduled due items.
- Prefer a compact chronological overview for timed commitments with a short separate section for due or untimed items. Deduplicate the same underlying event or reminder shown through multiple integrations; do not invent ordering, certainty, attendance, priority, or availability. Respect canceled/completed states and current source-of-truth data.
- When a source is delayed, unavailable, partially synced, or access has changed, disclose the incomplete scope rather than presenting the answer as exhaustive. Provide useful links to source modules and a concise empty state when nothing relevant is found. Refresh scope and permissions for each request, including after switching Homes.
- This on-demand response is **distinct from** the already approved optional once-per-local-day first-visit overview: explicit requests can be repeated, but do not trigger extra unsolicited overviews, independent deadline warnings, notifications, or background monitoring. Any suggested modification still uses an editable preview and explicit confirmation.
- Acceptance criteria: tomorrow/today and date rollover; mixed timed and untimed records; personal vs Home context; private or revoked records; multiple Homes; linked calendar duplication; incomplete sync; timezone boundaries; completed/canceled entries; empty results; disabled modules; repeat explicit requests; and mobile, keyboard, and screen-reader presentation.

### Confirmed Ora panel-only daily overview presentation (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** The optional daily overview is presented **inside Ora’s existing expandable panel only**. Do not create a dashboard greeting, separate daily-overview widget, banner, modal, or full-screen introduction for it.
- On the member’s eligible first visit of the local day, indicate that today’s overview is ready with a **subtle, accessible state on Ora’s existing entry point** (for example a restrained dot or short accessible label). Do not automatically open the panel, interrupt focus, or show the overview elsewhere.
- Opening Ora reveals a concise overview within the same lightweight interaction stream, with a few authorized highlights and relevant module links; no separate chats, folders, search, or dedicated overview screen. The member can dismiss the overview and the entry-point indicator together.
- The indicator must respect the member’s Ora enablement, proactive settings, quiet hours, dismissal state, reduced-motion preferences, and screen-reader accessibility. It must not be a persistent unread-message counter or imply a required action. Avoid repeated prompts across devices, refreshes, or Home switches.
- Preserve the previously approved **once-per-local-day** presentation rule: no repeated unsolicited overview; an explicit request can regenerate the current context’s overview. Personal/Home scope and permissions are checked again on opening and on navigation. Never reveal overview content in the entry-point indicator or on shared wall displays.
- Ora outage and empty-data states remain non-blocking; if no meaningful overview can be prepared, avoid a misleading ready indicator. Any record-changing quick action follows existing editable preview and confirmation rules.
- Acceptance criteria: indicator visibility, first eligible opening, dismissal, multiple devices, quiet hours, disabled Ora, empty overview, inaccessible data, context switching, responsive layouts, keyboard and screen-reader operation, and wall-display privacy.

### Confirmed Ora optional daily first-visit overview (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may present **one optional, concise daily overview** when a member first opens Madeora on a given local calendar day. This is a lightweight summary, not a separate daily-planning chatbot, mandatory onboarding step, or automatically executed workflow.
- Show only a small selection of relevant, authorized information, such as today's events, upcoming or overdue tasks and chores, and timely household reminders. Prefer clear source-of-truth records and existing priority fields; do not fabricate events, urgency, availability, or a comprehensive schedule. Include quick navigation to relevant modules where useful.
- The overview is **non-blocking and dismissible**, and must not appear more than once per member per local day across devices or personal/Home navigation. Opening Madeora repeatedly, refreshing, switching Homes, or reopening Ora must not cause duplicate daily prompts. An explicit member request to see today's overview may display it again.
- Respect personal Ora enablement, the applicable proactive-suggestion/reminder categories, Quiet/Balanced/Active frequency, quiet hours, dismissal suppression, and accessibility settings. If quiet hours or other suppression prevents the first-visit presentation, do not interrupt; the member can request the overview later. Essential module notifications remain separate.
- Scope the overview to the **currently selected personal space or Home** and the member's permissions; never combine private data from multiple Homes or expose other members' private details. Context changes require a freshly scoped overview only upon explicit request, not a second unsolicited daily prompt.
- Keep wording concise and use Madeora's interface language and Ora's established consistent friendly tone. Avoid detailed explanations unless asked. Any quick action that modifies data still requires the normal editable preview, confirmation, and module-specific permission/approval rules.
- Handle no relevant records with a brief optional empty state, and stale or unavailable records without misleading claims. An Ora outage must not block the dashboard; follow the approved graceful fallback.
- Acceptance criteria include first visit, repeated visits and multiple devices, timezone/day rollover, dismissed overview, quiet hours, disabled Ora, category settings, personal/Home switching, missing data, inaccessible records, wall-display privacy, and no automatic writes.

### Confirmed Ora reliance on module reminders (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Ora does **not** independently monitor approaching deadlines or generate additional proactive early-warning notifications. Deadline and due-date alerts are owned by each Madeora module and follow that module's configured reminder schedule, delivery rules, and permissions.
- Ora may display, summarize, or link to **existing module reminders** in its panel, optional daily overview, and permitted contextual surfaces, subject to personal Ora settings and current access. This must not create a second reminder schedule, duplicate notification, or independent deadline-monitoring service.
- Preserve the approved task-priority guidance: when the member opens Ora or encounters an eligible contextual suggestion, Ora may highlight a few upcoming or overdue tasks using existing dates and priorities. Such informational guidance is **not** a proactive advance deadline warning, must not independently trigger because a deadline is approaching, and must respect dismissal suppression.
- Turning Ora off, using Quiet mode, or dismissing an Ora suggestion must not alter the underlying module's reminders. Conversely, disabling a module reminder must not cause Ora to recreate that alert through a different channel. Essential system notifications remain governed by their own product rules.
- When asked directly about upcoming deadlines, Ora can retrieve authorized records and answer concisely, without changing reminders or deadlines. Never infer urgency from private or inaccessible records or imply a reminder was scheduled when it was not.
- Acceptance criteria: approaching deadline with and without module reminders; disabled module reminder; disabled Ora; Quiet mode and quiet hours; daily overview; existing task-priority suggestion; duplicate suppression; Home privacy and revoked access; and no unsolicited Ora early-warning notification.

### Confirmed Ora lightweight task-priority suggestions (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may occasionally highlight a small, manageable selection of relevant upcoming or overdue tasks based on explicit due dates, existing priority fields, and evident urgency in authorized Madeora records. This is optional guidance, **not** automatic prioritization, rescheduling, or task modification.
- Prefer recorded priority and deadlines over speculative assumptions. Make clear when items have equal or unknown priority; do not invent urgency, claim an objective ranking where none exists, or use sensitive personal inferences to determine importance. Avoid overwhelming members with long ranked lists.
- Suggestions may link directly to the relevant task or offer only existing, permission-checked quick actions. Any requested write must follow Ora's editable preview, explicit confirmation, affected-member visibility, and module-specific approval/notification rules. Never silently reassign, reschedule, complete, or edit a task.
- Respect the current personal/Home context, member permissions, per-member Ora enablement and reminder/suggestion category settings, Quiet/Balanced/Active mode, quiet hours, and smart dismissal suppression. Never leak another member's private tasks or mix data from different Homes without the established context confirmation.
- Keep the suggestion concise, with reasoning available on request rather than displayed by default. When records are stale, conflicting, missing due dates, or inaccessible, omit unsupported priority claims and offer a relevant navigation fallback. Essential module notifications remain distinct from optional Ora suggestions.
- Acceptance criteria include multiple overdue tasks, ties and missing priorities, upcoming deadlines, member-specific visibility, context changes, dismissals, Ora disabled/Quiet mode, stale task state, and no unintended record changes.

### Confirmed Ora occasional recurring-routine suggestions (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may occasionally suggest converting a clearly repeated, eligible behavior into a recurring Madeora item (for example a chore, task, or other module-supported recurrence). It must remain a lightweight, focused helper, not an autonomous routine manager.
- Base suggestions on a sufficiently clear pattern in records the member is currently authorized to access. Do not infer sensitive habits, combine private data across Homes, or claim certainty from sparse, irregular, or ambiguous observations. No automatic creation, modification, or deletion of routines.
- Show a concise proposed recurrence with an editable preview of the target personal/Home space, destination, cadence, start date, assignment and other required module fields; ask for essential missing details, and require explicit confirmation before saving. Honor existing recurrence semantics, module permissions, affected-member visibility, and notifications.
- Respect each member's Ora enable/disable state, suggestion-category toggle, Quiet/Balanced/Active frequency, quiet hours, and contextual placement. Rate-limit pattern suggestions, deduplicate them across surfaces, and suppress dismissed suggestions for the same situation until circumstances meaningfully change.
- Never suggest a routine that already exists in an equivalent form, has been intentionally stopped, or would conflict with current permissions or module rules. Explain unsupported recurrence options without inventing them; allow the member to decline without modifying existing records.
- Acceptance criteria cover genuine versus incidental repetition, an existing matching routine, repeated dismissal, changed circumstances, per-member/Home privacy, preview edits, confirmation, failed save, revoked permissions, and disabled Ora suggestions.

### Confirmed Ora safe defaults and essential-detail clarification (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** For supported Madeora actions, Ora uses existing, explicitly configured product/module defaults when they are applicable, authorized, and safe. It does not invent missing dates, times, assignees, destinations, or sharing scopes from uncertain assumptions.
- When an essential field has no safe default (for example, when a reminder should occur), ask a **concise, focused clarification** before offering an executable action. Where multiple fields are required, gather only what is needed in a simple, efficient flow; do not repeatedly ask for information already supplied.
- Show every filled-in default and user-provided value in the **editable action preview**, including the target personal space/Home, affected records or members, schedule, and consequences as applicable. Require explicit confirmation before any write, including otherwise routine actions; changing a preview field must update validation and impact information.
- Recheck required fields, permissions, applicable defaults, record freshness, Home context, and module-specific validation at confirmation. A changed or unavailable default must trigger a refreshed preview or clarification rather than silently changing the intended action.
- Never use a default to bypass a module's approval rules, cross-Home consent, sensitive-information safeguards, or restrictions on actions affecting other members. A default may suggest a value but must not be treated as consent.
- For unsupported or insufficiently understood requests, use Ora's approved brief clarification/fallback behavior rather than speculative inference. Handle cancellation, invalid input, stale data, and unavailable modules without partial or duplicate changes.
- Acceptance criteria: safe configured default; required time missing; multiple eligible destinations; no default available; member edits a proposed value; defaults changed mid-flow; Home switch; permission denied; validation failure; cancellation; confirmation; accessibility and concise localized copy.

### Confirmed Ora language follows Madeora interface (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Ora uses each member's currently selected Madeora interface language for its panel, contextual suggestions, quick actions, onboarding, confirmations, errors, reminders, and celebrations. There is **no separate Ora language setting** and no automatic language-switching based on the language of typed input.
- Ora's supported text understanding is limited to Madeora-related requests in languages officially supported by the product. If a request cannot be reliably interpreted in the selected interface language, use the approved concise clarification or unsupported-request fallback; never pretend to understand or execute an uncertain action.
- Changing the Madeora interface language changes subsequent Ora interface copy and responses consistently without modifying underlying records, member preferences, stored memories, or the original wording of user-entered content. Existing user-authored record names and text are not silently translated.
- On shared wall displays, Ora follows the wall display's configured interface language, not an individual member's private language setting. Respect existing Home boundaries and visibility restrictions.
- Localize concise action previews, undo labels, accessibility labels, notifications, and error states consistently; keep dates, times, and numbers aligned with Madeora's applicable locale settings. Missing translations must use the product's documented localization fallback rather than mixed-language invented copy.
- Acceptance criteria: no Ora-specific language selector; member language changes propagate to new Ora output; typed requests in unsupported languages fail safely; shared wall displays use display language; no unintended translation or disclosure of stored records.

### Confirmed consistent Ora personality without customization (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Ora has one consistent friendly, warm and lightly playful personality for every member. There are **no personality presets, per-member tone sliders, humor settings, or custom personality instructions**.
- Ora may still adjust its *situational wording* for clarity and appropriateness: brief for routine actions, celebratory for genuine achievements, and restrained and respectful for sensitive situations. This is context-sensitive communication, **not** member-specific personality customization.
- Preserve existing per-member Quiet/Balanced/Active suggestion frequency, category toggles, quiet hours, reduced-motion and accessibility preferences. These control delivery or presentation, not Ora's underlying personality.
- Keep Ora's text, contextual prompts, wall-display celebrations, onboarding, and error states consistent with the same character. Never use private personal context to personalize shared/Home or wall-display messages.
- Acceptance criteria: no personality configuration UI; consistent copy across members and devices; appropriate serious/error-state language; existing notification and accessibility preferences still work independently.

### Confirmed Ora affected-member action visibility (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** For supported Ora actions that assign, reassign, reschedule, or otherwise change records affecting other Home members, the mandatory pre-execution preview clearly identifies the affected member(s), the target Home and record, and the proposed change (including previous and new assignees or times where applicable).
- The requesting member must explicitly confirm the preview before any change. Apply the underlying module's existing role/capability permissions, validation, conflict rules, and approval requirements; Ora does **not** add a new affected-member acceptance or approval requirement, and it must not bypass one already required by the module.
- After a successfully committed action, use the affected module's existing notification behavior and each recipient's applicable notification settings to inform affected members. Avoid duplicate Ora-specific notifications, and never announce a change as complete before the module confirms success.
- Do not expose private member details or information from another Home in previews, notifications, or error messages. For partial failures, changed permissions, stale assignments, or concurrent edits, clearly report what did and did not change and offer a safe correction path; do not silently retry an action that could duplicate effects.
- Ora's supporting specification must include acceptance cases for one/multiple affected members, unchanged assignee, notifications disabled, module approval required, insufficient permissions, cross-Home isolation, stale previews, and successful or failed corrections/Quick Undo.

### Confirmed Ora smart suppression of dismissed suggestions (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Dismissing an Ora suggestion suppresses that specific suggestion for the current situation across the panel, dashboard widgets, module screens, and applicable proactive delivery surfaces. Do not repeatedly show it merely because time has passed, a page was reopened, or a member switched devices.
- Ora may propose a similar suggestion again only when a **meaningful, relevant circumstance changes**, such as a newly planned meal, a different shopping need, a materially changed deadline, or a new occurrence of a recurring event. A cosmetic change, ordinary refresh, or unchanged outstanding task is not sufficient.
- Keep suppression specific to the member, suggestion purpose, relevant record/occurrence and personal/Home scope. Dismissing a prompt does not dismiss other members' prompts, cancel an underlying chore/reminder, alter Home records, or mute essential operational alerts. Respect category toggles, Quiet/Balanced/Active modes, quiet hours, and Ora's personal disable setting independently.
- If a suggestion becomes completed, obsolete, unauthorized, or its source is removed, retire it rather than resurfacing it. Never leak the existence of records from another Home or from revoked permissions while comparing suggestions or determining whether circumstances changed.
- Offer a clear, accessible dismiss action without requiring a reason. Avoid escalating tone, pressure, repeated nudges, or repeated reminders of the same ignored proposal. A member's explicit new request can still access the relevant supported action without treating it as an unsolicited reappearance.
- Define suggestion identity, meaningful-change triggers, deduplication and suppression lifecycle at product level in Ora's supporting specification; do not create an elaborate suggestion-management interface. Acceptance criteria: cross-surface dismissal, app restart, unchanged situation, genuine new occurrence, completed/obsolete suggestion, personal vs Home separation, multi-member isolation, quiet hours, and permissions changing after dismissal.

### Confirmed Ora minimal suggestion explanations (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Ora presents proactive suggestions concisely, without routinely explaining why they were generated. Members can request an explanation when they want one; do not add automatic reasoning paragraphs, expandable explanation panels, or AI-style justifications to each suggestion.
- When asked, give a short, factual explanation based only on currently accessible Madeora records or an explicitly permitted remembered preference. Clearly distinguish observed information from a tentative suggestion; do not invent motivations, sources, certainty, or hidden reasoning.
- Explanations must respect the active personal/Home context, live visibility and permissions. Do not expose private or cross-Home data, inaccessible source records, sensitive memories, or information that has been deleted or revoked. If the basis cannot be shared, say so briefly without leaking details.
- Keep essential disclosures independent of this preference: action previews, destination and scope, confirmations, approval consequences, error states, privacy notices, and material effects must still be shown when required. Minimal explanation never weakens informed consent or safety.
- On wall displays, keep suggestions short and privacy-safe; no interactive explanation request is available. Respect member suggestion frequency, dismissal, accessibility, and Ora disable settings.
- Acceptance criteria: concise default suggestion; on-request factual explanation; missing or ambiguous basis; changed permissions or deleted source; personal/Home separation; required action disclosures; and display-only wall behavior.

### Confirmed Ora short-lived Quick Undo for safe actions (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** After a confirmed Ora action succeeds, show a concise result with a short-lived **Undo** control only when that particular action has a defined, safe and permission-authorized reversal. This is a convenience for correcting immediate mistakes, not a general action-history or rollback system.
- Specify supported reversible action types and the exact Undo availability window in the Ora supporting specification. Display Undo only while it remains available; expiration removes the shortcut, not the ordinary record-management or recovery options. Make the outcome and affected record clear.
- Undo must recheck live identity, current personal/Home context, permissions, record state, dependent actions and concurrent edits. Never silently overwrite another person's subsequent changes, reverse external side effects that cannot be recalled, bypass approvals, or undo points/rewards, financial-like balances, redemptions, notifications or integrations without an explicitly safe module-specific reversal rule.
- Where an action cannot be reversed safely, or the Undo window has elapsed, provide a direct link to the affected record or the existing authorized correction workflow; explain why Undo is unavailable without implying the underlying change was reversed.
- If Undo is requested during an outage, on a stale record, after a permission change, or twice, return a truthful status and avoid duplicate or partial reversal. Audit reversals consistently with the underlying module; distinguish an Ora Quick Undo from the existing Trash/recovery and chore correction mechanisms.
- Keep controls accessible on mobile, keyboard and screen readers. Acceptance criteria include successful Undo, expired Undo, non-reversible actions, concurrent changes, revoked permissions, cross-Home boundaries, retries, and partial multi-step workflow results.

### Confirmed Ora smart destination selection with editable preview (12 October 2026)
- **Reconfirmed 13 October 2026:** The selected behavior is option B: propose the configured default or clearest contextually relevant *permitted* destination in an editable preview, and ask for clarification when no candidate is clearly preferable. This reconfirmation introduces no new automatic selection or execution rights.
- **Approved option B:** When a Madeora request matches multiple eligible destination records (such as shopping lists, calendars, task lists, or other module targets), Ora first proposes an explicitly configured default for the current personal/Home context; otherwise it may suggest the most relevant eligible record based on the active screen and clear request context.
- **Never silently execute a write.** Identify the proposed destination by name and scope in an editable action preview, show the intended changes, offer a straightforward way to choose another authorized destination, and require explicit confirmation before applying them. Choosing a destination does not bypass any separate approval or workflow rules.
- Relevance is a proposal, not an authorization: do not infer permission from prior usage, memories, or an apparent default. Filter choices by current membership, visibility, module permissions, and record availability; revalidate them on confirmation. Never expose inaccessible destination names or data.
- If no safe default or relevant candidate can be determined, or several candidates remain equally plausible, ask one concise clarification rather than arbitrarily choosing. If no eligible destination exists, explain why and offer permitted navigation or creation options, without creating anything implicitly.
- Keep selection scoped to the currently active personal/Home context. Referring to another Home follows the separately approved explicit context-switch confirmation before any destination preview or action. A default in one Home must never be used as a default in another.
- Handle destination deletion, permission changes, stale previews, cancellation, and failed or partial workflows safely; preserve existing data, avoid duplicate changes on retry, and show clear outcomes. A preview edit updates the pending proposal only until confirmed.
- Acceptance criteria: configured default; relevant candidate with no default; equally plausible candidates; changing the destination; no eligible records; cross-Home request; revoked permission or deleted destination before confirmation; cancelled preview; duplicate-submit protection; mobile and accessible selection controls.

### Confirmed Ora current-space context with confirmed cross-Home switching (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora defaults to the member's currently selected personal space or Home for interpreting requests, retrieving information, and preparing actions. Clearly label the active scope in the panel and in any preview; do not silently combine information across Homes.
- If a member explicitly names or unambiguously refers to another Home they can access, Ora may recognize the destination but must **request confirmation before switching the active context or performing an action there**. Show the destination Home and intended scope before proceeding; do not silently change the application's selected Home.
- When multiple Homes match, the reference is unclear, or the requested destination is inaccessible, ask one concise clarification or explain the limitation without revealing unauthorized Home details. A remembered preference, recent interaction, or inferred household association never constitutes authorization to switch.
- On confirmation, recheck current membership, module-level permissions, record visibility, and availability before showing target data or preparing the action. Any write still requires the existing action/workflow preview and explicit confirmation; confirming a context switch does **not** also approve the write.
- Keep interaction history, memory retrieval, contextual suggestions, and cached previews scoped to their originating personal/Home context. On switching, visibly reset or segregate context-dependent material; never surface private personal information or another Home's data in the new scope. If permissions change or the member cancels, leave the active context unchanged and discard pending cross-context actions safely.
- Wall-display Ora remains display-only and locked to the authorized displayed Home; it cannot initiate cross-Home switching. Members with Ora disabled are unaffected.
- Acceptance criteria: default current-space queries; named accessible Home with explicit switch confirmation; ambiguous/inaccessible destination; cancellation; permission revoked between preview and confirmation; separate confirmation for writes; no cross-Home history/memory leakage; and consistent mobile, desktop, and accessible context indicators.

### Confirmed Ora member-specific overlapping-assignment warnings (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When a proposed timed chore, task, or other eligible assignment overlaps an existing responsibility of its intended Home member, Ora clearly identifies **which assignee is affected** and the conflicting time window in its editable action preview. This specializes the existing clear scheduling-conflict warning; it does not impose new hard scheduling restrictions.
- Show the titles, timing, and links of overlapping responsibilities **only if the requesting member is currently authorized to see those records**. For inaccessible personal records, do not disclose their existence, content, or inferred details; show only a generic availability indication where Madeora's existing sharing/availability rules expressly permit it. Never use another Home's private records for conflict detection.
- Let the requester adjust the proposed time, assignee, or individual action, or remove the action before confirmation. Do not automatically reschedule, reassign, cancel, or optimize anyone's responsibilities; do not suggest unverified free times.
- Respect module-specific overlap rules: a non-blocking warning can be explicitly acknowledged and confirmed if the module allows the overlap; a module-defined blocking conflict remains blocking. Preserve any independent affected-member approval and notification requirements.
- For small combined multi-member requests, show conflicts **per action and per assignee**, including overlaps between proposed actions in the same unconfirmed preview, without duplicating warnings. Revalidate membership, visibility, timezones, recurrence occurrences, existing assignments, and conflicts immediately before execution; if conditions change, refresh the preview and require renewed confirmation.
- If conflict information is unavailable or incomplete, say so rather than claiming the assignee is free. Preserve safe per-action outcomes and retries, without repeating successful assignments or sending extra notifications.
- Acceptance criteria: same-person overlapping chores; different assignees with no conflict; overlap between two proposed actions; permitted warning override; module-blocked overlap; inaccessible personal event; revoked access; another Home; changed assignment after preview; recurrence and timezone edge cases; partial source outage; accessible mobile and screen-reader warning.

### Confirmed Ora small multi-member combined actions (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may prepare a **small number of predefined supported actions involving different members of the same active Home** in one request (for example, assign kitchen cleaning to one member and bathroom cleaning to the requester). This extends the approved limited combined-action workflow; it does not authorize broad group distribution, automatic assignment recommendations, or unrestricted bulk operations.
- Show **one editable row per proposed action** in a combined preview, identifying the target record, action, assignee/recipient, Home scope, exact schedule and timezone where relevant, and assignment, notification, points/rewards and approval consequences. Allow editing or removing each action before confirming the precise final set.
- Resolve each named person to a uniquely identifiable, currently accessible member of the active Home. Ask a concise clarification for duplicate names, unclear pronouns, ambiguous action types or missing required time; never silently infer a recipient or expose inaccessible member information. Cross-Home requests retain their separately confirmed context-switch rule and must not silently mix Homes.
- **Validate every action independently** at preview and immediately before execution: membership, permissions, record visibility, module capability, scheduling/conflicts, affected-member notification preferences and module-specific approval rules. A single combined confirmation may approve only eligible explicitly shown actions and never bypasses a separate module approval.
- Report **per-action outcomes** (completed, pending approval, failed or uncertain). On partial failure, keep successful actions intact, offer separately confirmed safe retries only for failed actions after refreshing and revalidating, and prevent duplicate assignments, points or notifications. Never automatically roll back successful actions.
- Follow existing 24-hour unconfirmed-draft expiry, current-context navigation continuity, seven-day interaction history, accessible mobile previews, and privacy boundaries. Do not introduce a new messaging channel or send independent Ora notifications.
- Acceptance criteria: two assignments to different members; assignment to self plus another member; same-named members; member removed during preview; unauthorized assignee; differing notification preferences; approval pending; one failed action with safe retry; duplicate-submit prevention; mixed-Home request; edit/remove one action; small action limit; and no execution without confirmation.

### Confirmed Ora interpretation of requests involving other Home members (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When a member names another Home member in an Ora request, Ora may propose the **clearly supported, most plausible predefined action** (such as assigning a chore or creating a supported shared reminder) but must identify the action type explicitly. Do not treat mentioning a person as blanket authorization to assign work, notify them, or access their private information.
- In the **editable, pre-execution preview**, show the named recipient or assignee, relevant Home, destination record/module, precise requested date and time (with the requesting member's configured timezone unless explicitly overridden), and whether the action will assign responsibility, create a reminder, or trigger existing module notifications. Show relevant approval requirements and consequences; allow the member to change or cancel the proposed interpretation.
- **Ask a concise clarification** when assigning a task versus notifying someone (or another supported action) are equally plausible, when the person cannot be uniquely matched to an accessible Home member, or when essential intent or information is missing. Do not infer a recipient from memory, recent actions, or an inaccessible Home. For a reminder or scheduled action with no exact clock time, follow the approved exact-time clarification rule.
- Check current membership, permissions, record visibility, module capability, recipient notification preferences, and any Home-context switch at preview and execution. Never create a new direct-messaging or permission-request channel, override a member's notification preferences, reveal private records, or bypass existing module approvals. Confirming a cross-Home switch remains separate from confirming a write.
- Execute only the **explicitly confirmed** supported action; use the underlying module's notification and approval behavior, without duplicate Ora notifications. Clearly distinguish completed, pending approval, rejected and failed results, with safe separately confirmed retries where applicable.
- Acceptance criteria: unambiguous chore assignment; ambiguous “remind [member]” requiring clarification; duplicate member names; nonmember/inaccessible recipient; shared versus private destination; recipient notification disabled; permission denied; module approval pending; timezone difference; missing time; stale membership; cross-Home request; preview edit/cancel; no unauthorized or duplicate notification.

### Confirmed Ora member-timezone interpretation for timed actions (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** When a member supplies a clock time for an Ora action but does not specify a timezone, interpret it in the **member’s currently configured timezone**, regardless of whether the action targets a personal record or a shared Home record. Do not silently substitute the Home, calendar, device-location, or recipient timezone. An explicitly stated timezone overrides this interpretation where the destination module supports it.
- Clearly display the **calendar date, local clock time, and interpreted timezone** in every timed-action editable preview; allow correction before explicit confirmation.
