If a different Home/calendar timezone governs storage, recurrence, notification, or other downstream scheduling behavior, disclose the relevant difference and consequences in the preview and apply the module’s established rules without silently changing the member’s requested instant or wall-clock intent.
- If the member timezone is missing or invalid, the explicitly named timezone is unrecognized, or the local time is nonexistent/ambiguous during a daylight-saving transition, ask a concise clarification rather than guessing. For recurring actions, explain applicable DST behavior and ensure recurrence uses the confirmed timezone under module rules.
- Revalidate configured timezone, permissions, schedule conflicts, provider synchronization, and affected records immediately before execution; refresh the preview and require renewed confirmation if an important value changed. Cross-Home requests still require separate context-switch confirmation. No action executes without its ordinary confirmation.
- Acceptance criteria: personal reminder without explicit timezone; shared Home task with different Home timezone; explicit timezone override; member travelling without changing configured timezone; missing/invalid timezone; DST gap/overlap; recurring schedule across DST; timezone changed between preview and confirmation; external calendar timezone mismatch; accessible mobile display and no silent timezone substitution.

### Confirmed Ora exact-time clarification for unscheduled requests (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** When a member asks Ora to **create a reminder or scheduled action** but does not provide an exact clock time, Ora must ask for the intended time before preparing an executable action, even if the underlying module could support date-only or all-day entries. Do not infer a default time, silently create an untimed reminder, or choose a time from past behavior or personal memories.
- Ask one concise, localized question (for example, “What time tomorrow?”), retaining any supplied date, recurrence, destination and other valid details. A vague daypart such as “morning” is not an exact time; ask for a clock time. If the member explicitly requests an all-day or date-only item, distinguish that deliberate choice from an **omitted** time and apply the relevant module's support and validation; do not misrepresent an all-day task as a timed notification.
- Show the chosen date, exact time, timezone, recurrence and destination in the editable preview, and require explicit confirmation. Validate DST gaps/overlaps, past times, permissions, conflicts and module rules at confirmation. For recurrence, ask for a clock time when an occurrence needs a timed reminder; never assign one implicitly.
- This requirement governs **creation of reminders and scheduled actions**; it does not change the display of existing untimed tasks, all-day events or date-only responsibilities in daily/weekly overviews, nor prevent ordinary records that have no scheduling requirement from remaining untimed.
- Acceptance criteria: “remind me tomorrow” prompts for a time; “tomorrow morning” prompts for an exact time; an explicit 14:30 is preserved; explicit all-day/date-only intent is handled according to module capabilities; unsupported all-day intent is clarified; weekly recurring reminder missing a time; timezone and DST edge cases; cancel/invalid time; no write before confirmed preview.

### Confirmed Ora clearly labeled interpretation of ambiguous dates (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** For natural-language date phrases with multiple reasonable interpretations (such as “next weekend,” “this Friday,” or “end of the month”), Ora may propose one reasonable **specific calendar date or date range** based on the current date, member locale, timezone, and applicable module conventions. It must not silently treat that interpretation as certain.
- Clearly label the interpretation and display the **exact day, date, time and timezone when relevant** in the editable action preview. The member can correct the date before explicitly confirming; never execute a write from the phrase alone. Preserve any explicitly supplied date or time rather than replacing it with an inferred one.
- **Ask a concise clarification instead of choosing** when interpretations are similarly plausible, when ambiguity materially changes the outcome (such as a deadline, recurrence start, appointment involving others, or time-sensitive reminder), or when a reliable interpretation cannot be made. Do not learn personal date-phrase meanings implicitly or use memories to override explicit instructions.
- Revalidate the proposed date and affected records at confirmation, including timezone, daylight-saving transitions, past dates, recurrence rules, permissions, conflicts and any cross-Home scope. Show corrections or errors before execution and apply existing module approvals and notifications.
- Acceptance criteria: ambiguous “next weekend” with preview correction; locale-specific week start; near-midnight and timezone differences; month/year boundary; DST transition; consequential deadline requiring clarification; explicit date taking precedence; recurring schedule start; context switch; stale preview; and no unconfirmed action.
- **Reconfirmed (13 October 2026):** The member explicitly selected option B again: propose a clearly labeled interpretation in an editable preview, but require clarification when ambiguity is consequential. This reconfirms the existing rule and does not change the separate approved rules for exact-time clarification or member timezone defaults.


### Confirmed Ora helpful empty daily/weekly overview states (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When a requested daily or weekly overview has no visible matching records, Ora shows a concise, friendly **empty state** and at most **one or two relevant, predefined, permission-aware actions** (for example, create a task or open the calendar). Do not invent commitments, fill an empty day with unsolicited activity suggestions, or imply the member must schedule something.
- Distinguish **no visible items** from **no items matching active filters** and from **incomplete or unavailable data**. State the active day/week, personal/Home context and applicable filters; when a relevant source is stale, disconnected, loading or unavailable, label the result as incomplete and do not assert the schedule is free. Do not disclose hidden records, restricted counts, or inaccessible sources in a way that leaks private information.
- Offer only actions supported by enabled modules and the member's current permissions. Keep choices optional, subtle and consistent with the standardized overview format, including responsive mobile and accessible keyboard/screen-reader controls. If no safe action applies, provide the factual message and an appropriate permitted navigation link, or no action.
- Creating or modifying a record through an empty-state action follows Ora's existing guided workflow, editable preview, fresh validation, explicit confirmation, module approval and recovery rules. Opening a module does not modify data. Preserve selected date/week and filters on return within the same personal/Home context; recheck freshness and permissions after navigation or context changes.
- Acceptance criteria: genuinely empty day and week; filtered zero matches; unavailable Google Calendar alongside empty local modules; stale source; private/hidden records; member with no create permission; disabled module; create-task shortcut and cancellation; Home switch; mobile, keyboard and screen-reader behavior; no auto-created item or independent reminder.

### Confirmed Ora standardized daily and weekly overview format (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Ora uses **one consistent, product-defined presentation format** for all members' daily and calendar-week overviews. Do not offer per-member chronological-versus-module-grouped layout settings, remember overview layout preferences, or automatically adapt the format from usage patterns.
- Keep the established structure: daily items with a scheduled time appear chronologically, while untimed responsibilities are clearly separated; weekly overviews are organized day by day with the same daily structure. Apply consistent headings, record-type identification, status, and concise information hierarchy across members, screen sizes, and light/dark themes.
- Preserve previously approved functionality without treating it as a layout preference: explicit module/assignee/completion filters, expandable supporting records, contextual inline actions, factual busy-day notes, partial-data disclosures, and editable privacy-filtered copy previews. Filters change **which items appear**, not the standardized presentation format.
- Maintain permission-aware content, current personal/Home scope, local date/time handling, accessibility, responsive behavior, and wall-display privacy. A member's view may differ in **content** because of permissions or filters, but not in the underlying overview format.
- Acceptance criteria: identical structural format for two members with different permissions; timed versus untimed daily items; day-grouped week; applied and cleared filters; expanded sources and inline actions; partial-source warning; desktop/mobile and accessible reading order; no remembered or learned layout setting.

### Confirmed Ora copyable privacy-filtered overview sharing (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may prepare a **copyable text summary** of an existing daily or calendar-week overview for an **explicitly selected intended audience**. This is a member-controlled export for manual sharing, not direct messaging, publication, automated delivery, or a new Home-wide shared record.
- Before generating shareable text, ask the member to choose the intended audience or recipient context (for example, a named member of the active Home). Do not infer that everyone in a Home may see every Home-related item. Filter every included event, task, chore, reminder, description, assignment, and linked detail to what **both the requester and the selected audience are authorized to see** under current record visibility and module rules. Never include private personal-space items merely because the requester can see them, or leak the existence, timing, busy status, or counts of excluded records.
- Present the exact proposed text in an **editable, reviewable preview** before enabling Copy. Clearly identify the intended audience and active Home/personal context, note omitted private information without revealing it, and label partial or stale source coverage. Include only relevant details needed for the summary; avoid unnecessary sensitive notes, internal identifiers, and links that recipients may not access.
- Copy is an explicit member action, not a send action. Explain that **once copied outside Madeora, the application cannot enforce recipient restrictions or revoke the text**. Do not automatically share through Ora, invite recipients, post to a Home feed, or create notifications. Where recipient-specific visibility cannot be verified, do not claim the summary is safe for that audience; ask for a narrower selection or offer a clearly identified manually reviewed draft without a privacy-safety guarantee.
- Revalidate membership, permissions, record visibility, active context, and source freshness immediately before preparing the final copyable version; if anything changed, refresh and require renewed review. Preserve the original private overview unchanged, including its filters and expanded items; do not treat the copy as a data modification or extend Ora's interaction-history retention. Shared wall displays cannot copy private overviews.
- Acceptance criteria: named Home member audience; mixed shared/private records; requester-only event; recipient without access to a module; another Home selected; ambiguous audience; permission change before copy; stale or unavailable calendar sync; filtered weekly overview; editable preview; explicit Copy; no automatic send; accessible mobile and desktop copy flow.

### Confirmed Ora simple meal-planner scheduling (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may schedule an **existing, accessible recipe** into a specific date and supported meal slot in Madeora's existing meal planner, through a bounded guided action. It must not generate recipes, invent meal slots, create multi-day meal plans, or automatically add ingredients to shopping lists.
- Resolve the intended recipe, meal-planner destination, date, meal slot and active personal/Home context. Where names, dates, slots or destinations are ambiguous, ask a concise clarification. Apply the approved natural-language date interpretation, member timezone default and exact-time clarification rules where a module actually requires a clock time; do not invent one for date/meal-slot records.
- Present an **editable preview** with the exact recipe, date, meal slot, destination, relevant participants/visibility and proposed operation. Check whether the slot already contains a planned meal or a conflicting entry; show accessible details and offer only module-supported choices (such as selecting a different slot or explicitly replacing an existing entry where permitted). Never silently overwrite, move or remove a meal.
- Enforce recipe visibility, meal-planner permissions, personal/Home isolation, sharing and affected-member notifications and approvals. Revalidate the recipe, target slot, existing entries, permissions and source freshness immediately before execution; if material data changed, refresh the preview and request renewed confirmation. **No scheduling without explicit confirmation.**
- Report success, pending approval, failure or uncertain outcome accurately. Safe retries must not create duplicate meal plans or repeat successful writes. Keep the current Ora interaction and applicable 24-hour unfinished draft rules, with accessible mobile/desktop controls and links to the scheduled meal or original recipe.
- Acceptance criteria: unique recipe and free dinner slot; multiple matching recipes; missing/inaccessible recipe; existing meal in the slot; explicit replace where allowed; conflict not resolvable in Ora; unsupported slot; personal recipe proposed for shared Home; cross-Home request; timezone/date boundary; changed recipe or slot before confirmation; approval/permission change; cancel without write; retry after uncertain result; accessible mobile and keyboard preview.

### Confirmed Ora reviewed recipe-ingredient shopping transfer (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora supports a predefined, bounded cross-module workflow to transfer ingredients from an **accessible existing recipe** (including a clearly identified planned meal's recipe) into a selected, permitted shopping list. It does not invent ingredients, infer pantry inventory, automatically buy products, or expand into general recipe generation.
- Identify the exact source recipe and relevant version, meal/serving context if available, destination shopping list and active personal/Home scope. If multiple recipes, meals or lists plausibly match, ask for clarification; never silently copy private recipe details into a shared Home list or cross Home boundaries.
- Show an **editable per-ingredient preview** with the source ingredient name, quantity, unit and destination. Allow the member to **exclude individual ingredients**, adjust supported quantities/units, and choose or change the permitted destination before confirmation. If the recipe has a supported servings adjustment, use only the Recipes module's established scaling rules and show the result; otherwise do not invent conversions or scaling. Preserve unsupported details visibly or ask rather than silently dropping them.
- Apply the approved conservative **duplicate check** to each ingredient against the selected list. Where a clear accessible match exists, offer **Update existing** or **Create new** when allowed, showing the exact resulting quantity; never silently merge, add quantities or overwrite items. Do not infer or assign shopping categories, in accordance with the approved shopping-category restriction.
- Require **explicit confirmation** of the exact included ingredients and operations. Revalidate recipe access/content, list permissions, current duplicates, quantities, supported fields and context immediately before writing; refresh the preview if meaningful changes occurred. Honor the small combined-action limit, module validations, notifications and any approvals. If a recipe exceeds the safe preview/action limit, offer a bounded selection or direct the member to the Recipes module rather than silently truncating or bulk-processing it.
- Report **per-ingredient outcomes** (success, pending approval, failure, uncertainty). Retry only failed, freshly validated, separately confirmed writes, without repeating successful additions. Support cancellation and existing 24-hour draft expiry and privacy-safe navigation continuity.
- Acceptance criteria: planned meal with one recipe; multiple recipe matches; missing or inaccessible recipe; personal recipe to shared list; multiple lists; excluded ingredient; edited quantity; supported and unsupported units; recipe change while preview is open; existing duplicate update/create choice; category not inferred; permission change; large recipe beyond bounded action limit; partial write failure and safe retry; mobile and accessible preview; cancellation without writes.

### Confirmed Ora shopping category handling (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Ora does **not** infer, recommend, preselect or automatically create shopping-list categories for items added or updated through its guided actions. Shopping categorization and organization remain in the Shopping module.
- Ora may display an existing category as read-only context when needed to identify a matching item, but must not change that category or imply that an uncategorized item has been categorized. If a member explicitly asks Ora to assign, change, create or reorganize categories, direct them to the Shopping module's supported controls rather than silently extending Ora's capabilities.
- Continue to parse supported item names, quantities and units and use the approved duplicate-check/update-or-create preview. Category inference must not affect duplicate detection or silently alter a confirmed item's destination, quantity or identity. Apply existing permissions, explicit confirmation and per-item outcomes.
- Acceptance criteria: add milk/pasta/shampoo with no category suggestions; existing category on a matched item; category mentioned explicitly in a request; category-restricted list; several items in one preview; navigation to Shopping for category changes; accessible mobile and desktop previews.

### Confirmed Ora structured shopping quantities and units (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora recognizes common explicitly stated shopping quantities and units in supported requests (for example, “two bottles of milk”, “500 grams of pasta”, or “1 litre of juice”) and maps them to the Shopping module's **existing structured quantity and unit fields**, where those fields support the values. Do not create new product inventory, purchasing, or unit-conversion features.
- For requests containing a small number of shopping items, prepare an **individual editable preview row per item** with item name, numeric quantity, unit or packaging description, target shopping list and personal/Home context. Preserve the member's original meaning, including decimal separators and language-specific unit terms; do not silently discard a unit or substitute a different quantity.
- Distinguish package counts (such as “2 bottles”) from measured amounts (such as “500 g”). Map only supported units and representations; if the module cannot represent a detail structurally, retain it visibly in an appropriate existing text field where supported, or ask for clarification rather than pretending the structured fields captured it. Do not assume bottle sizes, convert units, calculate prices, or combine quantities automatically.
- Ask a concise clarification for unclear numbers, conflicting quantities, unrecognized units or item boundaries that could materially change the request. Do not invent quantities or units for unspecified items. Show the exact stored interpretation before **explicit confirmation**.
- Apply the approved conservative duplicate detection separately to each item. If a clear match exists, offer **Update existing** versus **Create new** (where permitted) and show the resulting quantity explicitly; never automatically add quantities together or merge items. Respect shopping-list permissions, validation, notifications, current Home, and safe retry/idempotency rules.
- Revalidate target list, current item values, duplicate matches, supported fields and permissions immediately before execution. Report individual success, pending, failure or uncertainty, without repeating successful writes. Remain within Ora's bounded combined-action limit.
- Acceptance criteria: “two bottles of milk”; “500 grams of pasta”; litre and decimal quantities; localized unit aliases; package count versus volume; unspecified quantity; unsupported unit; ambiguous phrasing; multiple items; existing duplicate with editable update/create choice; read-only list; stale duplicate; per-item partial failure and retry; accessible mobile preview and cancellation.

### Confirmed Ora conservative duplicate detection and update choice (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When Ora prepares a supported create action, it checks for a **clear matching existing record** within the same authorized destination and active personal/Home context, where the module exposes reliable duplicate-identification information. Examples include an existing shopping-list item with the same normalized item name, or a clearly matching task. This is a conservative suggestion, not a universal fuzzy matching or automatic merging system.
- When a clear accessible match exists, show the existing record and its relevant safe details in the **editable action preview**. Offer an explicit choice to **update the existing record** (with editable changes and consequences) or **create a separate new record** if the module permits duplicates. Do not silently update, merge quantities, replace data, or create a duplicate without the member's selected, confirmed action.
- Respect module-specific identity, duplicate, quantity, recurrence, approval and validation rules. Never infer equivalence from a weak keyword match or assume two similarly named records are the same; ask for clarification when matching is uncertain, and do not block valid intentional duplicates unless the module itself prohibits them.
- Search only records the member may currently view and modify as applicable; never reveal hidden record existence, private details, or records from another Home. If an existing match is visible but not editable, do not offer Update; explain the permitted choices without exposing restricted details.
- Revalidate the selected record, destination, permissions, current values and duplicate status immediately before confirmation and execution. Handle simultaneous additions or edits, deleted records, stale previews and partial data with a refreshed choice or clear error. Prevent duplicate writes on retry, but distinguish intentional new records from accidental repeated submissions.
- Show the exact action and per-record outcome; retain existing preview, explicit confirmation, notification, approval, Trash and safe retry rules. This does not add broad duplicate cleanup or automated bulk merges.
- Acceptance criteria: shopping item already present; same title in different lists or Homes; update quantity versus create separate item; weak or multiple matches; existing item read-only; intentional duplicate; concurrent creation; stale/deleted match; unavailable search source; permission changes; retries without duplicate writes; accessible mobile choice and cancellation.

### Confirmed Ora limited inline overview actions (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** In daily and weekly Ora overviews, eligible individual items may expose a **small, predefined set of contextually relevant inline actions** (for example, mark an assigned task complete or propose a due-date change). This is a shortcut into approved module workflows, not full inline record editing or unrestricted bulk operations.
- Show only actions supported for that record type, enabled module, active personal/Home context, and current member permissions. Do not expose restricted actions, private record details, or unavailable operations through action labels or counts. Every item retains a direct link to its source record.
- Selecting an inline action opens the **existing editable action/impact preview** identifying the exact record, proposed change, scope, affected members, and any consequences such as chore points, approvals, or notifications. Require explicit confirmation; follow all existing module validations, approval requirements, recurrence-scope rules, conflict warnings, and Trash/recovery protections. Never interpret a tap on an overview item as confirmation of a write.
- Revalidate record identity, freshness, visibility, assignment, membership, permissions, and current Home context immediately before execution. If the record changed, disappeared, or became ineligible, explain and offer refresh/navigation instead of silently modifying another record. Show accurate success, pending-approval, failure, or uncertain results; prevent duplicate effects on retry and offer only the previously approved safe retry/undo paths.
- Preserve the member's selected day/week, filters, expanded supporting items, and navigation context after a successful or cancelled action; refresh affected rows and summary counts without exposing newly restricted information. A failed or cancelled action must not falsely appear completed. Do not create additional reminders or independently reschedule other records.
- Keep inline controls compact and accessible on desktop and mobile, with descriptive labels, keyboard and screen-reader operation, visible loading/disabled/error states, and no reliance on hover alone. Shared wall displays remain display-only and cannot execute these actions.
- Acceptance criteria: eligible task completion; chore requiring approval/points update; due-date edit; unsupported event action; filtered overview; stale/deleted record; revoked permission; Home switch; pending/failed/uncertain outcome; duplicate retry; recurring occurrence scope; cancel and return; mobile touch and keyboard/screen-reader access; display-only wall view.

### Confirmed Ora simple daily/weekly overview filters (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora supports a small set of straightforward, visible, removable filters on its already-approved **daily and calendar-week overviews**: record module/type (such as calendar, tasks, chores, or reminders), assigned member where that concept applies, and completion status where supported. This is not advanced cross-module querying, custom date-range filtering, or an expansion of Ora's record-search capability.
- Members may request a filter directly (for example, “What chores do I have this week?”) or refine the current overview with a simple follow-up (for example, “Show only mine”). Display active filter chips or equivalent accessible controls, with individual removal and an obvious **Clear filters** action. Keep the selected day/week and active personal/Home context visible; do not silently retain a filter in unrelated new interactions.
- Only offer applicable filter values drawn from records and member identities the requester is authorized to see in the active context. Never reveal restricted records, hidden assignments, private calendar details, other-Home data, or counts derived from inaccessible items. “Assigned to me” means the current member; clarify ambiguous assignees or unsupported status concepts rather than guessing.
- Apply filters consistently to the underlying eligible records and any displayed counts, busy-day observations, and expandable supporting items. Clearly label filtered results as a **subset**, not a complete daily/weekly schedule; distinguish “no matching visible items” from unavailable, stale, or incompletely synchronized sources. Recheck visibility and source freshness when refreshing or opening linked items.
- Filters are read-only and do not create reminders, change assignments, or alter records. Any subsequent action still requires the approved editable preview, current permission checks, module approvals, and explicit confirmation. Preserve current filters during same-context navigation under the approved continuity rules; reset or segregate them when switching personal/Home contexts or ending the interaction.
- Acceptance criteria: module-only view; “assigned to me”; selecting another visible assignee; completed versus incomplete; unsupported completion state; combined simple filters; clearing one/all filters; zero matches; partial Google Calendar availability; privacy-restricted records; Home switch; return from linked item; mobile, keyboard, and screen-reader filter controls.

### Confirmed Ora simple permission-aware record lookup (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may look up **existing Madeora records** using a recognizable title, keyword, or straightforward date reference (such as “find the home insurance renewal task” or “show the chore scheduled for Friday”). This is a small, focused lookup capability, not advanced natural-language search, semantic document analysis, complex multi-filter queries, or a separate global search product.
- Search only **enabled, supported modules and records visible to the requesting member in the active personal space or Home**. Respect per-record visibility, Home membership, module permissions, private calendars, and connected-provider access; never search other Homes or personal data implicitly, reveal restricted titles/counts, or use hidden records to shape suggestions.
- Return a **short, clearly labeled set of relevant matches** with enough safe context to distinguish them (record type, title, and relevant date or location in the app) and a direct link to the original record. Do not invent matches. If there are no matches, say so and offer an appropriate module link; if there are several plausible matches, let the member choose rather than silently selecting one.
- Handle simple date references in the member's timezone and distinguish date-filtered matches from title/keyword matches. Clearly disclose unavailable, stale, or partially searched sources; never claim a complete result set when a relevant module or integration could not be checked. Limit results and offer module navigation for broader exploration.
- Recheck record existence, permissions, and active context when opening a result or using it in a follow-up action. A lookup alone is read-only; editing or deleting a found record still requires the existing editable impact preview, module validations, approvals, and explicit confirmation. Preserve current-interaction context only under the approved navigation and privacy rules.
- Acceptance criteria: exact title; partial keyword; simple relative/absolute date; multiple matching records; no results; permission-restricted records; personal versus Home scope; disabled module; stale integration; record deleted or access revoked before opening; mobile/keyboard/screen-reader results; and confirmed follow-up edit without silent execution.

### Confirmed Ora navigation continuity within active context (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When a member navigates from Ora to another Madeora screen and returns **within the same active personal space or Home**, preserve the current interaction, the most recent daily/weekly overview, expanded supporting-item state, and any unfinished guided action, rather than resetting Ora to its welcome state.
- Maintain a lightweight, accessible return path to the same panel state, including the selected date/week, relevant scroll position and expanded items where feasible. Opening an original record and returning must not imply a new action or confirmation. This is current-interaction continuity, not separate chat threads, pinned conversations, or resuming old conversations from the seven-day history.
- Preserve unconfirmed action drafts only under the existing **24-hour draft expiry**, Resume/Discard, and fresh-validation rules. Navigation does not extend the draft lifetime, authorize writes, or preserve stale permissions as valid. Before resuming a draft or opening linked items, recheck record existence, current membership, visibility, permissions, and source freshness; refresh or clearly disclose stale overview information.
- On personal/Home context change, sign-out, ended interaction, access revocation, or deletion of referenced data, clear or segregate context-dependent panel content and previews. Never show personal or other-Home details in the new context, including through cached expanded items or back-navigation. Preserve non-sensitive panel UI preferences only where consistent with privacy rules.
- Keep behavior consistent on desktop and mobile, including browser back navigation, panel minimize/reopen, keyboard/focus return, and unavailable data states; do not interrupt the underlying module's unsaved forms.
- Acceptance criteria: open an overview item and return; expand/collapse source items then navigate; resume a draft before and after 24-hour expiry; permission change while away; cross-Home switch and back; sign-out; record deletion; stale sync on return; mobile back-navigation and accessible focus restoration.

### Confirmed Ora short conversational follow-up context (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora understands simple references within the **current interaction**, such as “And Friday?” following a daily overview, “Show more details” about the most recent result, or “Move it to Sunday” referring to one clearly identified record. Carry forward only the relevant intent and explicitly established constraints; update only what the member changes.
- Do not treat the seven-day rolling interaction history as permission to resolve references across separate interactions or build extended conversational threads. If the current referent, destination, timeframe, or intended action is unclear, ask a concise clarification instead of guessing.
- Bind contextual references to the originating personal/Home scope and visible records. Recheck membership, permissions, visibility, record existence, source freshness, and current active context before displaying details or preparing an action. Never use inaccessible information to disambiguate a reference or leak data across Homes.
- Follow-ups that propose changes still use the existing editable preview, module-specific validation and approvals, and explicit confirmation. A previous answer or confirmation never authorizes a new write; preserve draft expiry, safe retry and duplicate-prevention behavior.
- Clear or segregate active references on personal/Home context switch, end of interaction, sign-out, permission revocation, or deletion of the referenced item. Where the referent becomes unavailable, explain briefly and offer an authorized way to select another record.
- Acceptance criteria: day-to-day follow-up; details of a single listed record; multiple plausible records; follow-up after switching Homes; deleted or permission-revoked source; expired or ended interaction; change request requiring a fresh preview; and accessibility on desktop/mobile.

### Confirmed Ora simple edits to existing recurring series (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may prepare **simple edits to existing recurring records** where the relevant Madeora module supports those edits, including basic day, time, start-date, or other standard schedule-field changes. This extends Ora beyond recurrence creation but does **not** authorize complex recurrence rules, custom exceptions, broad restructuring, or unsupported provider operations.
- Identify the exact existing series and ask the member to choose an **explicit occurrence scope** before confirmation: **This occurrence**, **This and following occurrences**, or **Entire series**, offering only the scopes supported by that record's module and integration. Never silently assume that changing the next occurrence changes the whole series, or vice versa.
- Show an **editable before-and-after preview** with the series identity, selected occurrence and scope, old and new recurrence details, affected future instances, personal/Home destination, assignees, notifications, conflicts, and points/rewards implications where relevant. Clearly distinguish completed history, individual overrides, and future generated occurrences; do not rewrite completed historical contributions.
- Apply existing module-specific recurrence editing rules, permissions, approvals, cross-Home confirmations, timezone/DST handling, conflict warnings, and Google Calendar synchronization constraints. Revalidate current series state and authorization immediately before execution; if an external provider or module cannot support the chosen scope, explain the limitation and direct the member to its native controls instead of approximating or partially applying a different scope.
- Require **explicit confirmation** of the precise scope and edits. Show accurate outcomes, avoid duplicate occurrences or duplicate side effects on retry, and handle partial failures or uncertain provider synchronization without claiming complete success. No automatic rescheduling of unrelated events. Respect Ora's 24-hour unfinished draft limit and lightweight accessible interface.
- Acceptance criteria: next occurrence only; this-and-following; entire series; unsupported scope; recurring task/chore and linked calendar record; already completed occurrences; per-occurrence overrides; DST and timezone change; conflicting schedule; permission revocation; concurrent edit; provider sync failure; preview correction; confirmation and safe retry.

### Confirmed Ora standard recurrence creation (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may prepare recurring tasks, reminders, chores, and other records only where the relevant Madeora module already supports recurrence, using a **bounded set of standard predefined patterns**: daily, weekly, monthly, and selected weekdays. This does not authorize arbitrary recurrence rules, complex exceptions, custom intervals, or advanced series editing through Ora.
- Translate a clear natural-language recurrence request into an **editable schedule preview** displaying the target module and personal/Home scope, title or action, cadence and selected weekdays where applicable, start date, time and timezone when relevant, end condition if supported, assignee, and any other module-required fields. Never silently invent missing consequential values; ask concise clarification for ambiguity (including monthly dates that may not exist in every month).
- Show that the action creates an ongoing series rather than a one-time item, including affected members, notifications, and points/rewards implications where relevant. Require **explicit confirmation** of the precise recurrence before saving. Do not silently create duplicate series or change an existing one; when an equivalent recurrence exists, explain and offer navigation to it instead.
- Enforce existing module recurrence behavior, permissions, membership, visibility, approval, assignment, notifications, and cross-Home context rules. Validate both at preview and execution, including daylight-saving/timezone handling and schedule conflicts; Ora may warn about clear conflicts but never reschedules automatically. Unsupported schedules should be explained with a link to the module's advanced controls, not approximated without consent.
- Existing recurrence-series modifications and deletions remain governed by their separate module-specific scopes and workflows; this decision only approves standard-pattern creation through Ora. Honor the 24-hour unconfirmed-draft expiry, individual outcomes and safe retries, and the existing lightweight, accessible text-only panel.
- Acceptance criteria: daily/weekly/monthly/selected-weekday creation; missing start time or timezone; monthly 29th–31st; DST transition; shared assignment and permission change; conflicting calendar item; duplicate series; module-specific approvals; unsupported advanced recurrence; preview edits; explicit confirmation; partial combined-action failure; and duplicate-preventing retry.

### Confirmed Ora small reviewed multi-record updates (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may prepare a **small, bounded set of updates to multiple existing records** in a single request, but only for explicitly supported, eligible operations in Madeora modules. This is not unrestricted bulk editing, broad filter-based mass changes, or autonomous record maintenance. If the request exceeds the supported limit or includes unsupported operations, explain the limitation and direct the member to the relevant module instead of silently truncating the selection.
- Show an **editable, itemized preview** identifying every affected record, its personal/Home scope, proposed before-and-after values, and material consequences such as chore completion, points, reward balances, notifications, assignments, or linked records. The member can review, adjust or remove individual rows; clearly distinguish the selected records from similarly named or hidden ones. Never infer that a chore was actually completed solely from a vague phrase such as “all my completed chores.”
- Validate each record's current existence, visibility, ownership, permissions, eligibility, status, and module-specific rules both when preparing the preview and immediately before execution. Preserve independent approvals, notifications, points/reward safeguards, and cross-Home context confirmation. If a record changed or became ineligible, identify it and require correction/removal and renewed confirmation of the exact set; do not silently change the selection.
- Require **one explicit confirmation of the exact itemized set**, except where a module requires additional per-record approval or confirmation. Execute only confirmed eligible updates, protect against duplicate submission and repeated points/rewards, and report **per-record success, failure, pending approval, or uncertain outcome**. On partial failure, keep successful updates intact and offer safe, separately confirmed retries only for failed records after checking for prior completion; never repeat successful writes or automatically roll back unrelated updates.
- Respect Ora's 24-hour unfinished-preview expiry, seven-day interaction history, lightweight text-only interface, accessibility and mobile bottom-sheet constraints. Destructive deletion remains governed by Ora's separate eligible-record deletion rules and is not implicitly authorized as a multi-record edit.
- Acceptance criteria: two to several eligible records; mixed ownership/permissions; ambiguous selection; more records than supported; individually editable preview; points/reward side effects; stale record or changed permissions; extra module approvals; partial success and safe retry; idempotent repeated submission; cross-Home boundary; and accessible mobile review.

### Confirmed Ora eligible-record deletion with explicit confirmation (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may prepare deletion of specifically identified, eligible individual Madeora records where the underlying module already permits deletion through its ordinary interface. Ora is not a shortcut for bulk destructive administration, permanent deletion, account/Home removal, or actions lacking a predefined supported deletion capability.
- Present an **editable, explicit impact preview** identifying the exact record, personal/Home ownership and destination, affected shared members or dependent items where relevant, and whether the action moves the record into the existing seven-day individual-item Trash/recovery flow. Clarify ambiguous matches rather than guessing; distinguish removal of a shared link from deletion of a Home-owned original.
- Require affirmative confirmation for the precise deletion shown. Apply the same live membership, visibility, ownership, module-specific deletion permission, approval and notification rules as the normal UI, both when presenting and immediately before execution. Never expose or delete inaccessible records; no permission escalation through Ora.
- Honor the existing seven-day individual-item recovery and applicable attachment handling, without extending it or altering the separate 30-day Home/account recovery lifecycle. If a module requires extra confirmation for a particular destructive operation, retain that requirement; Ora's ordinary preview does not override it. Permanent/irreversible or bulk trash-emptying actions remain in their existing dedicated interfaces unless separately approved for Ora.
- On completion, report the exact outcome and offer the existing authorized Trash/Restore route where available. Quick Undo may appear only if a safe module-defined reversal is supported; do not imply restoration is guaranteed. Handle stale/deleted records, simultaneous edits, expired permissions, failed actions and duplicate retries without repeat deletion or misleading success. For combined actions, report deletion outcomes individually.
- Acceptance criteria: uniquely identified personal record; Home-owned shared record with affected-member impact; ambiguous names; insufficient permission; deletion with linked attachments; record changed or already deleted before confirmation; restore eligibility and expiry; module-specific additional approval; combined-action partial failure; and no irreversible account/Home or bulk deletion through Ora.

### Confirmed Ora compact permission-filtered capabilities guide (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Include a compact, member-invoked **“What can Ora do?”** guide inside Ora's existing panel/mobile bottom sheet. Organize a bounded catalog of supported Madeora actions by module, with short plain-language example requests and an indication of read-only versus change-producing actions. Do not add a standalone help application, general AI command directory, or interactive full-screen tour.
- Populate the guide only with capabilities currently available to that member in the selected personal space or Home, considering enabled modules, role, membership, granular permissions, visibility, feature availability, and wall-display restrictions. Never list inaccessible records or imply access to private content; avoid exposing disabled or hidden modules merely through help entries. If nothing is eligible, explain that succinctly without showing misleading examples.
- Keep the content consistent with Ora's explicitly approved predefined quick actions, limited guided cross-module workflows, combined-action limits, and text-only Madeora scope. Examples must not promise autonomous scheduling, broad web research, voice controls, unsupported actions, or permission requests. A short guide can distinguish common information lookups from actions that require an editable preview and explicit confirmation.
- Offer an unobtrusive entry point from Ora's panel and optionally its completed first-use introduction. Members may browse by module and select an example to prefill a request or start the existing safe guided flow; selecting an example must never perform a write automatically. Provide a simple return to the current Ora interaction without losing an unfinished draft.
- Refresh guide entries when the active Home/personal context, enabled modules, or permissions change, including while the guide is open. Cross-Home access still requires the established explicit context switch; no guide entry can bypass permission validation at preview and execution. When Ora is disabled, its guide is unavailable along with Ora, while ordinary Madeora module help remains independent.
- Keep labels and examples concise, localized to the Madeora interface language, screen-reader and keyboard accessible, usable in the mobile bottom sheet, and consistent with reduced-motion settings. The guide is informational and not a new source of reminder notifications or behavioral personalization.
- Supporting specifications should enumerate the supported example requests by module, eligibility, entry and empty states, and navigation back to Ora. Acceptance criteria: different module entitlements; disabled module; personal/Home switch; permission revocation while open; no eligible capabilities; example prefill and confirmation; preserved unfinished draft; mobile/desktop accessibility; no restricted information leakage.

### Confirmed Ora permission-aware unavailable-action guidance (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When a member asks Ora to perform a Madeora-supported action that their current role, membership, record visibility, module capabilities, or Home settings do not permit, Ora gives a **brief, plain-language explanation** that the action is unavailable. It may suggest a genuinely permitted alternative or point to the relevant existing Home/module settings or authorized administrator workflow, where appropriate.
- Ora never bypasses permissions, grants access, sends an implicit permission request, or invents a new approval workflow. A settings link is guidance only: show it only if the member is allowed to access that settings view; otherwise explain that an authorized Home manager controls the capability without exposing private administrator details.
- Do not reveal restricted record names, participants, private content, hidden Home membership, or the existence of sensitive resources through an error, alternative, or link. Distinguish insufficient permission from missing data, unsupported functionality, disabled modules, stale context, and temporary outages whenever that distinction is safe and known.
- Evaluate permissions before presenting quick actions and again before any preview or execution; if permissions change mid-flow, block the action, invalidate its draft/preview as needed, and show the updated explanation. Cross-Home requests still require the separately approved context-switch confirmation and never gain access merely by switching.
- Keep the response concise, friendly, localized to Madeora's interface language, and accessible; offer at most a few relevant alternatives without nagging or implying the action succeeded. Preserve existing module approval, notification, audit, and recovery rules.
- Acceptance criteria: denied role/capability; private or inaccessible record; accessible versus inaccessible settings; permitted alternative; permission revoked after preview; ambiguous or wrong Home; disabled module; offline/error distinction; mobile and screen-reader presentation; no information disclosure or unauthorized write.

### Confirmed Ora context-aware predefined quick actions (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora offers a small, curated set of **predefined quick actions** appropriate to the member's current Madeora module, screen, and selected personal/Home context. Examples include adding an item from a shopping-list screen or preparing a supported task action from a task screen. This is screen-contextual discovery, **not** adaptive behavioral profiling or an unrestricted AI action planner.
- Show only actions supported by the current module and available to the member under live role, membership, visibility, and capability permissions. Never reveal private records, unauthorized destinations, or actions from another Home. A context change refreshes the available actions and removes stale suggestions.
- Keep presentation restrained: a few concise optional actions in Ora's panel and, where already approved, small dismissible prompts on relevant screens. Do not create intrusive overlays, a separate action catalog, or additional navigation. Respect Ora's personal disable setting, Quiet/Balanced/Active modes, category preferences, quiet hours where relevant, and smart dismissal suppression for proactive prompts.
- Selecting a quick action opens the existing focused input/preview flow; it does **not** execute a write. Apply safe defaults, ask for missing essential details, show editable scope/destination/affected members, check scheduling conflicts when applicable, and require explicit confirmation and existing module approvals before changes. Read-only navigation may open the relevant authorized screen without a write confirmation.
- If a screen has no suitable authorized quick action, omit the contextual choices rather than displaying misleading disabled actions. Handle loading, unavailable modules, stale data, permission revocation, and failed actions using Ora's established graceful fallback, retry, and safe Undo rules.
- Supporting Ora and screen-by-screen specifications must identify each module's allowed quick actions and states without prescribing technical architecture. Acceptance criteria: distinct actions on shopping/task/calendar screens; no eligible action; role restrictions; personal/Home switching; dismissal across surfaces; mobile bottom sheet; disabled/unavailable Ora; editable preview and explicit confirmation; accessibility and reduced motion.

### Confirmed Ora clear scheduling-conflict warnings (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When Ora prepares a scheduling-related action, it checks relevant **currently accessible** Madeora records for clear, material overlaps or module-defined conflicts. This is a focused validation aid, not an autonomous scheduling or calendar-optimization service.
- In the editable action preview, show a concise, specific warning describing the proposed overlap and the affected time or record **only to the extent the requesting member is permitted to view it**. Never reveal private event titles, participants, locations, or existence through another member's inaccessible calendar; use a generic availability warning only where the existing sharing model authorizes that availability information.
- Allow the member to change the proposed date/time or navigate to the appropriate module. Do **not** automatically move, shorten, reassign, or cancel any existing record, and do not invent alternative free times. Where existing module rules allow an overlapping action, the member may explicitly confirm despite a non-blocking warning; hard module validation failures remain blocking.
- Recheck permissions, visibility, record freshness, timezone, recurrence occurrence, and relevant overlaps immediately before execution. If the situation changes after preview, refresh the warning and require renewed confirmation; an unavailable or incomplete conflict check must not be presented as a clean schedule.
- Apply the same behavior to supported individual, combined, and resumed-draft actions, without cross-Home searching or leakage. Avoid repetitive alerts for the same overlap within a single preview; respect accessibility and concise Ora copy.
- Acceptance criteria: obvious overlap; no overlap; permitted non-blocking override; blocking module rule; private/inaccessible calendar; another Home; changed or deleted event; timezone and recurring occurrences; stale preview; unavailable conflict data; combined actions; keyboard and screen-reader warning.

### Confirmed Ora safe individual retry after partial combined-action failure (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When a confirmed combined Ora request produces mixed results, show a distinct, truthful outcome for **each action**: completed, failed, pending module approval, or uncertain. Never present the whole request as successful unless every required action actually succeeded.
- Offer **Retry** only for an action confirmed to have failed and eligible for safe resubmission. Completed actions remain completed and must **never be executed again** as part of the retry. Pending or uncertain outcomes require status reconciliation before any retry; do not assume a timeout means failure.
- Before retry, refresh source and destination data, membership, Home context, visibility, permissions, module-specific constraints, and existing-record state. Present a refreshed preview of **only the failed action** and require a separate explicit confirmation. If the action is no longer valid, explain the issue and provide a relevant correction or navigation option.
- Protect against duplicate writes with stable action identity and outcome reconciliation. Do not silently reverse successful actions or repeat related notifications, points, approvals, or other side effects. If the retried action succeeds, update its result without rewriting the history of other actions.
- Keep retry controls lightweight within Ora's existing panel/bottom sheet; no dedicated action-history or autonomous recovery system. Respect the approved seven-day interaction retention and temporary draft rules without implying failed actions can be retried indefinitely. Never expose results or retry controls after permission or Home access is revoked.
- Acceptance criteria: mixed success/failure; retry of one failed action; successful action never repeated; timeout/unknown outcome; duplicate-submit prevention; permissions revoked before retry; changed or deleted destination; module approval pending; cross-Home context; retry failure; clear accessible per-action status and confirmation.

### Confirmed Ora limited combined actions in one request (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora may recognize and prepare a **small number of distinct, explicitly supported Madeora actions** from one text request, provided each action maps to a predefined capability or approved guided workflow. This is not general-purpose planning, unrestricted automation, or support for arbitrary long action lists.
- Present an **editable combined preview** that separates every proposed action and clearly identifies its operation, values, destination, personal/Home scope, affected members, and relevant consequences. Allow members to adjust or remove individual proposed actions before confirming; never silently add inferred actions or assume omitted essential details.
- Validate **each action individually** for required fields, safe defaults, source freshness, destination ambiguity, membership, record visibility, permissions, module-specific approval requirements, and cross-Home restrictions. Ask a concise clarification where needed. A single confirmation may authorize only the exact eligible actions shown together; it must not bypass any module's independent approval or affected-member notification rules.
- Before execution, revalidate the full preview. If one action is invalid, clearly identify it and let the member correct or remove it rather than silently proceeding with a different set. Show an accurate per-action result for success, failure, pending approval, or uncertainty; do not claim the combined request wholly succeeded if only some actions completed. Prevent duplicate writes on retries and do not automatically roll back unrelated successful actions.
- Combined unconfirmed previews follow the approved **24-hour temporary draft expiry**, Resume/Discard behavior, and fresh-validation requirements. Supported safe Quick Undo may be offered for eligible completed actions individually; otherwise link to the relevant record or correction workflow. Respect the seven-day interaction-history boundary and Ora's text-only, lightweight interface.
- Acceptance criteria: two supported actions in one request; mixed valid/invalid actions; editable/removable preview rows; missing information; different destinations and affected members; cross-Home request requiring separate context confirmation; permission change; module approval; partial execution and retry; duplicate prevention; temporary draft resume/expiry; accessible mobile presentation; and no action without explicit confirmation.

### Confirmed Ora temporary unfinished-action drafts (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora temporarily preserves unfinished, unconfirmed action drafts when its panel or mobile bottom sheet is minimized/closed, when members navigate elsewhere, or when a session ends. Members can resume a recent draft rather than repeating a guided workflow. A draft is **not** a completed Madeora record and must never execute automatically.
- Use a bounded **24-hour expiry from the last deliberate edit** as the initial product rule; expiration must not be extended by merely opening Ora, viewing the draft, or background refreshes. Expired drafts are removed from the resume interface, with a concise explanation when a member returns. The seven-day recent-interaction history is independent and must not revive an expired action draft.
- Preserve only the information necessary to resume the supported Madeora-specific action, including the intended destination, editable values, and originating personal/Home context. Never expose a draft across members or Homes; a context switch must segregate it and may offer a safe return to the original context only after confirmation and fresh access checks. No draft appears on wall displays.
- On reopening, show a small **Resume / Discard** choice when an eligible draft exists; never reopen the panel or force a resume automatically. Discard removes the draft, not any previously confirmed underlying records. Avoid accumulating multiple competing drafts: if a member starts a new action while another draft is pending, offer to resume or discard the previous draft before replacing it.
- Before displaying sensitive draft details or enabling confirmation, recheck sign-in, Home membership, record visibility, module permissions, current destination, source data, defaults, conflicts, and applicable workflow rules. If source records changed, visibly refresh the preview and require renewed explicit confirmation; if invalid or unauthorized, block execution and offer a safe correction, restart, or discard. Never silently substitute a different Home or record.
- A final confirmation is always required, even for a previously previewed draft. Failed or interrupted execution must not be treated as success; prevent duplicate submissions, and keep any already-confirmed changes distinct from the uncommitted draft. Disabling Ora prevents draft access; clearing Ora interactions or deleting an account/Home follows the relevant privacy/deletion policy and must not expose stale drafts on re-enabling.
- Acceptance criteria: minimize/close and reopen; sign-out and sign-in within expiry; expiry after 24 hours without deliberate edits; session/device continuity where supported; Resume/Discard; competing new action; context switch; revoked permission; changed or deleted destination; refreshed preview and renewed confirmation; interrupted execution; disabled Ora; mobile keyboard/accessibility; no wall-display draft leakage.

### Confirmed Ora responsive mobile bottom sheet (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** On mobile, Ora opens from its unobtrusive persistent entry point as a **responsive bottom sheet**, initially compact and expandable for longer text interactions, previews, or guided workflows. It is not a separate full-screen chat destination or a movable floating window.
- Preserve the underlying Madeora screen, navigation position, unsaved form state, and scroll position when opening, expanding, minimizing, or dismissing Ora. Expanded height must adapt to available viewport space, safe areas, orientation, and the on-screen keyboard without obscuring input or confirmation controls.
- Provide clear, accessible expand/collapse and close affordances, predictable keyboard/focus management and focus restoration, screen-reader labels, sufficient touch targets, and reduced-motion transitions. Avoid accidental dismissal of unfinished input or an unconfirmed workflow; warn or preserve a recoverable draft as appropriate.
- Keep the active personal/Home context visible in the sheet and in action previews. Scope recent interactions and suggested actions to that context; enforce permissions and explicit confirmation for changes, including cross-module workflows. The sheet must not cover or silently trigger underlying controls.
- Proactive suggestions must never force the sheet open. When Ora is disabled, its mobile entry point and sheet are hidden; when unavailable, the same sheet presents the approved graceful fallback. Wall displays retain their separate display-only Ora behavior.
- Acceptance criteria: compact/expanded sheet, keyboard and safe-area adaptation, portrait/landscape, long content and scrolling, dismissal with pending draft, screen-reader/focus behavior, context switch, permission changes, reduced motion, disabled/unavailable Ora, and no loss of underlying screen state.

### Confirmed persistent expandable Ora access (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora has a subtle, persistent and recognizable entry point throughout Madeora's authenticated personal and Home interfaces, opening an **expandable assistant panel** rather than forcing navigation to a separate page. Keep the entry point visible but unobtrusive and accessible on desktop and mobile; adapt placement to avoid covering navigation, forms, and primary actions.
- The panel provides **lightweight recent interaction continuity** (not separate chats or a searchable archive), context-aware suggestions and permitted quick actions, with clear attribution to the current personal/Home context. Users can expand, collapse/minimize, reopen, and dismiss the panel easily without losing their conversation or current page position. Preserve sensible panel state when navigating, with an accessible focus return.
- Ora can refer to the active screen and authorized records only; switching Homes or personal context must not leak conversation content, retrieved data, or suggestions from another context. Clearly indicate context and enforce fresh authorization for historical messages, proposed actions, and linked records.
- Keep the bounded recent interaction stream usable with readable timestamps, loading/empty/error states, keyboard and screen-reader support, responsive sizing, and reduced-motion transitions. Proactive messages honor previously approved interruption preferences and never forcibly reopen a minimized panel.
- Ora's visual identity, personality, privacy rules, and explicit confirmation requirements remain authoritative; no consequential action is executed simply by opening or viewing the panel.
- Acceptance criteria: persistent entry point works across core screens; panel opens/minimizes without losing work; history and contextual actions respect Home switches and permission changes; mobile layout and keyboard navigation remain usable; reduced-motion users get equivalent feedback.

### Confirmed Ora abstract animated visual identity (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora has a distinctive, recognizable **abstract animated identity**, not merely a static icon and not a full character/mascot. Its core signature remains consistent across personal dashboards, Homes, assistant interactions, and wall displays.
- Define subtle, legible visual states for **listening, thinking, helping, and celebrating**, plus a neutral/resting state. Transitions communicate meaningful status without falsely implying active listening, background processing, or completed actions.
- Keep motion restrained and purposeful, visually integrated with Madeora's approved calm light/dark design, solid accent controls, and soft atmospheric background; do not introduce a competing decoration or gamification system.
- Respect reduced-motion preferences: replace animation with equivalent static state cues and accessible text/status announcements where appropriate. Avoid flashing, distracting loops, excessive battery/CPU use, and reliance on motion or color alone.
- Ora must be unobtrusive and dismissible/minimizable where appropriate; animation never obscures content, steals focus, or exposes private information on shared/wall surfaces. State changes reflect actual interaction and permission boundaries.
- Acceptance criteria: every state is distinguishable in both themes and in reduced-motion mode; listening state appears only when an actual authorized listening interaction exists; celebration animations honor mute/quiet settings and never disrupt primary tasks.

### Confirmed warm, playful, adaptive Ora personality (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora has a recognizable **warm, playful, and adaptive** personality. It is friendly and encouraging, with occasional light humor and contextual celebrations, without becoming overly chatty, childish, or performative.
- Ora uses **one consistent personality for everyone**, without member-selectable tone or personality modes. Proactive notification frequency, category controls, and accessibility settings remain independently configurable; they do not change Ora's character.
- Adapt tone to the situation: celebrate achievements naturally; communicate routine suggestions clearly; use a **restrained, respectful, direct** voice for serious, sensitive, stressful, disappointing, or safety-relevant situations. Never joke about private difficulties or mistakes.
- Avoid repetitive canned praise, forced familiarity, guilt, pressure, manipulative encouragement, or pretending to have emotions or experiences. Keep useful information and actions easy to identify beneath the personality.
- Honor existing permission, privacy, accessibility, reduced-motion, and notification controls across chat, dashboard prompts, shared/Home surfaces, wall displays, and other Ora outputs. Do not infer or expose sensitive context to personalize public messages.
- Acceptance criteria: no personality or tone customization controls exist; Ora uses a consistent recognizable voice across members; serious contexts suppress playful copy; no private context leaks to other Home members.

### Confirmed display-only Ora on wall displays (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** On shared wall displays, Ora has a **display-only** presence for privacy-safe household reminders, celebrations, and helpful suggestions. It must not offer text input, an interactive assistant panel, personal interaction history, or controls that initiate Ora workflows.
- Restrict content to the wall display's explicitly authorized Home and display visibility policy. Never show private member preferences, personal memories, private calendar entries, individual reward balances, private conversations, sensitive details, or content from another Home. Do not infer that a Home member's permission grants a publicly visible display the same access.
- Keep messages brief, calm, and contextual; use unobtrusive placements without pop-ups or blocking dashboard content. Respect display-specific settings, muted categories, quiet hours, accessibility and reduced-motion options. Avoid showing personal notifications merely because a member has enabled Ora's Active mode.
- Where a suggested action requires input or confirmation, direct the member to their authenticated personal device; never imply the wall display executed an action. Hide or refresh content promptly when the active Home, display authorization, or underlying permissions change, including while offline or cached.
- Acceptance criteria: display-only rendering, no microphone or text-entry affordances, no personal interaction history, privacy-safe content across Homes and members, permission changes and cache invalidation, restrained celebrations, responsive layouts and reduced motion.

### Confirmed Ora clarification and unsupported-request fallback (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** For an ambiguous request that maps to a supported Madeora action, Ora asks **one concise, targeted clarification at a time** or offers a small set of unambiguous choices. Ask only for information essential to proceed; do not invent intent, records, dates, members, or Home destinations.
- When a request is unsupported or outside Ora's bounded Madeora-specific capabilities, clearly state the limitation in friendly, brief language and suggest up to three relevant supported actions or Madeora screens where useful. Do not attempt open-ended autonomous planning, general AI conversation, web research, or fabricated results.
- Distinguish ambiguity from missing permissions, missing data, unavailable features, and transient failures. Explain the actual blocker without disclosing inaccessible records or implying they exist; offer a safe next step only when one is available.
- Clarification alone never authorizes a write. For supported actions and guided workflows, retain the approved impact preview, explicit confirmation, validations, permission checks, and error/recovery behavior. Cancel or abandon safely without side effects when the member declines or the request cannot be resolved.
- Keep clarification context within Ora's seven-day rolling interaction stream; revalidate current personal/Home scope and permissions on every response, particularly after a context switch. Never use stored memories as authority to override the member's explicit current choice.
- Acceptance criteria: single focused clarification for ambiguous supported requests; safe handling of multiple matching records/Homes; accurate out-of-scope fallback with relevant alternatives; distinct permission/data/error messaging; no unauthorized disclosure, guessed actions, or writes before confirmation; accessible text and action-button options.

### Confirmed Ora panel and subtle contextual placement (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora's expandable panel is its primary interaction surface. Ora may also offer small, contextual, dismissible suggestions inside relevant dashboard widgets and module screens, tied to the member's current task and authorized data. Do not introduce intrusive pop-ups, modal interruptions, or omnipresent assistant banners for ordinary proactive suggestions.
- Contextual prompts should be brief, visually restrained, and actionable: a clear reason or benefit, an optional direct action or link to the Ora panel, and an obvious dismiss control. They must not obscure primary controls, reduce content readability, or shift layouts unexpectedly; support responsive screens and wall displays without clutter.
- Honor each member's Quiet/Balanced/Active frequency mode, category toggles, quiet hours, and reduced-motion/accessibility preferences consistently across the panel and contextual placements. Quiet mode must not be bypassed by moving the same prompt to a widget. Essential system alerts remain separate from Ora suggestions.
- Avoid repeated prompts across surfaces: dismissing a suggestion suppresses the same suggestion in the panel and other placements for an appropriate period; do not resurrect stale or already-completed prompts. Member-specific dismissals do not silently change other members' preferences.
- Never reveal personal or Home information in a contextual prompt unless the current viewer has access in the current context. Re-evaluate permissions and data freshness before displaying or acting; any consequential action still requires the previously approved preview and explicit confirmation.
- Acceptance criteria: panel-first assistance, relevant and dismissible inline suggestions, cross-surface deduplication and dismissal, quiet hours and category controls, responsive/accessibility behavior, privacy on Home/context switches, and no intrusive pop-ups.

### Confirmed Ora proactive frequency and category controls (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each member can independently choose **Quiet, Balanced, or Active** for Ora's proactive behavior. Default to **Balanced** for new members; frequency changes apply to future Ora prompts, not authoritative task or calendar records.
- **Quiet:** Show useful prompts unobtrusively when the member opens relevant Madeora surfaces, with minimal proactive interruptions. **Balanced:** Deliver selectively timed, relevant prompts while grouping repetitive items. **Active:** Allow more frequent useful prompts, still subject to deduplication, rate limits, and anti-spam safeguards. None of these modes bypasses notification permissions or creates a guarantee of delivery.
- Offer simple independent on/off **category controls for reminders, suggestions, and celebrations**, alongside the overall frequency choice. Disabling a category suppresses Ora-originated proactive prompts of that kind across applicable surfaces/channels, without disabling the underlying module or changing existing records.
- **Respect member-configured quiet hours** in their local time zone: defer or suppress nonessential proactive interruptions during that window, avoid a burst of stale prompts afterward, and continue to permit user-initiated interactions and unobtrusive in-app access. Essential operational/system alerts and separately configured module notifications are not silently reclassified as Ora suggestions; make the distinction clear.
- Maintain the previously approved per-member notification-channel preferences; do not expose an overly complex rule builder in Ora's simple settings. Home owners/admins cannot silently override another member's personal Ora frequency, category, or quiet-hour choices.
- All modes must enforce current personal/Home permissions, relevant privacy restrictions, muted/dismissed states, accessibility and reduced-motion settings. Shared/wall displays must never reveal a private prompt merely because a member chose Active.
- Acceptance criteria: mode changes, each category toggle, quiet hours crossing midnight and time-zone changes, duplicate suppression, resumed delivery after quiet hours, per-member isolation, channel preferences, Home switching and revoked access, and the distinction between Ora prompts and essential system alerts.

### Confirmed contextually proactive Ora behavior (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Ora is **contextually proactive**, not exclusively reactive or a constantly interrupting companion. It may initiate relevant, timely achievement celebrations, reminders, and actionable suggestions grounded in the member’s upcoming tasks, routines, and authorized Home activity.
- Each member can configure Ora’s proactive interruption frequency and notification channels, including a quiet/minimal setting. Distinguish noninterruptive dashboard suggestions from push/email interruptions; honor mute, dismiss, and quiet preferences without repeatedly resurfacing dismissed prompts.
- Prioritize useful, time-sensitive and personally relevant prompts; group or suppress repetitive prompts, avoid notification overload, and do not imply that a suggestion is a confirmed fact or action already taken.
- Enforce current account, Home, module, and item permissions and privacy boundaries before selecting context, generating content, or delivering it through notifications, shared dashboards, and wall displays. Do not leak personal data across Homes or reveal opted-out leaderboard participants.
- Ora may propose actions but must obtain the required explicit confirmation before consequential changes, and must never bypass existing approval workflows, permissions, or audit rules. Provide clear opt-outs and accessible controls for proactive behavior.

### Confirmed Ora-led subtle achievements and leaderboard celebrations (12 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Madeora celebrates personal milestones and eligible Home leaderboard achievements through **Ora**, the friendly in-product assistant.
