Ora is the principal voice and presentation for encouragement, making achievements feel personal rather than adding a separate noisy gamification system.
- Use restrained, tasteful animations and brief contextual Ora messages (for example, a personal chore milestone or a new monthly leaderboard position); allow members to dismiss or mute celebrations. Respect reduced-motion settings and avoid interrupting important tasks or flooding notifications.
- Achievement notifications are **optional and configurable**. Avoid repeated announcements for the same milestone or retroactive correction; correct or withdraw stale achievements without misleading users.
- **No automatic bonus points** are granted for milestones, celebrations, or leaderboard positions. Existing earned-point and reward rules remain authoritative.
- Ora must enforce all Home permissions and privacy choices, including leaderboard opt-outs. Do not reveal hidden members, their scores, inferred rankings, or private reward balances in celebration messages, wall displays, notifications, or summaries. Personal milestones may be shown privately even when a member opts out of public leaderboard participation.
- Ora's fixed signature appearance and the approved calm Madeora visual language remain intact; celebrations must not introduce a competing gamification visual system.

### Confirmed individual Home leaderboard opt-out (12 October 2026)
- When a Home enables its optional leaderboard, eligible Home members **participate by default**, subject to existing Home permissions and privacy restrictions.
- Each member can **opt out at any time** and later opt back in for that Home independently; their choice does not require manager approval and does not affect chore assignments, earned points, available or reserved balances, redemption eligibility, or reward history.
- An opted-out member must not appear or be identifiable through leaderboard rankings, positions, member counts or aggregates that reveal their contribution, historical period views, dashboard widgets, wall displays, notifications, exports, Ora, or cached leaderboard data. Rankings of visible members are recalculated without exposing hidden members.
- Home managers may enable or disable the leaderboard for the Home but cannot override a member's personal opt-out. Preserve the preference through ordinary membership changes where appropriate; account deletion and privacy rules still take precedence.
- Provide an understandable per-Home participation control and explain that opting out hides leaderboard participation, not the member's chore completion records from people otherwise authorized to see them.

### Confirmed Home leaderboard tie-breaking (12 October 2026)
- Within the selected weekly, monthly, or all-time leaderboard period, rank eligible participating members first by **earned reward points**, highest first.
- When members have equal earned points, rank the member with **more completed chores during that same period** higher. Count each qualifying completed chore occurrence once for its actual contributor; approved completions count only once approved, attributed to their actual completion time, and corrections/reversals must update the count without duplicating history.
- If both earned points and completed-chore counts are equal, members **share the same rank** (competition ranking, e.g. 1, 1, 3). Display tied members in a stable, noncompetitive order without implying an additional tie-breaker.
- Manual bonuses may affect earned points but do not increase completed-chore counts. Respect all previously approved leaderboard privacy, permissions, Home-local period boundaries, and audit-history rules.

### Confirmed Home leaderboard ranking periods (12 October 2026)
- When a Home enables its optional earned-points leaderboard, provide **weekly, monthly, and all-time** ranking views. Members permitted to view the leaderboard can switch among these periods.
- Each Home can configure the default ranking period; **monthly** is the initial default until changed by an authorized Home manager.
- Rankings use **points earned within the selected period**, not current spendable or reserved balances, and must respect the already-approved member participation, privacy, authorization, anonymization, and wall-display rules.
- Define consistent Home-local calendar boundaries for weeks and months, an unambiguous all-time start, tie handling, and treatment of approved chore completions, bonuses, reversals, and later corrections in supporting specifications. Never double-count transactions or retroactively rewrite audit history.
- Clearly label the active period and ranking metric, including when a period has no eligible activity; keep the Home's selected default consistent across supported views without exposing excluded members.

### Confirmed reward balance privacy and optional Home leaderboard (12 October 2026)

- A member’s available, reserved, and total reward point balances are private by default: visible to that member and to Home members with explicit authorization to manage/view member reward balances. Membership alone does not grant access to another member’s balance or transaction details.
- Each Home may optionally enable a leaderboard that displays **earned points** for participating members; the leaderboard does not disclose private available balances, reserved points, redemption histories, or manual adjustment reasons.
- Leaderboard visibility and participation must respect Home-level settings, applicable member privacy controls, and existing module/capability permissions; members excluded by privacy settings are not identified or inferable through rankings, totals, exports, wall displays, Ora, or notifications.
- Define the leaderboard's reporting period and ranking rules clearly in its UI, and avoid treating earned-point rankings as spendable balances. Changes to visibility apply to all surfaces and cached views.
- Preserve the existing anonymization requirements for permanently deleted accounts (Former member) and historical Home records.

### Confirmed manual reward point bonuses and adjustments (12 October 2026)
- **Authorized Home reward-point managers may grant discretionary bonus points and apply positive or negative corrections** to a member's Home-specific reward balance. These are explicit manual transactions, not edits to historic chore completions or prior ledger entries.
- **Require a meaningful reason and explicit confirmation** before posting any adjustment. Preview affected Home/member, signed point change, current available and reserved balances, and resulting balance or any restriction.
- **Keep a complete immutable, permission-aware audit trail** with initiator, recipient, Home, reason, timestamp, amount, before/after balances and links to related correction or reversal entries. Use new compensating ledger transactions for reversals rather than rewriting history.
- Recheck current Home membership and specific adjustment permission at commit. Do not allow ordinary chore completion or reward approval permissions to imply balance-adjustment authority. Preserve Home-specific balances and the approved no-transfer and member-departure policies.
- **Prevent overspending and inconsistent balances:** never silently consume reserved points, alter pending reward reservations, or create spendable negative balances through a negative adjustment. If a requested debit exceeds available points, block it with a clear explanation and require an explicitly supported resolution rather than partially applying it.
- Protect against duplicate submissions and concurrent redemption/approval/adjustment races. Notify the affected member of completed adjustments in-app without disclosing sensitive details to unauthorized people; specify notification preferences and exceptional former-member handling in supporting specifications.
- Acceptance criteria cover bonus award, positive/negative correction, required reason, insufficient available balance, concurrent transactions, duplicate retry, permission revocation, and audit-preserving reversal.

### Confirmed Home reward points on membership departure (12 October 2026)
- **Unused reward points belong to the Home-specific member ledger and remain preserved when a member voluntarily leaves or is removed from that Home.** Do not automatically expire, forfeit, transfer, or spend the balance merely because membership ends.
- While absent, the former member cannot view or spend the Home balance through ordinary member access, initiate redemptions, or use pending reservations. Retain ledger entries, completed contributions, redemptions, and audit history under the approved historical attribution and privacy rules.
- **If the same account rejoins the same Home, restore access to that preserved balance**, subject to current membership, permissions and any authorized intervening corrections. Do not create a new balance, duplicate credits, or combine balances across Homes. Joining a different Home never transfers these points.
- On departure, pending approval-required redemptions and their reserved points must be safely resolved: cancel unresolved requests and release reservations to the preserved Home ledger exactly once, while retaining history and informing the affected user as allowed by notification/privacy rules. Existing fulfilled/unfulfilled reward obligations remain traceable for authorized Home managers and must not be silently deleted.
- Rejoining does not restore prior roles or permissions automatically. Whole-account permanent deletion, whole-Home deletion and restoration follow their separately approved lifecycle and anonymization policies; this decision does not promise points can be accessed after the underlying account or Home has been permanently deleted.
- Supporting specifications must test voluntary departure, removal, rejoining with the same account, concurrent approval/departure, reserved balance release, historical corrections, and membership changes without point loss or double spending.

### Confirmed reward points never expire (12 October 2026)
- **Earned Madeora Home reward points never expire automatically.** There is no age-based expiry, inactivity timeout, scheduled forfeiture, or automatic periodic balance reset. Members may save earned points indefinitely toward larger rewards while their point account remains valid.
- Points leave the available balance only through an authorized redemption/reservation, an explicitly authorized and audited correction or adjustment, or other separately approved account/Home lifecycle rules. Pending reward reservations are not expiration and must be released or finalized under the approved redemption workflow.
- Show accurate available, reserved, earned, and spent balances. Do not introduce expiration warnings or expiration configuration in reward settings. Changes to chore reward values do not retroactively change previously earned points.
- Document exceptional membership departure, account deletion, Home deletion, point-ledger corrections, and inaccessible-account handling in supporting specifications without inventing a silent expiry or contradicting established ownership, retention and recovery policies.

### Confirmed unable-to-fulfill reward resolution (12 October 2026)
- **An authorized Home reward-fulfillment manager can resolve a redeemed reward marked Unable to fulfill by either refunding its original redeemed points or arranging a replacement reward.** Neither outcome is automatic; require explicit selection and confirmation with a clear impact preview.
- **Refund:** Credit the original redeemed point amount back to the original member exactly once, using a new linked compensating ledger entry rather than deleting or rewriting the original redemption. Preserve the original reward cost, redemption, failure reason, refund decision, actor and timestamps. Do not automatically reverse unrelated point adjustments or re-award capacity without an explicit applicable policy.
- **Replacement:** Record the agreed replacement, responsible Home member, and outstanding fulfillment obligation linked to the original redemption. Do not deduct points a second time for the replacement; preserve traceability until the replacement is fulfilled or itself cannot be fulfilled. A replacement is not treated as delivered simply because it was selected.
- Notify the affected member of the resolution and its consequences without exposing private details to unauthorized members. Recheck permission, membership, point-account availability and current reward state at confirmation; prevent double refunds, duplicate replacements and conflicting simultaneous resolutions.
- Record the resolution choice, reason/notes where supplied, reviewer, date and any resulting point transaction in the audit history. Preserve history when a reward is disabled/deleted or a participant leaves. Supporting reward specifications must define handling of inaccessible former-member accounts, failed refunds, replacement failure, and correction of mistaken resolutions without silently creating inconsistent balances.

### Confirmed reward fulfillment tracking (12 October 2026)
- **Track reward fulfillment separately from point redemption and approval.** A successfully immediate-redeemed or approved reward enters **Pending fulfillment**; an approval-pending request is not yet a redeemed reward and must not enter fulfillment.
- Fulfillment states are **Pending fulfillment**, **Fulfilled**, and **Unable to fulfill**. Show these distinctly from redemption approval and point-ledger status; record fulfillment status, actor, timestamp, and optional explanatory notes in the reward history.
- Members with explicit Home reward-fulfillment management permission can mark redeemed rewards fulfilled or unable to fulfill. Recheck Home membership, permissions, and current state on every action; prevent duplicate fulfillment and conflicting concurrent changes.
- **Unable to fulfill does not silently refund, release, or deduct points again.** Keep the original redemption and point history intact and clearly flag the case for authorized resolution; the policy for refunds/replacements requires a separate explicit decision. Do not imply the member received the reward.
- A reward definition being edited, disabled, or deleted must not erase outstanding fulfillment obligations. Preserve an accessible permission-aware fulfillment queue and immutable historical transaction links.
- Supporting specifications must cover immediate and approved redemption, member departure, manager changes, notification/privacy behavior, correction of erroneous fulfillment states, auditability, and concurrency.

### Confirmed reward availability and redemption limits (12 October 2026)
- **Home reward managers may optionally configure availability start and end dates, a total redemption limit, and per-member redemption frequency limits** (for example, once per week). Rewards are **unlimited by default** when these controls are unset; existing manual enabled/disabled state still applies.
- Show eligibility, availability windows, remaining capacity when limited, and the next eligible redemption time clearly to members; apply the Home timezone consistently to date boundaries and weekly limits.
- For approval-required rewards, **pending requests reserve capacity as well as points** so concurrent requests cannot exceed total or per-member limits. Approval consumes the reserved capacity; rejection or cancellation releases it exactly once. Completed redemptions count toward applicable limits; no double-counting on approval.
- Check Home membership, reward enabled state, dates, per-member limits, total capacity and available points atomically when requesting or immediately redeeming; revalidate applicable permissions and request state at approval. If a reward becomes disabled or unavailable while a request is pending, present a clear resolution rather than silently granting it or forfeiting reserved points.
- Changes to reward limits or availability must preview their effect on pending requests and already-redeemed history. Do not retroactively invalidate completed redemptions or silently cancel pending requests. Prevent races, duplicate reservations and overspending; record management changes in audit history.
- Specify edge cases for calendar boundaries, member departure, concurrent redemption, restored requests, edits to reward definitions, and exhausted capacity in the supporting reward specification.

### Confirmed cancellation of pending reward redemptions (12 October 2026)
- **The requester may cancel their own pending approval-required reward redemption** while it remains pending. This does not require reward-approval or Home-management permission, but does require verifying their identity and request ownership.
- **An authorized Home manager may cancel another member’s pending redemption request** when permitted by the Home’s reward-management permissions. Require a cancellation reason for manager-initiated cancellations, record the reason and actor in the audit history, and notify the requester in-app; honor applicable optional email preferences without exposing sensitive reward details unnecessarily.
- On valid cancellation, **atomically mark the request cancelled and release its reserved points** back to the requester’s available balance. No reward is granted, and the points must be released exactly once.
- Cancellation is unavailable once a request is approved, rejected, or already cancelled; handle races with review, membership changes, and repeated actions by rechecking live state and returning a clear result. Do not silently undo completed redemptions.
- Show the request, reservation amount, cancellation consequences, and appropriate confirmation before a manager cancels another person’s request. Preserve ledger and audit history for accountability.

### Confirmed independent reviewer for reward redemptions (12 October 2026)
- **A reward-redemption requester must never approve their own approval-required reward request**, even if they hold explicit reward-approval permission, are a Home Administrator, or own the Home. This rule is intentionally different from permitted self-approval for chore completions.
- Approval requires **a different currently authorized Home member** with explicit reward-approval permission. Recheck requester identity, reviewer identity, Home membership, permission, request status, reward eligibility and reserved points at the moment of approval; reject attempts to approve through alternate sessions or concurrent actions.
- If no other eligible reviewer is available, keep the request pending and clearly explain the blocker; do not auto-approve, release reserved points without a valid cancellation, or allow requester self-approval. Provide a permission-aware way to cancel or arrange a different authorized reviewer.
- Record requester, reviewer, decision, timestamp, and point-ledger transitions in the audit history. Reviewer permissions do not confer an exception to this separation-of-duties rule.

### Confirmed optional approval for reward redemption (12 October 2026)
- **Each Home reward can be configured for immediate redemption or approval-required redemption.** Immediate redemption is the default unless explicitly configured otherwise; only members with the relevant Home permissions may manage reward definitions and approval requirements.
- **Immediate rewards:** Verify current Home membership, reward availability, eligibility, and sufficient *available* points; atomically deduct the stated cost and create one recorded redemption. Prevent duplicate requests and overspending during concurrent redemptions.
- **Approval-required rewards:** On request, verify eligibility and sufficient available points, then **reserve** the reward cost so those points cannot be spent elsewhere. Show the request as pending, without treating the reward as granted or the reserved points as finally spent.
- **Approval:** Recheck the reviewer's current explicit reward-approval permission, requester eligibility, reward validity, and pending state. On approval, atomically convert the existing reservation to a final deduction and record the approved redemption once; do not deduct twice.
- **Rejection or cancellation:** Release the reservation back into available points and record the outcome; do not grant the reward. Define permissions for cancelling pending requests separately; requester self-approval is prohibited under the confirmed independent-reviewer rule.
- Preserve a clear ledger distinguishing **earned, available, reserved, and spent** points, with contributor/requester, reward, point cost snapshot, timestamps, reviewer, and decision history. A later reward price edit must not silently change existing pending reservations or completed redemptions.
- Handle changes in Home membership, reward deletion or unavailability, permission revocation, balance corrections, concurrent approvals, and processing failures safely and visibly; do not silently lose reservations, duplicate rewards, or create inconsistent balances. Define notification and fulfillment workflows in the supporting reward specification.

### Confirmed uniform base chore points across eligible members (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Each chore has one configured base reward-point value that applies equally to every eligible Home member who actually completes that occurrence. Do not offer member-specific base point overrides or dynamic per-member bonuses for the same chore.
- Display the same potential base points to all eligible members in assigned chore cards, unassigned-pool claim cards, and chore details, subject to Home visibility. The person who claims an unassigned occurrence gains no points merely by claiming it; award only on valid completion, after approval if required.
- Preserve the existing **per-occurrence reward-value snapshot** and the established **full/reduced/zero late-completion policy**. A late completion can result in fewer awarded points than the shared base amount, but not because of the contributor's identity. Clearly distinguish base, estimated, pending and finally earned points.
- Actual contributor attribution governs who receives the points, even if another authorized member submits completion. Reassignments, self-release, recurrence, delayed approval, corrections and concurrent claims must not create member-specific reward rates, retroactive value changes or duplicate ledger entries.
- Only authorized Home reward/chore managers may edit base values; preview the effect on future occurrences and keep existing occurrence snapshots and earned history intact unless an explicit authorized correction is made. Validate nonnegative point values and permission changes. Acceptance tests cover equal base points for different eligible members, first-come claims, on-time versus late completion, approval, contributor correction and multiple Homes.

### Confirmed per-occurrence chore reward value preservation (12 October 2026)
- **Snapshot the configured reward points onto each recurring chore occurrence when that occurrence is created.** Each occurrence retains its own reward value, independent of later edits to the recurring chore template or series.
- **Changes to a recurring chore’s reward value apply only to future occurrences created after the change.** Already-created incomplete, overdue, skipped, pending-approval, and completed occurrences retain their existing reward values unless an authorized user explicitly corrects an eligible occurrence.
- At approval, calculate any on-time, reduced-late, or zero-late award using **the occurrence’s preserved reward value and applicable late-completion policy**, together with its reported actual completion time; never substitute the series’ current reward value or the value at approval time.
- Authorized corrections must show the affected occurrence, old and new points, contributor and resulting balance impact; preserve an audit trail and reconcile previously awarded or redeemed points safely. Do not silently retroactively recalculate completed history or bulk-update pending approvals.
- Maintain consistency when recurrence instances are generated in advance, edited with This occurrence / This and following / Entire series scopes, or approved concurrently with a reward-setting change. Prevent duplicate points and include boundary and concurrency tests in supporting specifications.

### Confirmed reward timing for delayed chore approvals (12 October 2026)
- **Calculate chore completion reward points from the actual reported completion timestamp, not the later approval timestamp.** An approval delay must never turn an on-time completion into a late one or reduce the contributor’s earned points merely because review was slow.
- Apply the chore’s configured on-time/late points policy against the occurrence’s original due date and the reported completion time. Keep submission time, reported completion time, approval decision time, approver, and final points distinguishable in the audit history.
- For approval-required chores, points remain pending and unusable until an authorized reviewer approves; award the calculated points to the actual contributor once, on approval. Rejection awards none. Correcting an approved completion timestamp or contributor must reconcile points with a traceable adjustment, not duplicate awards.
- Validate reported completion timestamps and edits against permissions and plausible chronology; do not allow unreviewed backdating to silently bypass lateness rules. Show reviewers the claimed completion time and resulting reward impact before approval. Resolve timezone and daylight-saving boundaries consistently with the occurrence’s Home scheduling timezone.
- Preserve completed history and future rotation schedules. Cover delayed reviews, disputed timestamps, simultaneous approvals, policy changes, and already-redeemed points in supporting specifications and acceptance tests.

### Confirmed pending chore-approval reminders and lifetime (12 October 2026)
- **Pending chore-completion approval requests do not expire automatically.** They remain actionable until explicitly approved, rejected, withdrawn, or invalidated by an applicable permission, membership, deletion, or occurrence-state change. No points are earned while pending.
- **Remind currently authorized reviewers after 24 hours** if a submitted completion still awaits a decision. Thereafter, offer **configurable daily grouped reminders** rather than a separate reminder per outstanding chore; honor each reviewer’s reminder and email preferences, while keeping pending requests discoverable in-app.
- Group reminders by Home and reviewer where appropriate, clearly indicate outstanding counts, and link to the secure permission-aware approval queue. Do not disclose sensitive chore details in emails or send reminders to members who no longer have approval permission.
- Stop reminders promptly once a request is resolved or invalidated. Avoid duplicates, respect notification quiet settings where specified, and revalidate the request and reviewer permission before any reminder or action.
- Preserve original submission and reported completion timestamps so delayed approval does not silently rewrite when work was performed. Define late-points calculation separately using the approved completion policy, and cover long-pending requests, concurrent decisions, account/Home recovery, and notification failure in acceptance criteria.

### Confirmed optional Ora-led chore points notifications (13 October 2026)
- **Approved customized option B:** Members may opt in to notifications when chore points are **awarded, adjusted or reversed**. These notifications should be expressed through **Ora's established warm, playful personality**, for example a brief cheerful congratulations when points are earned, rather than a generic mechanical notification. This is a narrowly scoped notification presentation requirement, not authorization to expand Ora's paused assistant capabilities.
- Ora's celebration is an optional presentation layer for an authoritative reward-ledger event: display the actual awarded amount and resulting balance only where the recipient is authorized to see them. Never celebrate a claim, unapproved submission, rejected completion or pending points as earned; adjustments and reversals must use accurate, tactful wording rather than congratulations.
- Members can enable or disable this notification category and choose available delivery channels. Respect existing notification preferences, quiet hours, accessibility and reduced-motion settings; avoid intrusive overlays or forced animations. Use concise in-app Ora messages where supported, and safe plain-language fallbacks for push/email or when Ora is unavailable or disabled. Do not require the full Ora assistant to be active for reward notifications to work.
- Group or suppress repetitive messages to prevent spam; use the finalized ledger transaction identity for idempotency so retries, approval replays and synchronization never trigger duplicate cheers. If an award is later reversed, do not silently leave a misleading earned-points celebration as the sole record; show the accurate ledger state.
- Respect Home isolation, membership changes, recipient privacy and wall-display rules; do not expose private balances or contributor details on shared screens. No automatic milestone, target-achievement or leaderboard celebrations are added by this decision beyond any separately approved existing Ora behavior.
- Acceptance criteria: approved award and immediate non-approval award; pending/rejected submissions; adjustment and reversal; opt-in/off; quiet hours; Ora disabled; reduced motion; duplicate event retries; revoked Home access; shared wall display; accessible fallback messaging.

### Confirmed subtle Ora chore-point celebration presentation (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When an opted-in member receives a finalized chore-point award, Ora may **briefly appear in context with a cheerful, subtle animated message**. Preserve Ora's established character, Madeora's restrained visual language, and the existing narrow scope of reward notifications; this does not resume broader Ora feature design.
- The celebration is **nonblocking, short-lived and dismissible**, never steals focus, interrupts forms or obscures essential dashboard controls. Keep it accessible on desktop and mobile; respect screen-reader announcements, keyboard navigation, and `prefers-reduced-motion` by replacing motion with a static, equally informative message. Do not add sound, confetti, full-screen effects or compulsory animation.
- Save the corresponding award notification in the **notification center** so the user can review it after the transient celebration disappears. The transient appearance and persistent notification refer to **one underlying ledger event**, not duplicate awards or separate notifications. Retried deliveries must not replay repeated celebrations.
- Respect per-member reward notification opt-in, Ora presentation preferences, quiet hours, notification channels and display context. During quiet hours or when transient celebrations are disabled, keep eligible events accessible through permitted noninterruptive channels and the notification center without unexpected pop-ups. Provide a plain-language fallback if Ora is unavailable.
- Show only verified, authorized point amounts for approved awards; adjustments and reversals use tactful correction messages **without congratulatory animations**. Never celebrate pending, rejected or unapproved submissions. Suppress private member balances on shared/wall displays unless explicitly authorized; follow existing display-only, restrained wall Ora behavior.
- Acceptance criteria: immediate approved award; approval-delayed award; duplicate event delivery; notification center persistence; dismissed toast; quiet hours; disabled notifications; reduced motion and screen reader; narrow mobile viewport; Ora unavailable; adjustment/reversal; permission revocation; wall-display privacy.

### Confirmed grouping of nearby Ora chore-point celebrations (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When multiple finalized chore-point awards for the **same member and Home** arrive close together, Ora combines them into **one brief, subtle celebration** showing the summed **net positive awarded points** and the number of **distinct rewarded chore occurrences**. Preserve individual underlying reward transactions, chore records, timestamps and approval history; grouping changes presentation only, never reward accounting.
- Use a short, bounded grouping window for consecutive awards, with a clear maximum wait before showing the celebration; never delay ledger updates or reward availability while waiting to group. A single award still receives its normal eligible celebration. Repeated deliveries of the same transaction or replay after reconnect must not inflate counts or trigger another celebration.
- The notification center retains an accurate, accessible record of **each individual award**, with an optional grouped summary that links to the underlying items; do not replace detailed history with an aggregate or double-count points. Grouped announcements must identify that multiple chores contributed without exposing private chore details in shared contexts.
- Do not mix different members, Homes, point currencies, or distinct reward-event types. Adjustments and reversals are **not positive chore completions** and receive accurate, tactful correction notices rather than being rolled into a congratulatory total. If a correction occurs after a celebration, the ledger and notification history must show the updated truth.
- Respect each recipient's notification opt-in, quiet hours, muted celebrations, reduced-motion and accessibility settings. Avoid queues of delayed popups after quiet hours or reconnection; favor a concise, noninterruptive summary where allowed. No sounds, confetti, leaderboard implications, or expanded Ora assistant capabilities.
- Acceptance criteria: one award; five awards in quick succession; awards outside the grouping window; mixed Homes/members; duplicated/retried events; pending versus approved; delayed approval; reversal after award; concurrent device sessions; quiet hours; muted celebrations; screen reader and reduced motion; complete individual ledger and notification-center auditability.

### Confirmed optional personal weekly chore point targets (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each eligible Home member may optionally set, edit, pause or remove **their own nonnegative weekly chore-point target** for the selected Home. Targets are personal and voluntary; Home managers cannot impose or silently modify another member's target under this decision. Do not introduce default mandatory targets.
- In the **separate optional weekly points widget**, display the member's approved earned chore points for the Home-local week against their chosen target, including an accessible numeric fraction and progress indicator. A missing target displays no target-based progress; a zero target is treated as disabled rather than a misleading instantly achieved goal. Clearly distinguish earned points from pending approval, potential assigned points and unassigned pool points, none of which advance target progress before being earned.
- Targets are scoped to a single Home and member; do not combine points across Homes or expose private targets in shared widgets or wall displays without explicit applicable visibility authorization. In the shared Home points overview, show another member's target/progress only if that member has permitted such visibility; otherwise show authorized earned/potential totals without revealing the target.
- Weekly targets reset **progress** each new Home-local week while retaining the member's chosen target until edited or disabled. Editing the target affects the current and future displayed target, not the historical reward ledger; preserve a stable record of historical earned totals and avoid rewriting past achievements. Timezone/week-boundary changes, late approvals, corrections and reversals recalculate the applicable week's earned progress according to the existing reward attribution rules.
- Targets do **not** change chore assignments, eligibility, claiming limits, point values, approval requirements, late rewards, payouts, rankings or notification rules. No automatic bonus or penalty for meeting or missing a target. Keep progress informational and noncompetitive.
- Acceptance criteria: enable/edit/disable own target; no target and zero-target handling; progress before/after approval; late completion and reward reversal; weekly rollover; separate Homes; another member's private target; wall-display redaction; concurrent updates; responsive accessible progress labels.

### Confirmed optional shared Home weekly chore points overview (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** The existing **separate, optional weekly points dashboard widget** may be configured as a **shared Home overview** displaying each participating, authorized member's **approved earned chore points for the selected Home-local week** and **potential points from their assigned, unfinished chore occurrences**. Do not introduce rankings, competitive ordering, podiums, or member-comparison analytics in this shared view.
- Show earned and potential assigned points as **separate, clearly labeled values** for each member; do not treat claimed, submitted/pending approval, rejected, skipped, closed or merely scheduled chores as earned. Pending approval can be identified separately where permitted. Apply existing late-point adjustments, approval rules, ledger corrections and deduplication. Unassigned-pool potential points remain a distinct, unallocated total, never credited to a member before an eligible claim and approved completion.
- The widget is **opt-in and independently addable/removable**, separate from the prominent weekly chore schedule. Members see only participants and totals their Home role and privacy settings authorize; support member-level participation/visibility restrictions and wall-display redaction. Do not reveal hidden member names, counts or points through aggregates, filters or empty states. The selected Home is always explicit; never aggregate multiple Homes.
- Use consistent Home-local week boundaries and per-occurrence attribution, including claims, releases, reassignments, late completions, approval reversals and historical edits. Refresh after any relevant state or permission change; display accessible responsive member rows/cards, loading/empty/error states and explanatory potential-versus-earned labels.
- **Scope:** This choice does not turn the widget into a leaderboard, impose weekly claiming limits, or add automatic rewards. An independently documented optional leaderboard, if enabled elsewhere, must not silently replace or rank this noncompetitive shared points overview.
- Acceptance criteria: add/remove shared widget; two members with different earned/potential totals; pending and late approval; unassigned points separated; claim/release/reassignment; member participation and revoked access; Home isolation; weekly timezone boundary; responsive and shared-wall privacy; concurrent ledger corrections.

### Confirmed chore-card points and separate optional weekly points widget (13 October 2026)
- **Approved customized option A:** Display the applicable potential reward points on individual chore cards in the Home dashboard, including assigned chores and claimable unassigned occurrences. **Do not embed a mandatory weekly member-points summary inside the main chore schedule**; preserve the schedule’s focus on who does what and when.
- Provide a **separate, optional, independently addable/configurable Home dashboard widget** for weekly points. It summarizes each authorized member’s **earned points this week** and **points still available from their assigned, incomplete chores**, with potential points from **unassigned, claimable chores** displayed separately and not attributed to any member until claimed. It may be added, removed, positioned and resized through established dashboard customization, independently of the chore schedule widget.
- Define earned points using the existing reward ledger and approved completion rules: pending-approval completions are **not earned**; rejected/skipped chores contribute none; account for late-completion reward adjustments, corrections and reversals without double counting. Label potential assigned and unassigned points as **possible, not guaranteed**, and avoid adding them to earned totals. Use the selected Home’s local week boundary consistently, including recurring occurrences and reassignment/claim changes.
- Recompute both widgets after claim, completion, approval, rejection, skip, reschedule, reassignment, reward adjustment or permission changes. Only include eligible visible records within the selected Home; enforce member-level visibility rules, especially on shared/wall displays. Show clear empty/loading/error states, accessible text distinctions, and avoid exposing private points through unauthorized counts.
- This decision does **not** introduce mandatory leaderboard rankings, points projections, performance analytics or automatic reward adjustments. Any previously approved optional leaderboard remains a distinct configuration, not a requirement of the chore schedule.
- Acceptance criteria: chore cards with points; no embedded weekly summary; add/remove weekly points widget; member earned vs pending vs assigned-available; unassigned pool points separately; late rewards and approvals; overlapping claims; week boundaries; Home isolation; revoked visibility; mobile and wall-display layouts.

### Confirmed compact expandable Home chore overview and unassigned pool (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** The prominent Home chore overview uses a **compact presentation with expansion** in both approved member-by-day matrix and daily grouped cards layouts. Show a small, configurable maximum number of visible chore items per member/day (with a sensible readable default), followed by a precise **+N more** indicator for hidden eligible occurrences. Expanding the relevant cell/group reveals its full permission-filtered chore list and available actions without navigating away; provide an accessible collapse control.
- Keep a **separate, clearly labeled, expandable Unassigned this week pool** visible alongside the assigned schedule, including a meaningful count and available point amounts for eligible claimable occurrences. Show a compact initial selection and **+N more / Show all** action. Each eligible occurrence exposes its scheduled day, title, available points and a permission-controlled **Claim / Assign to me** action, subject to the already-approved self-claim and manager-authorization rules. Claiming never awards points by itself.
- Respect the selected Home-local week and the existing day/week switch. The unassigned section follows the selected week, and in day mode makes it clear whether the visible pool is filtered to that day and offers an explicit way to view the whole week. Preserve expanded/collapsed state where practical when changing layout, without showing stale counts or silently hiding newly overdue/pending items.
- **Count semantics:** The +N count is the number of additional *visible and authorized* chore occurrences within that specific member/day or unassigned grouping after filters, not a count of inaccessible chores, duplicate assignments or recurring series. Display empty states for no chores and no unassigned chores. Clearly distinguish hidden-by-collapse from hidden-by-filter; never suggest that undisplayed chores are completed.
- In expanded views, preserve existing quick-completion, checklist, approval, reassignment and claim restrictions. Show status and points clearly, with text/icon cues independent of color. Update counts, member/day groups, available points and claim buttons promptly after completion, approval, rescheduling, claiming, reassignment or permission changes; reject stale concurrent claims safely.
- The compactness setting is a **presentation preference** for the widget, not a change to scheduling, assignments, rewards or historical records. On mobile, expand readable day/member groups rather than compressing cells; support keyboard and screen-reader expansion, touch controls, light/dark themes and privacy-safe wall-display variants that do not expose interactive claim/approval actions without authentication.
- Acceptance criteria: 1/0/many chores per member/day; configurable limit and accurate +N counts; expand/collapse in both layouts; unassigned week pool with points and claims; day/week switching; filters and Home switching; large households; overdue and pending approval; concurrent claim; revoked permissions; narrow mobile and wall display; synchronized updates without duplicate point awards.

### Confirmed member-proposed unassigned chores and manager approval (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Current members of a Home may **suggest a new unassigned chore** for that Home, including a proposed title, instructions, optional checklist/category/estimated duration, proposed scheduled day or week, and **suggested reward points**. A suggestion is a proposal, not an active chore occurrence or an entitlement to points. Only authorized Home administrators/chore managers can approve and publish it into the **Unassigned this week** pool (or its appropriate scheduled week).
- A proposal moves through explicit **Draft (optional) → Pending review → Approved/published or Rejected/withdrawn** states. Show proposer, submitted time, proposed details and point amount to authorized reviewers; show status and reviewer feedback to the proposer. Reviewers may reject with a reason or revise the proposal before approving, with the final scheduled date, checklist and reward points clearly shown and explicitly confirmed. Notify the proposer of the decision according to notification preferences.
- **No self-awarded points:** Proposers cannot approve their own proposals unless they independently hold the explicit Home chore-management permission. Even approved suggestions award **zero points until a member claims and actually completes the published occurrence**, and any required completion approval succeeds. Proposing a chore never reserves it for the proposer; it becomes equally claimable by all eligible members under established self-claim rules.
- Validate reasonable titles, dates, nonnegative permitted point values, duplicate or near-duplicate active proposals/chores and category/permission references. A manager may merge or decline duplicates without silently generating multiple reward opportunities. Keep proposal review separate from the existing **chore completion approval** queue and clearly label the two workflows.
- Publishing is atomic and idempotent: create one authorized, unassigned chore occurrence in the selected Home with the approved point value and relevant snapshot; do not create an unapproved recurring series or change established rotations. If recurring scheduling is desired, the manager must explicitly configure and authorize it under existing recurrence controls. An approved chore follows all established claim, due-date, checklist, approval, late-points, history and deletion rules.
- Revalidate membership, reviewer rights, point-setting rights and proposal version at decision time; prevent concurrent double publication, stale approval, edits after approval, cross-Home exposure and reward manipulation. Members losing access cannot publish or claim through old links. Keep a permission-aware record of proposer, reviewer, decision, revisions and timestamps, with accessible pending/empty/error states and private wall-display behavior.
- Acceptance criteria: submit and withdraw proposal; approve with unchanged or revised points; reject with feedback; manager with and without point-setting permission; proposer who is also an authorized manager; no reward for proposal or claim; approved chore claim/completion/approval; duplicate suggestions; concurrent reviewers; week change; revoked membership; Home isolation and responsive views.

### Confirmed optional individual chore reminders (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** An eligible assigned member may configure an optional reminder for a specific chore occurrence, either for a chosen date/time or relative to its due date. This supplements existing standard chore notifications and approval-reviewer reminders; it does not add multiple reminder sequences, repeated follow-ups, or overdue escalation.
- The reminder interface clearly distinguishes **specific date/time** from **relative to due date**, previews the resolved local delivery time, and supports editing/removal. A relative reminder requires an actual due date/time or an explicitly defined Home-local reference time for date-only chores; never silently invent a deadline hour. Validate missing, past, changed, and removed due dates and show any necessary rescheduling.
- Reminders are personal notification preferences, not a change to the shared chore's assignment, due date, checklist, points, approval requirement, or recurrence. Honor the recipient's enabled notification channels, quiet hours, timezone and accessibility preferences; show an unavailable-channel state instead of promising delivery. Only send chore details to currently authorized recipients, never to public wall displays or members who have lost access.
- On reassignment, self-release, skipping, cancellation, expiry, completion submission, approval/rejection, deletion, membership revocation or recipient notification changes, revalidate whether a reminder remains appropriate; suppress or cancel obsolete reminders and do not automatically transfer one member's private reminder settings to another member. A new assignee can configure their own reminder.
- For recurring chores, reminders are explicitly scoped to the selected occurrence unless an already-approved series-edit workflow is used to configure future occurrences. Preserve past occurrence history, respect **This occurrence / This and following / Entire series** scope controls where applicable, and never create duplicate reminders for one occurrence through generation, retries or overlapping edits.
- Revalidate assignment, permissions, occurrence version, delivery time and settings when dispatching. Handle DST/timezone changes, simultaneous claim/release or reassignment, delayed delivery and retry deduplication without exposing unauthorized chore information.
- Acceptance criteria: absolute and relative reminders; date-only and missing due dates; quiet hours and disabled channels; recurring occurrence versus series; unassigned claim followed by release; reassignment and absence; completed/pending-approval/skipped/expired chores; permission revocation; DST; concurrent edits and retries; mobile-accessible configuration.

### Confirmed per-chore eligibility for claiming unassigned chores (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** An approved, published unassigned chore occurrence is claimable by **all members of its Home who already have general chore-claiming permission by default**. Authorized chore managers may optionally restrict a chore to a **specified set of eligible Home members**. This is an additional eligibility condition, not a replacement for Home membership, role permissions, claimability, expiry, or completion rules; it does not introduce age/qualification-based automation or a second claim-approval step.
- In the chore editor, provide a clear **Who may claim?** choice between **All permitted Home members (default)** and **Selected Home members**, with an accessible member selector, explicit empty-selection validation, and a preview of who is eligible. Only managers with chore-management rights may set or change the restriction. For recurring chores, apply the established **This occurrence / This and following / Entire series** scopes where applicable, preserving historical eligibility and assignments.
- Show eligible members a working **Claim / Assign to me** action and the normal potential-point preview. Members who can view but cannot claim a restricted chore may see a neutral **Restricted / Not eligible to claim** indicator without exposing private reasons, hidden member details, or an active Claim control; members lacking view permission must not see the chore or its counts at all. Filters and the weekly unassigned pool must not falsely imply a restricted chore is claimable by everyone. On wall displays, keep claiming disabled and apply privacy rules.
- Revalidate the **current Home, membership, general claim permission, selected-member restriction, occurrence status, expiry, version and assignment** atomically at claim time. First successful eligible claim wins; rejected or stale attempts return clear feedback. If a manager edits restrictions while a chore is already claimed, do not silently unassign the current member or revoke earned points; surface the existing assignment for explicit manager review under the established reassignment and correction rules.
- Eligibility restrictions do not alter the base point value, reward approval, late-point treatment, self-release, or independent claiming of future recurring occurrences. Members can claim unlimited occurrences **provided each one is eligible**. Do not add per-member reward multipliers, quotas, or automatic fairness rules.
- Acceptance criteria: default all-permitted behavior; restriction to one or multiple selected Home members; authorized edit and invalid empty selection; eligible claim succeeds; nonselected member blocked; general permission denied despite being selected; simultaneous eligible claims; revoked membership or restriction change during claim; claimed occurrence when restrictions change; recurring scope/history; separate Home membership; dashboard, calendar, mobile and wall-display privacy.

### Confirmed per-occurrence claiming for recurring unassigned chores (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Each occurrence generated by a recurring chore configured as **unassigned and claimable** is a separate first-come-first-served opportunity. An eligible member claims **only the explicitly selected occurrence**; a claim never reserves, assigns, locks or grants priority for future occurrences of that recurring series. Each future occurrence enters the appropriate Home-local weekly unassigned pool independently when scheduled and eligible.
- Preserve the recurring series definition, cadence, future assignment policy and rotations unchanged when an individual occurrence is claimed, released, completed, skipped or expired. Do not convert a one-off claim into ongoing ownership or infer preference from past claims. A manager may separately edit future series assignments only through existing explicit recurrence-scope and permission controls, never as a side effect of claiming.
- Display the occurrence's scheduled date/week, potential points, current claimability and recurrence context in the unassigned pool and detail view. Clearly distinguish **this occurrence** from **future occurrences**; do not show a misleading Claim series or Reserve next week action. Each occurrence follows its own approved publication, claim, self-release, late availability/expiry, completion approval and reward rules.
- A member may independently claim successive weeks' occurrences when each becomes eligible, subject to normal Home membership, permission and first-successful-claim checks; no claim quota or preferential allocation applies. Overdue unclaimed occurrences and new current-week occurrences may coexist and must remain separately identifiable, with no duplicate reward or double counting in the optional points widget.
- Revalidate occurrence identity, current assignee, eligibility, expiry policy and version atomically for each claim; handle two claimants of the same occurrence, separate simultaneous claims across different weeks, schedule edits and revoked membership without leaking other Homes' information. Maintain independent historical assignees and actual completers for each occurrence.
- Acceptance criteria: claim this week without assigning next week; new recurring occurrence remains unassigned; same member claims multiple weeks individually; different members claim successive occurrences; concurrent claims to same versus different occurrences; release or expiry of one occurrence does not affect others; manager edits series with explicit scope; overdue and current-week coexistence; reward deduplication and cross-Home isolation.

### Confirmed unlimited eligible self-claims of unassigned chores (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Eligible Home members may claim **any number of currently available, approved, unassigned chore occurrences**; Madeora imposes **no weekly quota**, maximum number of extra chores, or cap on the potential reward points that may be claimed. Do not add fair-share priority, automatic redistribution, or an artificial claim cooldown. First-successful-claim, first-served remains the rule for each individual single-assignee occurrence.
- **Eligibility still applies per occurrence:** Respect the selected Home's membership, chore-specific assignment restrictions, approved/published and claimable status, week-expiry policy, current assignee, permissions, and concurrent claim checks. Unlimited claims never mean unlimited access to restricted chores or permission to claim an occurrence already owned by another member.
- **Rewards remain completion-based:** Claiming any number of chores does not itself earn points. Actual completion, mandatory checklist steps, late-point policy, optional manager approval and reward-ledger deduplication continue to govern awarded points. Distinguish potential points for claimed/available chores from approved earned points and pending points in the separate optional weekly points widget; avoid suggesting potential rewards are guaranteed.
- **User experience:** Do not show a remaining-claims counter, weekly cap warning, claim lockout based on total claims, or member ranking that changes claim eligibility. Keep the compact expandable unassigned pool, clear Claim action, self-release before submission, member/day overview and accessible error states; update all views after successful claims and releases.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Member claims several chores in one week without limit; large potential point total without artificial rejection; independent concurrent claims on distinct occurrences; competing claims on the same occurrence produce one winner; restricted/expired/assigned chore rejected; points only on qualifying completion/approval; recurring occurrence isolation; self-release and re-claim; permission revocation; Home isolation; weekly widget totals without double counting.

### Confirmed self-release of voluntarily claimed chores (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A Home member who personally claimed an eligible unassigned chore may use **Release / Return to unassigned** to relinquish that **specific occurrence** without manager approval, provided they remain authorized and **have not submitted it for completion**. This is distinct from reassigning a chore originally allocated by a manager, which follows the existing reassignment rules. Managers retain their separate authorized release/correction capabilities.
- Offer the action in the member's assigned chore details and appropriate dashboard views, with an explicit confirmation explaining that their assignment and prospective points will be relinquished. Only the member who voluntarily claimed the occurrence (or an authorized manager) can release it; do not enable ordinary self-release for manager-assigned, rotated, or transferred chores unless independently authorized under existing rules.
- On a successful release, clear the member's assignment, record the actor/time and previous claimant in the occurrence history, and return the occurrence to the appropriate **current-week Unassigned**, **Overdue unassigned**, or other eligible claimable view based on its original scheduled date and configured expiry policy. Do not reset or move its scheduled date, alter recurring rotations or future occurrences, or automatically extend an expired claim window. If its policy or status no longer allows claiming, show the applicable closed/nonclaimable outcome rather than falsely advertising it as available.
- Release is unavailable after completion submission, during pending approval, or after approval/completion; members must use existing rejection/correction workflows where applicable. Where checklist progress or draft completion notes exist, warn before release, prevent the next claimant from inheriting the prior claimant's private draft or misleading progress, and preserve only the authorized historical/audit record. No points are earned, deducted or transferred solely by release; any earned rewards and historical completed occurrences remain unchanged.
- Validate Home membership, ownership of the voluntary claim, permissions, occurrence version and current state atomically; handle release-versus-completion, release-versus-manager reassignment and release-versus-another-claim races with a single definitive outcome and clear refresh. Update matrix, daily cards, unassigned pool, chore lists, Calendar overlay and separate points widget consistently. Do not allow release from read-only wall displays.
- Acceptance criteria: self-claimed chore released before submission; manager-assigned chore cannot be self-released; draft checklist/note handling; overdue release with remain-available policy; end-of-week closure policy; recurring occurrence isolation; released chore claimed by another member; attempted release after submission/approval; concurrent changes; points neutrality; permissions and cross-Home isolation; mobile and keyboard access.

### Confirmed configurable end-of-week expiry for unclaimed chores (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized Home chore managers may configure **per chore** whether an eligible, still-unclaimed occurrence **remains claimable as overdue** after its scheduled Home-local week ends or **automatically closes at the end of that week**. The default is **remain available as overdue**. Display the chosen policy in chore creation/editing, details and the unassigned pool, with a clear indication of what will happen at the week boundary.
- Apply the policy to the specific **unclaimed occurrence**, not to a whole recurring series or to already claimed/completed/submitted/approved/skipped occurrences. For recurring chores, carry the configured policy to new occurrences while retaining the applicable policy and actual outcome in historical occurrence records; changes to recurring settings follow established occurrence/series editing scopes. Do not delete historical entries or disrupt future rotations.
- **Remain available:** At week rollover, retain an unclaimed occurrence in an explicitly discoverable **Overdue unassigned** section or filter, even though it no longer belongs to the current week's scheduled occurrences. Keep its original scheduled date, show lateness and any existing late-points adjustment, and allow an eligible member to claim it under the established atomic first-come-first-served rules. Never silently move its due date into the new week or inflate current-week point totals.
- **Close after week:** At the end of the chore's scheduled week according to the selected Home's timezone and week-start convention, transition an unclaimed occurrence to an explicit **Closed — unclaimed (expired)** historical state; remove its Claim action and available-points totals. Closing awards no points, is not treated as completed, and does not change future recurring occurrences. Distinguish expiry from a manager's deliberate **Skipped** action and from a missed but still claimable chore. A manager may review history and use existing authorized correction/rescheduling workflows where applicable, never silently reopen an expired occurrence.
- Preserve the already-approved separation between **proposal approval**, **immediate self-claiming of approved chores**, and **optional completion approval**. Proposed-but-unapproved chores do not become claimable or expire as published occurrences. If a member claims an occurrence just before the cutoff, the committed claim wins; if expiry commits first, the claim fails with a clear stale/expired message. Revalidate permissions, current status, schedule and Home membership on every action.
- In both Home dashboard layouts, Chores lists/history, optional Calendar overlay and the separate weekly points widget, clearly distinguish current-week unassigned, overdue-available, and closed-unclaimed records. Keep available points separate from earned and pending points; do not double count overdue chores across weeks. Respect filters, empty states, mobile/wall-display privacy, notifications and Home isolation.
- Acceptance criteria: default overdue availability; explicit close-after-week policy; Home-local week rollover and DST; unclaimed recurring occurrence with next occurrence generated normally; claimed/pending/completed occurrences unaffected; overdue claim with late-point rules; expiry with zero reward; expiry-versus-claim race; manager edits and recurrence scopes; overdue discoverability outside current-week pool; history and points reconciliation; permission revocation; mobile and wall displays.

### Confirmed optional daily manager overdue-chore summary (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized Home chore managers may individually opt in to **one daily summary per Home** covering overdue chore occurrences, self-claimed chores approaching their configured automatic-release deadline, and occurrences that genuinely require manager intervention. The summary is **off by default** and never substitutes for existing overdue indicators in chore views.
- The manager can choose a preferred delivery time using their applicable timezone and notification preferences. Respect quiet hours by deferring delivery to the next permitted window; do not bypass muted channels or send an unnecessary empty summary. Avoid multiple catch-up digests after offline periods.
- Group items by actionable state and show chore title, responsible member when authorized, original due date, current status, upcoming automatic-release time when relevant, and an authorized deep link to inspect or act. Distinguish overdue-but-still-assigned, overdue-unassigned, pending review/rework and soon-to-be-auto-released cases without implying that every overdue chore needs action. Show counts and concise details; allow navigating to the full filtered view.
- **Avoid duplicate alerts:** Deduplicate by Home, recipient and summary day; summarize unchanged issues once in that day's digest, not repeatedly as individual alerts. If the same occurrence remains unresolved across days, it may appear in subsequent daily digests. A change after delivery appears in the next digest unless another existing, independently enabled notification policy applies.
- Respect Home membership and current role/permissions at delivery and opening time, and exclude inaccessible private data. No summary is sent after manager access is revoked. A recipient managing multiple Homes receives appropriately separated, context-labeled information without cross-Home disclosure.
- Summaries are informational: they do not automatically reassign, release, approve, skip, change deadlines or award points. Actual automatic release remains governed by the separately approved per-chore grace-period policy and is evaluated independently of digest delivery.
- Handle timezone/week rollover, daylight saving, no qualifying items, simultaneous state changes, disabled notifications, permission revocation and repeated scheduler attempts safely and idempotently.
- **Acceptance criteria:** opted-out manager gets no digest; opted-in manager gets at most one useful daily Home digest; quiet hours defer; overdue and approaching-release items are accurately grouped; unchanged items are not spammed within a day; no stale unauthorized details are exposed; no empty digest; summaries do not alter chores or reward balances.

### Confirmed automatic release of overdue self-claimed chores (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C:** Authorized Home chore managers may enable a **configurable automatic release after a grace period** for a chore occurrence that was voluntarily claimed from the unassigned pool and remains overdue and **unsubmitted**. Default to **disabled** until explicitly configured; define a nonnegative grace duration relative to the occurrence's existing due date in the Home timezone, and clearly preview the release time and policy in chore details and management settings. The grace period never silently changes the due date or late-reward rules.
- At the configured deadline, if the occurrence is still self-claimed, overdue, not submitted, not in Needs rework or pending approval, and otherwise eligible, automatically remove its assignee, preserve claim/release history with an **automatic release** reason and timestamp, and remove that member's potential assigned points. No points are awarded, deducted or transferred merely because of release. Notify the former claimant according to their notification settings and quiet hours; refresh all relevant chore, calendar and points views.
- The released occurrence returns to the appropriate **Overdue unassigned** pool **only if** its existing claimability/end-of-week expiry policy permits it. If the occurrence is no longer claimable under that policy, transition to the applicable closed/nonclaimable outcome instead of displaying an invalid Claim button. Do not restart the grace timer, shift the due date, or extend expiry. Any subsequent new eligible claim is a distinct recorded claim subject to the same configured policy and existing first-come-first-served checks.
- **Scope and safeguards:** Do not automatically release manager-assigned, rotated or transferred chores; submitted/pending-approval, Needs rework, completed, approved, skipped, cancelled or deleted occurrences; or future recurring occurrences merely because an earlier one is overdue. Existing manager reassignment/release workflows remain available. Warn that draft checklist progress and notes may be lost from the claimant's active view; keep private drafts inaccessible to future claimants while preserving permitted audit records.
- Evaluate release idempotently and atomically against current assignment origin, state, due date, grace policy, Home permissions and occurrence version. A completion submission, manual release, reassignment, schedule change, policy edit or claim race must have one consistent outcome, with no duplicate unassigned entry or duplicate notifications. For changes to grace settings or due dates, preview impact on currently overdue claimed occurrences and require explicit confirmation before applying a change that would cause immediate release; respect existing recurrence editing scopes.
- **Acceptance criteria:** disabled default; manager enables and changes grace duration; self-claimed unsubmitted chore released after deadline; completion submitted just before deadline is retained; pending approval and Needs rework excluded; manager-assigned chore excluded; overdue pool versus closed outcome follows existing expiry policy; former claimant's draft stays private; repeat scheduler execution is idempotent; policy/due-date changes with immediate-effect preview; timezone and DST boundaries; concurrency; notifications and quiet hours; point balance unchanged; recurring occurrence isolation and Home privacy.

### Confirmed no chore reservations (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Madeora does **not** offer temporary chore reservations, future-dated holds, waitlists, reservation expiry timers, or a separate reserved status for unassigned chores. Selecting **Claim / Assign to me** is an immediate, responsibility-bearing assignment of that chore occurrence, subject to existing eligibility, Home permissions, first-successful-claim concurrency and occurrence lifecycle rules.
- Members who intend to do a chore later may claim it now and remain responsible for its existing due date, reminders, lateness rules and reward conditions. Claiming does **not** reschedule the chore, postpone its due date, or guarantee it remains assigned if an authorized manager subsequently changes it.
- An eligible self-claimed chore can be released using the existing brief **Undo** or ordinary self-release workflow before submission, subject to current-state revalidation; releasing is not an expired reservation. Preserve claim/release history and update the unassigned pool, schedule, calendar and potential-point displays without awarding points.
- Recurring unassigned chores are claimed per occurrence only; claiming one does not reserve future occurrences. Do not introduce reservation controls in cards, filters, widgets, mobile or wall displays, templates, or Ora notifications.
- **Acceptance criteria:** claim is immediate; no Reserve or Schedule claim action exists; future-due chores retain their due date after claim; simultaneous claims have one winner; releasing safely returns only the eligible occurrence; no auto-expiration or phantom hold; pending/submitted/completed chores cannot be released through a reservation path; Home isolation, accessibility and points history remain correct.

### Confirmed instant chore claiming with brief Undo (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Selecting **Claim / Assign to me** on an eligible, published, unassigned chore occurrence commits the claim immediately, **without a blocking confirmation dialog**, and shows a brief, accessible success message with an **Undo** action. Show the chore's available points and applicable lateness/approval caveats in its card/details before the action; no points are earned merely by claiming.
- **Undo semantics:** Undo requests release of that same self-claimed occurrence back to the unassigned pool, using the already-approved self-release permission and validation rules. It is a **new checked action**, not a rollback that erases the original claim: retain claim and release timestamps/actors in history. Offer Undo only for a short, clearly defined UI window; after it disappears, the member can use the normal Release action if still eligible. Never guarantee Undo after another change has occurred.
- **Revalidate at Undo time:** Permit release only if the occurrence remains assigned to the same member, was voluntarily self-claimed, has not been submitted, completed, approved, skipped, deleted, reassigned or otherwise locked, and the member still has the relevant permission. Handle simultaneous manager changes, membership removal, stale versions, expiry and schedule changes with clear failure feedback and a refreshed view; never overwrite a newer assignment or duplicate an unassigned occurrence.
- **Consistency and feedback:** Update both Home chore layouts, unassigned pool, lists, eligible dashboard widgets, optional calendar and potential-point summaries after successful claim and Undo/release. Provide accessible success/failure announcements and keyboard/touch access; keep Undo usable without relying on hover or animation. Do not expose a claim or Undo control on read-only wall displays. Respect per-chore member eligibility, Home isolation, first-successful-claim concurrency, recurrence occurrence scope and notification preferences.
- **Specification reconciliation:** Earlier text saying to show points 'before confirmation' describes **pre-claim information**, not a required confirmation dialog; earlier references to manager approval of a claim do not apply to an already-approved, self-claimable occurrence. Existing manager-controlled assignment and separate completion approval remain unchanged.
- **Acceptance criteria:** one-tap successful claim with visible Undo; pre-claim point information; immediate dashboard and pool updates; Undo restores unassigned state without points; normal release after Undo expires; Undo rejected after submission, reassignment, permission loss or concurrent edit; exactly one winner for simultaneous claims; idempotent retries; recurrence isolation; screen reader, keyboard, mobile and wall-display behavior; reward ledger unchanged by claim/release.

### Confirmed immediate first-come-first-served claims for approved unassigned chores (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Once a proposed unassigned chore is approved and published (or an authorized manager directly publishes an unassigned occurrence), every **eligible** member of the selected Home may claim it immediately using **Claim / Assign to me**, on a **first-successful-claim, first-served** basis. **Do not introduce a second manager approval step for claiming an already-approved, claimable occurrence.** Proposal approval and optional approval of completed work remain distinct processes.
- **Clarification of earlier claim governance:** For an occurrence explicitly published as **claimable in the unassigned pool**, the Home's existing assignment and membership checks still determine who is eligible, but no generic manager-reassignment approval can be imposed on the self-claim action. A chore restricted to manager assignment or a named assignee is **not claimable** and must not be advertised with an active Claim button. Manager approval remains applicable to reassignment of *someone else's assigned chore* under its separately approved request flow. Do not silently expand a member's access or bypass a configured prohibition on self-claiming.
- Make the claim operation atomic, permission- and version-checked, and idempotent. For a single-assignee occurrence, only one concurrent claimant can succeed; others see **Already claimed** with updated assignee and availability. Reject stale, completed, skipped, submitted, deleted, inaccessible, or otherwise nonclaimable occurrences. Never award points merely for claiming; earning depends on actual completion and any required completion approval.
- On success, move the occurrence out of **Unassigned this week** into the claimant's scheduled chores, update both chore layouts and the optional weekly points widget, and notify relevant members according to existing preferences without disclosing private Home data. The claim applies only to the selected occurrence; future recurring rotations and other occurrences remain unchanged. Existing release, history, late-points and reward-ledger safeguards continue to apply.
- Acceptance criteria: eligible immediate claim with no manager queue; member-suggested chore approved then claimed; claim rejected for restricted member/chore; two simultaneous claims with exactly one winner; retry without double assignment; already-claimed feedback; points not awarded on claim; completion-approval remains separate; recurrence isolation; Home switch and membership revocation; responsive and wall-display privacy.

### Confirmed weekly unassigned chore pool and self-claiming (13 October 2026)
- **Approved user requirement:** The prominent Home chore dashboard includes a clearly labeled **Unassigned this week** pool alongside the member-by-day matrix and daily grouped cards.
