It shows eligible, currently unassigned chore occurrences scheduled for the selected Home-local week, with the chore title, scheduled day/due date, category where present, estimated duration where present, points available under the occurrence's snapshotted reward and late policy, and relevant completion/approval status. Keep unassigned chores visible and discoverable without falsely attributing them to a member. A selected-day view can filter to that day while offering an explicit route to the full weekly pool.
- **Self-claim action:** An eligible Home member may explicitly **Assign to me / Claim chore** from the unassigned pool, chore details or an authorized dashboard widget. This changes the assignee of that **individual occurrence** to the claiming member, moves it into their assigned view and removes it from the unassigned pool. Do not alter the recurring series, future rotation, or other occurrences. Display the expected points and any lateness/approval caveat before confirmation; claiming does **not** itself award points or guarantee an award.
- **Eligibility and governance:** Honor Home chore assignment permissions and any administrator-configured restriction on self-claiming; do not allow members to claim chores outside their current Home, hidden/inaccessible chores, skipped, completed, pending-approval or otherwise ineligible occurrences, or chores requiring a specific assignee or manager assignment. Where Home rules require manager authorization for a reassignment or claim, obtain it rather than silently bypassing the rule. Clearly distinguish claiming a genuinely unassigned occurrence from requesting reassignment of another member's chore; do not take an assigned chore without the established request/acceptance process.
- **Points and completion:** A member may voluntarily claim additional eligible chores beyond their original assignments to earn **more points by actually completing them**, subject to existing checklist requirements, due-date/late points policy, actual-contributor attribution, optional completion approval, and reward ledger safeguards. A claimed chore is not completed until the member completes/submits it; approval-required rewards remain pending until approval. Do not award points for claims, skips, cancelled claims or duplicate completions.
- **Availability and conflicts:** Prevent two members from successfully claiming the same single-assignee occurrence through an atomic, version-checked claim; show a clear already-claimed message and refresh all dashboards, calendar overlays and chore lists immediately. If an occurrence supports multiple assignees, respect its defined capacity and completion/points semantics rather than manufacturing extra rewards. Handle membership changes, simultaneous edits, absence conflicts, revoked permissions, and due-date/week changes explicitly.
- **Unclaiming:** Allow an authorized manager to return an eligible uncompleted claimed occurrence to the unassigned pool; an ordinary member may release their own **voluntarily self-claimed**, not-yet-submitted occurrence under the confirmed self-release policy. Record the claim/release actor and time in the relevant chore history without rewriting previous completed records or rotations. Revalidate eligibility and explain when a pending/completed occurrence cannot be released.
- **Presentation and acceptance:** Show the weekly pool in both matrix and grouped-card modes as a dedicated, accessible unassigned area, with a clear empty state, member/date/category/status filters that do not misleadingly hide unassigned work, and privacy-safe wall-display behavior (no public claiming). Test current-week/day navigation, recurring occurrences, points previews, claim → completion → approval, self-release, manager reassignment, concurrent claims, overdue reward adjustments, filtered views, mobile/keyboard access and strict cross-Home isolation.

### Confirmed default weekly Home chore overview (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** On opening the Home dashboard, the prominent chore overview defaults to the **current Home-local week**, with **today visibly highlighted**. This applies to both approved presentation layouts: member-by-day matrix and daily grouped cards. It is a default time range, not a requirement to use the matrix layout or to enable the separate Calendar module.
- Provide an explicit **Week / Day** view control. Switching to **Day** focuses on the selected day (initially today unless the member has deliberately selected another date); switching back to **Week** shows the week containing the selected day. Preserve the selected matrix/cards layout and all applicable member, category and status filters across switches. Keep date navigation and a clear **Today / This week** return action available.
- Show the current week according to the selected Home's configured timezone and week-start convention; highlight the current Home-local date, including when the viewer's device timezone differs. Handle daylight-saving transitions and midnight/week rollover correctly, without silently moving scheduled occurrences. Indicate dates clearly and distinguish overdue chores from chores scheduled for today or later.
- The overview must remain understandable when a member has no chores today, when a whole week is empty, when several chores or members share a day, and when some entries are hidden by filters or permissions. Never suggest filtered-out work is complete. Respect existing completion, approval, absence, reassignment and recurrence rules; day/week switches must not create or mutate chore occurrences.
- On compact screens, the weekly default may render as a readable day-focused slice with accessible navigation and a clear indication that the **week** is selected, rather than squeezing seven illegible columns into view. Daily mode is an explicit member choice; wall-display presentations must remain readable and privacy-safe. Changes in visible dates or filters must synchronize with the Chores module and optional Calendar overlay without creating duplicate records.
- Acceptance criteria: initial current-week selection; correct today highlight; Week ↔ Day switch in both matrix/cards layouts; selected-day preservation; previous/next week/day navigation; return-to-today action; Home timezone and week start; midnight/DST transitions; mobile and wall displays; empty/overdue/filtered states; Home switching and permissions; no recurrence or reward mutation.

### Confirmed selectable Home chore overview layouts (13 October 2026)
- **Approved combined options B and C:** The prominent Home dashboard chore overview offers **two user-selectable layouts**: **Member-by-day matrix** (Home members as rows, days of the selected week as columns, with assigned chores in each cell) and **Daily grouped cards** (each day presented as a section containing chores grouped by assigned member). Both are first-class presentation options for the same underlying chore occurrences; neither is a separate calendar or duplicated task data.
- Provide a clear, accessible **layout switcher** in the chore overview's widget settings and, where space permits, directly in the widget. Remember the selected layout per dashboard/widget and Home context, using a consistent default without forcing every member to use the same layout. Switching preserves the chosen week/date, member/category/status filters, permissions and any currently opened chore where feasible.
- Both layouts clearly identify **who does what on which day**, including unassigned chores, multiple assignees where supported, due/overdue, completed, skipped and pending-approval states. Provide day/week navigation, open-chore details and authorized quick completion/approval actions consistent with the approved chore widget policy. Do not imply a missing chore is completed merely because it is filtered out.
- **Responsive behavior:** On wider screens the matrix can display a full week with legible member rows; on narrower screens adapt through accessible day selection or bounded scrolling without tiny unreadable cells. Daily grouped cards should reflow naturally on mobile. Both layouts must work in light/dark themes and provide privacy-safe, large-format wall-display states. Use text/icons plus color, never color alone.
- This choice supplements the existing optional Chores week/month calendar and main Calendar **Show chores** toggle; it does not replace them. Calendar drag-and-drop scheduling remains governed by the approved permissions, impact previews and occurrence-versus-series safeguards, regardless of dashboard layout.
- Acceptance criteria: toggle matrix ↔ daily grouped cards; multiple members and days; empty/unassigned/overdue/pending states; filter and week preservation; distinct Home/widget settings; keyboard and touch navigation; narrow mobile and wall display; permissions and Home isolation; synchronized updates without duplicate occurrences or rewards.

### Confirmed interactive chore calendar and Home schedule overview (13 October 2026)
- **Approved customized option C:** Chores are a primary Home-dashboard feature and must provide a clear, at-a-glance **who-does-what-on-which-day** schedule. Provide an optional interactive chore calendar inside the Chores module and an independently configurable **Show chores** overlay in Madeora’s main Calendar. The Calendar module remains optional; Chores and its Home dashboard work fully without it. Calendar displays reference the original chore occurrences, never create duplicate events or reward records.
- The Home dashboard offers a prominent configurable **chore schedule overview**, with a useful default **week-by-day, member-by-member** presentation; users can distinguish each member’s assignments, unassigned chores, upcoming/due/overdue occurrences, pending approvals, completed and skipped occurrences at a glance. On narrow screens, provide an accessible day-focused/scrollable alternative; on wall displays, provide readable large-format, privacy-safe summaries. Use names and status text/icons as well as colors, never color alone. Respect Home timezone, week start and date boundaries.
- Within the Chores calendar, support **week and month views**, day detail, member and category filters, clear multi-assignee/rotation presentation where applicable, and opening an occurrence for its checklist, notes, approval state and other permitted details. The main Calendar overlay has a separate visible toggle and respects existing Calendar view/filter behavior and permissions. Neither view implies Google Calendar synchronization or exports chore occurrences to external calendars.
- Authorized managers may **drag an eligible individual occurrence to another date or eligible member** in the interactive Chores calendar, with keyboard/touch-accessible equivalent controls. Before committing, show the proposed old/new date and assignee, and any impacts on reminders, overdue status, reward timing, absence periods and pending requests; confirm meaningful changes. **Recurring series, future rotation order and other occurrences stay unchanged** unless separately edited through the already-approved explicit occurrence/series workflow. Do not silently move series or bypass existing recipient-request and manager-authorization rules.
- Validate permissions, eligibility, current occurrence version, dates, approval/completion state and Home boundaries on every interaction. Completed, approved, skipped, locked, pending-approval or otherwise ineligible occurrences are not silently draggable; explain restrictions and route to authorized workflows when available. Handle collisions, concurrent changes, canceled drags, failed writes, and undo only when safe. Recalculate visible schedule immediately after successful changes, and synchronize dashboard, Chores history and Calendar overlay.
- Acceptance criteria: week-by-member dashboard overview with multiple members and days; clear empty/overdue/pending states; week/month Chores calendar; main Calendar toggle on/off; Calendar module disabled; Home switch and cross-Home isolation; recurring occurrence versus series; rotation preserved; drag date/member with impact preview; touch/keyboard alternatives; permissions and self-approval boundaries; responsive and wall-display accessibility; timezone/DST; concurrent changes and failed actions. This does not introduce automatic workload balancing or bulk recurring-series editing.

### Confirmed custom Home chore categories (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each Home can maintain its own set of custom chore categories, each with a required unique name within that Home and a chosen accessible display color. An authorized chore manager may create, rename, recolor, reorder and retire categories. Categories are strictly Home-scoped: no cross-Home sharing or automatic copying.
- Each chore has **at most one category**, selected from its owning Home; leaving a chore uncategorized is valid. New chores, recurring series and chore templates may use an existing Home category where applicable, but moving a chore between Homes is not implied. An occurrence inherits its series category unless an authorized occurrence-scoped change is explicitly supported under the existing recurring-edit scope rules; preserve historical category labels/snapshots for completed occurrences.
- Provide consistent category display and filters in chore lists, individual and searchable Home-wide occurrence history, and configurable chore dashboard widgets. Include an **Uncategorized** filter, clear combined-filter behavior, and appropriate empty states. Color must never be the sole means of identification; names remain visible in light/dark and accessible wall-display views.
- Renaming or recoloring a category updates current authorized views without altering chore schedules, checklists, assignments, approval states or reward values. Retiring/deleting a category requires a preview of affected chores and a deliberate choice to remove their category association; never delete the chores or rewrite historical occurrence records. Prevent deletion when the action cannot be safely resolved.
- Revalidate Home membership and category-management permissions for writes and chore-visibility permissions for reads, including filters, counts, history, widgets, search, notifications and wall displays. Prevent category IDs from one Home being assigned to another Home's chores. Handle concurrent edits, stale selections and revoked access with clear errors and synchronized views.
- Acceptance criteria: create/rename/recolor category; duplicate and blank names; single-category selection and uncategorized chore; category filters across lists/history/widgets and combined filters; recurring and completed historical occurrences; delete with impact preview; unauthorized editor/viewer; cross-Home isolation; concurrent changes; mobile accessibility and color-independent indicators. No separate room/location taxonomy is introduced.

### Confirmed limited bulk chore occurrence management (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized Home chore managers may multi-select eligible **individual chore occurrences** and perform three bulk operations: reassign to an eligible Home member, explicitly skip selected occurrences, or change their due dates. No bulk modification of recurring series definitions, rotation rules, reward values, approval requirements or checklist templates is included.
- The selection UI identifies the currently selected Home, chosen occurrences and action eligibility. Before applying any change, show a **per-occurrence impact preview** with the original and proposed assignee, due date or skip status; clearly identify ineligible, already completed, pending-approval, locked, missing or otherwise conflicting occurrences and the reason. Require explicit confirmation; never silently apply an action to a whole series or another Home.
- Bulk reassignment uses existing Home permissions and eligibility checks and is a manager-initiated action, distinct from a member's recipient-acceptance request flow. Notify affected members as appropriate and preserve original attribution and history. Bulk skipping must not award points or erase existing completion, approval or audit records. Due-date changes follow Home-local timezone, date validation, existing reminder behavior and documented late-reward policy; show any consequences before confirmation.
- For recurring chores, changes apply only to the **selected occurrence(s)**, never future occurrences or the rotation sequence. Existing recurring edit scopes remain available in the individual chore editor. Do not allow bulk edits to retroactively alter approved/completed occurrence records or earned reward ledgers.
- Revalidate every selected occurrence, permission, Home membership and version at commit. Support safe partial results with clear success/failure counts and reasons, idempotent retries, conflict resolution and no double awards. Keep the searchable Home chore history and dashboard widgets synchronized, with accessible mobile multi-select, empty states and cancellation.
- Acceptance criteria: mixed eligible/ineligible selection; multi-member reassignment; skip and due-date changes; completed and pending-approval records; recurring occurrence isolation; overlapping edits; permission revocation; cross-Home isolation; DST/date-only dates; notification updates; reward correctness; partial failures and retries.

### Confirmed searchable Home-wide chore completion history (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Provide both an **individual chore occurrence history** and a **central searchable Home-wide chore history**. The central view is strictly scoped to the currently selected Home, never merges Homes, and shows only records the current member is authorized to view.
- Each occurrence entry identifies the chore, original scheduled/due date, assigned member(s) where permitted, actual completer (if any), reported completion timestamp, current outcome (upcoming/open, overdue/missed, skipped, submitted/pending approval, approved/completed, rejected, withdrawn or corrected as applicable), and approval decision, reviewer and decision timestamp when relevant. Distinguish *missed/overdue* from an explicitly skipped occurrence; do not invent a completed timestamp for unresolved occurrences.
- Provide text search by chore name and accessible record content, and combinable filters for **member, chore, date range, completion state and approval state**, with explicit filter chips, clear/reset controls, predictable sorting (newest relevant occurrence first by default), and meaningful empty/no-results states. Date filtering uses the Home's documented scheduling timezone and clearly indicates whether it filters scheduled date or completion date; default to scheduled date and offer a deliberate alternative when needed.
- Individual chore history links to the same underlying occurrence records as the Home-wide view. Opening an entry displays authorized checklist snapshots, completion notes, original due time, reassignment/absence/skip outcomes, and approval or reward details where the member has the corresponding permission; avoid duplicating or rewriting records when a chore is renamed, archived, rescheduled or deleted.
- History is read-oriented: it does not grant new approval, correction, reward-management or deletion permissions. Any available actions reuse the existing permission-checked workflows and preserve immutable ledger/audit requirements. Corrections and reversals remain traceable without counting a single completion or award twice; pending approvals never appear as approved completions or earned points.
- Enforce Home isolation and field-level permissions in search results, counts, exports if separately supported, deep links and wall displays. Recheck access after role changes, member removal and context switches; follow established retention and former-member attribution rules. Support pagination/large histories, accessible mobile filters, loading/error states, and concurrent updates without stale approval indicators.
- **Not included:** completion-rate analytics, workload comparisons, member rankings, trends or new reward summaries. Acceptance criteria cover filtered multi-member histories; overdue versus skipped; approval/rejection and late approval; reassignment; recurring series edits; correction/reversal; Home switching and permission revocation; long history; timezone/DST; and duplicate processing prevention.

### Confirmed reusable chore templates (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized Home members may create, name, edit, duplicate and retire reusable **chore templates** scoped to a single Home. Templates contain a title, optional instructions, ordered optional checklist steps with mandatory/optional flags, optional estimated duration, and optional **suggested** reward points. Templates are not live chores and cannot themselves be completed, assigned or rewarded.
- When creating a chore from a template, show an editable preview of the copied fields and require the creator to configure or confirm the actual chore's assignment, schedule or recurrence, approval requirements, and final reward points under existing Home rules. Never copy assignees, absence periods, pending approvals, completion records, or occurrence histories from a source chore. A suggested reward is not a grant and must pass the same permissions and validation as manually entered rewards.
- New chores created from a template are independent snapshots: editing, retiring or deleting the template never silently changes existing chores, occurrences, checklists, completion history or reward values. Checklist progress starts unchecked for each occurrence; preserve per-occurrence definitions, duration and reward snapshots according to existing rules. Templates do not create multi-chore routines, automatically assign members, or preconfigure recurring rotations.
- Template browsing and management respect the owning Home, Home role permissions, member removal and context switching. No automatic sharing or copying across Homes; unauthorized members cannot discover titles, content or reward suggestions. Provide useful empty, invalid, deleted and unavailable states and prevent duplicate creation on repeated submission.
- Acceptance criteria: create/edit/use/retire template; preview and override fields; optional versus mandatory checklist steps; missing duration or points; permission-controlled reward suggestions; independent chores after template edits; recurring checklist resets and historical snapshots; Home isolation; concurrent edits and retries; mobile and accessible controls.

### Confirmed optional per-occurrence chore completion notes (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When marking a chore occurrence complete or submitting it for required approval, the completing member may add an **optional short plain-text completion note** about the work performed or a relevant observation. Notes are not required for completion and are not a separate chat or discussion feature. Photo evidence and completion-photo attachments are not introduced by this decision.
- The note belongs to the **specific occurrence**, not the reusable chore definition or recurring series. It must not automatically appear on subsequent occurrences. Preserve notes with historical completion and approval records when recurring schedules, assignments, checklist definitions or estimated durations change.
- Display the note alongside the occurrence's completion state, actual completer and completion timestamp in authorized chore details/history and the approval review surface. Reviewers must be able to read the submitted note before approving or rejecting; a note does not replace mandatory checklist steps or approval.
- Provide an accessible optional input with a documented, reasonable character limit, whitespace normalization and clear validation. Permit submission with no note. Preserve a draft while the completion form remains open and show an error without losing the draft if submission fails.
- Only a member authorized to complete the occurrence may submit its completion note. Viewing requires current permission to see that Home chore occurrence; reviewers additionally need valid approval permission. Apply the same restrictions in dashboard widgets, notifications, wall displays, exports and search. Never leak note contents in unauthorized previews or notifications.
- Treat a submitted note as part of the occurrence's completion record. Corrections or reversal follow existing chore correction/approval permissions and preserve the prior submitted record as required for reward and security auditing; do not silently overwrite a note already relied upon for approval. Pending and completed records remain attributable to the actual submitter.
- Revalidate permissions and occurrence state on submission, approval and subsequent display; handle duplicate taps, simultaneous completion, reassignment, revoked membership, skipped occurrences and recurring occurrence boundaries without attaching a note to the wrong instance or awarding rewards twice.
- **Acceptance criteria:** completion with/without a note; required approval displaying the note; recurring next occurrence starts without the prior note; unauthorized viewer/reviewer cannot read it; checklist enforcement; rejection and correction; concurrent submission and retry; accessible mobile input and safe notification previews.

### Confirmed request-based chore reassignment (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A currently eligible Home member may request transfer of an assigned, outstanding chore occurrence to another eligible member of the same Home. The proposed recipient can explicitly **accept** or **decline**. A request alone never changes the assignment, completion state, reward recipient, approval state or rotation schedule.
- Provide a clear request flow showing chore, occurrence/date, existing assignee, proposed recipient, optional short explanation, and current request state (**Pending**, **Accepted**, **Declined**, **Cancelled**, **Invalidated**). Notify the recipient through their allowed channels and show the request in-app; allow the requester to cancel while pending. Prevent duplicate active requests for the same occurrence and confusing competing transfers.
- Acceptance must revalidate Home membership, chore visibility, recipient eligibility, current assignment, occurrence state, and any existing manager-only reassignment permissions. **Recipient acceptance does not override Home reassignment rules:** where manager authorization is required, route an accepted request for explicit authorized manager confirmation before effecting the transfer; expose this awaiting-confirmation state. A manager may independently reassign under existing permissions, which invalidates stale requests. Never silently grant reassignment rights to ordinary members.
- Apply the accepted and authorized transfer only to the **specific occurrence**, including recurring fixed-assignment and rotation chores. Preserve the original recurring series, cadence, rotation sequence, future assignees, due date, checklist progress and per-occurrence reward value. The new assignee becomes responsible for the outstanding occurrence; actual completion attribution and rewards continue to follow the established actual-contributor and approval rules.
- Restrict requests to eligible outstanding occurrences. Completed, skipped, deleted or pending-approval occurrences are not silently reassigned; use existing correction/approval rules where applicable. Handle overlapping absence periods without automatic acceptance or rejection, and display relevant absence conflicts to authorized decision-makers without disclosing private reasons.
- Make declines and cancellations non-destructive: original assignment remains. On member departure, permission revocation, concurrent completion, deletion or manager reassignment, invalidate pending requests with a comprehensible status and appropriate notification. Apply optimistic concurrency/idempotency so simultaneous accepts, manager actions and retries cannot cause double transfers or duplicate reward events.
- Respect Home-scoped privacy, notification preferences and wall-display restrictions; record the transfer decision, authorized actor and timestamps where required for operational/security or reward audits without introducing a general task activity timeline. Accessible mobile actions and explicit empty/error states are required.
- Acceptance criteria: request/accept/decline/cancel; manager approval where needed; invalid or unauthorized recipient; recurring rotation unaffected; fixed chores; checklist and duration preserved; absence overlap; stale requests after completion or member removal; simultaneous decisions; rewards and approval compatibility; notifications, privacy and mobile accessibility.

### Confirmed unassigned chore pool filtering and sorting (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** The Home unassigned chore pool offers optional filters for **category, due date/date range, reward points, and estimated duration**, plus sorting by **due date** or **points**. The initial/default view stays simple, showing available chores in chronological due-date order with overdue work clearly distinguished. Users can reset filters in one action.
- Filters operate on individual available chore occurrences within the selected Home and selected day/week or explicitly chosen overdue scope, never on a recurring series as a whole. Show active filter indicators and accurate matching/total counts; distinguish an empty pool from no matches under current filters. Preserve filters when switching between approved matrix and daily-card layouts where sensible, and make date scope explicit.
- Category filtering includes an **Uncategorized** option. Duration filtering supports **unspecified duration** explicitly rather than inventing a duration; point filtering uses the currently claimable reward preview under existing late-point rules, with a clear indication where final points depend on completion/approval timing. Sorting by points must specify ascending/descending direction and use a deterministic due-date/title tie-breaker.
- **Eligibility and privacy:** Respect general chore-claim permissions and optional per-chore member eligibility. Visible but ineligible chores may appear with a clear non-claimable state where existing Home visibility permissions allow; never expose private details or imply they can be claimed. Filtering/sorting cannot bypass eligibility, approval, absence, claim concurrency, or Home boundaries. Keep an accessible way to show only claimable chores.
- **Claim safety:** Filters and sort order are presentation only; claiming always revalidates the current occurrence's availability, eligibility, reward preview, and authorization atomically. If another member claims it or the occurrence changes, update the list and explain the failure without stale success or double awards.
- **Consistent surfaces:** Apply this to the dedicated unassigned pool in the Home chore dashboard's matrix and grouped-card views and the equivalent chore list/pool access where available; do not impose these filters on unrelated widgets or automatically recommend chores. On narrow screens use compact accessible filter controls; wall displays remain read-only and privacy-safe.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Filter by each supported field and combinations; clear/reset filters; sort due date and points both directions; category/unspecified-duration cases; no-match versus empty pool; overdue and week/day scope; eligible/ineligible members; concurrent claims and stale points; recurring occurrence isolation; responsive keyboard/touch operation; Home switching and permission changes.

### Confirmed no chore difficulty levels (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Chores have **no separate difficulty level** field, rating, badge, category, or Easy/Moderate/Difficult scale. Chore managers and members use the existing optional estimated duration, instructions, mandatory/optional checklist steps, and visible reward points to understand the work involved.
- Do not offer difficulty filters, difficulty-based assignment or claiming restrictions, difficulty-driven point suggestions, or automatic point modifiers. A chore's existing category is organizational, not a proxy for difficulty.
- Keep this rule consistent across chore creation/editing, reusable templates, suggestions and approvals, the unassigned pool, lists, member-by-day matrix, daily cards, calendars, history, dashboard widgets, and mobile/wall displays. Do not add difficulty fields implicitly through imports or defaults.
- Reward values remain manager-controlled and equal in base value for all eligible members for the same occurrence, subject to already-approved completion, approval and lateness policies. Estimated duration remains informational and must not drive rewards or assignment automatically.
- Acceptance criteria: create/edit/view/claim a chore without any difficulty control; duration and instructions are optional and displayed when present; categories and points remain independent; filtering and templates contain no difficulty setting; existing chores remain valid without migration or fabricated difficulty values.

### Confirmed optional chore estimated duration (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized Home chore managers may optionally specify a positive estimated duration for a chore, entered in minutes or hours and stored/displayed consistently as a duration rather than a clock time. Leaving it blank means **no estimate**, not zero minutes. Validate sensible positive values and display an accessible, localized minutes/hours format without implying precision beyond the supplied estimate.
- Show the estimate in chore details, editing and applicable chore list/dashboard widgets, including My Chores, All Home Chores and Pending Approvals where the member can access that chore. Keep compact widgets readable, with a clear distinction between estimate and due time; do not leak durations or existence of restricted chores on wall displays.
- For recurring chores, define the estimate at series level and apply established **This occurrence**, **This and following occurrences**, and **Entire series** edit scopes when relevant. Preserve the estimate associated with historical completed and pending-approval occurrences; changes must not silently rewrite history or override an occurrence-specific estimate.
- Estimated duration is **informational only**. It does not automatically adjust reward points, on-time/late reward calculation, due dates, assignments, chore rotations, workload distribution, approval rules, checklists, reminders, or availability decisions. No actual-time tracking, timesheets, automatic effort balancing or workload analytics are introduced by this choice.
- Require appropriate edit permissions and concurrency validation. If a member loses access, hide the estimate along with the chore. Handle missing estimates, duration edits during an active occurrence, invalid values, timezone-independent formatting, and narrow mobile layouts.
- Acceptance criteria: absent and present estimates; minutes/hours input and display; permission-controlled editing; details and filtered widgets; recurring occurrence versus series edits; historical preservation; no side effects on rewards, approvals, assignments or rotation; wall-display privacy and accessible mobile presentation.

### Confirmed optional recurring chore checklists (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized chore creators/managers may attach an optional reusable, ordered checklist of steps to a one-off or recurring Home chore. Each step has a short label and may be marked **mandatory** or optional; a chore without a checklist retains its existing completion workflow. Provide accessible controls to add, rename, reorder, toggle mandatory status and remove steps, with clear validation for blank or duplicate/confusing entries.
- Checklist definitions belong to the chore's owning Home and are visible only to members permitted to view that chore. For recurring chores, **each occurrence receives its own independent checklist progress**, initially unchecked; completing an earlier occurrence never prechecks the next one. Preserve the exact checklist definition used for completed and pending-approval occurrences, including during later template changes.
- Members with permission to work on an occurrence can tick/untick steps and see progress. **All mandatory steps must be checked before submitting or finalizing that occurrence as complete**, including via dashboard widgets, bulk actions and any other quick-complete surface; optional steps never block completion. The existing optional approval workflow still applies after checklist validation, and points are finalized only according to that workflow.
- When a recurring chore checklist is edited, use the established recurring-edit scopes (**This occurrence**, **This and following occurrences**, **Entire series**) where applicable; preview effects on in-progress occurrences, do not silently erase recorded step progress, and preserve completed historical snapshots. Explicitly resolve removed or newly mandatory steps on outstanding occurrences before saving; do not retroactively invalidate already approved completions or rewards.
- Skipped occurrences do not require completed steps and earn no rewards. Rejected approval submissions return to an editable outstanding occurrence with its existing checklist progress retained for correction. Reassignment preserves the occurrence's checklist and progress while enforcing new member permissions. Checklists do not independently create reward events or alter rotation schedules, recurrence, due dates or assignment.
- Revalidate permissions and occurrence state on each step update and completion; handle concurrent step edits, stale/offline clients, retries, removed members, archived/deleted chores and inaccessible Homes without lost updates, duplicate approvals or double points. Offer clear unchecked/partial/ready/pending/complete states and responsive accessible controls, and suppress private details on wall displays.
- Acceptance criteria: no checklist; optional versus mandatory steps; one-off and recurring reset; independent overlapping/overdue occurrences; quick completion blocked by unchecked mandatory steps; pending approval and rejection; checklist edits by recurrence scope; completion-history preservation; reassignment and skips; permissions, concurrency and responsive/wall-display privacy.

### Confirmed recurring chore pauses and Home vacation periods (13 October 2026)
- **Approved customized option B:** Authorized chore managers can pause an **individual recurring chore** until a selected date or indefinitely, and explicitly resume it. In addition, authorized Home managers can define a **Home vacation period with an inclusive start and end date** when nobody is home, temporarily pausing **all Home chore scheduling** within that date range. This is a distinct Home-wide vacation exception, not unrestricted permanent Home-wide pause controls.
- For an individual recurring pause, suppress generation of scheduled occurrences inside the paused interval; do not backfill those occurrences on resume. Preserve the original series cadence, rotation position rules, configuration, past occurrences and reward history. A scheduled end automatically resumes normal future generation; indefinite pauses require explicit resume. Explain inclusive dates, Home timezone and next expected occurrence.
- For a Home vacation, show an impact preview of all affected one-off and recurring chore occurrences, including assigned and unassigned work, rotation, reminders, due dates and any automatic overdue release timers. On confirmation, **suspend chore scheduling and related reminders for the vacation dates**, and prevent paused occurrences from being treated as missed, overdue, claimable or point-earning. Recurring occurrences falling entirely in the interval are skipped for scheduling, not shifted or generated later; preserve cadence and future rotation order. Do not silently delete or modify existing completed, approved, submitted, Needs rework or historically rewarded records.
- Existing outstanding occurrences overlapping the period require a clearly previewed, safe vacation exception treatment; preserve their record and attribution, and do not automatically reject submissions, reverse points, or release assignments. A chore already overdue before vacation is not automatically forgiven: show it for manager review rather than silently rewriting its original deadline. For one-time chores, require the vacation-creation review defined below (Keep original date, Reschedule or Skip), allowing flagged deferred decisions; never silently shift dates or lose occurrences.
- At the end of the vacation, normal future scheduling resumes automatically; do not flood the Home with backfilled occurrences, overdue notifications or catch-up rewards. Authorized managers can edit/cancel a vacation with a preview; preserve already-made explicit decisions and require review of newly affected occurrences. Handle overlapping vacations and individual pauses consistently (the stricter pause remains in force), Home timezone/DST boundaries, changed membership and concurrency.
- Distinguish **member-specific temporary absences** from a **whole-Home vacation**: individual absences continue to use the existing explicit keep/skip/reassign workflow, whereas a confirmed nobody-home vacation applies a Home-wide scheduling pause. Surface the vacation state in calendars, chore dashboards, unassigned pools and wall displays without exposing optional private travel details. Permission-check all actions and provide accessible notifications of material schedule changes according to preferences.
- Acceptance criteria: individual pause with end date and indefinite pause; resume; vacation across two inclusive dates; overlapping personal absences and series pauses; one-off versus recurring work; pending approval and Needs rework protected; outstanding overdue before start; automatic overdue release suspended appropriately; no phantom late penalties, points or backfill; preserved rotation/history; timezone and DST; vacation edit/cancel; authorized manager only; mobile and wall-display privacy.

### Confirmed member-scoped vacations and participant approval (13 October 2026)
- **Approved customized option B — authoritative clarification:** Vacations are not exclusively whole-Home events. Every vacation has an explicit **participant set** of one or more Home members. Members may create and manage vacations **for themselves** (including a school trip); authorized Home managers may create and manage vacations for **any selected subset or all Home members**. This supersedes older statements that only managers can create vacations or that every vacation automatically pauses all Home chores. A vacation affecting all current Home members is a **whole-Home vacation**; partial-participant vacations are **member-scoped absences**, not Home-wide scheduling shutdowns.
- A regular member creating a vacation is included as its participant automatically and can edit/cancel their own participation and permitted dates. They may **suggest** additional Home members, but suggestions remain **Pending acceptance** per invitee; only each invited member's explicit acceptance adds that person as a participant. Declining or ignoring an invitation does not affect that person's routines. A member cannot silently enroll, pause routines for, or act as another member. Managers can directly select participants using their Home management authority, with visible attribution and permission checks. Respect established child/member permission policies and guardian-managed accounts where applicable; do not assume a child can override guardian controls.
- The vacation's overall **Upcoming / Active / Ended / Cancelled** status remains derived from dates as previously approved; invitations have **separate per-member Pending / Accepted / Declined / Withdrawn** states, never masquerading as vacation status. A member-created vacation for oneself becomes effective for that member immediately; suggested others have no effect until accepted. Do not require manager approval merely to record one's own vacation or to send participant suggestions. Invitations are actionable requests and may notify the invitee under existing request-notification preferences; this is distinct from the previously prohibited broadcast notifications for vacation schedule changes.
- **Scoped scheduling:** For a partial vacation, apply vacation-related chore absence handling only to participating members and their assigned or rotating occurrences; preview conflicts and let authorized managers keep, skip or reassign affected work using existing absence workflows. Other members' chores, shared Home habits and meal planning continue normally. A vacation with **all Home members participating** can apply the existing whole-Home chore pause and optional shared-habit / meal-planning pause settings, with the established one-time-chore review, previews and overlap rules. If an invitation is accepted later and thereby makes participation whole-Home, require authorized manager confirmation and impact review **before** enabling Home-wide pauses; until then, keep participant-scoped behavior. If participants withdraw or membership changes, recalculate scope prospectively with preview, without silently disrupting others' schedules.
- Multiple vacations may overlap. Calculate per-member absences independently, and apply Home-wide module pauses only for confirmed whole-Home pause intervals; do not combine two separate partial vacations into a Home-wide pause merely because their participant sets collectively cover everyone unless a manager explicitly confirms the whole-Home effect. Keep each vacation's dates, optional name, participant roster, invite responses, author, decisions and history separate. No retroactive skipped-occurrence creation, points changes, penalty, automatic release or duplicate reminders.
- Calendar banners and the optional dashboard vacation widget show permitted participant scope (e.g. **My school trip**, **3 members away**, **Whole Home away**) without exposing private trip names or other members' details on wall/shared displays. Home managers may inspect participation and affected schedules according to permissions. Existing silence rule continues for routine vacation edits; actionable invitations and existing opted-in pre-vacation manager reminders are distinct.
- Validation and edge cases: inclusive valid start/end dates, Home-local timezone, membership changes, removed members, self-withdrawal, late acceptance, declined/expired invitations, manager-added participants, cancellation, concurrent acceptance and edits, overlap, permissions, accessible invitation decisions, responsive/mobile and wall privacy. Preserve previously finalized work, history and rewards. Acceptance criteria include self-only child school trip; manager-selected subgroup; member suggesting another member who accepts or declines; invite pending with no effect; all-member transition requiring review; overlapping partial and whole-Home vacations; withdrawal; no unauthorized pause of nonparticipants.

### Confirmed expiration of unanswered vacation invitations (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A suggested participant invitation that remains **Pending** automatically becomes **Expired** at the beginning of the invitee's **proposed individual vacation start date**, using the Home's local timezone. An unanswered invitation does not enroll the invitee, mark them absent, alter their chores or reminders, or count toward a whole-Home vacation pause. This invitation status is separate from the vacation's Upcoming / Active / Ended / Cancelled status.
- Show the proposed date and clear response deadline when inviting and in the recipient's pending-invitations view. At expiration, remove Accept/Decline actions and show Expired with the original proposer, proposed dates and timestamps in permission-aware invitation history. Do not silently treat expiration as acceptance or a rejection by the member; no vacation-change broadcast is generated.
- A creator or authorized manager may issue a **new invitation** with a valid future proposed start date when participation is still relevant; never reactivate an expired invitation or backdate an accepted absence. A re-invitation needs fresh explicit acceptance and its own audit record. If the group vacation has already ended, require a separate future vacation instead of reviving the expired invitation.
- Handle simultaneous acceptance and expiration atomically: an acceptance confirmed **before** the start-date boundary is effective, while a stale response at or after the boundary fails with a clear refresh/reinvite message. Declined, withdrawn, cancelled or superseded invitations are not converted to Expired. Changes to proposed dates require a new or versioned pending proposal and a clearly updated deadline, without silently extending an old invitation.
- Recalculate participant scope and whole-Home pause eligibility only after a valid acceptance; preserve existing participants' effective dates, finalized chores, points and history. Respect guardian-managed account rules, access permissions, accessible responsive controls and wall-display privacy.
- Acceptance criteria: pending invite expires at proposed start in Home timezone; invitee remains unaffected; early acceptance works; boundary race rejects stale acceptance; late re-invitation uses a new future date and consent; decline/cancel/supersede remain distinct; multiple participants and staggered dates; no unapproved Home-wide pause or retroactive reward changes.

### Confirmed individual participant vacation date ranges (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A group vacation has an overall inclusive start/end date range, but each accepted participant may have an **individual inclusive start and end date** within that group interval. This explicitly supports staggered arrivals and early departures without creating separate vacations. The group range is the envelope of effective participant dates, not a claim that everyone is away for the entire span; update it consistently when effective dates change, while clearly distinguishing pending proposed dates from accepted dates.
- Apply personal absence and chore scheduling effects **only for each member's effective individual dates**. A partial group vacation never pauses chores, habits or meal planning for members who are at Home. Whole-Home pauses are effective only for intervals when all current Home members are confirmed absent simultaneously **and** an authorized manager has confirmed the Home-wide pause and module settings after an impact preview; staggered participation cannot silently produce a Home-wide pause across the entire group envelope. Preserve overlapping-vacation rules and independent per-member absences.
- The creator or authorized manager may propose individual dates when inviting participants. Each invited member sees their own proposed range and personal scheduling impact before accepting. Later changes to an invited member's accepted dates follow the already-approved **per-participant date-change acceptance** process; unaccepted proposals do not change effective dates. Managers' directly managed participant actions remain subject to established authority and child/guardian restrictions. Members may edit their own permitted dates without silently changing anyone else's.
- Calendar and vacation widget may display the overall vacation span, but participant details and personal chore views must show the **actual per-member dates**; clearly label dates for viewers with permission and avoid exposing other members' private absence details on wall/public displays. Keep the vacation's status derived from its effective overall dates and invitation states separate. A participant whose dates have ended must not appear currently away merely because another participant remains away.
- Validate inclusive ranges, start <= end, Home timezone and DST, pending versus effective dates, partial acceptance, changes that extend the group envelope, early returns, withdrawal, cancellation, overlap, concurrent edits and changed Home membership. Show impact previews before consequential edits; preserve previously completed/approved work, earned points, history and skipped past occurrences; never backfill missed chores or duplicate pause effects.
- Acceptance criteria: four members with staggered departure/return dates; one member away while others' chores continue; invited member accepts their own date range; one member declines a date extension; overall calendar span remains accurate; Home-wide pauses apply only during confirmed full-absence overlap; no historical point or scheduling changes; accessible mobile and privacy-safe wall display.

### Confirmed participant approval for shared vacation date changes (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When the creator or an authorized manager proposes changing the start or end dates of a vacation shared with invited participants, the change is a **proposal per invited participant**, not an immediate rewrite of every participant’s schedule. Each invited participant must explicitly accept the revised inclusive dates before those dates affect their own participation, chores, reminders or other eligible routines. Until acceptance, their previously accepted dates and corresponding scheduling effects remain in force; declining or not responding preserves those dates. Respect guardian/child permission policies for accepting on behalf of a managed child.
- Show the proposing actor, old and proposed dates, and a clear personal scheduling impact preview before each acceptance. The creator's own dates may update upon their confirmed edit, but no one may use that action to silently change another invited member's dates. Manager-directly-added participants remain subject to existing manager authority and permission rules; do not falsely imply every manager-controlled change requires a participant invitation. Display differing effective dates per participant accurately, rather than presenting one misleading group-wide interval.
- A participant may accept or decline the proposed change independently. Track proposal version, proposer, recipients, decisions and timestamps; superseded proposals cannot be accepted accidentally. Handle partial acceptance, edits during an active vacation, early returns, concurrent responses, withdrawals, membership changes, overlapping vacations, cancellation, and stale requests. If the participant withdraws, cancel their pending change request without affecting others.
- Recalculate per-member pauses and eligible chore occurrences only after that member's effective dates change, with the established impact review, no historical backfill, no changes to finalized points or completed work, and no interruption to unrelated members. Whole-Home pause settings must not silently expand based on an unaccepted member date change; if participant changes alter whether everyone is away, require the existing authorized manager review of Home-wide effects.
- Pending change proposals are visible to their intended recipients and authorized managers, with accessible Accept/Decline actions and privacy-aware calendar/widget status. Follow established invitation notification preferences and the confirmed silent vacation-update policy: do not broadcast ordinary date edits as announcements. Acceptance criteria: two invitees independently accept/decline; old dates remain for a nonresponder; a proposal during vacation; creator changes only their own dates; manager-authorized adjustments; proposal superseded or withdrawn; overlap and all-member status recalculated without unintended pauses or reward changes.

### Confirmed member withdrawal from group vacations (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A participating Home member may withdraw **themselves** from a group vacation, including one created by a manager, without requiring the creator to remove them. Withdrawal never removes other participants, cancels their vacation, or grants the member permission to edit manager-controlled Home-wide pause settings. Respect established guardian/child account permissions when self-service actions are restricted.
- Before confirming withdrawal, show a clear **personal scheduling impact preview**: dates and participation being removed; their own assigned, rotating and unassigned-eligibility-related chores that may resume or need review; any remaining overlapping absences; and whether the withdrawal means the vacation no longer covers the whole Home. Explain that previously skipped occurrences, earned points and historical decisions are not restored or rewritten.
- After confirmation, mark that person's invitation/participation **Withdrawn**, preserve who withdrew and when in permission-controlled history, and remove them from the effective participant set prospectively. Keep the vacation active for remaining participants; if none remain, end its scheduling effect and flag the empty-participant vacation for authorized management rather than inventing a new status.
- If withdrawal breaks an existing **confirmed whole-Home pause**, recalculate its applicability immediately for future dates: do not continue to suspend every member's chores or shared habits/meal planning merely because the vacation was originally whole-Home. Show the manager any newly affected household schedules and require authorized review of manager-controlled keep/skip/reassign decisions. Do not silently unskip past work, change other members' assignments, or let a member override explicit manager scheduling decisions.
- Other confirmed vacations and individual chore pauses continue independently. Handle withdrawals before, during or after the vacation, concurrent participant changes, stale client actions, removal from the Home, and a withdrawal that would leave no participants; idempotent retries must not duplicate schedule effects. A withdrawn member may be invited again only through the established acceptance/management permissions, not automatically rejoin.
- Keep the previously approved **silent vacation-change** behavior: update calendar banners, vacation widget and affected views without a broadcast; show a local confirmation to the withdrawing member. Existing actionable requests and necessary manager review surfaces remain available, subject to privacy and notification rules.
- Acceptance criteria: withdraw from member-created and manager-created groups; preview impacts; preserve others' participation; partial-to-non-whole-Home transition; overlapping vacations; manager-controlled scheduling preserved; empty roster; history and reward integrity; guardian controls; concurrent changes; responsive and accessible UI.

### Confirmed automatic Home vacation statuses (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Every Home vacation displays a clear, automatically derived status: **Upcoming** before its inclusive start date, **Active** throughout its inclusive start/end dates, **Ended** after its end date, or **Cancelled** if explicitly cancelled by an authorized manager. No manual status picker or separate activation/ending action is required.
- Evaluate Upcoming, Active and Ended against the **Home-local current calendar date** (not the viewer's device timezone); cancellation takes precedence over date-derived states and never reactivates automatically. A vacation ending early recalculates its status and effective module pauses according to the confirmed impact-preview workflow.
- Show statuses consistently in Home vacation management, calendar banners and details, the optional dashboard vacation widget, and relevant pause summaries. Use readable labels rather than color alone; distinguish separate overlapping vacations and the resulting combined pause timeline. The widget prioritizes Active then Upcoming vacations and does not present Ended or Cancelled periods as forthcoming.
- Cancelled vacations stop contributing future module pauses and pre-vacation reminders but remain available in permission-controlled history with their dates, settings, cancellation attribution and previous decisions. Ended vacations likewise retain history. Neither status change silently deletes records, alters previously finalized rewards, backfills missed occurrences, or sends dedicated vacation-change notifications.
- Managers still explicitly confirm creation, edits and cancellation with their established previews; the resulting status then updates automatically. Respect permissions, Home isolation, wall-display privacy, accessibility, timezone/DST boundaries, stale clients and concurrent updates.
- Acceptance criteria: future/first-day/last-day/past vacation; cancellation before or during a vacation; shortening and early ending; two overlapping vacations with independent statuses and combined pauses; Home timezone differing from device timezone; historical visibility, silent updates and no duplicate scheduling or notifications.

### Confirmed silent Home vacation change updates (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Creating, editing, shortening, ending or cancelling a Home vacation updates authorized calendar banners, vacation widgets, relevant chore/habit/meal-plan views, pause indicators and management history **without sending a dedicated vacation-change announcement or notification** to Home members, including members whose routines are affected. Do not introduce an all-Home announcement or targeted vacation-change alert.
- This decision supersedes earlier generic wording suggesting notifications for material vacation schedule changes. Existing **separately triggered** notifications remain governed by their own approved rules: explicitly reassigned/skipped chores, individually configured chore reminders when not paused, optional manager pre-vacation reminders, and essential pet-care/task notifications. Do not disguise vacation-change notifications as routine alerts or duplicate them through overlapping vacation updates.
- Provide immediate, accessible in-product feedback to the manager performing the change (confirmation, impact preview, errors), and reflect the changed state when other members next view relevant surfaces; this is not a push/inbox vacation-change notification. Respect privacy, permissions, quiet hours for unrelated notifications, Home timezone, concurrency, and audit history.
- Acceptance criteria: create/edit/cancel/early-end a vacation affecting one or multiple members without dedicated member notifications; refresh authorized calendar/widgets/module views; preserve opted-in pre-vacation reminders and unrelated critical pet-care notifications; overlapping vacations do not cause duplicate alerts.

### Confirmed early ending of Home vacations with impact preview (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When an authorized Home manager shortens an active or future vacation, including ending it immediately, show a **before-confirmation impact preview** identifying the original and new inclusive end dates, effective paused modules, affected chores, shared Home habits, meal planning, reminders and the next scheduled occurrences. The manager must confirm before the change takes effect; an unauthorized member cannot shorten or end a vacation.
- Recalculate the effective pause timeline for each module and date, accounting for **overlapping Home vacations** and independent recurring-chore pauses. Resume a module only on dates no longer covered by any applicable pause; do not imply that ending one vacation overrides another. Clearly identify routines that will remain paused.
- After confirmation, resume **future** scheduling on the original cadence, including eligible chore occurrences, shared Home habits and meal-planning routines, from the newly active dates onward. **Do not backfill** occurrences skipped while the vacation was active, retroactively mark habits missed, create catch-up meal entries, alter historical reward totals, or shift recurring rotations merely because the vacation was shortened.
- Preserve completed, approved, pending-approval, Needs rework and historical records, plus explicit Keep/Reschedule/Skip decisions already made for one-time chores. Preview one-time chores newly outside the vacation and require manager review of any unresolved decisions; do not silently restore a deliberately skipped chore or rewrite an explicitly rescheduled date. Avoid duplicate notifications or auto-release actions on resume; apply existing overdue/release rules prospectively and show any outstanding pre-vacation issues for review.
- Make the calendar vacation banner, dashboard vacation widget, module pause indicators and authorized history reflect the revised end date promptly. Respect Home-local inclusive date boundaries, timezone/DST, concurrency and idempotent retries; notification preferences, quiet hours and wall-display privacy remain in force.
- Acceptance criteria: shorten a future vacation; end an active vacation today; preview and confirm resumed modules and dates; overlapping vacations keeping one module paused; indefinite chore pause unaffected; no backfilled chores/habits/meals; preserved history, points, rotations and explicit one-time decisions; stale manager edit conflict; mobile and accessible preview; privacy-safe updates.

### Confirmed overlapping Home vacations with combined module pauses (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized Home managers may create separate Home vacation periods whose inclusive date ranges partially or fully overlap. Preserve each vacation as its own editable, cancellable, non-recurring record with its own dates, author, selected module pauses and audit history; do not automatically merge or reject overlapping periods.
- **Combined pause rule:** For each Home-local calendar date and module, pause the module whenever **at least one active, confirmed vacation** covering that date pauses it. Chores are paused by every Home vacation; shared Home habits and Home meal planning are paused only when at least one overlapping vacation has explicitly enabled that module. A module resumes only after the **last applicable pause** ends or is cancelled, respecting independent individual chore pauses. Never allow an unselected switch in one vacation to override a selected switch in another.
- On create, edit, shorten, extend or cancel, preview the **effective combined timeline** by date and module, including overlapping sources, earliest possible resumption, affected recurring schedules, reminders, one-time chore decisions and protected submissions. Explain that removing one vacation may not resume a module while another pause remains. Require authorized confirmation of material changes and re-review newly affected one-time chores; preserve earlier explicit Keep/Reschedule/Skip decisions unless separately changed.
- Apply suppression and recovery **once per affected occurrence**, not once per overlapping vacation: no duplicate skips, notifications, auto-release timer changes, reward adjustments, backfilled chores, missed-habit penalties or meal-plan generation. Previously completed/approved/pending/rework work and existing points stay protected. Respect Home timezone and inclusive date boundaries, concurrency, idempotent retries, member permissions, notification settings and wall-display privacy.
- Acceptance criteria: partially and fully overlapping ranges; differing habits/meal switches; a module remaining paused when the first vacation ends; one vacation edited/cancelled while another continues; a separate indefinite recurring chore pause; overlapping one-time chore review without duplicate decisions; DST, cross-month dates and concurrent manager edits; no duplicate rewards, missed penalties or reminders.

### Confirmed optional pre-vacation manager reminders (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized Home managers may opt into a pre-vacation reminder for each Home vacation. This is optional and off by default; a manager can configure how many calendar days before the vacation start it should be delivered, including disabling it for an individual vacation. Do not introduce a mandatory notification or a separate vacation-preparation checklist.
- At the scheduled reminder time, summarize actionable **outstanding chore decisions** (including one-time chores needing keep/reschedule/skip review and existing claimed chores requiring attention), upcoming pause settings, and relevant household arrangements that can be identified from accessible Madeora records. Pet-care schedules remain active; surface relevant upcoming pet-care arrangements without assuming that pet care is paused or that care has been arranged.
- Show only current, actionable items. If a previously identified item has been resolved, omit it. Include direct links to the vacation review and permitted relevant records. Do not invent preparations, disclose private member details, or expose information from another Home or personal space.
- Respect each recipient manager's notification channel preferences, quiet hours, permissions and muted settings. Avoid duplicate reminders for the same manager and vacation at the same configured lead time; if dates/settings change, recalculate pending reminders without replaying already-delivered duplicates. For overlapping vacations, keep their identity and reminder preferences separate while avoiding redundant issue descriptions where possible.
- Reminders are informational: they do not silently reschedule, skip, approve, release, complete or otherwise modify chores, habits, meals, tasks, goals or pet care. A vacation cancelled before delivery produces no reminder; past-start vacations do not generate retroactive reminders.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Manager can enable/disable and configure lead days; a timely reminder contains accurate outstanding decisions and relevant arrangements; resolved items disappear; quiet hours and permissions are honored; edits/cancellations prevent stale or duplicate notices; other Home members do not receive manager-only details.

### Confirmed optional Home vacation names (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When creating or editing a Home vacation, authorized Home managers may enter an **optional descriptive name** (for example, “Summer holiday” or “Weekend away”). The name is display metadata only: it does not change the inclusive date range, Home-wide chore pause, optional module switches, overlap rules, eligibility, reminders, or permissions.
- If the name is empty or cleared, show the localized generic label **“Home vacation”** everywhere; do not require a name or infer one from dates, destinations, participants or other private information. Trim surrounding whitespace, validate a reasonable maximum length, and reject empty-after-trimming input as equivalent to no name. Treat the name as plain text, safely escaped in all surfaces.
- Use the same permitted name consistently in Home vacation settings, impact previews, the main calendar's multi-day banners, vacation dashboard widget, permission-aware detail views and relevant history. When multiple vacations overlap, preserve distinct names and records; show generic fallback labels where needed, along with dates to distinguish them.
- **Privacy:** A custom name may reveal travel or absence details. Apply existing Home-level access checks, and show a **generic “Home vacation” label** on wall/shared/public displays or any surface whose audience cannot be verified. Do not expose custom names through notifications, previews or calendar integrations beyond their authorized audience; do not introduce destination, description or itinerary fields.
- Changing a name is a metadata edit with normal manager authorization and change history, but does not regenerate chore occurrences, reopen one-time chore reviews, alter points or emit schedule-change notifications. Date/module edits still use the existing impact review and confirmation flow.
- Acceptance criteria: unnamed and named vacations; rename and clear name; trimmed/overlong text and safe rendering; generic fallback; calendar/widget consistency; overlapping named vacations; unauthorized edit blocked; wall-display privacy; no scheduling or reward changes on rename.

### Confirmed optional Home vacation dashboard widget (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Offer an optional, compact **Home vacation** widget in the Home dashboard widget catalog; do not add it to every dashboard automatically. Members with Home dashboard customization permission may add, remove, resize and arrange it under existing widget rules. Do not introduce a separate vacation dashboard or override existing layouts.
- Show the **next upcoming confirmed vacation** with its Home-local inclusive start/end dates and a concise, permission-safe label. While a vacation is underway, prioritize the **currently active vacation** and indicate that it is in progress; after it ends, advance to the next upcoming period. If none is active or upcoming, show a calm empty state rather than stale dates. Historical and cancelled vacations are not presented as upcoming.
- Display the **effective modules affected** during that vacation: chores always paused; shared Home habits and meal planning only when selected. Where vacations overlap, explain combined effective pauses and dates clearly rather than incorrectly implying all modules resume with the displayed vacation. Keep the widget compact, with a detail affordance for more complex overlaps; avoid inventing countdowns, travel locations or personal absence details.
- Provide a link/action to open the relevant vacation banner or date range in the **main Home calendar**, honoring its vacation visibility toggle appropriately without changing actual scheduling. If the member lacks permission to view detailed vacation settings, show only permitted summary information; editing remains restricted to authorized Home managers through the established review and confirmation workflow.
- Respect Home isolation, per-member dashboard configuration, privacy on wall/shared displays, mobile layouts, keyboard and screen-reader accessibility, clear date labels and color-independent statuses. Update promptly when vacations are created, edited, cancelled, started or ended, and when overlapping module selections change; do not generate duplicate notifications or alter module pauses through widget display.
- Acceptance criteria: optional add/remove and empty state; next versus active vacation; inclusive dates and Home timezone; chores-only and optional habits/meal modules; overlapping vacations with combined effective pauses; calendar navigation and visibility toggle; read-only versus manager access; privacy on wall displays; responsive accessible states; immediate sync on changes without scheduling side effects.

### Confirmed Home vacation banners on the main calendar (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Display each confirmed Home vacation as an optional **all-day, multi-day banner** spanning its inclusive start and end dates in the Home's main calendar. Provide a **Show Home vacations** visibility toggle alongside existing calendar content toggles; toggling changes presentation only, never vacation scheduling or module pauses. Do not create a separate vacation calendar.
- Show a concise, accessible Home vacation label and date span; authorized members may open permission-aware details showing selected module pauses and effective overlapping effects. Only authorized Home managers may create, edit or cancel vacations from calendar entry points, using the established impact preview, one-time chore review and confirmation workflow. Other members receive read-only details permitted by their Home role.
- Overlapping vacations remain **separate banners**, not merged records; ensure visually understandable stacking/overflow and a clear indication that combined module pauses may outlast an individual banner. In month/week/day and responsive views, handle cross-week, cross-month and cross-year spans, all-day placement and Home-local timezone/DST consistently. Do not treat vacation banners as ordinary draggable calendar events; date changes require manager permissions and the vacation review workflow.
- Respect Home isolation and wall-display privacy: show only appropriate generic labels in shared/public views, without exposing private travel destinations, absence reasons or member-specific whereabouts. Support keyboard/screen-reader navigation, color-independent distinctions, readable contrast, and accessible visibility-toggle state.
- Keep calendar banners in sync with vacation creation, edit, cancellation and effective pause changes without duplicates; historical periods remain discoverable where the calendar date range includes them. A cancelled vacation is not presented as active. Calendar hiding never suppresses underlying chores, habits or meal-planning vacation rules.
- Acceptance criteria: toggle on/off; all-day inclusive multi-day span; cross-month dates; overlapping distinct banners and combined pauses; read-only member versus manager editing; preview on edits; mobile/wall privacy; accessible interaction; timezone/DST; updates without duplicates; no effect on actual vacation scheduling when hidden.

### Confirmed non-recurring Home vacation periods (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Each Home vacation is an explicitly created, bounded date range with an inclusive start and end date. Home vacations **do not recur automatically** annually or on any other schedule; do not offer recurrence controls, background annual generation or implicit reuse of last year's dates.
- Authorized Home managers create and confirm each new vacation independently, reviewing the affected chore occurrences, one-time chore Keep/Reschedule/Skip decisions, and optional shared Home habit and meal-planning pauses every time. Previously created vacations may be viewed for reference, but never silently copied or activated.
- An ended vacation stays in Home history with its original dates, module selections, manager decisions and change record; ending it does not schedule another vacation. Existing vacation edit/cancel, overlapping-range, timezone, permission and notification rules continue to apply.
- Acceptance criteria: create a vacation for a specific date interval; no repeat/yearly toggle; no vacation appears the following year without a new explicit manager action; independent preview and confirmation for a later vacation; historical vacation preserved without modifying prior rewards or records.

### Confirmed module-specific Home vacation behavior (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A Home vacation pauses **Home chores by default** under the existing vacation and one-time chore review rules. During creation and editing, authorized Home managers can independently opt to suspend **shared Home habits** and **Home meal planning** for the same inclusive vacation dates. Both optional module switches default to off; show the selected modules in a confirmation preview. Do not apply Home vacation settings to personal contexts or other Homes.
- **Shared Home habits:** If enabled, suppress scheduled shared-habit occurrences, routine reminders, missed-status penalties and streak-breaking effects attributable solely to vacation dates. Preserve previous completions, audit trails, contribution records and pre-vacation streak state.
