Resume the original cadence after vacation without retroactive missed occurrences, artificial completions or backfill; clearly distinguish paused dates from completed dates in habit history and widgets. Personal habits and non-suspended shared habits continue normally.
- **Home meal planning:** If enabled, suspend routine planned meal slots and meal-related reminders during vacation, without deleting recipes, meal-plan entries, shopping lists or prior plans. Display vacation dates as paused, preserve any explicitly scheduled entries for review, and resume regular recurring meal patterns after the period without creating duplicate catch-up meals or shopping-list ingredients. Do not silently erase or move existing custom meals or recipe links.
- **Tasks, goals and pet care remain active** under their own rules, including deadlines, reminders and critical pet-care schedules. The vacation must not mute important pet-care notifications or change task recurrence or goal progress. Existing personal and member-specific absence settings remain separate.
- Managers can edit vacation dates and module switches with a preview of newly affected and restored occurrences; preserve recorded work and any deliberate per-occurrence overrides. Apply Home permissions, privacy, notification preferences, quiet hours, Home timezone, overlapping vacation ranges and idempotent updates. Display clear paused-state indicators in relevant calendars, dashboards and mobile/wall views without disclosing private travel information.
- Acceptance criteria: chore-only default; independent opt-in/out for Home habits and meal planning; active tasks/goals/pet care; no missed shared-habit penalties or streak loss solely from a pause; no deleted meal plans or duplicate generated shopping items; no retroactive catch-up; editing/cancelling/overlapping vacations; separate personal/Home contexts; permission and privacy checks; accurate history and reminders.

### Confirmed one-time chore handling during Home vacations (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When an authorized Home manager creates or changes a Home vacation, present a **mandatory review of affected one-time chore occurrences** whose scheduled or due dates fall within the inclusive vacation interval. For each eligible occurrence the manager explicitly chooses **Keep original date**, **Reschedule**, or **Skip**; never automatically move a one-time chore to the return date.
- The preview identifies chore title, original date/due time, assignment or unassigned status, reward points, and any reminders or overdue-release implications, subject to permissions. Support individual decisions and a reviewable bulk choice for eligible occurrences; require confirmation of the resulting changes. A manager may defer unresolved decisions, but those chores remain clearly flagged as **Needs vacation review**, with vacation suspension protections until resolved.
- **Keep** preserves the original date and occurrence identity and clearly indicates that the Home vacation suppresses routine chore activity/reminders during the interval; after vacation, apply existing overdue and reward rules without manufacturing a completion or automatically excusing lateness. **Reschedule** requires a valid explicit replacement date/time and previews affected reminders and assignment; retain history and preserve the same occurrence and point value. **Skip** marks only that occurrence skipped with no points, preserving the audit trail.
- Do not modify already completed, rewarded, pending-approval, or Needs rework occurrences through this review; show them as protected/informational and use existing authorized correction workflows where needed. Chores overdue before vacation require separate explicit review, not automatic forgiveness. Suspend relevant auto-release timers during vacation in accordance with the existing Home vacation policy.
- Editing, shortening, extending or cancelling a vacation reruns the impact review for newly affected chores; preserve prior explicit decisions and never silently revert reschedules or skips. Apply Home timezone and inclusive date boundaries, permission checks, concurrency/version checks and idempotent retries. Reflect outcomes in calendar, dashboard, history and notifications, with wall-display privacy and accessible mobile review.
- Acceptance criteria: vacation with multiple one-time chores; each Keep/Reschedule/Skip choice; mixed/bulk review; deferred unresolved decision; no silent date movement; due-time validation; existing overdue and protected pending/approved chores; vacation edits and cancellation; reminder and auto-release behavior; permissions, concurrency and accurate points/history.

### Confirmed vacation-aware recurring chore rotation (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** For **future, not-yet-finalized rotating chore occurrences**, temporarily exclude Home members with a **confirmed vacation absence covering that occurrence's scheduled date** from the eligible rotation candidates. Assign the turn to the next available, otherwise-eligible participant in the configured rotation order. This is a vacation-specific exception to the earlier general temporary-absence rule requiring explicit manager decisions; ordinary non-vacation absence signals continue using that review workflow.
- Preserve the recurring cadence, configured participant order and baseline rotation position; do **not** make returning members repay missed turns, retroactively reassign past occurrences, or permanently remove absent participants. After the absence ends, include them again in the normal sequence without catch-up assignments. The same occurrence retains its established base point value, regardless of the substitute member.
- Check the **individual participant's accepted effective dates**, not just the group vacation envelope, pending invitations or proposed unaccepted changes. Members who are withdrawn, declined, expired, or otherwise not confirmed absent remain eligible. Apply other eligibility and Home permission rules; never substitute an ineligible member. A confirmed whole-Home pause takes precedence and suppresses scheduling instead of attempting to rotate among absent members.
- If **no eligible available rotation participant** exists, flag the occurrence as **Needs assignment review** for an authorized manager rather than inventing an assignee, skipping silently, or assigning an absent member. Allow manager-approved per-occurrence skip/reassignment under existing rules. For already-generated, claimed, submitted, Needs rework, approved or otherwise explicitly managed occurrences, preserve their state and require existing authorized impact review; do not silently override them when vacation dates change.
- Preview future rotation effects when vacations or participant dates are added, accepted, withdrawn, shortened or cancelled. Recalculate only eligible future occurrences not protected by prior decisions; preserve timestamps, completed history, approval outcomes, rewards and recurrence order. Handle simultaneous vacations, overlapping intervals, Home timezone/DST, changes to rotation membership, stale clients and idempotent retries without double assignments.
- Display vacation-driven substitute assignments clearly in the chore calendar, weekly dashboard, member-by-day matrix, unassigned pool where relevant and history, respecting privacy on wall displays. Apply established notification preferences and the silent vacation-change rule; do not broadcast routine vacation edits.
- Acceptance criteria: three-person rotation with one away; staggered group vacation dates; absent member returns without catch-up; multiple absent members; no eligible substitute; pending invitation does not remove eligibility; manager-protected existing occurrence; whole-Home pause precedence; simultaneous changes; point parity and history preservation.

### Confirmed per-occurrence vacation rotation override (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized Home chore managers may explicitly override vacation-based automatic rotation skipping **for one specific future chore occurrence**, including keeping an otherwise-skipped absent member assigned or assigning that occurrence to an absent member. The vacation, participant absence dates, recurring series, rotation order and later occurrences remain unchanged.
- Before confirmation, show a clear conflict warning and impact preview identifying the chore, occurrence date, affected member, confirmed absence dates, current/proposed assignee, eligibility, points and relevant reminders. Require deliberate confirmation; never silently bypass vacation exclusions or Home permissions. Managers cannot assign members lacking required chore eligibility or access; this override relaxes vacation availability only.
- Preserve explicit overrides when vacation dates or rotation projections are recalculated; do not overwrite them through automated substitution. An authorized manager can later edit or remove the override using existing per-occurrence reassignment and review controls. Do not modify already completed, submitted, pending-approval or Needs rework occurrences through the automatic rotation mechanism; use their established protected workflows.
- A whole-Home vacation pause still suppresses generation/scheduling during its applicable interval; an individual rotation override does not unpause the Home or override module-wide vacation settings. Do not fabricate missed turns, alter late-reward policies, award points on assignment, or create duplicate occurrences. Preserve audit details of who approved the exception and when.
- Surface the exception to authorized members in chore details, schedule previews and history, without exposing unnecessary vacation details on wall/shared displays. Follow the previously approved silent vacation-change behavior and existing reminder preferences; handle concurrency, timezone boundaries and idempotent saves.
- Acceptance criteria: manager keeps an absent member for one occurrence; manager assigns another absent-but-eligible member; unrelated occurrences still skip absences; ineligible member blocked; whole-Home pause precedence; existing submission protected; vacation dates change without erasing explicit override; concurrent edit conflict; no reward/rotation corruption.

### Confirmed no-available-participant rotation resolution (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** If a future rotating chore occurrence has **no eligible available participant** after confirmed vacation exclusions and other eligibility checks, put it in **Needs assignment review**. Do not silently skip, postpone, assign an absent member, award points, or change the underlying rotation.
- An authorized Home chore manager must explicitly resolve that occurrence using one of three actions: **Skip occurrence** (no reward, preserve reason/history), **Assign another eligible Home member** (including a member outside the configured rotation, without adding them to the series), or **Override vacation availability for one otherwise-eligible rotation participant** (using the already-approved conflict warning and per-occurrence impact preview).
- Show a review comparing the scheduled date, unavailable rotation participants, applicable vacation/eligibility conflicts, chosen assignee or skip, points, and reminder/late implications. Validate assignment eligibility, Home permissions and the member's actual accepted vacation dates at confirmation. An override never bypasses eligibility or a whole-Home chore pause.
- The review state is distinct from an ordinary claimable unassigned chore: it must not appear as freely claimable until a manager deliberately chooses a valid assignment or otherwise changes the occurrence. Keep the original due date unless the manager separately approves a due-date change under existing controls.
- Resolve only the selected occurrence. Preserve the series cadence, baseline rotation order and later occurrences; returning members have no catch-up debt. Protect claimed, submitted, pending-approval, Needs rework, approved, completed and explicitly managed occurrences from automatic overwrites. Revalidate on concurrent changes or vacation edits; avoid duplicate resolutions and rewards.
- Show actionable review indicators to authorized managers in chore management, calendars and dashboard views; give affected members only permission-appropriate assignment details. Follow the silent vacation-change policy and existing individual notification settings. Keep an auditable decision history.
- Acceptance criteria: all rotation members away; outside-rotation eligible substitute; vacation override with warning; skip; no eligible Home member; Home-wide pause; unresolved review not claimable; return from vacation; simultaneous manager decisions; protected submissions; no points awarded on assignment or skip.

### Confirmed targeted chore volunteer requests (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** For a rotating chore occurrence in **Needs assignment review** because no rotation participant is available, an authorized Home chore manager may optionally send a volunteer request to one or more **selected eligible Home members** instead of immediately assigning or skipping it. This is a manager-initiated targeted offer, not an open pool listing or automatic assignment.
- Preview the chore title, occurrence date/due time, instructions/checklist, estimated duration when available, base points, invited recipients and any relevant availability conflicts before sending. Exclude members without Home/chore eligibility or access; do not expose private vacation details of other members. Requests are optional and recipients can accept or decline; silence does not constitute consent.
- **First valid acceptance wins:** On acceptance, atomically revalidate the occurrence remains unresolved, the invitee remains eligible and available under applicable vacation rules, and the offer remains open. Assign that one occurrence immediately to the first valid accepter, mark other outstanding offers closed/unavailable, and update all authorized views. Simultaneous acceptances must produce exactly one assignee; losing recipients receive clear non-punitive feedback. Never award points for volunteering or assignment.
- While offers are open, keep the occurrence in a clearly labelled **Awaiting volunteer** review substate, distinct from claimable unassigned chores; it must not also be directly claimable. The manager may cancel offers or choose an existing skip/direct-assignment/vacation-override resolution, which closes pending offers before committing the decision. Do not override already claimed, submitted, pending-approval, Needs rework, completed or skipped occurrences.
- A recipient may decline without penalty. If all recipients decline, become ineligible or leave, or the manager closes the request, return to **Needs assignment review** unless another valid manager resolution was committed. Do not automatically postpone, skip or broaden invitations. If the due date passes, surface overdue status and keep manager review actionable under existing late rules.
- Respect notification opt-in, quiet hours and permissions when delivering offers and outcomes; keep a visible in-app pending request for each invitee even when push delivery is suppressed. Record inviter, recipients, invitation/response times, accepted assignee, closures and manager decisions in permission-aware history; do not publish recipients' private availability.
- Preserve the recurring series, original rotation, other occurrences and points rules. A whole-Home chore pause prevents assignment while applicable; a manager must use the already-approved explicit per-occurrence override to assign someone absent, not bypass it through volunteering. Revalidate after vacation/eligibility/date changes and handle stale offers, member removal, retries and simultaneous manager actions idempotently.
- Acceptance criteria: targeted multiple-recipient offer; first accepter assigned; concurrent acceptance; decline/all decline; no response; manager cancellation/direct resolution; permission or eligibility revoked; confirmed vacation conflict; Home-wide pause; late due date; notification suppression; no duplicate assignments, points or rotation changes.

### Confirmed minimal volunteer request details (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Targeted chore volunteer requests show only the information needed to decide whether to help: chore title, scheduled/due date and time, reward points, relevant chore instructions/checklist and optional estimated duration, plus **Accept** and **Decline** actions and the existing response deadline. Do **not** include a reason for seeking volunteers, a generic explanation that the regular rotation is unavailable, a manager-written explanation, or the identity, vacation status or absence details of unavailable rotation members.
- Keep the invitee-facing offer concise and accessible across in-app requests, notifications and responsive surfaces. Notification previews must not expose additional private information; opening the request may show the ordinary permission-authorized chore details without revealing the reason for the volunteer request.
- The manager-facing resolution view may retain the established **Needs assignment review** context and eligibility/conflict information under existing permissions; this decision limits the **recipient-facing volunteer request**, not the authorized manager's diagnostic view or audit trail.
- Accepting still uses the first-valid-acceptance-wins atomic assignment workflow; declining has no penalty. Requests expire at the existing chore due date, and all permissions, Home privacy, quiet hours, rotation and points rules remain unchanged.
- Acceptance criteria: invitee sees chore details, points, deadline and response controls; no generic or personalized explanation appears in invitation or notification; no absent-member details leak; manager retains authorized review context; accepted/declined/expired and concurrent response behavior remains unchanged.

### Confirmed manager visibility of volunteer invitation responses (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized Home chore managers can inspect the response status of **each individually invited member** on a targeted volunteer request: **Pending**, **Accepted**, or **Declined**, with invitation time and response time when applicable. Do not require or solicit decline reasons; declining remains penalty-free.
- The manager review shows recipient-specific outcomes while the offer is open and in permission-aware history after it closes. Show **Expired** for unanswered invitations closed at the occurrence due date, and **Closed / no longer available** for pending recipients when another member accepts or the manager resolves or cancels the request; distinguish these outcomes from a voluntary decline. Identify the successful assignee without suggesting multiple acceptances can win.
- Only managers with existing permission to manage the relevant chore and Home can view the complete recipient/response list. Each invitee sees only their own response and the request's overall availability, not who else was invited or how others responded. Never expose unrelated members' vacation details, private availability or decline motivations, including through notifications, wall displays or shared dashboard widgets.
- Keep status and timestamps synchronized with first-valid-acceptance-wins assignment, expiry, manager cancellation, revoked eligibility and concurrent responses. Times use the Home timezone for display with unambiguous ordering; stale or offline views refresh and cannot accept closed requests. Do not add separate notification spam for individual declines.
- Acceptance criteria: manager sees pending and declined recipients and their response times; first accepted recipient and closure of other pending offers; expiration and cancellation distinct from decline; no decline reason field; unauthorized members cannot see the response roster; privacy on wall displays; simultaneous accept/decline/expiry events show a single consistent history.

### Confirmed withdrawal of individual pending volunteer invitations (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** An authorized Home chore manager can withdraw **one or more individually selected Pending invitations** from an active targeted chore volunteer request without cancelling the entire request. Other pending invitations remain open and eligible for first-valid-acceptance-wins assignment until the occurrence due-date cutoff.
- Provide a manager-only **Withdraw invitation** action on each pending recipient, with a clear recipient-specific confirmation identifying the affected chore and member. Withdrawal takes effect immediately; mark that invitation **Withdrawn by manager** with actor and timestamp. Do not represent it as Declined, Expired or Accepted, and do not require or collect a reason.
- The withdrawn recipient's Accept/Decline actions become unavailable. If they attempt to accept from a stale screen, notification, or offline state, reject the response with a neutral message that the offer is no longer available. Their existing in-app request may show the withdrawn state, without exposing other invitees or their responses; notification delivery follows existing preferences and quiet hours.
- A manager may withdraw only still-pending invitations: a committed acceptance and its chore assignment cannot be undone by withdrawing an invitation, and declined/expired/closed/previously withdrawn records remain immutable. If all invitations are withdrawn or otherwise no longer actionable and nobody accepted, close the volunteer request and return the occurrence to **Needs assignment review**; never make it publicly claimable, assign someone automatically or award points.
- Withdrawing an invitation does not extend the existing request deadline, change its due date, add new recipients, or modify the underlying recurring rotation, chore reward, other occurrences, or vacation decisions. Adding recipients is separately permitted under **Confirmed adding recipients to active volunteer requests**; withdrawal itself does not add recipients or extend deadlines.
- Apply permission and eligibility checks on every action, including concurrent manager withdrawals, first acceptance, expiry, cancellation, direct assignment or skip. Resolve races atomically against current invitation and occurrence states; preserve exactly one valid assignee and one auditable outcome per invitation, with idempotent retries.
- Update manager response roster, recipient request view and authorized dashboards immediately. Keep invitation creation, withdrawal actor/time and final outcome in permission-aware history; never expose recipient rosters or private vacation details to other invitees or wall displays.
- Acceptance criteria: withdraw one of several pending invitations while others remain actionable; withdraw the final pending invitation; stale acceptance after withdrawal; accepted/declined/expired invitation cannot be withdrawn; simultaneous withdrawal and acceptance or due-time expiry; unauthorized withdrawal blocked; correct distinct history and privacy; no duplicate assignment or points.

### Confirmed adding recipients to active volunteer requests (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized Home chore managers may **add eligible recipients to an already active targeted volunteer request** while the chore remains in **Awaiting volunteer** and before its existing due-date cutoff. Adding recipients does not cancel, replace, reset or interrupt invitations already pending; the original request remains the same request with its original expiration deadline.
- In the manager-only request detail, offer **Add recipients** with a review of currently eligible Home members not already invited on that request. Preview each selected recipient, the chore, points, due time and any availability or permission conflict; confirm before sending. Do not allow duplicates or silently reinvite members who declined, were withdrawn, expired or had their invitations closed. A new request, where permitted, is required for a fresh invitation to such a member.
- Each newly invited member receives their own **Pending** invitation with its own invitation timestamp, but **all recipients share the original chore due-date deadline**. Clearly show the remaining response time; if the due date is too close or already reached, block addition rather than creating immediately expired or misleading offers. Do not extend the due date or expiration as a side effect.
- Existing Pending invitations retain their state, response options and original invitation timestamps. Every recipient remains subject to **first valid acceptance wins**: acceptance atomically assigns exactly one occurrence and closes all other outstanding invitations, including newly added ones. Adding recipients cannot reopen an already accepted, cancelled, expired or otherwise closed request, or an occurrence resolved by a manager.
- Revalidate current Home membership, chore-specific eligibility, vacation availability, whole-Home pause, manager authority, occurrence status and deadline at confirmation and delivery. Handle concurrent additions, withdrawals, acceptances, declines, expiry and manager resolutions atomically and idempotently; if a conflict wins, report which recipients were not added without duplicate offers or assignments.
- Update the authorized manager's per-recipient status roster and auditable history with newly added invitees, invitation timestamps and manager actor. Invitees see only their own invitation and the ordinary chore details, never the full roster or other members' vacation information. Apply established notification preferences, quiet hours and accessible in-app request behavior; no points are earned by invitation or acceptance.
- **Reconciliation:** The earlier statement in **Confirmed withdrawal of individual pending volunteer invitations** that adding recipients was out of scope applied to that earlier decision only and is superseded by this explicit approval. Withdrawal of pending invitations remains independently available; no full recipient-list replacement workflow is introduced.
- Acceptance criteria: add one or several new eligible recipients while earlier invitations stay Pending; shared original due deadline; reject duplicate/previously invited recipient; reject add after acceptance, expiration or manager resolution; concurrent acceptance and addition; simultaneous manager additions; due-date and vacation eligibility changes; correct timestamps, recipient privacy and no duplicate assignment or rewards.

### Confirmed volunteer request expiration at chore due date (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A targeted volunteer request for an unresolved rotating chore occurrence automatically expires when that occurrence reaches its **existing due date and time**, interpreted in the Home scheduling timezone. Do not add a separate per-request response deadline or leave unanswered requests open indefinitely after the due time.
- Until expiration, invited eligible members can accept or decline under the established first-valid-acceptance-wins rules. At expiration, close every still-pending invitation, mark its outcome **Expired**, and return the occurrence from **Awaiting volunteer** to **Needs assignment review** if no valid assignment or other resolution has already been committed. The chore remains overdue when applicable; do not silently skip, reschedule, reassign, release to the public unassigned pool, or award points.
- An acceptance committed before the due-time cutoff remains valid and must not be undone by a delayed expiration job. An acceptance at or after expiration must be rejected with clear feedback, even if the expiry process has not yet run. Make the acceptance/expiration boundary atomic and idempotent so simultaneous responses, retries and background processing cannot produce duplicate assignments or contradictory states.
- If the manager changes the due date while invitations are pending, preview the impact and recalculate the expiration against the confirmed new due date; if the new due date is already past, expire pending offers immediately. Closing requests early through a manager decision, cancellation, all declines, loss of eligibility or Home pause continues to follow existing rules and must not create a second expiration event.
- Show the response deadline on the invitee's in-app offer and the manager's review view. After expiry, remove acceptance controls, retain permission-aware invitation/expiration history, and expose an actionable **Needs assignment review** indicator to authorized managers. Do not send a dedicated expiration notification unless existing notification settings explicitly provide one; respect quiet hours and avoid duplicate alerts.
- Acceptance criteria: no response through due date; one acceptance before deadline; acceptance exactly at/after cutoff; concurrent acceptance and expiry; timezone/daylight-saving boundaries; due-date extension and shortening; all invitees decline; manager resolution before expiry; stale/offline acceptance; retry-safe expiration; no point award, duplicate assignment or unintended rotation changes.

### Confirmed no reopening expired volunteer requests (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Once a targeted chore volunteer request has expired at the chore occurrence's due date, it is permanently closed and **cannot be reopened, extended, or renewed in place**. Expired invitees cannot accept the old offer; its outcome, recipients, timestamps and responses remain in the permission-aware history.
- To seek volunteers again, an authorized Home chore manager must **first explicitly change that occurrence's due date to a valid future date** using the existing due-date editing workflow, with the required impact preview and confirmation. The manager must then **manually create a new targeted volunteer request**, review/select eligible recipients again and send it. Do not automatically recreate invitations, carry over acceptances or re-notify previous recipients.
- The new request is a distinct record with its own creation time and expiration at the **new occurrence due date**; the old request remains Expired. While no new request is active, the occurrence stays in **Needs assignment review** unless the manager resolves it through an existing authorized action. A date change alone never sends an invitation or assigns the chore.
- Preserve the recurring series and all other occurrences: a renewal-related date change applies only to the explicitly selected occurrence and must not silently modify the rotation, existing reward amounts, completion history or the series schedule. Show any lateness/reward implications of moving the deadline before confirmation; do not retroactively change already-finalized points or protected submitted/approved states.
- Revalidate permissions, recipient eligibility, vacation availability, Home-wide pauses, current occurrence status and due date at each step. If another manager assigns/skips the chore, the occurrence becomes protected, or the date is no longer in the future before sending, prevent creation and show actionable feedback. Handle concurrent manager edits, stale screens and retries without duplicate requests.
- Acceptance criteria: expired request has no reopen control; unchanged overdue due date blocks a new request; future due-date change alone creates no invitation; manager manually sends a new request to reviewed recipients; old and new histories remain distinct; new request expires at new due time; protected occurrence and concurrent resolution prevent duplicate invitations/assignments; no unintended points or rotation changes.

### Confirmed manager correction of accepted volunteer assignments (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** An authorized Home chore manager may use a dedicated **Correct volunteer assignment** action after a targeted volunteer request has been accepted, **only before completion has been submitted**. This corrects the assignment of the selected chore occurrence; it does not reopen the accepted volunteer request or reactivate invitations closed by the winning acceptance.
- Require a **manager-entered reason** and a pre-commit **impact preview** showing the occurrence, original volunteer, intended resolution (release to manager review or reassign to a specifically eligible Home member), due/overdue status, eligibility/vacation conflicts, notifications and points implications. The manager must explicitly confirm; do not silently change the original due date, recurrence, rotation or other occurrences.
- Once confirmed, revoke the original assignee's ability to submit that occurrence, then either place the occurrence in **Needs assignment review** for an explicit next decision or directly assign the manager-selected eligible replacement using existing chore assignment and vacation-override safeguards. Do not automatically issue another volunteer request. Any new volunteer request must comply with the current due-date and fresh-request rules.
- **State boundary:** Allow correction only while the accepted occurrence is assigned and **not submitted**. Block this dedicated action for submitted, pending-approval, Needs rework, approved/completed, skipped or otherwise protected occurrences; use their existing workflows rather than retroactively erasing work or rewards. If the assignee submits before the manager commits, reject the stale correction and refresh.
- Preserve the accepted invitation and its original **Accepted** outcome in immutable permission-aware history, alongside the correcting manager, reason, timestamp, previous/new assignee or unassigned review state. Show a distinct **Assignment corrected** event; never rewrite acceptance as Declined, Withdrawn or Expired. Only authorized managers may view full recipient and reason details; notify affected assignees according to existing settings without exposing other invitees.
- Revalidate current manager permission, Home membership, replacement eligibility, vacations, whole-Home pauses, occurrence status and due date at confirmation. Commit changes atomically and idempotently against simultaneous submission, other manager correction, reassignment or cancellation. Never produce duplicate assignments, acceptance events, rewards or altered rotation turns; acceptance alone never awards points.
- Acceptance criteria: manager corrects accepted-but-unsubmitted volunteer assignment with required reason and impact preview; returns occurrence to review or reassigns eligible member; accepted request stays closed and auditable; submitted work blocks correction; conflicting vacation or Home pause blocks invalid replacement; simultaneous submission/correction and manager edits resolve safely; affected member feedback and permission-aware history; no duplicate points or rotation modifications.

### Confirmed temporary chore absence periods (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** An eligible Home member may register a temporary absence with an inclusive start/end date, in the Home scheduling timezone. This is a planning signal, **not** an automatic skip, reassignment, rotation change, or declaration that the member cannot perform any chore. Allow authorized editing, early ending and removal of an absence.
- Show the member and authorized chore managers a permission-aware review of chore occurrences assigned during the absence, including fixed assignments, recurring rotation occurrences and already overdue occurrences where relevant. Distinguish confirmed scheduled occurrences from future projections, and indicate existing completed, skipped, pending-approval and reassigned states; do not silently mutate any of them.
- For each eligible outstanding occurrence, an authorized chore manager explicitly chooses **keep as assigned**, **skip this occurrence**, or **reassign this occurrence** to an eligible Home member. Preview affected dates, assignees, pending approvals and possible reward implications before confirmation; provide a clear unresolved state when decisions are deferred. An absent member may propose changes but may only execute actions their existing permissions allow.
- Skipping uses the established per-occurrence skip rules and earns no points; reassignment changes the scheduled assignee of that occurrence, not the actual contributor of completed work. Neither action changes the recurring cadence, original rotation order, future series assignment rules, preserved historical records or reward value. Pending approvals and completed occurrences require their existing correction/approval workflows rather than being silently skipped or reassigned.
- On adding, changing or ending an absence, refresh the affected-occurrence review; do **not** automatically reverse prior explicit skip/reassignment decisions. Handle overlapping absences, several unavailable rotation members, occurrences generated after the initial review, member departure, timezone boundaries and concurrent manager edits without duplicate or lost occurrences.
- Keep absence dates and reasons private to authorized Home participants; an absence reason is optional and should not be required or exposed on public wall displays. Notify affected members of confirmed reassignment or skip only under existing Home notification preferences and permissions. Support clear mobile review, empty states, and recovery from partial failures.
- Acceptance criteria: one-off and recurring fixed chores; rotation without shifting future order; overlapping and shortened absences; preview and selective skip/reassignment; no automatic changes; overdue and pending-approval occurrences; reward preservation; permissions and privacy; newly generated occurrences; concurrent edits and idempotent retries.

### Confirmed configurable chore dashboard widgets (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Home dashboards offer configurable **My Chores**, **All Home Chores**, and **Pending Approvals** widget views. A widget is always scoped to one explicitly selected Home; never combine data from separate Homes or personal contexts implicitly. Pending Approvals is available only to members currently authorized to review those submissions.
- Each widget may filter by eligible assignee(s), occurrence/completion or approval status, and relevant scheduled/due date or date range. Clearly identify active filters, sort order, date boundaries and timezone; distinguish overdue, submitted/pending approval, rejected, completed and upcoming states as applicable. Filters must not imply access to chores the viewer cannot see.
- Authorized members can submit/mark chore occurrences complete directly from a widget, including choosing the actual contributor where required. For chores configured for approval, quick completion produces **Pending approval**, not final completion or awarded points. Authorized reviewers can approve or reject eligible pending submissions directly from the Pending Approvals widget, with an appropriate confirmation/impact preview and optional rejection explanation following the established workflow.
- Revalidate Home membership, chore visibility, completion/reviewer rights, contributor eligibility, occurrence version, approval settings and reward rules at action time. Respect the approved self-approval policy; preserve distinct assignee, submitter, contributor and reviewer attribution. Award points only once on eligible final completion/approval and prevent duplicate submissions, conflicting decisions and retry double-awards.
- Widget settings are dashboard-specific, with meaningful empty/loading/error/permission-revoked states and safe handling of deleted or unavailable chores. Changes synchronize with the Chores module and other authorized views. Responsive and wall-display presentations must suppress sensitive actions/details when access or interaction is not appropriate; never expose unauthorized titles, counts or pending-approval details.
- This decision does **not** add drag-and-drop reassignment, rotation editing, bulk chore management or full schedule editing to dashboard widgets. Acceptance criteria cover each view and filter, permissions, self-approval, another member's chore, pending-to-approved/rejected transitions, points accounting, recurring occurrences, stale/concurrent actions, membership revocation and responsive/wall-display privacy.

### Confirmed chore-approval reviewer notifications (12 October 2026)
- **Notify currently authorized chore-completion reviewers in-app when a completion is submitted for approval.** Surface pending items in their permission-aware approval queue and provide a direct route to review the request.
- **Email notifications are optional per reviewer**, disabled unless that reviewer opts in. Honor their notification preferences independently; email must not expose sensitive Home or chore details beyond what is appropriate for email.
- **Group or digest related requests sensibly** to avoid notification spam while keeping individual requests actionable and discoverable. In-app notification delivery must not depend on email preferences or email success.
- Avoid notifying members who lack current approval permission; recheck authorization when opening or acting on a notification. Handle withdrawn, already reviewed, deleted, or stale requests gracefully and avoid duplicate notifications from retries.
- Keep approval decisions and point awards governed by the approved chore-approval workflow, including permitted self-approval. Supporting specifications must define preference controls, grouping rules, notification state transitions, and acceptance criteria.

### Confirmed self-approval of chore completions (12 October 2026)
- **A Home member who holds the explicit chore-completion approval permission may approve their own submitted or performed chore completion**, including when they are both contributor and reviewer. No separate reviewer is required solely because the approver is the contributor.
- Ordinary chore-completion permission does **not** confer approval authority. Recheck current Home membership, explicit approval permission, occurrence state, contributor eligibility, and applicable rewards policy at approval time; do not bypass any other validation or Home privacy rules.
- Preserve distinct submitter, contributor, and approver fields even when they refer to the same person. Clearly label self-approved completions in the permission-aware audit trail, with decision time and awarded points.
- Award points only after approval and exactly once; protect against duplicate or conflicting approvals, concurrent edits, and stale requests. Rejection and later correction follow the established chore-completion and rewards rules.
- This explicit self-approval decision supersedes the earlier conditional wording about preventing self-approval unless approval permissions allow it; approval permission itself permits self-approval, without an additional Home-level toggle.

### Confirmed optional approval of chore completions (12 October 2026)
- **Reconfirmed 13 October 2026 (Option B):** Chore approval remains an optional per-chore setting controlled by authorized Home administrators/chore managers; submitted completions show **Pending approval** until a permitted reviewer approves or rejects them. Associated reward points are finalized only upon approval. This reconfirms the existing workflow rather than creating a second approval mechanism; explicit approval permissions (including previously approved self-approval) still govern who can review.
- **Home chore managers may enable or disable completion approval individually for each chore**, subject to the relevant Home management permissions. Approval is off unless explicitly enabled; changes apply prospectively and do not retroactively revoke finalized completions or awarded points.
- For chores requiring approval, an authorized member submitting a completion creates a **Pending approval** record rather than a finalized completion. Preserve the scheduled assignee, claimed actual contributor, submitter, reported completion time, and expected points; make the pending state visible to permitted participants and reviewers. Do not award, spend, or count pending points as earned.
- **Only an authorized reviewer with the appropriate Home approval permission may approve or reject** a pending completion. Approval finalizes completion and awards the applicable on-time/late points exactly once to the actual contributor. Rejection requires a reviewer reason, awards no points, and enters **Needs rework** with the original submission retained; the contributor may correct and resubmit under the confirmed rework workflow.
- Recheck reviewer authorization, contributor eligibility, applicable reward policy, and current occurrence state at decision time. Prevent self-approval unless the Home's explicit approval permissions allow it; do not implicitly grant approval through ordinary completion permission.
- Preserve an audit trail of submissions, approvals/rejections, actors, timestamps, contributor and point outcomes. Show pending items and review actions to authorized reviewers without exposing private information to unauthorized Home members.
- Pending approvals do not pause or shift future recurring chore occurrences or rotation order. Prevent duplicate submissions, competing decisions, and double awards; clearly surface stale or conflicted decisions and safely retry failed reward posting.
- Supporting specifications must define reviewer notifications, how long pending approvals remain actionable, late-policy timing for reported versus approved completion, and the behavior of pending records during edits, skips, deletion, membership changes, and Home recovery, without contradicting the approved reward and correction rules.

### Confirmed rejected chore completion rework and resubmission (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When an authorized reviewer rejects an approval-required chore completion, they **must supply a meaningful rejection reason**. The occurrence enters a distinct **Needs rework** state instead of silently reverting to ordinary incomplete. The original submission, reported completion timestamp, contributor, reviewer, reason, decision time, and expected points remain in the permission-aware occurrence history; **no points are awarded for a rejected submission**.
- Keep the occurrence assigned to its existing assignee/claimant and make the rejection reason and required next action visible to the eligible contributor and authorized reviewers. The contributor may correct the work, update the occurrence's checklist and optional completion note, and **resubmit for approval**; each attempt has its own submission timestamp and claimed actual completion timestamp. Do not overwrite or erase prior attempts or rejection decisions.
- Mandatory checklist steps must be satisfied at resubmission. Preserve prior checklist evidence in history while allowing the member to recheck or redo steps as appropriate; do not misrepresent the rejected attempt as approved. Revalidate permissions, membership, occurrence state, recipient eligibility and the Home's approval policy at each submission and review.
- Use the approved due-date/late-points policy with the **reported completion time of the approved attempt**, not the review time. A rejected earlier attempt never earns points, reserves a reward or guarantees an on-time award for later rework. Show the potential reward and any lateness impact before resubmission; finalize a single award only upon approval.
- Needs rework is separate from Pending approval, Completed, Skipped, and ordinary outstanding/overdue; indicate overdue status alongside rework when applicable. Do not automatically return the occurrence to the unassigned claim pool, alter its recurring series, shift rotations, or modify future occurrences. Authorized managers may still use established correction/reassignment procedures.
- If the occurrence becomes invalid, is deleted, or the contributor loses access, suppress inappropriate actions and retain permitted history. Prevent concurrent reviewer decisions, duplicate submissions, stale resubmissions and duplicate reward ledger entries; make errors recoverable.
- **Supersession:** This decision replaces the older optional-explanation / revert-to-outstanding wording in the optional chore-completion approval section **for rejected completion submissions**; all other approval and reward rules remain intact. Acceptance tests cover mandatory reason, visible Needs rework state, retained attempt history, checklist correction, resubmission, late-point calculation, reviewer self-approval where authorized, permission revocation, concurrent decisions and repeat rejection.

### Confirmed correction and reversal of completed chores (12 October 2026)
- **Authorized users may either undo a completed chore occurrence (reopen it) or correct its recorded completion details**, including the actual contributor, completion timestamp, and other editable completion metadata. These actions require the relevant Home chore-management and reward-adjustment permissions; ordinary completion permission alone does not implicitly grant correction rights.
- **Automatically reconcile awarded reward points** when a completion is reversed or corrected: reverse the original award and apply the correct amount to the correct contributor, honoring the original occurrence's applicable late-completion policy and the corrected completion time. Do not silently leave incorrect balances, double-award points, or change unrelated historical rewards.
- **Preserve an immutable, permission-aware audit history** of original completion, every correction/reversal, actor, timestamp, reason if supplied, affected contributor, and before/after point adjustments. Show the current effective completion state while retaining the historical record.
- Reopening a chore restores its appropriate outstanding/overdue status without changing the original recurrence schedule or future rotating assignees. A corrected completion likewise must not rewrite unrelated occurrences or future assignments.
- Display an impact preview of status and point changes before confirmation, including effects on redeemed or insufficient point balances; do not silently create invalid balances or claw back fulfilled rewards. Define an explicit exception-resolution workflow in the rewards specification where direct reconciliation is impossible.
- Recheck permissions, membership, concurrent updates, and idempotency on save; report partial reward-reconciliation failures clearly and provide safe recovery. Respect Home visibility and account-deletion anonymization rules.

### Confirmed completion of another member’s chore (12 October 2026)
- **A Home member with appropriate chore-completion permission may mark a chore assigned to another member as completed**, without requiring reassignment or an additional approval step. Do not grant this ability to users who lack the relevant Home permissions.
- **Record the actual contributor separately from the scheduled assignee and the actor who submitted the completion**, including the completion timestamp. Show accurate attribution in task history and relevant Home views; do not silently rewrite the original assignment or future recurring rotation.
- **Award completion points to the person who actually performed the chore**, not automatically to the scheduled assignee or the person recording completion. Apply the chore's configured on-time/late points policy to the actual completion and award points only once.
- Where the person recording completion is not the actual contributor, require explicit selection/confirmation of the contributor, limited to eligible Home members, and clearly show who receives points. Recheck permissions and membership eligibility before saving.
- Handle simultaneous completion attempts and reward failures without duplicate completion or point awards. Preserve original assignee and actual contributor in history, subject to the confirmed privacy and account-deletion anonymization rules.

### Confirmed late completion rewards for overdue chores (12 October 2026)
- **Overdue Madeora chore occurrences remain completable** by authorized users, including after later occurrences in a recurring rotation have been scheduled or completed. Completing an overdue occurrence does not alter the recurrence cadence or rotation order.
- Each chore has a configurable **late-completion points policy**: **full points (default)**, **reduced points**, or **zero points**. Show the policy and expected reward before completion. The reduced-points amount or rule must be explicitly configured and cannot exceed the normal reward; define rounding and validation in the rewards specification.
- Determine lateness against the occurrence's scheduled due date/time and applicable Home timezone. Record original due date, actual completion time, actor, policy applied, and points awarded; never falsely mark late work as on time.
- Apply the policy consistently to fixed and rotating chores and preserve historical attribution. Award points once only, including during retries or concurrent completion attempts. Skipped occurrences do not earn points, and existing earned rewards are not silently recalculated if a chore's policy changes later.
- Recheck task and rewards permissions before completing or changing the late-reward policy. Surface any failed reward award separately from the completion state and provide safe reconciliation rather than silently losing or duplicating points.

### Confirmed skipping recurring chore occurrences (12 October 2026)
- **Authorized users may explicitly skip an individual recurring chore occurrence, with an optional reason.** Do not require a reason or treat a skipped occurrence as completed.
- A skipped occurrence closes as **Skipped** rather than remaining overdue; retain its scheduled date, original assignee, skip timestamp, optional reason, and actor in permission-aware history.
- **Award no completion points or rewards for a skipped occurrence.** Preserve already-earned historical rewards on other occurrences; do not silently grant, reverse, or reassign points.
- **Keep the recurring schedule and configured rotation order unchanged.** The next occurrence goes to the next scheduled participant on its original cadence, even if the skipped occurrence was overdue.
- Recheck chore-management permissions before skipping. Show the result clearly and avoid duplicate or conflicting state transitions, including simultaneous completion and skip attempts.
- The optional skip reason must follow Home visibility and privacy permissions; do not disclose it outside authorized views. Specify any authorized correction or reversal of a skip in the supporting task specification.

### Confirmed missed rotating chore behavior (12 October 2026)
- **Automatic chore rotation follows the scheduled order and cadence even if an earlier occurrence is missed or overdue.** A missed occurrence does not pause, delay, or shift assignments for later occurrences.
- Each missed chore occurrence **remains separately overdue and assigned to its original scheduled member** until it is completed, explicitly skipped, reassigned by an authorized user, or otherwise resolved under the task rules. Do not automatically transfer it to the next rotation participant.
- Example: if Alex misses Week 1, Sam still receives Week 2 on schedule; Alex's Week 1 chore remains overdue independently.
- Preserve each occurrence's original assignment, due date, completion/skip history, and reward attribution; avoid duplicate occurrences or accidental reward awards. Make concurrent overdue and upcoming occurrences distinguishable in task and Home views.
- Manual reassignment of an overdue occurrence does not alter future rotation order unless an authorized user explicitly edits the recurring series using the approved scope controls.

### Confirmed fixed and rotating recurring chore assignments (12 October 2026)
- **Recurring Home chores support both fixed member assignments and automatic rotation among selected Home members**, chosen per recurring chore. A fixed assignment keeps the configured assignee(s) until explicitly changed; rotation assigns successive eligible occurrences according to a **user-configurable member order**.
- Authorized users can select rotation participants, arrange their order, inspect upcoming assignees, and change the assignment mode subject to Home chore-management permissions. Show the upcoming assignment sequence before saving.
- Rotation must be deterministic and must not silently rewrite completed occurrences, earned rewards, or historical attribution. Apply the confirmed recurrence editing scopes (**This occurrence**, **This and following occurrences**, **Entire series**) to assignment changes, preserving completed history.
- When a participant leaves the Home, loses eligibility, or cannot be assigned, do not assign tasks to an unauthorized or departed member. Clearly flag the affected chore/occurrence for authorized reassignment or rotation repair; preserve prior completion records and apply the established no-account-deletion-notifications rule where applicable.
- Supporting task specifications must define how skips, missed chores, manual overrides, member additions/removals, rotation-order edits, and simultaneous updates affect the next assignment, without duplicate or lost occurrences.

### Confirmed optional task calendar views (13 October 2026)
- **Approved customized option B with main Calendar visibility:** Tasks offers an **optional dedicated week/month calendar view** of accessible tasks with due dates and recurring occurrences. Members can continue using task lists exclusively; neither a task calendar nor a calendar view is mandatory for task creation, completion or navigation.
- The **main Calendar module also offers an optional Show tasks toggle/filter** to overlay eligible tasks alongside calendar events. This is a view of the same task records, **not conversion into calendar events**, not automatic Google Calendar synchronization and not a requirement to display tasks. Default visibility and remembered preferences must be explicit, context-aware and consistent with existing calendar settings; turning tasks off hides only the overlay and never changes or deletes tasks.
- Calendar displays due-date tasks on their due dates, distinguishing open, completed, overdue and recurring occurrences. Tasks without due dates are not arbitrarily placed on a day; offer a clear route back to unscheduled tasks in the Tasks module. If start dates are present, distinguish them from due dates without inventing a scheduled duration or timed event. Task reminders do not imply a calendar event.
- Both calendar surfaces provide task details on selection and authorized edit/complete actions, subject to the same task rules, including shared completion, subtasks, recurrence scope and permissions. Provide separate filters where appropriate for task lists, assignees, status, priorities and labels; preserve the main Calendar's existing event filters and do not silently alter event visibility.
- Enforce personal/Home context isolation, Home membership, task/list permissions and event privacy for each rendered entry, count, search result, tooltip, widget and wall display. No task may reveal a private calendar event, and no private task may appear because an event calendar is shared. Respect optional module enablement: if Tasks is disabled or inaccessible, do not show task entries or offer unusable task controls; if the Calendar module is disabled, Tasks remains fully usable including its own optional calendar view.
- Handle same-day task density, multiple occurrences, timezone/day boundaries, overdue tasks, recurrence exceptions, tasks edited or completed in another view, empty states and offline/stale data without duplicates. On small screens provide an accessible agenda/list alternative; distinguish tasks visually and semantically from events without relying on color alone.
- Acceptance criteria: opt into/out of Tasks week/month view; toggle task overlay in main Calendar; independent use of Tasks when Calendar is disabled; due-date and undated tasks; recurring occurrences and overdue status; correct completion synchronization; task/event privacy; context switching; Google Calendar events remaining independent; permission revocation; mobile, keyboard and screen-reader navigation.

### Confirmed reusable task templates (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized members can create, name, edit, use and delete reusable **task templates** belonging to exactly one personal space or Home. A template can prefill a task's **title, description, ordered simple subtasks, priority and reusable labels**. Templates are separate from existing tasks and recurring-task series; no whole-list templates, assignment rules, relative due dates, workflow stages or automatically generated sequences are included.
- Provide a **Save as template** action for an accessible task and a **Create from template** action in task creation, plus a manageable template library in the current context. Show an editable task preview before creation; the member explicitly chooses an eligible destination task list. A generated task is an independent new task, not synchronized to later template edits.
- Copy subtask text and order but **reset every subtask to incomplete** and create the new parent as incomplete, regardless of the source task's completion state. Do not copy source comments, attachments, completion actor/time, creation history, reminders, assignees, recurrence rules, task-specific dates, or audit/reward events. Use the template's selected priority, defaulting to Normal when unspecified, and apply only labels still valid in the destination context.
- Templates are owned by their personal space or Home, not by an individual Home member. Home template visibility and editing follow established Home roles/permissions; leaving the Home removes access but does not remove its templates. Personal templates remain private. No automatic copying or sharing of templates across personal spaces and Homes.
- Validate nonempty trimmed template names and task titles, documented field lengths, duplicate/confusing template names and subtask limits. When a referenced label is deleted or unavailable, disclose and omit it from the new task rather than silently recreating it. Prevent creation into archived, deleted or inaccessible lists.
- Recheck permissions, context, template version, list status and label eligibility at confirmation. Protect against concurrent edits, repeated submissions and interrupted saves; never create duplicate tasks or partial checklists on retry. Changes to a template never retroactively update tasks already created from it.
- Template deletion requires confirmation and removes the template without deleting tasks created from it; apply existing eligible Trash/recovery policies where applicable. Keep template management accessible on mobile and ensure widgets, search and Ora never expose inaccessible template content.
- Acceptance criteria: create from scratch/from task; independent generated task; reset completed source and subtasks; edit template without affecting created tasks; missing label; invalid/duplicate name; permissions and Home departure; same-context task-list selection; stale template/list; double-submit; deletion without task deletion; accessible preview and errors.

### Confirmed independent tasks without cross-task dependencies (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Tasks are independent records. Madeora does **not** provide formal prerequisite, blocked-by, blocks, finish-to-start, or other dependency links between separate tasks. Task creation, editing, scheduling, assignment, completion, and recurrence never require another task to be completed first.
- Existing single-level subtasks remain a checklist within one parent task, not a cross-task dependency mechanism. The established parent/subtask completion rules remain in effect.
- Members may describe an intended order in task descriptions or comments and manually set dates, priorities, and list ordering, but Madeora must not interpret that text or ordering as an enforced or automatically tracked dependency.
- List views, dashboard widgets, task calendars, reminders, recurring occurrence history, templates, and bulk operations must not introduce blocked statuses, dependency indicators, automatic successor scheduling, or completion restrictions based on separate tasks.
- Acceptance criteria: two related tasks can be created and completed in either order; a task remains actionable if another task is overdue, deleted, archived, or inaccessible; subtasks continue to follow their existing rules; no cross-Home or personal-to-Home dependency relationship is exposed or inferred.

### Confirmed basic task activity information (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Ordinary tasks display only basic activity metadata: creator and creation timestamp, most recent update timestamp (and updater when already available and authorized), and completion state with actual completing member and completion timestamp where applicable. Do **not** add a user-facing chronological task activity timeline or comprehensive field-by-field before/after change history through this decision.
- Present basic metadata in the task details with readable local date/time and accessible labels; keep absent completion data blank or clearly marked Not completed. For shared multi-assignee tasks, show the actual completing member, not every assignee. Reopening removes the current completed state while retaining necessary integrity records under existing rules.
- Preserve **previously approved, distinct records** that are necessary for functionality or accountability, including recurring occurrence completion history, comments with author/timestamps, chore/reward approval and points ledgers, attachment lifecycle, and security/administrative audit requirements. These are not a general-purpose task change timeline and must not be deleted or exposed more broadly because this option is simpler.
- Apply task, list, personal-space and Home permissions to creator/completer identities and timestamps; handle departed members without granting them access. Keep task metadata synchronized across task lists, widgets and relevant detail views, without exposing restricted identities on wall displays.
- Acceptance criteria: new task, edited task, completed and reopened task, multi-assignee task, recurring occurrence history, comments and rewards remaining intact, moved task, departed creator/completer, restricted task and wall display, concurrent updates and timezone formatting.

### Confirmed basic bulk task actions (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** In the full Tasks module, authorized members may select multiple accessible tasks and perform four basic bulk operations: **complete**, **change priority**, **add/remove/replace labels**, or **move to another named task list in the same personal space or Home**. Do not add bulk assignment, scheduling, deletion, recurrence-series modification, or dashboard-widget bulk actions through this decision.
- Provide explicit multi-selection with selected count, individual deselection, select-visible-results controls, and a clear action chooser. A preview must identify the affected tasks, source/destination context and lists, exact proposed changes, any excluded or ineligible items, and impacts on shared task completion, rewards or recurrence occurrences. Never silently select tasks hidden by filters or inaccessible to the actor.
- For bulk completion, apply the established **explicit parent completion** rule even when subtasks remain unchecked; warn about incomplete subtasks. A multi-assignee task has **one shared completion event**, attributed to the actual actor. Completed tasks must not receive duplicate completion events, notifications or rewards. For recurring tasks, a selected generated occurrence is completed individually; do not complete the entire series or future occurrences implicitly.
- For bulk priority edits, use the approved Low/Normal/High/Urgent or unspecified states; for labels, clearly distinguish **add**, **remove** and **replace** so existing unrelated labels are not accidentally lost. Validate that selected labels belong to the current context. For list moves, offer only active accessible destination lists within that same context and preserve task identity, subtasks, comments, attachments, assignees, recurrence settings, audit and history.
- Recheck each task's current permissions, version, state, ownership, recurrence scope and destination-list eligibility immediately before confirmation and execution. Apply the same per-task rules as individual actions, including task locks or approvals where relevant. If some items become ineligible, show which and why; never treat partial success as full success or silently override concurrent edits.
- Provide a final confirmation with an exact count and action summary, per-item results on completion, and safe retry behavior without duplicated side effects. Keep task lists, All Tasks, widgets, reminders, history and notifications synchronized. Honor privacy and Home isolation; no cross-Home or personal-to-Home bulk moves.
- Acceptance criteria: mixed statuses and permissions; tasks with unfinished subtasks; multi-assignee shared completion; already completed task; recurring occurrence versus series; priority change; label add/remove/replace; same-context list move; archived/inaccessible destination; filtered and paginated selection; concurrent edit or membership revocation; partial failure and retry; mobile keyboard/screen-reader operation; rewards awarded once.

### Confirmed recurring task occurrence history (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Every recurring task provides a permission-aware **occurrence history** listing each generated past occurrence's scheduled local date (and time where applicable), status, actual completion timestamp, and actual completing member. Clearly distinguish **Completed**, **Missed/Overdue**, **Skipped** where supported, and other applicable states without conflating an overdue open occurrence with a permanently failed one. Do not introduce completion-rate analytics, streaks, or performance scores through this decision.
- Show the original scheduled date even when an occurrence is completed late or rescheduled; display the actual completion timestamp separately, with timezone-aware formatting. For shared tasks with multiple assignees, record the **actual actor once**, not one completion per assignee. For a completion later reversed, show the corrected current state and preserve necessary permission-aware audit attribution without implying it remains completed.
- History belongs to the recurring series, with a clear path from each history row to its occurrence when accessible. Provide chronological ordering, useful status/date filtering or pagination for long histories, understandable empty states, and accessible mobile and screen-reader presentation. Do not create historical entries for future occurrences merely because they are forecast by the schedule.
- A scheduled occurrence that passes its due date without completion remains separately **Overdue** until resolved under existing task/chore rules; clearly label it as missed in the history presentation without automatically skipping, deleting, completing, shifting, or reassigning it. If no due time was set, respect the established date-only and timezone semantics rather than inventing a deadline hour.
- Keep history intact when authorized members pause/end a recurrence, edit only future occurrences, change assignees, move the task between lists in the same context, or leave the Home. Retain the original completing actor's historical attribution subject to privacy policy; do not grant departed members access. Historical corrections must follow existing edit-scope and audit permissions, never silently rewrite past completions or rewards.
- Apply task/list/Home permissions to history visibility and actor identity; restrict wall-display, notifications, search, widgets and Ora to the same authorized information. Recheck permissions and occurrence version on correction or navigation. Prevent duplicate history entries from retries, delayed scheduling, concurrent completion, or recurrence-series splitting; surface recoverable error states.
- Acceptance criteria: recurring task with no prior occurrences; on-time and late completion; missed occurrence still overdue; skipped chore occurrence; multi-assignee single completer; reopened/corrected completion; recurrence paused/ended; changed schedule and assignee; Home member departure; moved list; series split; timezone/DST boundary; concurrent completion and retry; inaccessible history; long history on mobile.

### Confirmed configurable task dashboard widgets (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Task dashboard widgets can target one accessible named task list or the **All Tasks** aggregation for the currently selected personal space or Home. Multiple widgets may have independent configurations; never silently mix tasks from different contexts.
- Each widget offers configurable filters for **assignee(s), completion/status, priority, start/due-date range or relative date state, and reusable task labels**, using the same meanings as the full Tasks module. Support combined filters, clear/reset controls, understandable empty states, and an explicit indication of the current list and active filters. The All Tasks view only combines lists the viewer may access.
- Display essential task identity, source list where relevant, due/start information, priority, assignees and progress when space permits; provide a direct route to the full task and list. Apply responsive layouts and accessible text labels rather than color-only indicators. On a wall display, follow its separate visibility and interaction rules.
- Authorized members can **complete or reopen an individual task directly in the widget** with immediate, reversible feedback where eligible; preserve explicit parent completion for tasks with subtasks, one shared completion for multi-assignee Home tasks, and per-occurrence completion for recurring tasks. Do not add bulk-editing, drag-and-drop task organization or full task editing to widgets through this choice.
- Show only tasks and fields authorized for the current viewer. Offer completion controls only when the member has the required task-level permission; never infer permission merely from being an assignee or viewing the widget. Recheck membership, permissions, current task status and version at action time, and report stale/conflicting changes without overwriting them.
- Keep widget results, counts and completion states synchronized with full task lists and other authorized sessions. Handle list renames, archival/deletion, changed labels/assignees, revoked access, offline/retry and missing data with explicit safe states; never silently substitute another list or leak previously cached private task content.
- Acceptance criteria: single-list and All Tasks widgets; independently configured widgets; combinations of all five filter dimensions; no matches; accessible and inaccessible lists; unassigned and multiply assigned tasks; completed and recurring occurrences; tasks with subtasks; permission denied or revoked; concurrent completion; archived/deleted source list; mobile and wall-display presentation.

### Confirmed individual task reminders (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized members may configure an optional reminder for an individual personal or Home task, either at a **specific date and time** or **relative to its due date** (for example, one day before). This supplements, rather than replaces, Madeora's existing standard due-date notifications. No automatic multi-reminder sequences, overdue escalation, or repeated follow-ups are introduced.
- The reminder editor clearly distinguishes the two modes, previews the resolved notification time in the relevant timezone, allows changing or removing a reminder, and validates a future or otherwise meaningful delivery time. A due-date-relative reminder requires a due date; if that date is removed or changed, show the effect and require review rather than silently delivering at an unintended time. Date-only due dates must have an explicit documented reference time for relative reminders; never infer an unspecified deadline hour.
- Reminder delivery respects the task's owning personal/Home context, current visibility and assignment permissions, each recipient's notification channel and quiet-hours preferences, and the existing notification policy. For Home tasks with multiple assignees, clarify intended recipients and avoid sending duplicate alerts; do not disclose private task content in notifications to unauthorized recipients or public wall displays.
- A reminder does not change task start dates, due dates, priority, completion or recurrence. On completion, deletion, archiving, unassignment or loss of access, cancel or suppress pending reminders as appropriate. For recurring tasks, support the established **This occurrence**, **This and following occurrences**, and **Entire series** editing scopes; preserve completed history and ensure that each occurrence generates at most its intended reminder.
- Revalidate recipient eligibility, current task version, schedule, timezone and notification settings at delivery and on edits. Handle daylight-saving transitions, past times, simultaneous updates, delayed delivery and retries without duplicate notifications. Display a clear unavailable/disabled state if the user's chosen channel is not enabled; do not promise delivery where the platform cannot support it.
- Acceptance criteria: one-off absolute reminder; due-date-relative reminder; task without due date; due-date change/removal; timezone and DST boundaries; recipient preferences and quiet hours; shared multiple-assignee task; completion before reminder; permission or membership revocation; recurring occurrence versus series edits; concurrent edits and retry deduplication; accessible mobile controls.

### Confirmed reusable task labels (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Personal spaces and Homes each maintain their own reusable, manually managed **task labels**. Authorized members can assign **multiple labels** to a task, including none. Labels supplement task lists, priorities, dates and status; they do not replace those concepts or introduce hierarchical labels, automatic labeling or saved smart views.
- Label management supports creating, renaming, changing an optional color and removing labels, with permission checks appropriate to the owning personal space or Home. Require a non-empty trimmed name, documented length limits and validation for confusing duplicates within the same context. Offer a neutral default when no color is chosen; use accessible text labels and contrast, never color alone to communicate meaning.
- Authorized task editors can add/remove labels from an accessible task using a searchable multi-select, with clear current selections and keyboard/screen-reader support. Task labels remain within the task's originating context even if identically named labels exist elsewhere. Moving a task between lists **within the same context** preserves its labels; cross-context moves remain disallowed under the existing task-list rules.
