- Task lists, All Tasks, search and relevant dashboard widgets support filtering by one or more selected labels, with a visible clear-filter action and accurate empty/count states. Do not expose inaccessible tasks or private labels in suggestions, filters, search, notifications, Ora or wall displays. No automatic labeling is implied by task names, priorities or due dates.
- Renaming a label updates its display wherever used without altering tasks' completion, assignment, recurrence, audit or rewards. Removing a label requires an impact preview showing how many accessible tasks use it and confirmation; removal detaches that label from affected tasks rather than deleting tasks. Revalidate current permissions and label usage at confirmation; protect against concurrent edits and retries.
- Recurring task series and occurrences follow existing scope controls for label edits (**This occurrence**, **This and following occurrences**, **Entire series**) and preserve historical completed occurrences. Subtasks inherit their parent task's labels and do not have independent labels. Maintain synchronization across authorized task views and widgets.
- Acceptance criteria: create/rename/color/delete labels; invalid/duplicate labels; no label and multiple labels; filter one or more labels and clear filters; move between lists; personal/Home isolation; permission revocation; recurring edit scopes; label removal with affected tasks; simultaneous edits; accessible color contrast and mobile multi-select.

### Confirmed multiple named task lists and All Tasks view (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each personal space and each Home can contain **multiple independently named task lists** (for example Administration, Renovation and Errands). An **All Tasks** view aggregates only lists and tasks the current member may access within the explicitly selected context. This does not introduce Kanban boards, custom workflow stages, milestones or project management.
- Authorized members can create, rename, reorder and archive task lists, with clear empty, loading, error and archived states. Validate non-empty names, documented length limits and confusing duplicate names within a context; use an explicit, predictable list-selection control when creating tasks. Do not automatically move tasks between personal spaces or Homes.
- Tasks belong to exactly one active list at a time. Authorized members can **move** a task between accessible lists in the **same personal space or Home**, using an explicit destination selector and confirmation when the move affects visibility or access. Preserve task identity, descriptions, priority, dates, subtasks, comments, attachments, assignees, recurrence and completion history, subject to destination permissions; do not duplicate completion events, rewards or notifications.
- Before moving, verify permission to view/edit the source task and create/manage tasks in the destination, as well as current membership, list state and task revision. Block moves to deleted, archived or inaccessible lists, moves across Homes or between personal and Home contexts, and stale or repeated requests; provide a clear recoverable failure state without losing the original task.
- All Tasks supports existing task filters, sorting, status and priority indicators across eligible lists, always labeling a task's source list when ambiguous. Changes to a list or task synchronize to list views, All Tasks, dashboards and permitted search/Ora surfaces without exposing inaccessible list names, counts or task details.
- Archiving or deleting a list containing tasks requires an explicit impact review and resolution of those tasks; do not silently delete, relocate or hide active tasks. Follow established Trash and seven-day recovery rules for eligible deletions, with permissions and destination availability rechecked on restore.
- Acceptance criteria: create and rename lists; duplicate/invalid names; personal and Home separation; All Tasks filters and accurate counts; task move with full metadata and recurring occurrences; insufficient source/destination permissions; archived destination; concurrent task changes; retry without duplication; list archival with active tasks; accessible mobile and wall-display-safe views.

### Confirmed optional task start and due dates (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Personal and Home tasks may have an optional **start date** and an optional **due date**, independently configurable. A task may have neither, either, or both; when both are present, the start date must not fall after the due date. Dates use the owning personal space or Home timezone and clearly distinguish calendar dates from any separately approved time-of-day reminders.
- A start date marks when work is expected to become relevant, **not an access or completion lock**. Authorized members can find, view, edit and, where permitted, complete a future-start task before that date. A due date marks the deadline; overdue status applies to an incomplete task after its due date, without changing its manually assigned priority.
- Offer clearly labeled **Upcoming**, **Active** and **Overdue** task filters, with consistent behavior for tasks without dates and for completed tasks. Upcoming means an incomplete task whose start date is in the future; Active includes incomplete tasks whose start date is today/past or unset and that are not overdue; Overdue means incomplete tasks with a passed due date, including those with a future start date only if an inconsistent legacy record exists and is flagged for correction. Avoid implying these categories are necessarily exhaustive of completed tasks.
- Members can configure ordinary task-list and dashboard views to **hide future-start tasks by default or include them**. Hidden tasks remain discoverable through an explicit Upcoming view/search and are not removed from counts or records; preserve accessibility and indicate active filters. Authorized notifications and existing due-date behavior are governed by their separate notification rules, not silently disabled by a list filter.
- On creation and editing, validate date order, present clear field descriptions and allow either date to be cleared. For recurring tasks and chores, use existing occurrence-versus-series editing scopes, calendar/timezone rules and completed-history protections; a start date must not silently change recurrence cadence, chore rotation or due dates. Existing assignments, priorities, subtasks, attachments, comments and shared completion remain unchanged when dates are edited.
- Revalidate task permissions, membership and latest record state on save; handle concurrent edits, timezone/date-boundary changes, stale views and offline/retry conditions without overwriting newer values. Apply the same privacy boundaries to task widgets, Ora and wall displays.
- Acceptance criteria: no dates, start-only, due-only, both dates, invalid reversed dates, today boundary, upcoming/active/overdue filters, future-start completion, hide/show future-start tasks, overdue tasks, recurrence scope edits, chores, timezone transition, simultaneous edits, inaccessible Home tasks, mobile and accessible labels.

### Confirmed multiple Home task assignees with shared completion (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A Home task may have **zero, one or multiple eligible Home-member assignees**, while retaining **one shared task completion state**. Any assignee with permission to complete the task may complete it for the group; do not create individual per-assignee completion checkboxes, mandatory unanimous sign-off or separate subtask assignments. Personal tasks retain their existing personal-context assignment rules.
- The task detail, creation/edit forms, assignee filters, notifications and dashboard views show the current assigned members clearly and accessibly. Show who actually completed or reopened the shared task and when, distinguishing the completion actor from the full assignee list; never imply that every assignee individually completed it. Members who can view but not edit assignments cannot add or remove assignees.
- Assignment changes require the existing Home task-management permission and must select current eligible members of the same Home only. Recheck membership and permissions before save, completion, notification and any reward-related action. If an assignee leaves or loses eligibility, remove their active assignment or flag it for authorized resolution under existing member-departure rules without deleting historical attribution. Never leak assignments across Homes or to unauthorized viewers.
- For recurring tasks, support the existing **This occurrence**, **This and following occurrences** and **Entire series** scopes for assignment edits; preserve completed occurrences and historical attribution. Each occurrence has its own single shared completion state. This decision does **not** change the separately approved fixed/rotating chore assignment and reward rules; any overlap must be resolved under those established chore-specific rules.
- A single completion triggers completion effects and eligible notifications **once per task occurrence**, not once per assignee. Any points, rewards or credit attribution must follow the existing module-specific policy and actual completion actor; do not silently award full credit to every assignee. Reopening follows existing permissions and history rules.
- Protect against simultaneous completions, duplicate requests, stale assignment lists, permission revocation and failed updates. Refresh affected views and show the final actor/status; do not overwrite newer edits or emit duplicate rewards/notifications. Preserve accessibility on mobile and wall-display privacy restrictions.
- Acceptance criteria: no assignee, one assignee and multiple assignees; one member completes for all; non-assignee with and without completion permission; attribution; assignment filters; simultaneous completion; reassignment; member departure; recurring scope edits; chore rotation unaffected; cross-Home access denied; notifications/rewards issued at most once; accessible controls and audit.

### Confirmed chronological task comments (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Personal and Home tasks support chronological **plain-text comments** for permitted members. Each comment displays its author and creation timestamp, with a clearly identified edited timestamp when applicable. Do not introduce threaded replies, @mentions, reactions or discussion-triggered mention notifications through this decision.
- Authorized viewers see comments in oldest-to-newest order with consistent timezone-aware timestamps, readable multiline text and clear empty, loading and error states. Comment creation requires non-empty content after trimming, with documented length limits, safe text rendering and accessible validation. Comments are separate from the editable task description and cannot modify task status or assignment.
- A member may **edit or delete only their own comments** while retaining access and the relevant task-comment permission. Provide explicit edit/cancel/save actions and confirmation before deletion where appropriate. Other members cannot impersonate an author or change another member's comment; administrative moderation, if needed, must be separately specified and authorized rather than assumed.
- Task-level visibility and permissions govern reading and writing comments, including personal versus Home ownership, task assignment, membership changes, archived/completed tasks and recurring occurrences. Recheck access on each request and save; do not leak comment content, authorship or counts through search, notifications, Ora, widgets or wall displays to unauthorized viewers.
- For recurring tasks, comments belong to the **specific task occurrence** on which they were posted; they do not automatically replicate to future occurrences or change a recurring template. Preserve comments with the occurrence's lifecycle and history, including deletion and eligible seven-day task restoration. On permanent account removal, follow existing attribution anonymization rules without misattributing another member's text.
- Guard against concurrent comment edits, stale submissions, repeated sends, interrupted saves and deleted/unavailable parent tasks. Never silently overwrite a newer comment revision. Show accurate pending/saved/failed states and allow safe retry without duplicating a comment. Authorized clients receive synchronized comment updates.
- Acceptance criteria: create, edit and delete own comment; reject blank/over-limit input; author and timestamps; chronological ordering; another member's comment read-only; no permission or departed Home member; completed/archived task; recurring occurrence separation; concurrent edits; safe retry; task Trash and restoration; accessible mobile and keyboard operation.

### Confirmed task document and image attachments (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized members can upload, view, preview where supported, download and remove **documents and images attached to personal or Home tasks**. Support multiple attachments per task within documented per-file and total limits; attachments are optional and do not change task completion requirements. Do not introduce attachment folders, annotations, file version history or cross-task reusable file libraries.
- Show each attachment's safe display name, file type, size, upload status and permitted actions. Provide inline image/document previews only for supported safe formats; otherwise provide a permission-checked download. Preview failure must not remove the underlying attachment or imply it was successfully previewed. Include clear empty, uploading, failed, blocked and removed states.
- Validate allowed formats, file sizes, quotas and file safety before acceptance; enforce existing personal-account or Home storage allowances based on the owning task context. Failed, cancelled or interrupted uploads must not appear as saved; permit safe retry without duplicate files. Reject unsafe or unsupported content with actionable messages, and prevent unauthorized access through direct attachment links, thumbnails or cached previews.
- Attachment visibility and upload, removal, preview and download permissions **inherit the parent task's context and access controls**, including assignment and Home membership rules where relevant. Revalidate permissions at every operation, after membership changes and when a task is moved, deleted or restored. Never expose attachment content or metadata in widgets, Ora, notifications, search, activity feeds or wall displays beyond the viewer's authorization.
- Removing an individual attachment uses Madeora's existing **seven-day file recovery** behavior where eligible; deleting a task applies its existing task/attachment recovery lifecycle. Recovery must recheck task existence, access and quota. Do not treat task attachment removal as deleting an independently stored Photos-library asset unless separately authorized by an existing rule.
- Recurring task occurrences must clearly distinguish files attached to a series/template from those attached to a particular occurrence. Any future- or whole-series attachment change follows the approved recurring edit scopes and impact review, without retroactively rewriting completed occurrences or leaking files to unauthorized assignees.
- Protect against concurrent task edits, duplicate uploads, stale removal requests and partial failures. Keep authorized task views synchronized; ensure mobile, keyboard and screen-reader access to attachment controls.
- Acceptance criteria: attach multiple supported documents/images; preview supported and download unsupported-preview formats; oversized/unsupported/malicious file; personal/Home quota exhaustion; cancelled and retried upload; read-only viewer; unauthorized download URL; membership revocation; concurrent removal; recurring occurrence attachment; task deletion and seven-day restoration; accessible error states.

### Confirmed task priority levels (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Personal and Home tasks support an optional explicit priority with four ordered values: **Low, Normal, High, Urgent**. New tasks default to **Normal** for display and sorting, while members may leave priority unspecified where the interface supports an unset value; distinguish unset from explicitly selected Normal in editing and data handling. Priority is user-entered, not inferred or automatically changed by due dates, overdue status, Ora, or other activity.
- Authorized members may set, change, or clear a task's priority in creation and editing workflows. For recurring tasks, apply the established **This occurrence**, **This and following occurrences**, and **Entire series** edit scopes, with impact review and completed-history protection. Subtasks inherit their parent's priority and have no independent priority controls.
- Provide priority filters (including an **Unspecified** choice when relevant) and ascending/descending priority sorting in task lists, alongside existing due-date and status controls. Use a documented, stable secondary sort for equal priorities; filtering and sorting must not change task ownership, recurrence, or completion. Dashboard task widgets may show priority and support consistent filtering where configurable, without implying automatic urgency.
- Show restrained, accessible text/icon indicators that do not rely on color alone; maintain contrast in light/dark themes and wall-display layouts. Avoid using notification styling or intrusive alerts solely because a task is marked Urgent. Task reminders and notifications continue to follow their separately approved rules.
- Apply current personal/Home visibility, edit and assignment permissions to every priority change and display, including widgets, search, Ora and wall displays. Revalidate authorization and current task state on save; protect against concurrent edits and stale recurrence instances. Keep authorized views synchronized and preserve audit metadata under existing task policies.
- Acceptance criteria: default and unspecified priorities; all four levels; filter/sort with ties; edits without permission; recurring occurrence vs future vs series edits; subtasks inherit priority; completed and overdue tasks; conflicting edits; responsive keyboard/screen-reader use; light/dark contrast; no priority leakage between Homes.

### Confirmed simple task subtasks (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A task may contain an optional, single-level checklist of subtasks. Each subtask has a required short title, an independent completed/not-completed state, and an explicit display order; members can add, rename, reorder, complete, reopen, and remove subtasks where they have permission to edit the parent task. No nested subtasks, independent subtask assignees, due dates, dependencies, or recurrence rules are introduced.
- Display parent progress as the number of completed subtasks out of the total, including a clear empty-checklist state. Completing every subtask **does not automatically complete the parent**. The parent task is marked complete only through an explicit authorized parent completion action, even if some subtasks remain incomplete; warn about remaining incomplete subtasks before confirming that action.
- Subtasks inherit the parent task's personal/Home context, visibility, permissions, lifecycle, and audit protections. A user who can view but not edit the parent can view authorized subtasks but cannot change them. Subtask titles and completion status must not leak through notifications, widgets, Ora, or wall displays when the parent is inaccessible.
- Parent task deletion, Trash recovery, archiving and restoration apply to its subtasks together. Reopening a completed parent does not silently reset its subtask completion states. Editing or removing a subtask does not silently change parent completion. When recurring tasks are supported, each generated task occurrence has its own subtask checklist and completion state; template edits and changes to one occurrence follow the existing recurring-task scope rules, with no retroactive rewriting of completed occurrences.
- Revalidate permissions, task/occurrence identity, completion state and item version at confirmation; prevent lost updates from concurrent editors and duplicate completion toggles on retries. Keep task list views, progress indicators, dashboard widgets and authorized activity views synchronized, with accessible checkbox labels and keyboard/touch interaction.
- Acceptance criteria: create parent with no or multiple subtasks; reorder and rename; complete/reopen individual subtasks; all subtasks completed but parent still open; explicitly complete parent with unfinished subtasks; parent reopened without resetting checklist; permission denied; concurrent edits; recurrence occurrence isolation; deletion and seven-day recovery; privacy in widgets and wall displays.

### Confirmed recurring task and chore editing scope (12 October 2026)
- When editing a **recurring Madeora task or chore**, offer **This occurrence**, **This and following occurrences**, and **Entire series**, with a clear impact preview of affected occurrences, assignments, dates, and other relevant fields before applying changes.
- **This occurrence** changes only the selected instance without altering the recurrence definition or other instances. **This and following occurrences** applies the change starting at the selected instance and continuing forward, leaving earlier instances unchanged. **Entire series** updates the series definition and eligible occurrences throughout the series, but does not retroactively rewrite completed historical records.
- **Preserve completed history**, including completion status, original assignees/contributions, earned points, and historical totals, under the established privacy/anonymization rules. Historical completed occurrences are read-only for the purpose of bulk recurrence edits; corrections to history, if supported, require a separate explicitly authorized workflow.
- Show clear outcomes for edited overdue, in-progress, individually overridden, or already generated future occurrences; avoid duplicate occurrences or silently overwriting independent exceptions. Specify field-level and recurrence-boundary behavior in the task module's supporting specification.
- Recheck permissions for the selected scope and affected personal/Home items; do not allow an edit to bypass assignment or module permissions. Handle concurrent edits and invalid schedules with actionable errors, without partial silent changes.
- This policy applies to recurring tasks and chores, independently of the existing recurring calendar event rules and the confirmed recurring-task deletion scopes.

### Confirmed recurring task and chore deletion scope (12 October 2026)
- When deleting an occurrence of a **recurring Madeora task or chore**, offer three explicit choices: **This occurrence**, **This and following occurrences**, or **Entire series**. Show an impact preview before confirmation, identifying affected future occurrences and assignments.
- **This occurrence** removes only the selected instance without changing other occurrences or the recurrence rule. **This and following occurrences** ends the selected occurrence and all later scheduled instances while retaining earlier history. **Entire series** stops the complete recurring series and removes its outstanding occurrences from active views.
- **Preserve completed historical occurrences, contribution attribution, and reward/accountability history** under the established privacy and account-deletion anonymization rules. Deleting a recurrence must not silently erase past completed work or rewrite historical totals.
- Apply the approved seven-day individual-item recovery policy to deleted eligible occurrences/series, with restoration subject to meaningful conflict detection, valid recurrence rules, current permissions, and no silent duplication of already generated tasks.
- Scope choices must respect personal/Home ownership, configurable task permissions, assignment state, and notifications; no user may delete occurrences they cannot manage. Define boundary behavior for overdue, in-progress, and generated future occurrences in the task-module specification.
- These choices govern Madeora recurring tasks and chores; the separately approved recurring Google Calendar event and provider-sync behavior remains unchanged.

### Confirmed Trash navigation placement (12 October 2026)
- **Personal Trash is accessed from personal settings; each Home’s Trash is accessed from that Home’s settings.** Do not add a dedicated primary-sidebar Trash destination or require a separate sidebar utility menu.
- Keep the main sidebar focused on everyday modules while making the Trash entry clearly labeled and discoverable within the corresponding settings screen. Navigation must retain the correct personal/Home context and must never cross privacy boundaries.
- Show the existing central Trash view for that scope with module, item-type and deletion-date filters, permission-aware records/actions, seven-day recovery deadlines, individual and selected-item bulk restoration, and authorized permanent deletion.
- Home settings may expose the Trash entry only as permitted by the user’s current Home rights; opening a direct link must recheck authorization. Lack of permission to view a deleted record must not leak its existence through counts, previews or search.
- Supporting screen specifications must cover responsive settings navigation, empty/loading/error states and a clear return path to the originating settings screen.

### Confirmed central personal and Home trash organization (12 October 2026)
- Provide **one central trash view for the personal space and a separate central trash view for each Home**. These are distinct privacy and ownership scopes; do not combine private personal items with Home-owned items or expose another Home's trash.
- Each central trash view aggregates eligible individually deleted items and attachments across supported modules, with filters for **module, item type, and deletion date**. Show meaningful item identity, deletion date, and seven-day recovery deadline where authorized; include clear empty, loading, error, and expired states.
- **Permission-aware visibility and actions are mandatory**: only show records and metadata the viewer is currently authorized to access, and recheck current ownership, Home permissions, and record-level rights before restore or permanent deletion. The Home trash is not automatically visible in full to every member.
- Support the already approved **individual and selected-item bulk restoration**, meaningful conflict handling, and authorized early permanent deletion/empty-trash actions in these central views. Bulk operations must not bypass per-item permission checks or silently act on hidden items.
- Respect the distinct **seven-day recovery for individually deleted items/files** and **30-day recovery for entire accounts/Homes**; whole-entity recovery is not managed by the ordinary central trash view. Files used by active items must remain available according to the approved attachment rules.

### Confirmed deletion and recovery of parent items with attachments (12 October 2026)
- **When a Madeora parent item (such as a recipe or memory) is deleted, its exclusively attached files/media enter recoverable trash together with that item.** Restoring the parent item restores those attachments and their relationships as one coherent operation.
- **Files also referenced by other active items must remain available to those items** and must not be deleted, hidden, or made inaccessible solely because one parent item was deleted. Determine exclusivity using actual authorized references and ownership boundaries, not just display location.
- Apply the approved **seven-day individual file/attachment recovery period** to exclusively attached files placed in trash by the parent deletion; retain storage accounting until permanent removal. The parent record follows the confirmed seven-day individual-item recovery period so restoration of attachments is meaningful; do not silently shorten the separate 30-day account/Home recovery periods.
- A parent restore must not resurrect attachments already irreversibly deleted through an authorized action or whose recovery period has ended; clearly report any incomplete recovery and avoid broken references. When the parent is permanently removed, release exclusively attached files only when no other valid references or retention requirements remain.
- Preserve personal/Home ownership and access permissions, and ensure independent Home-owned copies and their attachments are not affected by deleting a personal original or another Home's copy. Recheck permissions and handle partial failures safely.

### Confirmed early permanent deletion from file trash (12 October 2026)
- **Authorized users may permanently delete selected recoverable files or empty the relevant personal/Home file trash before the normal 7-day recovery window ends.** This is optional; files left in trash still expire automatically after seven days.
- Require **explicit, informed confirmation** before irreversible deletion, stating that the selected files/attachments cannot be recovered through Madeora afterward. For bulk empty-trash actions, clearly identify the scope and affected storage pool and show the number of items where practical.
- Enforce current ownership, Home permissions, record/file access and any retention restrictions for **each file**, both when presenting the action and when executing it. Do not allow users to empty other people's personal trash or permanently remove Home files beyond their granted permissions.
- On successful permanent deletion, release the file's charged storage allowance and update storage usage/warnings. Handle partially failed bulk actions honestly, identifying which items remain and avoiding misleading success states; log security-relevant actions.
- **The early-delete option applies to individually trashed files/attachments only.** It must not bypass the separately confirmed 30-day account or whole-Home recovery periods, legal holds, or mandatory retention rules. Do not delete files still required for restoring a suspended account/Home merely because an unrelated trash action was requested.

### Confirmed Home file permanent-deletion permission (12 October 2026)
- **Use the same Home file-deletion permission for both moving Home-owned files to trash and permanently deleting those files early from trash.** Do not introduce a separate permanent-deletion permission or restrict the action to the Owner alone.
- Any member currently authorized to delete a particular Home-owned file may permanently delete that same file or include it in an authorized bulk **Empty trash** action, subject to the same file-level scope and current access restrictions. Members without file-deletion permission cannot perform either action.
- Continue requiring the separately approved **explicit irreversible-deletion confirmation** for early permanent deletion, even though the permission is shared. Recheck permissions for every affected file at execution; bulk actions must skip and report unauthorized items.
- This choice does not override legal retention, the seven-day standard file recovery window, or the separate 30-day recovery periods for whole accounts and Homes.

### Confirmed Home file restoration permissions (12 October 2026)
- **Any current Home member with the appropriate file-management permission for a particular Home-owned file may restore it from trash during the approved seven-day recovery window**, even when another member originally deleted it. Restoration is not restricted to the person who deleted the file or to Owner/Administrator roles alone.
- Recheck current Home membership, module/record access and file-management permission when displaying the restore action and immediately before restoration. Revoked or departed members cannot restore or view inaccessible files.
- Restore the file and eligible attachment associations to their proper Home-owned context without changing ownership or double-counting storage. If the parent item is unavailable or a naming/content conflict exists, provide a safe resolution path rather than silently overwriting data.
- Log who restored the file, handle concurrent restore/expiry/permanent-delete races safely, and clearly report failed or no-longer-available restorations. This permission does not extend the seven-day window or override irreversible deletion or whole-Home recovery rules.

### Confirmed progressive storage warnings (12 October 2026)
- **Show progressive in-app storage warnings at 80% and 95% usage** of each personal or Home file/media storage allowance. At 80%, use a gentle, actionable warning; at 95%, increase prominence without blocking unrelated workflows or interrupting normal use.
- For personal storage, notify the account holder. For Home storage, notify **only currently authorized Home storage managers**; do not broadcast quota alerts to members who lack the relevant management/access permission. Display current usage and the affected storage pool without revealing inaccessible files or private account details.
- At 100%, show the already approved capacity-exhausted state and enforce restrictions on new file/media storage, while text-based features remain available. Warnings are informational and do not themselves restrict uploads below the quota.
- Trigger threshold notifications when usage first crosses each threshold; avoid duplicate/spam alerts from repeated checks. Reassess when usage or the configured allowance changes and allow appropriate new warnings on a later crossing. Keep in-app storage status visible in settings.
- Supporting specifications must cover permissions, threshold crossing and resets, simultaneous uploads, accessibility, notification grouping, and quota reductions that place storage immediately above a threshold.

### Confirmed independent attachment ownership for Home copies (12 October 2026)
- When an eligible personally owned item is explicitly copied into permanent Home ownership, **copy its eligible attachments and media into independent Home-owned attachments** as part of the same transfer. The Home-owned item must not rely on continued access to the personal original's photos, videos, documents or other eligible files.
- Each authorized destination Home receives its own independent item and associated media; deleting or modifying the personal original, its attachments, or a copy in another Home must not remove or alter that Home's media.
- Apply the destination Home's visibility and content permissions to copied media. Do not copy private or unrelated attachments, external credentials, or media the requester is not entitled to transfer. Preserve references between the copied item and its own media without leaking source-private identifiers or other Homes' data.
- **Do not report transfer success until both the item and its required eligible attachments are safely available in the destination Home.** On failure, prevent broken partial copies and show clear retry/error states; define atomicity and cleanup behavior in the supporting specification.
- This is part of the approved independent-copy ownership-transfer workflow, not the normal reversible shared-item synchronization model.

### Confirmed retention of personal original after Home ownership transfer (12 October 2026)
- **Every permanent personal-to-Home ownership transfer creates an independent Home-owned copy while retaining the personally owned original.** This applies during normal account use and when preparing for account deletion; the original is never moved or automatically converted into Home ownership.
- The Home-owned copy has its own identity, permissions, edits, deletion lifecycle and eligible attachments. **No subsequent edits synchronize** between the personal original and any Home-owned copies, or among copies in different Homes.
- After successful copying, remove the transferred destination Home from the personal original's live-sharing selection, preventing two separately editable versions from appearing as one synchronized item. Other Homes not selected for transfer may continue to access the original through existing reversible sharing **while the personal account remains active**, subject to the owner's sharing settings and permissions. Explain the resulting access and versions in confirmation and item UI.
- During account deletion, the personal original remains part of the user's personal data throughout the recovery period and is deleted on final account deletion. Only successfully created, explicitly authorized Home-owned copies survive; unselected Homes lose access to the original when deletion takes effect under the existing account-deletion lifecycle.
- A failed, denied, cancelled or expired transfer must not remove the original or alter its existing sharing. Preserve references and attachments safely, and clearly distinguish independent copies from synchronized sharing.

### Confirmed personal-to-Home ownership transfers at any time (12 October 2026)
- **Users may initiate an explicit permanent transfer of eligible personally owned items into Home ownership at any time during normal account use**, not only when preparing for account deletion. The account-deletion transfer flow remains available as one entry point to the same capability.
- Apply the **same approved Home content-creation permissions, per-destination approval workflow, permitted self-approval, 30-day pending-request expiry, approver notifications, audit history, and authorization rechecks** regardless of when the transfer is initiated.
- For items shared with multiple Homes, create **independent Home-owned copies only for explicitly selected and authorized destination Homes**, as already approved; do not silently transfer to unselected Homes or expose private information. The personal original and its sharing relationships must have clearly specified post-transfer behavior before implementation, since outside account deletion the creator may retain an active account.
- Clearly distinguish **reversible sharing** from **permanent Home ownership transfer** in item actions and confirmation text; show resulting ownership, who may edit/delete, and whether copies stop synchronizing. No automatic transfer or implied consent.
- Account-deletion-specific requirements (pending-request cancellation and deletion of untransferred personal content) apply **only when the user actually requests account deletion**, not to routine transfers.

### Confirmed transfer of personally owned shared content before account deletion (12 October 2026)
- **Before requesting account deletion, users may explicitly transfer selected personally owned items currently shared with a Home into that Home's ownership.** Never transfer ownership automatically merely because an item was shared or because its creator deletes their account.
- Present eligible shared items and their destination Home clearly, with an explicit item-by-item selection and confirmation explaining that Home ownership is persistent and governed by Home permissions. Transfer is optional; account deletion must remain possible without transferring content.
- On confirmed transfer, the selected item becomes **Home-owned** and survives the original owner's subsequent permanent account deletion, subject to the Home's own retention and deletion rules. Apply the approved anonymization of the former owner's historical attribution after permanent account deletion.
- **Personally owned items not transferred** remain personally owned during the account's 30-day recovery period and are permanently deleted when account deletion becomes final; any Home sharing access to them must be revoked consistently with the account-deletion lifecycle. Do not silently convert shared items to Home-owned records.
- Because one personal item can be shared with multiple Homes as a **single underlying item**, transferring it to one Home must not silently grant that Home control over other Homes' sharing relationships or make private data visible elsewhere. The approved multi-Home resolution is to create independent copies for explicitly selected destination Homes; supporting specifications must define transfer eligibility, attachments, permissions, and atomic failure/rollback behavior before implementation.
- Existing Home-owned content and other members' personally owned content are unaffected.

### Confirmed multi-Home transfer copies before account deletion (12 October 2026)
- When a personally owned item is shared with multiple Homes and the account holder elects to preserve it before account deletion, they may **explicitly select one or more destination Homes** from those eligible to receive the item. Create a **separate, independently Home-owned copy for each selected Home**; do not designate a single Home as owner of the original shared item.
- Each resulting Home-owned copy has independent identity, access control, editing, future sharing, and deletion lifecycle. Changes in one Home must **not** synchronize to another Home's copy or give either Home control over the other. This is a narrowly scoped exception to the normal single-underlying-item sharing model, applicable only to an **explicit ownership-preservation transfer**.
- Show the user the selected Homes and explain that the copies will diverge independently. **Unselected Homes do not receive a copy** and lose access to the original personally owned item under the approved account-deletion lifecycle. Never copy content into a Home without explicit selection.
- Include eligible attachments and related data with each copy only to the extent authorized and necessary; prevent private information or other Homes' data from being carried into a destination copy. Apply the previously approved permanent-deletion attribution anonymization as appropriate.
- Treat multi-destination transfer as an explicitly confirmed operation with clear success/failure reporting and safe rollback or retry; do not misrepresent partially created copies as a fully successful transfer. Supporting specifications must define exact item/attachment eligibility and cross-reference handling.

### Confirmed permissions for receiving personal-to-Home ownership transfers (12 October 2026)
- **Direct transfer is permitted only if the transferring member currently has the destination Home’s permission to create Home-owned content of the relevant type.** Membership alone or permission to share a personally owned item does not grant permanent Home ownership rights.
- Otherwise, the transfer becomes a **pending request requiring explicit approval by a Home member authorized to accept/create that type of Home-owned content**. Show the destination, selected items, ownership consequences, and approver; do not create a Home-owned copy before approval.
- Evaluate each destination Home independently for multi-Home transfers: authorized destinations can proceed directly, while other destinations await their own approval. Clearly show per-Home status; do not claim an entire multi-Home transfer succeeded if any destination is pending or failed.
- Recheck requester membership, relevant permissions, item ownership, and approver authorization when approving and committing a transfer. A denied, withdrawn, expired, or unauthorized request creates no Home-owned copy. Do not allow account deletion to silently bypass pending approval or imply that pending items are preserved.
- Approval grants permission only for the explicitly selected content and destination; it does not change the requester’s general Home role or permissions. Notify the requester of approval or rejection, without exposing content to unauthorized approvers.

### Confirmed self-approval for personal-to-Home transfers (12 October 2026)
- **An authorized Home transfer approver may approve their own personal-to-Home ownership transfer request**, even when they lack the separate direct Home content-creation permission. Approval authority is sufficient for approving that specific request; it does not grant broader content-creation rights.
- The requester must still own the source item, be a current member of the destination Home, and hold the relevant transfer-approval authority **at the time of approval and execution**. Check all destination-specific and content-type restrictions. If they have neither direct creation permission nor valid approval authority, another authorized approver must approve.
- Present the same explicit ownership-transfer consequences and confirmation for self-approval as for any other approval. Record who requested, approved, and executed the transfer in the audit history, including when requester and approver are the same person.
- Never allow self-approval to bypass expiry, cancellation, Home membership changes, privacy restrictions, or the approved per-destination independent-copy behavior.

### Confirmed content transfer approval expiry (12 October 2026)
- **Personal-to-Home ownership transfer approval requests expire automatically 30 days after submission** if not approved, rejected, or cancelled earlier. The expiry is per request and per destination Home; it does not extend merely because other destinations were approved.
- Show the expiry date/time and pending/approved/rejected/cancelled/expired states to authorized participants. An expired request cannot be approved or create a Home-owned copy; the requester may submit a new request while eligible.
- Recheck ownership, membership, permissions, and item availability on approval. Permission loss, Home removal/deletion, or item deletion may invalidate a request sooner than its scheduled expiry.
- Expiration does not transfer content automatically and does not override the previously approved requirement to acknowledge and cancel outstanding pending requests before account deletion.

### Confirmed transfer approval notifications (12 October 2026)
- When a personal-to-Home ownership transfer requires approval, notify **currently authorized approvers through both an in-app notification and an email**. Do not send approval requests to members without the relevant content-acceptance permission.
- Emails must **not disclose private item contents, attachments, sensitive titles, or previews**. They may identify Madeora and the existence of a pending approval and direct the recipient to sign in to review it securely.
- Review links require authentication and a fresh authorization check before revealing the request or its contents; receiving a notification never grants access. Recheck permissions again before approval and transfer execution.
- Avoid duplicate notifications for the same request; distinguish requests by destination Home, and reflect approval, rejection, expiry, cancellation, or invalidation accurately in the in-app queue. A stale email link must not permit action on an inactive request.
- The requester's existing approval/rejection notifications remain in force. The separately confirmed account-deletion privacy rule still prohibits notifying other Home members about the account deletion or its resulting membership/task changes; transfer-request notifications must not reveal that deletion is planned.

### Confirmed pending content transfers during account deletion (12 October 2026)
- **Pending personal-to-Home ownership transfer approvals do not block account deletion indefinitely.** Before final deletion confirmation, clearly list every pending request, its items and destination Home, and warn that **unapproved items will not be preserved** as Home-owned content.
- Require the account holder to **explicitly acknowledge the warning and cancel all outstanding transfer requests** before proceeding with account deletion. Do not silently cancel them or imply that pending requests constitute successful transfers.
- Cancellation invalidates pending approval actions immediately, prevents late approvals from creating Home-owned copies, and leaves untransferred items personally owned until the normal permanent-deletion deadline. Already completed, authorized transfers remain intact.
- Account deletion retains its existing 30-day recovery period; cancelling pending requests does not automatically recreate them if the account is restored. The user may submit new requests after restoration if eligible.
- Show per-Home outcomes for multi-Home transfers, distinguishing completed copies from cancelled pending destinations. Keep account-deletion notifications to other Home members disabled under the previously approved privacy rule; cancellation should not disclose the account-deletion reason.

### Confirmed anonymization after permanent account deletion (12 October 2026)
- After the 30-day account recovery period ends and permanent deletion is completed, **preserve Home-owned completed activity records and historical totals**, including completed chores, contribution counts, and earned reward history where applicable, without retaining the deleted person's identity.
- Replace the deleted person's name and identifying attribution in Home-visible historical activity with the neutral label **“Former member”**. Remove profile links, avatars, account identifiers, and other personal identifiers from these Home-visible records. Avoid exposing identity through activity details, exports, leaderboards, search, notifications, or Ora responses.
- Historical counts and aggregate statistics remain accurate. Do not silently erase completed Home activities or reassign credit to another member. If multiple deleted users appear, avoid linkable stable identifiers in member-facing displays unless required for a specific approved product behavior.
- This rule applies to **permanently deleted accounts**, not ordinary Home departure, where limited historical display-name attribution was approved. During the 30-day recovery window, do not prematurely apply irreversible anonymization; account restoration remains possible.
- Define deletion/anonymization coverage and verification across all Home modules, cached views, exports, backups, and integrations in supporting specifications, subject to legally required retention and technical recovery safeguards.

### Confirmed limited historical attribution after Home departure (12 October 2026)
- When a member voluntarily leaves or is removed from a Home, preserve **limited historical attribution** for completed **Home-owned** activities, including past completed chores, task contributions and relevant rewards/points history. This may include the former member’s display name as recorded for the historical contribution.
- Do **not** retain a live link exposing the departed member’s profile, private information, current profile details, or personally owned items. Historical attribution is a minimal Home-owned record, not continued membership or access to their account.
- Keep future assignments unassigned and flagged for reassignment under the previously approved rule. Do not restore access to personally owned items previously shared into the Home merely to preserve history.
- **Account deletion is a distinct case:** after permanent deletion, preserve Home-owned activity records and totals but anonymize the person as “Former member” and remove identifying profile references, as specified above.
- Apply these limits consistently across Home activity views, leaderboards, exports, search, Ora, wall displays and caches, subject to the viewer’s Home permissions.

### Confirmed personal-to-Home sharing
- Personal items are **private by default**.
- A user may share an item with one, several, or all of their Homes using a checkbox-based selection.
- The user's default Home is preselected in the sharing interface, but the selection is editable before confirming.
- Sharing to multiple Homes retains **one underlying item**, not duplicated independent copies. Authorized changes synchronize across every shared Home.
- Access must be revoked appropriately when sharing is removed or membership changes.

### Confirmed dashboard ownership
- Personal dashboard layout belongs to and is configured by the user.
- Home dashboard layout is shared, with edits restricted to authorized Home members.
- Layouts adapt responsively to device sizes.
- Wall-display mode has its own layout rather than inheriting the standard dashboard verbatim.

### General security rules

- People may use Madeora individually or in a shared Home.
- Distinguish user-owned data, Home-owned data, and information explicitly shared with others.
- Permission checks must be enforced in backend operations, not merely hidden in the interface.
- Shared templates and changes are available only to appropriately authorized members.
- Ora and integrations must operate within the same permission boundaries as the user.
- Shared displays must not expose sensitive personal content by default.
- Define remaining invitation settings, member-removal authorization and task-reassignment edge cases, transfer edge cases, and audit behavior before implementation; the core ownership-transfer and departing-content policies are confirmed.

## 7. Onboarding and customization

### Agreed customization behavior
- Light and dark appearance settings; subtle neumorphism used selectively, never at the expense of readability.
- Madeora has a recognizable main brand color, while users may choose their own accents.
- Each Home member has an identifying color; automatic/manual assignment and conflict rules need precise supporting specification.
- Ora keeps a **fixed signature appearance** independent of member-selected accent palettes.
- Widget customization includes configurable individual widgets and configurable custom widgets (previously selected **option B**).
- Users can create personal dashboard templates and share them into Homes with permission controls (previously selected **option B**).
- Dashboard editing uses a **dedicated edit mode** and a **contextual side panel** for configuration.



- Setup should be **skippable**, with a useful initial experience.
- Offer curated presets and bundles to help users choose relevant modules.
- Allow later activation/deactivation of modules without losing user-owned information unexpectedly.
- Remember selected Home/personal context and relevant UI preferences.
- Offer curated color palettes plus a custom accent picker.
- Generate accessible light/dark accent variants; support automatic or manual Home/member colors.
- Provide contextual feature discovery rather than a lengthy compulsory tour.
- Recover gracefully when modules, integrations, or permissions are unavailable.

## 8. Data interoperability and imports

Cross-module connections are part of the product, not optional polish. Examples:
- Meal plan → recipe → shopping list.
- Home chores → task completion → progress/rewards.
- Calendar → daily dashboard → Ora planning summary.
- Pet care → household task/reminder → dashboard widget.

For imports (calendar, shopping lists, recipes, and household data):
1. Choose source and supported format.
2. Preview proposed records and mapping.
3. Validate fields and show errors clearly.
4. Detect possible duplicates conservatively; let the user resolve ambiguity.
5. Confirm import and report actual outcomes.
6. Support safe recovery where feasible.

Do not claim integration or import success before persistence/sync is confirmed.

## 9. Core user journeys to test

1. New user signs up, skips setup, and still has a useful dashboard.
2. User creates a Home, invites a member, and switches between personal and Home contexts.
3. Member adds a shared task, another authorized member completes it, and the dashboard updates.
4. User plans a meal, adds needed ingredients to shopping, and checks items off.
5. User customizes widget layout, refreshes, and sees preferences retained.
6. User creates and shares a dashboard template; unauthorized members cannot apply restricted changes.
7. User connects an external calendar, loses authorization, and still sees native events plus a truthful integration warning.
8. User asks Ora to reorganize tasks, reviews an editable proposal, confirms, and can recover from the change.
9. User opens wall-display mode and sees only appropriate shared information.
10. Imports preview data, detect potential duplicates, and accurately report successes and failures.

## 10. MVP quality bar and delivery discipline

**Previously agreed requirement:** every capability listed in the eventual MVP scope must be implemented end to end before declaring MVP complete. No nonfunctional placeholder flows. Each feature needs testable acceptance criteria, permissions, loading/empty/error states, and integration fallbacks where applicable.

### Agreed MVP scope (confirmed 12 October 2026)

**All three previously proposed development phases are part of the MVP.** Phase boundaries describe implementation sequencing, **not** optional post-MVP releases. The MVP is not complete until every included capability works end to end, with cross-module connections and complete user-journey testing.

**Explicitly included and required:**
- The features agreed across all three proposed phases (their original per-phase breakdown should be copied verbatim from the original phase plan if available; do not invent phase assignments).
- **Google Calendar bidirectional workflow**: automatic inbound event synchronization, creation of events from Madeora into Google Calendar, and explicit per-calendar personal/Home visibility choices.
- Dedicated **Home-managed pet profiles**, plus a **separate pet-task widget**.
- **Rewards/points in both personal and Home contexts**.
- Complete functional integrations between modules wherever the workflows require them.
- Clear, testable acceptance criteria for every MVP capability.
- Real working functionality; **no placeholders, nonfunctional mockups, or incomplete user journeys** counted as complete.

**Explicitly excluded from MVP:**
- Bill management.
- Package tracking.
- Smart-home control.

The module catalogue in this document provides product detail; this section records the binding inclusion/exclusion decisions. The original exact item-by-item three-phase breakdown was not recoverable from the accessible earlier-chat record during this revision. **Do not invent phase assignments or treat this gap as permission to reduce the agreed MVP.** This is a documentation gap, **not** an undecided MVP scope.

Recommended engineering acceptance checklist:
- Backend data model and authorization are implemented.
- CRUD and relevant cross-module workflows function end to end.
- UI supports normal, empty, loading, error, and permission-denied states.
- Integrations have authorization, stale-data, failure, and disconnect handling.
- Tests cover key user journeys and shared-data boundaries.
- Responsive, accessibility, and persistence behaviors are verified.

## 11. Technical architecture boundary (separate decision document)

The project specification defines **what** Madeora must do and the constraints it must honor. Architecture, technology selection, deployment, database design, event contracts, and detailed implementation tradeoffs belong in a **separate technical decision document**. Do not silently turn suggestions into approved architecture.

Implementation should proceed **dependency-first**, so shared foundations (identity, Home membership, permissions, data ownership, and context) exist before features that depend on them. If a requirement is ambiguous or contradictory, **stop and ask** instead of making a product decision without approval.

## 11.1 Documentation hierarchy
- `project.md` — overarching product vision, scope, binding decisions, global rules, visual language, and document index.
- Supporting product specifications — implementation-ready feature detail, workflows, state transitions, validation, permissions, edge/error cases, cross-module behavior, and acceptance criteria.
- Separate architecture/technical decision record — technology choices, contracts, deployment, data modeling, and rationale.

**Global privacy, security, and sharing rules override module-specific descriptions** in case of conflict. Explicitly flag conflicts rather than silently selecting an interpretation.

## 11.2 Calendar sharing — approved privacy policy

**Confirmed from original conversation screenshots (12 October 2026):** Users can select Google calendars for personal use or sharing with individual Homes; shared calendar events may show their actual titles, rather than only busy status. Connected calendars synchronize automatically and events created in Madeora are written back to Google Calendar.

**Approved decision (12 October 2026, option A):** Unshared calendar events are **completely private** to their owner. Other Home members must not see the event, its title, description, location, participants, start/end times, or even a generic busy/unavailable indicator. There is no implicit busy-only sharing. Connecting Google Calendar, belonging to a Home, or viewing a shared dashboard must never expose an unshared event. Explicitly sharing a selected calendar or event with a Home enables only the visibility that the owner authorized, subject to Home permissions. Apply this consistently to calendar views, widgets, Ora, notifications, wall displays, search, and API responses.

### Non-Ora module specification review (13 October 2026)
- **Review direction confirmed:** Pause further Ora feature decisions and prioritize unresolved product-level requirements in other MVP modules. This is a planning direction, not approval of new module behavior.
- **Pets:** Define required/optional pet-profile fields, medical and care information, scheduled responsibilities, historical records, and privacy/permissions beyond the already-approved Home-owned profiles and separate pet-task widget.
- **Photos (formerly Memories):** Core photo-gallery and album scope is approved; specify album ownership, sharing, uploads, limits, and photo-widget selection without introducing scrapbook features.
- **Shopping:** Define multi-list management, collaborative updates, checked-item lifecycle, and archiving/deletion behavior.
- **Recipes and meal planning:** Define recipe fields, servings, meal slots, conflicts, and planning workflows independently of Ora.
- **Habits and goals:** Define tracking schedules, missed days, streaks, goal progress, and interactions with rewards.
- **Dashboard:** Define widget sizing/placement constraints, per-widget configuration, and custom-widget limits.
- **Wall display:** Define device/session security, interaction boundaries, supported hardware, and shared-screen privacy.
- **Notifications:** Define channels, opt-in preferences, quiet hours, grouping, and delivery/failure states.
- **Priority:** Begin with pet-profile scope; present explicit choices for approval before adding binding behavior. Existing global privacy and Home-permission rules remain authoritative.

### Confirmed configurable meal-planner dashboard widgets (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Meal Planner dashboard widgets offer three user-configurable views: **Today**, **Selected day**, and a **Compact week**. The widget displays the current personal space or Home and its configured, enabled meal slots in the approved order; a selected day is explicit and can be changed without changing the underlying meal schedule. Today follows the relevant context's local calendar date; the week follows the configured start-of-week convention.
- **Content and navigation:** Show each planned recipe or custom meal with its meal-slot label, and clearly distinguish empty slots, unavailable recipes and custom entries. Provide permission-checked navigation to the full Meal Planner and to recipe details where the viewer can access them. A shared recipe's visibility does not imply access to its originating Home or other shared Homes. Never leak private ingredient, note or recipe metadata through previews or compact layouts.
- **Quick actions:** Where authorized, offer clear controls to add, edit, reschedule or remove an individual meal from the widget, subject to the existing meal planner's occupancy rules, recurrence choices and explicit conflict resolution. In recurring series, ask whether an edit affects only this occurrence or this and future occurrences. Destructive/replacement changes require the existing review/confirmation safeguards. Users without edit rights see read-only content and navigation they are entitled to use.
- **View configuration and layout:** Save each widget instance's chosen mode, selected date where applicable and display preferences without changing other widgets, Homes or members' meal-slot configuration. Today and selected-day modes present a readable slot-by-slot summary; compact-week mode shows each day with scannable meal labels, with progressive disclosure on narrow screens and wall displays. Never hide a planned meal without indicating that more entries are available.
- **Synchronization and safety:** Reflect confirmed changes and recurring occurrences consistently across widget and planner views. Recheck current permissions, meal versions, slot availability and conflicting entries at action confirmation; stale or concurrent changes trigger review instead of silent overwrite. Support loading, empty, offline/disconnected, failed-save, revoked-access and unavailable-recipe states with accessible keyboard and touch controls. No drag-and-drop meal-board editing or recipe suggestions are authorized by this choice.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Today, selected-day and week view; custom slot order; recipes and custom meals; recurring occurrences; read-only member; edit one vs future recurrence; occupied destination slot; recipe access revoked; date/week boundary; personal/Home context switch; multiple widgets with independent settings; narrow/mobile/wall display; stale edits and retry without duplicates; keyboard/screen-reader usability.

### Confirmed meal-plan date-range shopping transfer (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized members can choose an inclusive start and end date in a specific personal space or Home, collect ingredients from recipe-linked meals planned within that range, and transfer selected ingredients to **one explicitly selected, accessible shopping list** after reviewing and confirming an editable preview. This is user-initiated, never automatic on planning or recurrence.
- **Source selection:** Show the date range, context, included meal occurrences (including materialized recurring occurrences and exceptions), recipe names where visible, selected servings and the destination list. Count each actual planned occurrence once; do not infer extra meals from a recurrence or include removed/skipped occurrences. Include only recipes the acting member may currently view. Skip custom meal entries that have no structured ingredients, and explain the omission. Missing, deleted or inaccessible recipes are visibly excluded without revealing restricted ingredients; do not silently fabricate replacements.
- **Quantities:** Apply the approved proportional numeric ingredient scaling only when the recipe has a valid original serving yield and the meal has an explicitly selected valid serving count; otherwise use the recipe's stated quantities and label the absence of scaling. Keep ingredient units, packaging text and notes unchanged. Do not convert units, estimate missing quantities, deduct pantry stock, assume purchases or automatically merge similar ingredients.
- **Editable review:** Present ingredient rows grouped or traceable by meal/recipe, with their amounts, units, destination and any duplicate-active-item warnings. Members can exclude rows and edit names, quantities, units and notes before confirmation. Where repeated ingredients appear, preserve separate rows by default; any combining must be a deliberate reviewed manual edit, never a hidden aggregate. Offer clear empty, partial-success and excluded-source states.
- **Confirmation and permissions:** Revalidate date range, meal/recipe access, current recipe versions, selected servings, shopping-list write permission, list status and destination context at confirmation. Never transfer ingredients into another Home or personal space without separately authorized cross-context behavior; shared recipe visibility does not grant shopping-list access. If the preview becomes stale, require a refresh/review rather than silently using changed ingredients. Do not silently overwrite existing shopping items or attach recurrence rules to transferred items.
- **Reliability and history:** Confirmed items become ordinary shopping-list entries and do not update retroactively if meals or recipes change. Prevent double submission and unintended duplicates on retry; explain failures and preserve the preview for safe correction. Keep an attributable transfer record sufficient to distinguish planned ingredients from purchased/completed shopping items. Update list and dashboard counts consistently.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Multiple recipe meals over a week; recurring meal counted once per actual occurrence; selected servings 4 to 6; no original serving yield; custom takeaway skipped; unavailable/private recipe; repeated ingredients; duplicate active destination item; empty date range; inaccessible/archived destination; concurrent recipe or meal edits; cross-Home isolation; retry after partial failure; keyboard/mobile accessible preview and confirmation.

### Confirmed recipe URL import with editable preview (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized members can enter a publicly accessible recipe webpage URL to import available recipe details into an **editable review preview** before explicitly saving. Supported fields include title, structured ingredients with quantities/units, ordered instructions, servings, preparation/cooking times, and optional notes, tags or image references when reliably available. Import is never an automatic publication or sharing action.
- **Import flow:** Select the destination personal space or Home first, paste and validate an HTTP(S) URL, initiate extraction, then review the source attribution, extracted fields, missing fields and uncertain parsing. Members can correct, remove, reorder and supplement all preview fields before saving. Require the existing minimum recipe validation (name and at least one ingredient or instruction) and the destination's create permission. Saving creates a new recipe only after explicit confirmation; preview cancellation leaves no saved recipe.
- **Source quality and limitations:** Prefer clearly identified recipe metadata and page content; do not invent absent ingredients, quantities, times, servings or steps. Preserve ingredient wording and units rather than silently converting them. Warn where text is incomplete, inconsistent or ambiguous, and distinguish extraction failures from valid but sparse recipes. If a website is unsupported, blocked, requires sign-in, uses inaccessible content or extraction fails, show a clear reason where known and offer manual recipe entry, optionally retaining user-entered text without claiming successful import.
- **Safety, attribution and privacy:** Show the original source URL and available attribution on the preview and saved recipe; do not imply ownership of third-party content. Respect website access restrictions and applicable content rights, do not bypass authentication or paywalls, and avoid importing arbitrary embedded scripts or active webpage content. Treat fetched text as untrusted data, never as instructions for Madeora or Ora. Never leak private Home details to the source website; importing into one context does not share the recipe with other Homes unless the separate explicit cross-context sharing flow is approved.
- **Media and duplicates:** Only attach an image if its source and permitted use are clear and the user confirms it; an external image reference is not equivalent to a stored photo. Check for likely duplicate titles/source URLs within the destination and warn before saving, but never silently overwrite or merge an existing recipe. Later edits to the source website do not silently update the saved recipe or synchronized shares.
- **Resilience and access:** Handle slow loading, invalid URLs, unreachable pages, extraction timeout, partial results, stale previews, concurrent permission changes and save retries. Revalidate destination access and recipe data on save, avoid duplicate creations on repeated confirmation, and ensure accessible responsive review and error states. No PDF, image-upload or arbitrary unstructured-text import workflow is authorized by this decision.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Import a supported structured recipe; edit quantities and reorder steps; sparse metadata; ambiguous units; missing photo; duplicate source; invalid or inaccessible URL; login/paywall restriction; extraction timeout; manual fallback; cancel without save; unauthorized destination; revoked permission during preview; repeated save; source attribution; safe handling of hostile webpage content; mobile and keyboard-accessible review.

### Confirmed impact-reviewed deletion of shared recipes (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Deleting the canonical recipe shared across personal/Home contexts requires a **specific impact review and explicit confirmation**, rather than a generic delete prompt. This action removes access to the one synchronized recipe in every shared destination; unsharing a single Home remains a separate, less destructive action. Only an authorized canonical owner/manager with delete permission may initiate deletion; being a recipient or editor alone does not confer deletion rights.
- **Review before deletion:** Identify the originating context, the number and identity of affected destination Homes **only to an actor already authorized to manage those shares**, and the consequences for future meal-plan entries referencing the recipe. Show counts and useful dates of affected future occurrences where the actor may view them, without disclosing another Home's private meal plans, members or details. For inaccessible plans, use a privacy-safe impact warning rather than revealing protected information. Explicitly distinguish upcoming plans, historical entries, favorites and any currently open recipe-editing session.
- **Confirmation and effect:** Require an unambiguous confirm action after reviewing impacts; offer Cancel and, where appropriate, **Unshare from one Home** as an alternative. Do not automatically convert shared recipes into independent copies, substitute meals, delete meal-plan records, remove shopping items or rewrite already-confirmed ingredient transfers. Once deleted, the recipe is unavailable to all contexts and any referencing meal plans/widgets show the existing safe unavailable-recipe state; historical meal records remain subject to their own retention rules.
- **Seven-day Trash and restore:** Place the canonical recipe and exclusively attached media in recoverable Trash for **seven days**, subject to the approved whole-Home/account 30-day recovery precedence. Show deletion and scheduled expiry dates and the permitted restore action. Restore only with current owner permissions, validate that the originating context still exists, and review whether previous shares should be reinstated; never silently grant access to Homes or members whose authorization changed. Preserve references where valid and safely refresh affected meal plans, favorites, search and widgets. After expiry, do not promise restoration.
- **Concurrency and safeguards:** Revalidate canonical ownership, deletion permission, current shares, future-plan impact and recipe version immediately before confirmation. If the preview is stale, require a fresh impact review. Handle a recipe currently locked for editing without silently discarding an active editor's work; warn, coordinate or safely invalidate the lock with clear feedback and an audit event. Prevent duplicate deletion on retries and unauthorized restoration; record who deleted/restored, when and which authorized shares were affected without leaking cross-Home information.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Recipe shared with two Homes; future meals in one or several Homes; actor can manage shares but cannot read another Home's meal plan; no future plans; destination member attempts canonical delete; single-Home unshare versus global delete; recipe locked for editing; stale preview or revoked permission; interrupted/retried deletion; Trash visibility and seven-day expiry; restoration with changed memberships/shares; deleted recipe referenced by recurring meals; mobile, keyboard and screen-reader confirmation.

### Confirmed private recipe drafts and explicit publishing (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Members may save **incomplete recipes as persistent private drafts** and explicitly publish them into a personal or Home recipe library for which they have creation permission. A draft is visible only to its creator, including when its intended destination is a Home; it is not listed in the shared recipe library, search, favorites, meal planning, shopping transfers, dashboard widgets, or Ora actions.
- **Draft creation and content:** Allow Save draft before the published-recipe minimum is met, including a provisional name or entirely incomplete ingredients/instructions. Clearly distinguish Save draft, Continue editing, Discard draft and Publish. Provide a private My drafts view with last-edited date, intended destination and an easy resume action. Drafts remain private across devices for the signed-in creator; never show them on wall displays.
- **Publication:** Require an explicit Publish action and a clear review of the destination and resulting audience. Enforce the existing published-recipe minimum (name and at least one ingredient or instruction), validate fields, media and source attribution as applicable, and recheck membership and creation permissions immediately before publishing. Publishing to a Home must not silently create shares to other Homes; any cross-context sharing requires its own separately authorized workflow. Once published, the recipe follows originating-context ownership and existing recipe permissions.
- **Changes and permissions:** Drafts may be edited, renamed, redirected to another authorized destination, or discarded by their creator. Losing access to an intended Home must not leak or publish the draft there; offer a permitted personal destination or another authorized Home after review. A deleted Home or account follows applicable privacy/deletion rules. Never expose drafts to Home managers solely because the intended destination is their Home.
- **No version-history loophole:** A private draft is an **unpublished recipe**, not a historical version or branch of a published recipe. Do not use drafts to retain prior saved recipe bodies, revert published edits or circumvent the approved latest-only recipe policy. Editing a published recipe continues to use the exclusive editing lock and its normal Save/Cancel flow; unsaved local recovery for interrupted editing is not published recipe version history.
- **Reliability and acceptance:** Prevent duplicate publication on double-click/retry, handle autosave or manual-save failure without claiming success, warn before losing unsaved draft changes, and handle simultaneous sessions of the same creator without silent overwrite.
