Test empty/incomplete drafts, resuming on another device, creator-only privacy, Home permission revoked, destination deleted, publication validation, explicit audience confirmation, draft discard, repeated publish, wall-display exclusion, accessibility and responsive layouts.

### Confirmed latest-only recipe content without version history (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Recipes retain **only the latest successfully saved content**. Do not provide user-facing recipe version history, past-content comparisons, revision browsing, restore-to-previous-version actions, named versions, or drafts/branches as historical recipe revisions. Correcting an accidental saved change requires manually editing the current recipe.
- **Editing safety remains mandatory:** The approved exclusive one-editor lock, current record revision/concurrency token, permission revalidation, stale-save rejection, and safe handling of interrupted saves remain in force. A technical revision identifier used to reject stale updates is **not** a stored, recoverable previous recipe version.
- **Audit distinction:** Retain only the minimal attributable security/action audit metadata required by existing global audit rules (for example, editor and time, where authorized), without retaining or exposing previous recipe bodies as a disguised version-history feature. Reconcile any earlier references to recipe 'history', 'recovery' or 'versions' as current-record concurrency checks, deletion/Trash recovery, or metadata-only audit—not content-version restoration.
- **Cross-context implications:** A successful edit replaces the visible canonical recipe content for all authorized sharing contexts. Explain that saving shared changes affects all viewers and cannot be undone by reverting to an older saved recipe version. Existing meal-plan records and confirmed shopping transfers must not be silently rewritten. The seven-day Trash recovery for a **deleted whole recipe** remains available as separately approved; it does not restore prior ingredient/instruction revisions.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Single editor saves; accidental ingredient change requires manual correction; no history/restore UI; stale lock or save rejected; authorized change attribution without old content; synchronized shared recipe updated; existing meal/shopping snapshots unchanged; deleted recipe recoverable within Trash policy; permissions revoked before save.

### Confirmed exclusive recipe editing locks (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** A canonical recipe permits **one active editor at a time**, including when the recipe is explicitly shared across multiple Homes. Other authorized members can continue viewing the recipe but cannot enter an editable session until the lock is released. A lock is on the canonical recipe, not separately per Home, and is not an authorization grant.
- **Acquisition and visibility:** Acquire a short-lived exclusive editing lock when an authorized member enters edit mode, not merely when viewing a recipe. Display an understandable read-only state, the fact that editing is in progress, and an option to retry when available; avoid exposing the editor's identity to viewers who lack permission to know it across contexts. The editor sees lock status and a clear Save, Cancel or Finish action.
- **Expiry and recovery:** Release the lock on successful save, explicit cancel or leaving edit mode where reliably detectable. Use a bounded inactivity/connection timeout so crashes, abandoned browser tabs, lost connections and expired sessions cannot block editing indefinitely. Warn an active editor before an inactivity expiry when possible. An expired or revoked lock cannot authorize a later save; retain unsaved local draft changes where safe and offer a reviewed retry, without overwriting intervening changes.
- **Safety:** Recheck canonical recipe version, active lock ownership and current edit permissions on every save. If permission is revoked, the recipe is deleted/unshared, or a lock is lost, prevent the save and explain recovery options. Lock acquisition must be atomic so simultaneous attempts cannot both succeed. Never allow an unauthorized member to force-unlock or edit a shared recipe. If an administrator/owner recovery action is later provided, it must be explicitly permissioned, audited and disclose risk to the current editor.
- **Cross-feature behavior:** A lock blocks edits to the recipe's shared canonical content, not read-only use in search, meal planning or shopping previews. Shared viewers see only committed recipe changes after a save. Existing meal plans and confirmed shopping transfers remain unchanged, and a locked recipe does not freeze unrelated Home activities.
- **States and acceptance criteria:** Two authorized editors in one Home; editors in different shared Homes; view while locked; lock released by save/cancel; browser crash, idle timeout and disconnect; simultaneous acquisition; stale editor attempts to save after expiry; permissions revoked mid-edit; unsharing or deleting a locked recipe; mobile/tab navigation; accessible lock and retry messaging; no lost or silently overwritten edits.

### Confirmed context-owned canonical recipes and membership departure (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Every canonical recipe belongs to the **personal space or Home in which it was originally created**, not automatically to the member who entered it. A personal recipe remains owned by its originating personal space; a Home-created recipe remains owned by that Home. The creator is recorded for attribution and audit, but creator status alone does not confer perpetual ownership or access.
- **Departure from a Home:** When a creator or editor leaves, is removed from, or loses access to the originating Home, that Home retains its recipes, their content, history, photos and existing authorized cross-context shares. The departing member loses Home-derived viewing, editing, sharing and deletion privileges immediately; any outstanding recipe editing lock is revoked safely and subsequent saves are blocked. Do not remove recipes from other Homes' meal plans merely because their creator departed.
- **Ongoing administration:** Authorized managers of the originating Home control the canonical recipe's continued availability, editor permissions, sharing destinations, unsharing and impact-reviewed deletion according to the existing Home role rules. A recipient Home's managers cannot claim ownership or grant onward shares solely because their Home can view or edit a recipe. Personal-space owners retain equivalent authority for personally originated recipes.
- **Shared access after departure:** Existing authorized destination Homes continue to access the synchronized canonical recipe subject to their current sharing grants and membership permissions, even when the original creator no longer belongs to the origin Home. Never make continued sharing dependent on a departing creator's account or session. A departing member's separate membership in a recipient Home grants only that recipient's currently authorized access, not ownership or privileged origin metadata.
- **Lifecycle and exceptional states:** Origin-Home deletion, account deletion and recovery follow the separately approved context lifecycle and recovery rules; this decision does not create orphaned personal recipes, ownership transfer, independent Home copies or automatic publication. If the originating context becomes unavailable, use the existing unavailable-recipe and recovery behavior rather than silently transferring canonical ownership. Protect private cross-Home details in notifications, search, favorites and audit views.
- **Validation and acceptance:** Check creator leaves originating Home; Home manager remains and edits recipe; two recipient Homes retain existing shares; creator retains membership only in a recipient Home; creator loses all memberships; revoked editor with an active lock or unsaved draft; origin manager removes one share; unauthorized recipient attempts ownership or deletion; origin context deletion/recovery; concurrent membership removal and save; private Home identity remains concealed from unauthorized recipients.

### Confirmed explicitly synchronized cross-context recipe sharing (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C:** An authorized recipe owner/manager may explicitly share one recipe from its originating personal space or Home with individually selected other Homes to which they have appropriate access. A shared recipe remains **one canonical record**: authorized edits are synchronized to every currently authorized context, rather than producing independent copies. Never enable sharing automatically because a member belongs to multiple Homes.
- **Share controls:** Show the recipe's origin, each selected destination Home, effective visibility, permitted editors and the consequences of synchronized edits before confirmation. Adding or removing destinations is an explicit, permission-checked operation. A Home cannot share onward to other Homes without appropriate authority over the canonical recipe; recipients cannot infer or browse other destination Homes.
- **Permissions:** Enforce both canonical recipe permissions and each destination Home's membership and access rules on every view, edit, search, widget, meal-plan selection and Ora action. Editing rights are not implicitly granted by visibility; owners/managers may authorize editors per destination subject to global permissions. Losing membership or authorization immediately removes access, including search results, favorites and cached details. Private metadata and origin membership must not leak through sharing.
- **Synchronized changes:** Edits to ingredients, quantities, instructions, photos, notes and tags update the canonical recipe for all authorized viewers; prominently indicate that editing a shared recipe affects every shared context. Revalidate edit authority and version on save, prevent silent concurrent overwrites, and retain only attributable action-audit metadata under the latest-only recipe policy; do not retain recoverable previous recipe content. Context-local favorites and meal-planner records remain independent; changing a recipe never silently rewrites historical meals or confirmed shopping transfers.
- **Unsharing and deletion:** Unsharing a destination immediately revokes that Home's access without deleting the canonical recipe or other shares. Existing meal entries referencing a now-inaccessible recipe retain only safely displayable meal information and an unavailable-recipe state, never private ingredients or notes. Deleting/restoring the canonical recipe follows global Trash and recovery rules, with clear warnings about affected Homes; a destination Home cannot delete the canonical recipe merely by removing its share.
- **Media and lifecycle:** Photos follow the canonical recipe's media/storage and permission controls; sharing does not create unrestricted photo-library access or independent media copies. Show loading, stale, offline, revoked-access and failed-share states. Apply idempotent confirmation to prevent duplicate shares and safe recovery from interrupted changes.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Personal-to-one-Home share; Home-to-another-Home share with appropriate authority; multiple Homes with different view/edit rights; edit synchronization; destination-only favorite; conflicting edits; share removal; member leaves Home; canonical deletion/restoration; inaccessible recipe in existing meal plan; revoked photo access; duplicate share request; offline/stale confirmation; mobile and accessible sharing controls.

### Confirmed configurable recipe dashboard widgets (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Recipe dashboard widgets support a configurable source mode: **My Favorites**, **Recently Added**, **Highest Rated**, or **Random Recipe**. A widget is scoped explicitly to its selected personal space or Home and never aggregates inaccessible recipes from other contexts. Members can configure more than one recipe widget with independent settings.
- **Presentation:** Show a compact, readable recipe card or selection with title, its single optional cover photo (or accessible placeholder), and a clear action to open the recipe detail. Display a contextual rating and count for Highest Rated where available, but never expose other members' individual ratings or cross-Home averages. Respect responsive mobile, desktop and wall-display layouts and accessible image alternatives.
- **Selection rules:** Favorites uses the viewing member's own favorites within the active accessible context; Recently Added sorts by creation date with stable tie-breaking; Highest Rated sorts by the privacy-aware average from currently eligible ratings in the active context, with a documented tie-break and sensible unrated/empty state; Random Recipe selects from currently accessible published recipes, with a deliberate refresh action where offered rather than unexpected disruptive changes. Do not infer preferences or generate personalized recommendations.
- **Permissions and lifecycle:** Only currently published, non-deleted recipes visible to the viewer may appear. Private drafts, inaccessible shares and recipes from other Homes must not leak through titles, photos, counts, ratings or stale cached cards. Recheck access on opening; revoked or deleted recipes disappear or show a safe unavailable state. Editing, unsharing, Home departure, rating changes and favorites updates refresh relevant widget views without silently switching context.
- **Configuration and states:** Clearly identify the chosen mode and context; provide an editable widget configuration, meaningful empty-state guidance (including no favorites or no ratings), loading, offline, error and stale-data states. A random choice is not a recommendation or automated meal-plan entry. No recipe editing, shopping transfer or meal-plan mutation occurs merely by displaying or opening a widget.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Each of four modes; favorites differing by member; recent sorting and ties; context-local highest-rated ordering and unrated recipes; random refresh; one/no cover image; multiple widgets; personal versus Home selection; cross-Home shared recipe visibility; revoked sharing and deleted recipe; private draft excluded; narrow and wall displays; keyboard and screen-reader access.

### Confirmed single cover photo per recipe (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** A published recipe or private recipe draft supports **at most one optional cover photo**. This is the recipe's sole image; no additional gallery, step-attached images or multiple-photo upload workflow is in scope. Interpret earlier general references to recipe "photos" as this single optional cover photo.
- **Presentation:** Display the cover photo on recipe details and suitable library cards, with responsive crop/fit and accessible descriptive text where appropriate. Cooking Mode may show the cover in its overview, but must not imply step-specific photography. Missing photos use a neutral placeholder without preventing recipe creation, import, publication, meal planning or shopping transfers.
- **Editing and permissions:** Authorized editors may upload, replace or remove the cover while holding the approved exclusive recipe editing lock; save only the approved current state and respect latest-only recipe content. On synchronized cross-Home shares, the same canonical cover appears wherever the recipe is accessible, without granting unrelated Photos-library access. Draft photos remain private until explicit publication.
- **Imports and lifecycle:** A supported URL import may propose one source image in its editable preview, but must not silently download, publish or attach an image without the required confirmation and permitted use. Apply media limits, storage allowances, security checks and recipe deletion/seven-day recovery rules. Failed uploads or revoked permissions must leave existing recipe content intact.
- **Acceptance criteria:** No-photo recipe; one cover upload; replace/remove; second upload replaces rather than adds; imported image reviewed or rejected; private draft; shared recipe image updates; inaccessible shared recipe; upload failure; mobile responsive display and accessible alternative text.

### Confirmed step-by-step recipe cooking mode (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Accessible published recipes offer an optional, distraction-reduced **Cooking Mode** displaying one ordered instruction step at a time with large legible text, prominent previous/next navigation, progress indication and an easy return to the full recipe. Keep the original recipe unchanged; Cooking Mode is a viewing and execution aid, not an edit or publish action. If a recipe has no instruction steps, explain that Cooking Mode is unavailable and provide the normal recipe view instead.
- **Ingredients:** Provide a readily accessible ingredient checklist alongside the steps, with the currently selected serving-size scaling applied where available. Checking an ingredient is **session-local preparation progress**, not a shopping-list completion, recipe modification or shared Home status. Allow checking/unchecking and clearly indicate progress; ingredient names without numeric quantities remain unchanged.
- **Screen awake:** Offer an explicit, optional keep-screen-awake control **only on supported devices/browsers**. Explain unavailable or denied capability without breaking Cooking Mode; respect device settings, battery and user choice, and release the request on exit or when no longer appropriate. Never imply that Madeora can override device sleep behavior.
- **Session and synchronization:** Preserve the current step and checklist during ordinary in-app navigation where practical, but do not claim durable cross-device progress or silently share cooking progress with other Home members. If a canonical shared recipe changes, is deleted, or access is revoked during a session, show a clear change/unavailable state and safely refresh or exit rather than exposing newly restricted content or silently replacing active instructions. Cooking Mode does not acquire the exclusive recipe editing lock.
- **Usability:** Support keyboard, screen-reader, touch and responsive phone/tablet/desktop layouts; large touch targets, visible focus, accessible step announcements, readable contrast and no mandatory gestures. Show loading, empty, permission-denied and interruption states. No timers, voice control or AI cooking guidance are approved by this choice.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Enter/exit cooking mode; navigate first/middle/last step; ingredient checklist and reset; serving scaling; recipe without steps; unsupported or denied screen-awake request; device sleep/resume; another member edits a shared recipe; access revoked mid-session; no shared checklist or shopping mutation; keyboard/screen-reader and mobile use.

### Confirmed optional personal recipe ratings (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** An authorized member may optionally rate an accessible published recipe with **one to five whole stars**. A member has at most one current rating per canonical recipe and may change or remove it. Ratings are separate from existing personal Favorites and do not modify recipe content, ownership or editing locks. Drafts cannot be rated.
- **Personal and shared presentation:** Show the member's own rating as personal feedback. Where a canonical recipe is shared, display an average calculated only from ratings by members currently authorized to view that recipe in the active context; show the contributing rating count and an unrated state where none qualify. Never expose individual other-member ratings or identities, infer a rating from favorites, or imply the average represents every Home. A rating from another Home is not included merely because the underlying recipe is synchronized.
- **Privacy and lifecycle:** Compute access-filtered aggregates without exposing other Homes' membership, rating counts or feedback. Membership departure, unsharing, permission revocation, deletion and restoration must update eligibility and displayed averages; inaccessible recipes cannot be rated or queried. A recipe owner or Home manager does not automatically gain visibility into individual private ratings. Preserve ratings only under existing privacy and retention rules; deleting a canonical recipe must not leave publicly visible ratings.
- **Behavior and reliability:** Permit rating from the recipe detail view and optionally the accessible library card; make selection, edit, removal, save/error and loading states clear. Revalidate permissions and current recipe identity when saving. Prevent duplicate ratings on double submit, concurrent devices or retries; refresh aggregates consistently without silent loss. Ratings do not trigger recipe edits, shopping changes, meal changes or recommendations.
- **Acceptance criteria:** One-to-five-star selection; changing and clearing a rating; same recipe viewed from two Homes with different eligible audiences; favorites independent of ratings; no ratings; only one eligible rater; member leaves Home; share removed; access revoked during save; draft and deleted recipe; simultaneous rating changes; retry/idempotence; accessible keyboard, touch and screen-reader controls.

### Confirmed recipe search, tags, favorites and sorting (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** The Recipes library supports searching accessible recipes by **recipe name and ingredient name**, filtering by explicitly assigned manual tags, marking favorites for quick access, and sorting by **name, date added, or date last updated**. Do not add automatically inferred categories, smart recommendations, saved filter collections, or automatic dietary claims.
- **Search and filtering:** Search matches partial names and ingredient names with forgiving case/diacritic handling; clearly indicate when a match came from an ingredient. Tag filters only use tags explicitly attached to recipes and may be combined with search. Define and label multi-tag matching consistently; provide clear/remove-filter controls, empty-result states and predictable pagination or loading for large libraries. Search must never reveal inaccessible personal/Home recipes, ingredient details or counts.
- **Favorites:** Authorized viewers may toggle a recipe as a personal favorite; favorites are **per member**, not a shared Home-wide endorsement, and do not alter recipe ownership or other members' views. Show a Favorites filter or dedicated view. A favorite reference does not preserve access after permissions change, nor resurrect deleted recipes; restored recipes may reappear only where the member still has access.
- **Sorting:** Offer name, newest/oldest added and most/least recently updated where relevant, with clear selected order and deterministic tie-breaking. Dates reflect actual recipe creation and substantive edits, not mere views, favorites, searches or meal-plan scheduling. Search, tag filters, favorite filters and sorting should work together consistently.
- **Permissions and behavior:** Enforce context-specific access before query results and metadata are returned; show the active personal space or Home. Recipe edits, tag changes, deletions and restorations update visible search results safely across devices. Handle concurrent favorites and edits without duplicates or silent loss; maintain accessible keyboard/screen-reader search, filter and favorite controls on mobile and desktop.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Search by recipe title and ingredient, partial and accented terms, combined search/tag/favorites, missing tags, empty matches, duplicate names, stable name/date sorting, personal favorites differing between Home members, recipe edit changing results, inaccessible/deleted/restored recipe, large library loading, permissions revoked during use and accessible responsive interaction.

### Confirmed print-friendly weekly meal-plan overview (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized members can open a **print-friendly weekly overview** for the currently selected personal space or Home. The overview displays the chosen calendar week's dates, enabled meal slots in their configured order, and planned recipe-linked or custom meal titles. This is a print presentation of existing plans, not a new meal plan, export file, public share link or Calendar integration.
- **Preview and options:** Show a readable, privacy-aware preview before invoking the browser/device print workflow. Allow the member to include or omit optional meal notes for that printout; default to notes hidden to avoid accidental disclosure. Clearly identify the selected week and context, including locale-appropriate weekday/date labels, and provide a way to cancel without printing.
- **Privacy:** Enforce current meal-plan permissions and recipe visibility when generating the preview and again immediately before printing where feasible. Never reveal private recipe ingredients, private notes or information from other Homes merely because a meal references a shared recipe. For inaccessible recipes, show a neutral unavailable-recipe label rather than exposing restricted details. Treat printouts as potentially visible to people outside Madeora and display a concise privacy reminder.
- **Layout and states:** Produce a clean high-contrast layout that works on common portrait/landscape paper sizes, with sensible page breaks, legible text, no unnecessary navigation/sidebar/buttons, and clear differentiation between empty and populated meal slots. Support long titles, custom slot names, multiple days, recurring occurrences, empty weeks, and notes that span pages without clipping. The printed content should be understandable in light or dark UI mode and without background graphics.
- **Consistency and reliability:** Print a stable snapshot of the explicitly previewed week, warn if the plan changed materially before printing, and offer refresh rather than silently switching contents. Do not create or alter meals, recurrence rules, shopping items, recipe permissions or stored notes. Printing itself needs no new recipe editing rights. Handle denied access, loading/error states and unavailable browser printing with a clear explanation and in-app fallback view.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Week with recipes and custom meals; empty week; renamed/reordered slots; optional notes on/off; long titles and multi-page print; recurring meals; inaccessible shared recipe; member viewing a different Home; permissions revoked during preview; concurrent plan edit; mobile and desktop print; keyboard and screen-reader preview; dark theme print legibility; no PDF/image/calendar-file export or external sharing created by this feature.

### Confirmed whole-week meal-plan copying (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized members may copy the planned meals from one selected calendar week to another week **within the same personal space or Home**. Preserve the original week unchanged. This is an explicitly initiated, reviewed copy action, not a recurring rule or reusable named template.
- **Scope and mapping:** Show the source and destination week dates, timezone, context, and a day-by-day mapping to the same weekday and configured meal-slot identity. Include both recipe-linked and standalone custom meals, including notes and explicitly chosen serving counts, subject to current recipe visibility and enabled destination slots. Never copy private content into a context where it is not permitted.
- **Editable preview:** Display each proposed destination meal with its source and destination day/slot, allow individual meals to be excluded, and clearly flag unavailable recipes, disabled slots, stale source items, and occupied destination slots. Do not assume that a recipe becoming inaccessible can be copied as visible recipe content.
- **Conflicts:** Never silently replace, merge or duplicate meals in an occupied destination slot. For each collision require an explicit, permission-checked decision to skip that copied meal, keep the existing meal, or replace the existing meal after impact confirmation. Do not offer a replacement when the member lacks the required rights. Confirm the complete resulting plan before committing.
- **Recurring source entries:** Copy only the visible concrete occurrences for the source week, **not** their underlying recurrence rules. Warn when destination slots are already filled by recurring occurrences; never create a second recurrence or alter an existing series. A copied meal is a standalone destination entry unless the member separately configures recurrence through the approved recurrence workflow.
- **Reliability and permissions:** Recheck source read rights, destination write rights, recipe access, slot availability, source/destination versions and collisions on confirmation. Handle simultaneous edits, duplicate submissions, partial failure and retries without duplicate meals or silent loss; show which entries succeeded or were skipped and provide safe recovery where applicable. Update planner and widgets consistently; do not automatically change shopping lists or Calendar.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Full week copied; exclude selected meals; recipe and custom entries; servings/notes preserved; source and destination weeks differ; occupied slots and explicit resolution; source recurring meals without copying recurrence; destination recurrence collision; disabled/renamed slot; inaccessible recipe; Home isolation; changed permissions; stale preview; interrupted write/retry; accessible mobile, keyboard and screen-reader review.

### Confirmed optional recurring meal-plan entries (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized members can optionally repeat either a recipe-linked meal or a standalone custom meal **daily, on selected weekdays, or weekly**. Nonrecurring meals remain the default. This does not authorize automatically generated menus, meal recommendations or automatic shopping-list additions.
- **Rule configuration:** A recurring meal has an explicit personal-space or Home context, meal slot, start date, recurrence cadence and relevant weekday selection, with an optional end date. Show an understandable preview of the next occurrences in the context's timezone; validate nonempty weekday selections, valid dates, enabled slots and accessible recipes. Retain the custom meal title/notes or recipe reference and any explicitly chosen servings without inventing missing details.
- **Control and changes:** Permit authorized members to view, edit, pause, resume and end a recurrence. Clearly distinguish editing **one occurrence** from editing **this and future occurrences**; a single-occurrence change becomes an exception without altering the underlying series. Preserve past meal history and exceptions when editing or ending the series; never retroactively rewrite past entries. Explain how moving a single occurrence affects its recurrence.
- **Occupancy and conflicts:** Recurrent meals respect the planner's established occupied-slot rules. Before creating or rescheduling a recurrence, preview collisions with existing single meals, other recurring meals, disabled slots and existing exceptions. Never silently overwrite, merge or duplicate an occupied date/slot. If a future occurrence becomes conflicted after the rule is saved, show a visible unresolved conflict and require an explicit authorized decision to skip, move or replace it; do not displace existing meals automatically.
- **Idempotence and recovery:** Each recurrence rule and scheduled date/slot has a stable occurrence identity. Multiple devices, concurrent edits, retries, delayed jobs, time changes and outages must not create duplicate entries or flood the planner with missed occurrences. Pausing stops future occurrences without deleting past records; resuming shows conflicts and future dates before confirmation. Deleted or inaccessible recipes display the established safe unavailable-recipe state and never expose private recipe information.
- **Permissions and integrations:** Apply context-specific Home/personal permissions and revalidate recipe visibility, slot configuration, recurrence version and write access before saving. Day/week views and dashboard widgets show recurring status and exceptions consistently. Ora may only act within its separately approved permission-aware preview scope. No recurrence event automatically writes shopping items or calendar events.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Daily breakfast, Monday/Wednesday/Friday meal, weekly Friday pizza, custom and recipe-linked series, edit one versus future, pause/resume/end, overlapping one-off meal, two colliding series, renamed/disabled slot, recipe deleted or access revoked, concurrent edits, outage/retry without duplicates, independent Homes, accessible mobile and keyboard controls.

### Confirmed recipe and custom meal-plan entries (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A meal-plan entry can reference an existing accessible saved recipe **or** be a standalone custom meal with a required descriptive title and optional notes (for example, “Pizza takeaway,” “Leftovers,” or “Dinner at friends”). Creating a custom meal must not require creating a recipe, and must not silently create one.
- **Placement and editing:** Both entry types use an explicit date, enabled meal slot, and personal/Home context. Clearly distinguish recipe-linked entries from custom meals in day/week views, widgets and edit forms. Authorized members may create, edit, move to another date/slot, or remove either type subject to existing slot occupancy, conflict and permission rules; replacing an occupied slot requires an explicit reviewed action rather than silent overwrite.
- **Recipe-linked entries:** Retain a durable reference to the recipe and present its current accessible details without silently changing previously confirmed planning choices, including any explicitly selected serving count. If the recipe is deleted, archived, changed or access is revoked, preserve a safe historical meal-plan record and display an honest unavailable-recipe state without leaking restricted recipe details. Do not convert the record into an invented custom meal or automatically update shopping lists.
- **Custom entries:** Require a nonblank meaningful title, support optional free-text notes, and avoid invented ingredients, serving yields, shopping-list contents or nutrition. Members may later explicitly replace a custom entry with a selected recipe after review; no automatic matching or recipe creation.
- **Permissions and privacy:** Enforce personal/Home isolation and separate rights to view or edit meal plans and recipes. A recipe's presence in a shared meal plan must not expose otherwise private recipe details. Revalidate slot availability, record version and permissions before saving; handle concurrent edits, offline retries and failures without duplicate entries or lost changes.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Plan a saved recipe and a custom takeaway meal; add optional notes; show both in day/week views and widgets; edit and move entries; reject empty custom titles; occupied-slot conflict; inaccessible/deleted recipe; distinct Home and personal plans; revoked permissions and concurrent edits; accessible mobile and keyboard flows; no automatic shopping changes.

### Confirmed configurable meal-planner slots (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each personal space and Home has its own meal-planner slot configuration. Start with **Breakfast, Lunch and Dinner** as the standard slots. Authorized members can enable additional named slots (for example, Snacks or Supper), rename slots, change their visible order, and disable slots where appropriate. This does not approve unrestricted multiple meals within one slot, participant scheduling, or automatically generated meal plans.
- **Configuration and scope:** Slot settings belong to the relevant personal space or Home, not to individual dashboard devices. Display the current context clearly. Only members with the applicable meal-planner configuration permission can change Home slots; other members can view and use slots as their permissions allow. Do not propagate slot changes across Homes or into personal space.
- **Slot identity and validation:** Keep a stable underlying slot identifier separate from its editable display name so renaming or reordering never loses planned meals or breaks Ora and widget references. Require nonblank, distinct display names within a given context, with reasonable length limits and accessible ordering controls. Preserve the standard slot identities even when their names change; newly enabled custom slots get distinct identities.
- **Changes affecting existing meals:** Renaming and reordering slots must preserve all scheduled entries. Before disabling a slot that contains future or historical meals, explain the impact and offer a safe resolution supported by the planner; never silently delete, overwrite, move or conceal existing meal entries. Preserve historical records and show an appropriate archived/disabled slot label where needed. Re-enabling should not duplicate entries.
- **Planner interactions:** Day and week views, creation/edit forms, dashboard widgets and Ora's approved meal-scheduling preview use the currently enabled slots and their configured order. An already-open form must refresh or require review if a slot becomes unavailable before save. Existing occupied-slot conflict checks and explicit replacement requirements still apply. No automatic shopping-list writes follow from slot configuration.
- **States and acceptance criteria:** Default Breakfast/Lunch/Dinner; add Snacks; rename Dinner; reorder slots; independent configurations for two Homes and a personal space; invalid/duplicate names; disable empty slot; attempt to disable occupied slot; retain historical meals; concurrent rename and scheduling; permissions revoked mid-edit; responsive mobile, keyboard and screen-reader configuration; safe cancellation and recovery.

### Confirmed recipe serving-size scaling (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When a recipe has a valid, explicitly recorded original serving yield, members may select a different positive serving count for viewing or using that recipe. Madeora proportionally scales **numeric ingredient quantities** by `selected servings / original servings`. This is a temporary presentation/use adjustment and **must not modify the saved recipe**, its original yield, or its original ingredient amounts.
- Show both original and selected servings and clearly distinguish scaled amounts from stored recipe values. Preserve each ingredient's original unit, packaging description, preparation notes, order and instructions; **no automatic unit conversions**, assumed package sizes, ingredient-specific cooking adjustments, nutritional calculations, or smart rounding. Preserve useful precision and avoid misleading fractional display; allow members to inspect the exact value when display formatting rounds.
- Ingredients without a numeric quantity (such as “salt to taste”) remain unchanged and visibly unscaled. Where a quantity cannot be reliably scaled, retain the original descriptive text and label it rather than inventing an amount. If the original serving yield is absent or invalid, disable proportional scaling and explain that an original yield must first be provided; never assume four servings or another default.
- For a recipe-to-shopping-list transfer, carry the **currently selected serving count and resulting numeric quantities** into the existing editable per-ingredient preview, allowing adjustments and exclusions before explicit confirmation. Do not silently revise already planned meals or previously transferred shopping items if the recipe or selected servings later change.
- If a meal-planner entry supports a selected serving count, show the selected count and scaled ingredient view consistently, without adding unapproved automatic shopping writes. Check current recipe access and version when generating a new transfer; a stale source requires refreshed review. Follow existing permissions, Home boundaries, confirmation, validation, and accessibility requirements.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Scale 4 to 6 and 4 to 2; fractions and decimal quantities; unchanged unit/notes; ingredient with no quantity; missing/zero original yield; invalid selected servings; no unintended rounding or conversion; recipe unchanged after closing the view; previewed shopping transfer with adjusted servings; edited recipe while preview is open; accessible mobile and desktop controls.

### Confirmed structured everyday recipe fields (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Recipes are structured everyday recipe records, not free-form cards only and not an advanced nutrition-analysis system. A recipe supports a name, ordered ingredients with optional numeric quantities and units, ordered preparation instructions, optional serving yield, optional preparation time and cooking time, optional photos, manually managed tags, and optional notes.
- **Minimum required information:** Require a nonblank recipe name and at least one meaningful ingredient or instruction so an empty titled record cannot masquerade as a usable recipe. Other fields are optional, including photos, tags, serving count, times, notes, individual ingredient quantities/units and step-specific details. Show incomplete information honestly; never infer missing quantities, timings, servings or instructions.
- **Ingredient structure:** Each ingredient has a required ingredient name when that row exists, optional positive quantity and supported unit, and optional descriptive text for packaging or preparation. Allow ingredients with no stated amount (for example, “salt to taste”). Preserve the author's stated values; do not automatically convert units or invent package sizes. Members can add, edit, reorder and remove ingredients with accessible alternatives to drag-and-drop.
- **Instructions and time:** Support ordered, editable instruction steps; steps need not have their own duration. Preparation and cooking time are distinct optional nonnegative durations and must not be silently estimated. Show a combined total only when the available values support an unambiguous total; otherwise label known components individually.
- **Servings:** Optional positive serving yield with a clear unit/label where supported. Do not assume a default yield or automatically scale ingredient quantities until the Recipes module's serving-scaling behavior is explicitly approved. Ora's recipe-to-shopping transfer must not invent scaling rules.
- **Photos and tags:** Photos follow Madeora's existing media permissions, optimization and storage safeguards where applicable. Tags are optional, explicitly managed metadata; do not infer dietary or allergen guarantees from tags or ingredients. No nutrition, calorie, difficulty, equipment, or substitution calculations are introduced by this decision.
- **Ownership and lifecycle:** Recipes live in the permitted personal or Home context and follow established view/edit rights, sharing boundaries, audit expectations, recovery and responsive/accessibility requirements. Recipe edits must not silently rewrite historical meal-plan records or previously confirmed shopping transfers; define any future link-update behavior separately.
- **Validation and acceptance criteria:** Create minimal usable recipe; optional fields left blank; multiple ordered ingredients and steps; ingredient without quantity; fractional quantity and supported unit; unsupported unit/packaging note; missing servings/times; zero/invalid numeric values; optional photo failure; tag editing; concurrent edits; private versus Home visibility; recipe used by meal planner or Ora; accessible mobile editing and safe cancellation.

### Confirmed shopping dashboard widget list selection and quick actions (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each Shopping dashboard widget may display either **one explicitly selected active shopping list** or a **combined view of multiple explicitly selected active lists** within the widget's authorized personal or Home context. Do not introduce automatic prioritization, smart recommendations, or implicit cross-Home aggregation.
- **Configuration:** Provide accessible list selection and multi-selection, a clear single/combined mode, and predictable display ordering. Show only lists the viewer may access. Clearly label each item with its source list in combined mode, including when names coincide; preserve list-specific categories, manual item order and Active/Completed semantics without moving or merging records between lists.
- **Quick actions:** Eligible members may check/uncheck an item, use single-item quick-add, and open its originating list. In combined mode, quick-add must require an explicit target list selection or use an unambiguous, visible, user-configured destination; never silently guess which list receives the item. Enforce separate view, add and completion permissions per list and per context; do not expose restricted item names, counts or metadata.
- **Synchronization and states:** Reflect changes across Home members and devices; show loading, empty, partial-access, stale, conflict, offline and action-error states. Provide clear feedback and safe retry without duplicate item creation or repeated completion writes. Preserve active/completed separation and support accessible keyboard/touch interaction.
- **Lifecycle:** If a selected list is archived, deleted, recovered, or becomes inaccessible, exclude its items from active widget results and show a nonrevealing configuration notice or re-selection action; never substitute another list without approval. Revalidate access and item/list state at interaction time. Wall-display widgets remain governed by separate wall-display privacy and interaction rules, not by dashboard permissions alone.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Single list; two-list combined overview with distinct source labels; identical list names; list-specific quick-add; check/uncheck from combined view; partial read-only list; permission revocation; archived/deleted list; concurrent completion; offline retry; empty selections; mobile, keyboard and screen-reader usability.

### Confirmed recurring shopping items (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Shopping items may have an **optional daily, weekly, or monthly recurrence rule**. Members explicitly enable recurrence for selected items in an active personal or Home shopping list; ordinary items remain nonrecurring. Do not add predictive replenishment, consumption tracking, stock estimation, or purchase-pattern suggestions.
- A recurrence rule retains its target list, item name, optional structured quantity/unit, packaging description, notes, and explicitly assigned category when still valid. Provide a readable schedule preview and optional start date; define weekly weekdays and monthly date behavior clearly, including months without the chosen day. Use the owning context's configured timezone and consistent local calendar boundaries; avoid unintended extra occurrences at daylight-saving transitions.
- At each due recurrence, **create or reactivate at most one active matching item** for that recurrence rule. If an active item already exists from the rule, do not create another, silently increase its quantity, or reset its completion status. When a member checks off the active item, the next scheduled occurrence may make the item active again at its due time. Preserve prior completion records and distinguish each recurrence occurrence in history; do not falsely attribute automated reactivation to a member's purchase.
- Permit authorized members to view the next due date, edit the rule, pause/resume it, or stop recurrence without deleting historical item completions. Editing a rule must show whether it affects the next and future occurrences only; do not retroactively rewrite past records. Archiving/deleting the target list suspends new recurrence writes; restoring the list must not silently flood it with missed items. Explicitly resolve a deleted or unavailable category rather than automatically assigning a new one.
- Recheck active list status, item and rule version, permissions, ownership and duplicate-active-item conditions before each scheduled action. Handle multiple devices, delayed processing, concurrent check-offs, retries, outages and missed schedules idempotently. Never generate duplicate active items or replay a backlog of missed daily/weekly/monthly occurrences without member review. Provide clear status and actionable errors if recurrence cannot proceed.
- Recurring items are visible in the Shopping module with an accessible recurring indicator and schedule controls. Follow existing personal/Home isolation, manual categories, synchronized Active/Completed lifecycle, seven-day recovery and notification preferences. Recurrence does not imply automatic purchasing or an external retailer integration.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Daily milk, weekly bread and monthly supplies; manually selected weekday and monthly day; 31st in shorter months; completed item reappears only when next due; still-active item prevents duplicates; pause/resume and stop; edit quantity without rewriting history; archived/deleted list; category removed; two members checking an item simultaneously; outage and idempotent retry; overdue occurrences without backlog flood; read-only member; accessible mobile schedule preview.

### Confirmed multiple independent shopping lists (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each personal space and each Home can contain **multiple independently named shopping lists**, rather than one mandatory list per context. Lists are owned by exactly one personal space or Home, with separate items, quantities, ordering, Active/Completed sections, completion history, and archive/deletion lifecycle. This decision does not introduce folders, templates, reusable shopping sessions, or cross-Home list sharing.
- Authorized members can create, rename, manually reorder, archive, restore where eligible, and delete lists within their current permitted context. Require a non-empty, trimmed list name within the platform's existing text limits; allow duplicate names only when the interface can unambiguously distinguish lists, and do not silently merge lists or items. Provide a clear empty state and a way to create the first list; do not assume a default list exists unless one is explicitly configured.
- **List ordering:** Save a stable manual order per context, including consistent ordering across devices for Home members; newly created lists appear predictably. Reordering lists never reorders items within them. Respect concurrent edits and show refreshed state rather than silently overwriting a newer order.
- **Archiving:** An archived list remains available in a separate Archived view for authorized members, preserving its items and history, but is excluded from active selection, new-item destination suggestions, active dashboard counts and routine notifications. Show the consequences before archiving and allow authorized restoration. Do not archive individual items merely because the list is archived.
- **Deletion:** Distinguish archiving from deletion. Require explicit confirmation with the list name, item count and impact on widgets, links and existing integrations; apply the existing seven-day Trash/recovery policy and permissions. Do not move deleted-list items into another list automatically. Restoring a list should restore eligible items and membership references where valid, without restoring access revoked meanwhile.
- **Collaboration and privacy:** Home-owned lists synchronize for eligible Home members under the existing view/edit permissions. Personal lists remain personal. List switching, search, Ora destination selection, recipe-ingredient transfers, widgets and notifications must respect ownership, active/archived state and permissions. Never show lists or item details from another Home without authorized access.
- **Error and edge states:** Handle empty list collections, duplicate names, long/invalid names, permission revocation, last active list archived/deleted, stale active-list selection, offline/concurrent renames or reorders, unavailable list destinations, list recovery, and partially failed changes with actionable feedback. Do not silently redirect writes to a different list.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Create two Home lists and one personal list; rename/reorder; independent completion states; archive and restore with items; delete and recover with permission checks; two members editing concurrently; identical names; zero active lists; recipe and Ora actions targeting a selected list; read-only member; dashboard references to archived/deleted lists; responsive and accessible list navigation.

### Confirmed shopping item moves and copies within a context (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized members can **Move** or **Copy** individual shopping items between two distinct, active shopping lists owned by the **same personal space or Home**. Do not introduce cross-Home, personal-to-Home or bulk transfers through this decision.
- **Move** changes the item's list membership without creating an additional item; **Copy** creates a separate destination item while leaving the source unchanged. Preserve the item name, optional numeric quantity, unit, packaging description and notes in both operations. A destination category is assigned only by an explicit member choice when a valid category exists there; otherwise the item remains Uncategorized. Do not copy source-list category identifiers or automatically suggest categories.
- Offer a clear source/destination selector, distinct Move and Copy actions, and an editable before-and-after preview showing destination list, item details and the effect on the source. Keep current context explicit and never silently switch Homes or personal spaces. A move or copy must not occur into an archived, deleted or inaccessible list.
- Detect clear duplicate items already present in the destination and warn before confirmation. Offer an explicit choice to proceed as a separate item or cancel; where existing Shopping rules permit an update instead, present it as a distinct reviewed action. Never silently merge entries, aggregate quantities or overwrite an existing item.
- For an active item, the new destination entry remains Active. If the source item is completed, visibly disclose its completion state and offer an explicit choice to preserve completion or reopen it in the destination; never misrepresent a copied item's original completion actor/time as a new purchase. Keep the source's historical audit events and distinguish transfer provenance from actual completion events.
- Require permission to view the source and add items to the destination, plus appropriate edit/remove permission for a move; copying does not grant access beyond destination rules. Revalidate permissions, item version, source/destination status, duplicates and Home membership at confirmation. Protect against double transfers, stale concurrent edits, interrupted writes and repeated retries; report outcomes clearly and preserve a recoverable source if a move fails partway.
- Update synchronized list views, item counts and dashboard widgets; respect archived lists, seven-day recovery rules and existing audit/history behavior. A move or copy does not transfer ownership outside the originating personal space or Home.
- Acceptance criteria: move and copy name-only and fully specified items; category not present in destination; optional explicit destination category; duplicate destination item; active and completed items; same-list destination blocked; personal-to-Home and cross-Home attempts blocked; archived or deleted destination; source/destination permissions; simultaneous edits; failed move and safe retry; accessible mobile confirmation and undo/recovery where eligible.

### Confirmed structured shopping quantities and units (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Shopping-list items support an **optional structured numeric quantity**, an optional supported **unit**, an optional **packaging description** (such as bottle, pack or box), and optional free-text **notes**. An item name is required; quantity, unit, packaging and notes are not mandatory. This confirms the structured fields assumed by Ora's earlier shopping-quantity decision, without adding automatic conversions or purchase/inventory tracking.
- Offer common localized units, including pieces, grams, kilograms, millilitres and litres, with clear labels and sensible decimal precision. A package count such as **2 bottles** is distinct from a measured amount such as **500 g**; do not assume bottle size, convert between units, multiply package sizes or calculate prices. Preserve explicitly entered units and packaging descriptions when editing, completing, moving or transferring items.
- Permit quantities such as 2, 0.5 and 1.25 when meaningful; validate finite positive numbers, supported precision and consistent unit selection. Do not silently round, invent a default quantity, interpret an omitted quantity as a confirmed one, or erase notes when a unit changes. If an existing item cannot represent a requested quantity or packaging detail, offer a visible text fallback where supported or ask the member to adjust it.
- Show the quantity, unit and packaging description together clearly in active/completed lists, accessible edit controls, compact dashboard views and reviewed Ora/recipe transfers. Keep optional notes accessible without crowding list rows. Do not automatically aggregate duplicate items or quantities; follow the approved duplicate-choice and explicit confirmation rules.
- Apply existing personal/Home ownership, shopping-list permissions, categories, synchronization, completion history, archive/recovery, and concurrency rules. Validate stale edits and avoid duplicate writes on retries. No unit conversion, price estimation, pantry stock tracking, shopping suggestions or advanced package-size calculations are introduced.
- Acceptance criteria: item with name only; 2 bottles of milk; 500 g pasta; 6 apples; decimal litre amount; quantity with no unit; notes and packaging; localized decimal input; invalid zero/negative/non-numeric values; changing unit without conversion; concurrent edits; duplicate item; complete/uncheck; archive/recover; Ora and recipe preview; read-only member; accessible mobile controls.

### Confirmed shopping quick-add and reviewed multi-item entry (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Shopping lists provide both a fast **single-item quick-add** flow and a **reviewed multi-item entry** flow. Members can enter several items in one operation, for example one item per line; this is a manual Shopping-module workflow, not an Ora feature or a smart product recommendation system.
- **Single-item quick-add:** In a selected active list, enter a nonempty item name and add it with minimal interaction. Preserve the current list/context and show immediate, accessible success or error feedback. Optional structured quantity, unit, packaging, notes and manual category can be edited through the existing item controls; do not require those fields for quick entry.
- **Multi-item entry:** Provide a paste-friendly multiline input with one item per line, trimming surrounding whitespace and ignoring blank lines. Before any writes, display a separate **editable preview row for each parsed item**, including name and supported optional quantity/unit/packaging/notes where explicitly entered or subsequently edited, plus the destination list. Do not silently infer quantities or categories from ambiguous text; keep the full original line visible when interpretation is uncertain and allow correction.
- Members can edit or remove individual preview rows, cancel the whole operation, and confirm the remaining valid rows explicitly. Validate list status, names, supported quantities and units, row/operation limits, permissions and destination immediately before committing. Offer actionable row-specific errors and never silently redirect entries to another list or Home.
- Detect obvious duplicate names within the submitted batch and clearly matching existing items in the target list. Surface potential duplicates and allow the member to keep separate items, remove a draft row, or explicitly choose a permitted existing-item update; never automatically merge items, sum quantities or change completion states. Do not auto-assign categories, consistent with the approved manual-only category rule.
- Apply existing personal/Home isolation, list permissions, concurrency, synchronization, archive/deletion rules and safe retries. Report per-row success, failure or uncertainty; do not re-add successfully created items when retrying a partially failed batch. Keep the interface usable on mobile, keyboard and screen readers, including for large pasted lists within product-defined bounded limits.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Fast add of one name-only item; paste four names on separate lines; blank/duplicate lines; preview edits and exclusions; structured quantity added during preview; ambiguous quantity text preserved; archived/deleted list; read-only member; concurrent collaborator; cancellation; one invalid row; partial failure and idempotent retry; no category suggestions; accessible mobile bulk preview.

### Confirmed manual shopping categories and item ordering (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each shopping list may have **optional, manually managed categories** (for example, Produce, Dairy, Household) and a user-controlled item order. Uncategorized items remain fully supported. Neither the Shopping module nor Ora automatically suggests, infers, creates, or assigns categories; Ora's existing category-handling restriction remains authoritative.
- Authorized list editors can create, rename, reorder, and remove categories within a specific list, and explicitly assign, change, or clear an item's category. Category names must be nonempty after trimming, follow existing text limits, and be distinguishable within the list; never silently merge similarly named categories. No cross-list category synchronization, reusable category templates, or automatic sorting is implied.
- Removing a category **must not delete its items**: preview the number affected and move those items to Uncategorized after explicit confirmation. Renaming/reordering a category preserves its item references and completion status. Archived or deleted lists follow their existing access and recovery rules.
- Support manual reordering of active items within a category and of uncategorized items, with a predictable visible order across authorized devices. When an item changes category, preserve its quantity, unit, notes, completion status, and identity; place it predictably in the destination group without silently changing other items. Completed items remain in the separate Completed section, with category context shown where useful; completing/unchecking must not discard category assignment.
- Apply existing Home/personal isolation, view/edit permissions, accessible drag-and-drop alternatives, concurrency/stale-order handling, and safe retry rules. Read-only members can view permitted categories but cannot reorganize them. No hidden categorization by Ora or recipe-ingredient transfers.
- Acceptance criteria: create a list without categories; add and reorder categories; assign/unassign and reorder items; move items between categories; rename and remove a populated category with confirmation; complete/uncheck a categorized item; two collaborators reorder concurrently; read-only member; archived list; mobile keyboard/screen-reader ordering; Ora-added and recipe-transferred items remain uncategorized unless a member explicitly categorizes them in Shopping.

### Confirmed synchronized shopping item completion lifecycle (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Checking an active shopping-list item moves it immediately into a distinct **Completed** section of the same list. Unchecked and completed sections stay synchronized for all authorized collaborators within the list's personal/Home context; a completion is not a deletion or a separate shopping-trip/session record.
- An authorized member can check an item, uncheck it to restore it to Active, and clear completed items from the list. Show a clear item-level completion state, who completed it and when where permitted, and a recent-completions view/history consistent with existing retention and audit policies. Do not invent a permanent purchase-history system or shopping sessions.
- **Clear completed** requires an explicit, impact-aware confirmation indicating the number of items affected. Apply the project's existing deletion/recovery rules to removed records where applicable; retain only permitted historical/audit metadata, and avoid implying cleared items remain active. Unchecking is distinct from recovering a cleared/deleted record.
- Concurrent check/uncheck actions from multiple authorized members must be handled consistently: do not duplicate completions or overwrite newer changes silently. Display updated states promptly, provide understandable stale-state feedback, and support safe retry without double-applying writes.
- Preserve item quantities, units, notes and list assignment while toggling completion. Apply list view/edit permissions and Home boundaries to actions, history, notifications and widgets; read-only members cannot toggle or clear. Ora actions must follow the same lifecycle and confirmation rules.
- The list interface must provide accessible active/completed counts, an expandable or clearly separated Completed section, undo/uncheck controls, and empty states. Recent completed history must not expose items to former or unauthorized members.
- Acceptance criteria: check item on one device and observe another; uncheck and restore order/state; duplicate rapid check; concurrent check/uncheck; clear completed with multiple items; cancel clear; recover eligible cleared item; read-only list; deleted list; stale/offline action and retry; item with quantities/notes; empty Completed section; accessible mobile and wall-display read-only states.

### Confirmed optional numeric daily habit targets (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** In addition to existing binary check-ins, a personal or shared Home habit on a daily or selected-weekday schedule may optionally use a **positive numeric target per scheduled day**, such as 8 glasses of water or 30 minutes of reading. Members can record multiple incremental progress updates toward that daily target. This does not introduce a separate timed-session tracker, journal, or new weekly quantitative target type; existing weekly-frequency habits continue to count eligible check-ins toward their weekly frequency target.
- At creation or authorized edit, choose **Complete once** (existing default) or **Numeric daily target**; require a finite positive target, a concise unit label, and a suitable positive increment/precision consistent with that unit. Preview the difference between quantity progress and completing the daily opportunity. A target is met when the day's valid accumulated amount reaches or exceeds its target; show both amount and completion state. Do not count each increment as a separate scheduled completion, streak day, reward, or milestone.
- Provide accessible **Add progress** controls, a clear current/target amount, and an authorized correction flow for mistaken entries. Each increment records its contributor, quantity, scheduled local date, actual recording time and optional completion note where supported. Preserve individual contribution attribution for shared Home habits while aggregating one Home-wide daily total; members cannot silently overwrite another member's contribution. A shared day completes once when the combined eligible total reaches the target.
- Check eligibility, active schedule version, start/end dates, pause state, permissions and applicable personal/Home timezone on every update. Allow valid backdated increments only within the approved seven-day retrospective window; apply existing separate correction rules to previously recorded entries. Prevent duplicate increments from repeated taps, retries or concurrent devices, and show clear stale-state or invalid-quantity errors. Recalculate completion, streaks, insights and reminders if a correction moves a day's total below or above target.
- Preserve historical quantities and targets under the schedule/target version effective at the time; changes to tracking mode, unit or daily target apply prospectively from an approved effective date with an impact preview, never silently rewrite earlier progress. Pauses and ended periods create no required quantity or catch-up. Habit reminders, dashboard controls, quick Undo and notification-origin completion must not invent a quantity: where an action cannot safely specify an increment, open the in-app numeric check-in instead of treating a single tap as meeting the target.
- Completion notes remain optional and privacy-filtered; progress does not earn chore points or extra milestone rewards. On reaching the target, stop outstanding reminders for that daily opportunity, show one completion and at most one eligible celebration. Display daily quantities and meaningful completion rates in history, insights and widgets, with a clear distinction between partially progressed and completed days; keep personal data private and shared Home contributor details permission-aware.
- Acceptance criteria: create binary versus numeric habit; enter multiple increments toward 8 glasses; partial progress then reach target; overshoot; invalid/zero/negative input; shared contributions by two members; simultaneous updates/retries; correcting an increment below target; Undo of latest own increment versus another member's update; seven-day backdate; paused/ended/unscheduled day; historical target change; reminder and notification fallback; accessible mobile input and private wall-display behavior.

### Confirmed numeric habit daily excess and no carryover (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** A numeric habit records the **actual eligible quantity** for each scheduled local day, even when the accumulated amount exceeds its daily target (for example, **10/8 glasses**). The day counts as **one completed scheduled opportunity** once its target is met, not multiple completions, streak increments, awards or milestones.
- **No carryover:** Quantities above the daily target never count toward another scheduled day, including the next day, a skipped weekday, the next tracking period or a day after a pause. Each new eligible scheduled day starts at **0** against its own effective target. Never silently redistribute an entry to another date or turn excess into future credit.
- Allow valid additional entries after reaching the target, clearly display the uncapped actual total alongside the target and completed status, and retain individual contributor attribution on shared Home habits. Progress visuals may show a fully filled target indicator but must still disclose the actual above-target quantity in accessible text.
- Apply the original local scheduled date and historical target version to backdated entries and corrections. If an authorized edit or Undo reduces the total below target, recalculate that day's completion, streaks and insights under the established correction rules; later dates remain unaffected except for legitimately derived streak/statistic changes. Do not automatically resume a completed day's cancelled reminders merely because its quantity was corrected; use existing reminder eligibility rules.
- Keep quantity validation, privacy, concurrency and duplicate-protection rules unchanged. An above-target total is not an error; reject invalid individual increments rather than capping valid totals. This policy applies equally to personal habits and shared Home aggregate totals.
- Acceptance criteria: 8/8 completion; 10/8 displays actual total with one completion; next scheduled day starts at 0; multiple shared contributors exceed target; late/backdated excess remains on original date; Undo and correction across threshold; pause, end date, reactivation and timezone boundaries do not transfer excess.

### Confirmed numeric habit measurement units and limited conversions (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Numeric daily habits offer a concise list of common measurement units (including glasses, millilitres, litres, pages, minutes and repetitions) alongside a user-defined custom unit label. A numeric habit has one canonical target/display unit per effective configuration; the choice is not a full configurable measurement system or a timed-session tracker.
- Presets distinguish count-based units from standard compatible physical units. Support only explicitly defined, unambiguous conversions within a compatible dimension (for example, litres ↔ millilitres and hours ↔ minutes if hours is offered). Never infer a conversion between glasses and millilitres, between pages and minutes, or between arbitrary custom labels; a glass size is not universally defined. Custom units and nonconvertible presets are treated as labelled quantities without conversion.
- At creation and authorized edit, show unit, target, allowed input precision and example progress (for example, 500/2000 mL); validate positive finite targets and increments, prevent invalid precision or ambiguous input, and use locale-aware display without changing stored meaning. The check-in UI defaults to the habit's chosen unit; offer an alternate input unit only where an explicit safe conversion is supported and label the converted amount before confirmation. Do not require conversion for ordinary check-ins.
- Unit edits apply only from an approved future effective date with a preview; the separately approved same-day numeric **target-only** change does not authorize same-day unit changes. If switching between compatible units, convert the prospective target and increment settings transparently, preserving equivalent meaning; if switching to an incompatible or custom unit, require the manager/owner to explicitly confirm the new target and units rather than reinterpreting historical amounts. Preserve all prior entries, effective unit versions, completed statuses, streaks and historical insights as recorded under their original units; never silently recalculate past achievements.
- Shared Home habits use one common canonical unit and target per effective period. Aggregate authorized member contributions consistently and prevent duplicate increments, conversion rounding drift, stale-unit submissions and mismatched concurrent updates. Display contributor amounts in the effective canonical unit, with original entered values where useful in authorized history.
- Respect existing seven-day backdating, Undo, corrections, pause/end-date, privacy and notification-origin safeguards. An incompatible unit change does not enable moving quantities between days, and above-target quantities remain uncapped without carryover. Accessible screen-reader labels must state both number and unit; unsupported conversions provide clear feedback without guessing.
- Acceptance criteria: preset glasses/pages/minutes/repetitions; custom unit; 1.5 L converted to 1500 mL; no inferred glass-to-mL conversion; decimal and precision validation; locale display; compatible unit edit; incompatible edit with new target confirmation; historical backdate under old unit; shared contributors using consistent units; concurrent edit and stale check-in; above-target amount and no carryover; accessible progress labels.

### Confirmed numeric habit target effective-date changes (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** An authorized personal habit owner or shared Home habit manager may change a numeric daily target effective **today**, on the **next scheduled day**, or on a **selected future date**. Require an explicit impact preview and confirmation; no retroactive target edits or rewriting historical achievements.
- Preview the old and new target with unit, chosen effective local date, already recorded quantity and current completion status for today when applicable, forthcoming scheduled opportunities, reminders, streak implications and any overlapping pause or end date. Validate a finite positive target and compatible unit/precision, plus the editor's current permissions. An effective date must fall within a valid tracking period; if it falls after the habit's end date, explain that no opportunities will use it unless the end date is extended separately.
- **Today:** If today is an eligible, active scheduled opportunity, retain every recorded increment and its contributor and recording timestamp; compare today's existing total against the newly approved target only after confirmation. This may change today's partial/completed state and derived streaks, statistics, reminder eligibility and celebration eligibility. Clearly warn if raising the target would make an already completed day incomplete or lowering it would complete the day. Never manufacture or discard progress, retroactively award duplicate milestones, or create chore points. If today is paused, unscheduled or ended, do not fabricate an opportunity; explain the restriction and offer the next valid date.
- **Next scheduled day or future date:** Keep today's target, check-ins and finalized completion status unchanged; apply the new target to eligible scheduled opportunities starting on the selected effective date. A selected future date that is not itself scheduled establishes the target for the first eligible opportunity on or after that date, without creating a check-in on the unscheduled date. Existing earlier periods and achievements remain evaluated against their original effective targets.
- Maintain a permission-aware history of target versions, effective dates, editor and change times. The history and insights view must display the target applicable to each opportunity; historical entries and seven-day backdating use that day's effective target, not the newest target. Treat same-day target edits as explicit, auditable exceptions to the general prospective-only rule for schedule/unit changes, and do not permit them to modify days before today.
- Revalidate permissions, current local date, schedule/paused/ended state and concurrent check-ins immediately before committing. Resolve concurrent target changes and increments consistently, with clear stale-preview feedback, safe retries and no duplicate status transitions. For shared habits, use the Home timezone and one shared target version; do not overwrite any contributor's quantities. Keep existing no-carryover and limited conversion rules intact.
- Acceptance criteria: target 8→10 today with 8 already logged; target 10→8 today with 8 logged; future date and next eligible weekday; paused/unscheduled/ended today; future date after end date; seven-day backdate against historical target; overlapping schedule and unit edits; shared contributor concurrency; reminder and streak recalculation; accessible impact preview; unchanged prior dates and audit trail.

### Confirmed explicit confirmation for same-day numeric target completion changes (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When an authorized owner or shared Home habit manager edits a numeric daily target **effective today** and the change would switch today's status between **Incomplete/Partial** and **Completed**, require a separate, explicit confirmation of that status change before saving.
