This applies symmetrically to lowering a target that newly completes the day and raising one that makes a previously completed day incomplete. Do not silently commit such a change through an ordinary Save action.
- Show the existing quantity, previous and proposed target with unit, before/after completion state, and concrete effects on current streak, completion rate, milestone eligibility, active reminders and progress widgets. Use neutral language; do not imply that changing a target is a genuine newly recorded quantity contribution. Never award chore points or duplicate a celebration solely because a target was lowered.
- The existing impact preview remains required for all target edits; when today's completion state would **not** change, its ordinary confirmation is sufficient and no additional status-change confirmation is necessary. Future-effective edits cannot alter today's status and follow the existing future-effective workflow. Cancellation leaves target, quantity, completion and reminders unchanged.
- Immediately before committing, revalidate the current local date, eligible active opportunity, effective target, exact accumulated total, permissions, pauses and concurrent updates. If an intervening contribution or edit changes the projected status or effects, refresh the preview and require renewed confirmation. Commit target version and derived status atomically/idempotently; preserve contributor entries and historical dates unchanged, with permission-aware audit details of the confirmation.
- Acceptance criteria: 8/10 → 8/8 triggers confirmation and completes; 8/8 → 8/10 triggers confirmation and becomes partial; 5/10 → 5/8 retains incomplete status without extra confirmation; unchanged target rejected as no-op; cancel; stale shared contribution; paused/unscheduled today; accessible keyboard/screen-reader confirmation; no duplicate notifications, streak awards or celebrations.

### Confirmed daily-only numeric habit quantity targets (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Numeric habits support **daily quantity targets only**, including selected-weekday daily occurrences. Do not introduce cumulative weekly or monthly quantity targets, period-level target completion, or period-level quantity streaks. Existing non-numeric weekly completion-frequency habits remain supported under their established rules.
- Each eligible scheduled local day has its own effective positive target, unit, contribution total and completed/incomplete status. The day is completed once its own target is met; valid excess remains visible on that day and **never carries over**. Contributions from multiple days must not combine to complete an otherwise incomplete day.
- Weekly and monthly numeric views are **summaries only**, showing approved actual quantity totals, eligible-day averages and days meeting their individual historical daily targets. Their totals and averages are descriptive, not additional goals or reward triggers. Respect pauses, end dates, schedule and target changes, tracking periods, unit compatibility, backdating, corrections and shared Home privacy.
- Creation and editing UI must clearly label the target as **per eligible day** and must not imply that a weekly or monthly summary is a configurable target. If a user wants a weekly cumulative distance goal, the product may direct them to an appropriate existing goal feature rather than silently repurposing numeric habits. No automatic conversion of a daily target to a weekly target.
- Acceptance criteria: daily target reached on one day but not another; weekly summary totals both without awarding a weekly completion; selected weekdays; excess does not carry forward; historical daily target edit; paused and ended days excluded; shared contributions; no weekly/monthly target input or misleading weekly streak.

### Confirmed weekly and monthly numeric habit quantity summaries (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Numeric habit insights include simple **weekly and monthly quantity summaries** alongside existing completion-rate and streak information. For each selected calendar period, show (1) **total actual quantity recorded**, (2) **average quantity per eligible scheduled day**, and (3) **number of eligible days whose effective daily target was met**, with an explicit eligible-day denominator. Do not introduce detailed intraday charts, period comparisons or performance coaching under this decision.
- Define the average denominator as eligible scheduled opportunities in the selected period that have begun, excluding paused, unscheduled, pre-start, ended and future dates; **include eligible days with zero contributions**, so the average is not inflated. Show the number of eligible days and make a no-eligible-days state distinct from zero progress. Use the Home timezone for shared habits and the applicable personal timezone for personal habits; use the product's established locale-aware calendar week boundaries.
- Totals sum **actual accepted quantities**, including legitimate amounts above the target, without capping or carrying excess forward. Count each target-met day at most once, comparing its recorded total against the **target effective on that day**, including explicitly confirmed same-day target changes. A partially completed day contributes to quantity totals and the average but not to target-met days.
- Keep measurements meaningful: aggregate only compatible quantities after exact supported conversions into a clearly labelled display unit. If a selected period spans incompatible unit versions or arbitrary custom units, **do not invent a combined total or average**; split the summary by unit/version or show an explicit unavailable mixed-unit explanation while retaining per-day completion counts. Use approved decimal precision and safe arithmetic; avoid rounding that falsely changes target-met status.
- Historical schedule versions, tracking-period restarts, vacation/manual pauses, end dates, seven-day backdating, Undo and authorized corrections all determine the eligible days and quantities used. Past results remain tied to their historical effective target and unit; no retroactive reinterpretation after an edit. For an in-progress day or period, label summaries as **so far**, and refresh on confirmed changes.
- Respect personal/private and shared Home access rules. Shared quantity summaries use aggregate contributions without disclosing individual contributor identities; detailed entries remain available only through authorized correction/audit history. Use clear accessible text labels and empty/loading/error states across mobile, desktop and insights views; dashboard widgets need not acquire new analytics controls.
- Acceptance criteria: week/month with completed, partial and zero-progress eligible days; above-target contribution; paused and unscheduled dates excluded from denominator; future days excluded; current period labelled so far; historical target change; incompatible unit change; supported litres/millilitres conversion; correction/backdate/Undo; overlapping pauses; ended and reactivated tracking periods; shared privacy; timezone boundaries; zero eligible days; precision and accessible display.

### Confirmed interactive numeric habit dashboard widgets (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** An optional numeric habit dashboard widget displays the current eligible day's **actual accumulated quantity**, effective target and unit, a legible progress bar, and that habit's configured positive **quick-add buttons**. Manual quantity entry and editing the optional daily note remain in the habit detail view, reachable from the widget; do not place those editors inside the widget. This is distinct from the separately approved optional compact habit **insights** widget.
- Respect personal dashboard customization and Home dashboard permissions. Personal habits appear only on authorized personal surfaces; shared Home habits may appear on authorized Home dashboards and aggregate eligible contributions without revealing private contributor identities. The widget clearly labels the habit and date, and differentiates incomplete, completed, above-target, paused, ended, unscheduled, loading and unavailable states. Display the actual quantity even above target (for example 2,250 / 2,000 mL), while visually capping only the progress-bar fill; no excess carries forward.
- Each quick-add button invokes the **same validated, positive-only incremental check-in** as the habit detail view, including canonical units, up to three decimal places where appropriate, idempotency, member eligibility, timezone/day boundary, active schedule, pauses, end date, and current target/unit version. Show pending/success/error feedback, prevent duplicate rapid taps, and offer the existing brief Undo for a successful increment without reversing another contributor's entry.
- Refresh the displayed quantity, completion state, streak-derived indicators and reminder eligibility after confirmed updates from this widget or other authorized clients. If an intervening edit changes quick-add presets, units, target, permissions or today's eligibility, reject or revalidate stale actions and explain the change rather than submitting an unintended amount. Disable or hide contribution actions on ineligible days; still permit navigation to history and insights when authorized.
- On narrow layouts and wall displays, preserve readable progress and accessible touch/keyboard targets; show only a sensible subset of quick-add buttons when space is constrained, with an obvious route to the full habit detail view. A non-interactive wall display must never expose check-in actions. Screen readers announce habit, current amount, target, unit, completion status and each button's increment. Never rely on color alone.
- Acceptance criteria: add/remove/reposition widget; 750/2,000 mL and +250 mL check-in; above-target quantity; Undo; manual entry opens details; note edit opens details; shared concurrent additions; stale target/unit/preset; pause/end/unscheduled state; notification-triggered completion sync; permission revoked; responsive and wall-display read-only states; keyboard and screen-reader interaction; no duplicate increments or carryover.

### Confirmed numeric habit quick-add quantities and manual entry (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Numeric daily habits provide **configurable positive quick-add amount buttons** alongside an always-available manual quantity entry. For example, a water habit with a millilitre unit can expose **+250 mL** and **+500 mL** actions. Quick-add is a convenience for recording an increment, not a separate tracking type, automatic suggestion engine or change to the daily target.
- Authorized personal habit owners or shared Home habit managers may configure, edit, reorder or remove a small practical set of quick-add amounts per numeric habit; offer sensible editable initial values when the chosen unit permits, without assuming an arbitrary conversion (such as glasses to millilitres). The configuration belongs to the habit and is visible to eligible contributors; ordinary contributors cannot silently change shared presets. Validate positive finite quantities, supported decimal precision, canonical units and conversion compatibility; prevent duplicate/zero/negative presets and inaccessible controls.
- Selecting a quick-add button records **one incremental contribution** of its displayed quantity against the currently eligible scheduled local day. Manual entry accepts another valid positive quantity in the habit's unit (or an explicitly supported compatible unit) and shows the normalized quantity before saving where conversion is needed. Neither input silently replaces the existing total, caps valid above-target progress or carries excess to another day.
- Keep current/target progress and the actual above-target amount visible after each accepted contribution. Preserve individual contributor attribution for shared Home habits, and recalculate completion, streaks, insights and reminders only from committed increments. The daily opportunity completes once upon reaching its target; repeated additions after completion remain permitted under the approved excess-progress rule.
- Provide the approved **brief immediate Undo** for the contributor's latest successful quick-add/manual increment when safe, followed by the existing permission-aware correction/history flow. A concurrent contribution by another member must not be reversed or overwritten by Undo. Use idempotent writes to prevent double increments from rapid taps, retries, offline replay and duplicate notification actions; communicate pending, success and error states clearly.
- Revalidate habit status, schedule, pause/end state, member permissions, target/unit version and selected date at commit time. Quick-add buttons are not shown as active on paused, ended, unscheduled or unauthorized opportunities; seven-day backdated entries use the established date-selection and historical version rules rather than silently recording against today. When target or unit settings change, review affected presets and require correction of incompatible values without rewriting historical entries.
- Support touch, keyboard and screen readers with descriptive accessible labels including quantity and unit, sufficiently large targets, and no reliance on color alone. Compact dashboard controls may use the same validated quick-add actions; unsupported notification quick-completion continues to open the numeric check-in rather than inventing a quantity.
- Acceptance criteria: create/edit/remove/reorder presets; quick-add +250 twice and manual +125; reach and exceed target; custom unit; compatible conversion and unsupported conversion; invalid precision and duplicate presets; simultaneous shared contributions; double tap/offline retry; Undo own latest increment; concurrent contributor and stale target; paused/ended/unscheduled date; backdated entry; unit change invalidates preset; accessible mobile and keyboard input.

### Confirmed positive-only numeric habit contributions and corrections (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Normal numeric habit check-ins are **positive-only increments**. Both configurable quick-add actions and manual entry must accept only finite, strictly positive amounts in valid units and precision. Do not offer negative quick-add buttons, subtract-as-check-in controls, or direct replacement of the day's total as routine progress entry.
- **Correct mistakes rather than record negative activity:** Provide the already-approved brief immediate Undo for a just-committed contribution when safe, and a permission-aware history correction flow afterward. Authorized correction may void an erroneous contribution or amend its recorded quantity, with a clear before/after preview and reason/audit metadata where existing correction rules require it; it is not a new negative contribution. Preserve original and corrected versions and contributor attribution.
- **Totals and completion:** Recalculate the affected scheduled day's nonnegative total from valid, non-voided contributions, respecting the target and unit version effective on that date. If a correction takes progress below target, update completion, streaks, milestones, reminders and insights consistently; if it remains above target, retain the actual excess. Never carry amounts to another day or grant duplicate completion credits.
- **Shared Home habits:** A contributor's quick Undo can affect only their own eligible increment and cannot overwrite another member's contribution. Later corrections require the established permissions, and managers must not silently rewrite someone else's entries. Resolve concurrent contributions/corrections atomically and idempotently; reject stale or unauthorized edits with a clear explanation.
- **Dates and states:** Follow the existing seven-day backdating window for adding forgotten contributions and the separately approved history-correction policy for existing records. Do not use correction to create activity on paused, unscheduled or ended dates. Preserve historical targets, schedules, units, timestamps and change logs; do not treat correction timestamps as original completion times.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Reject zero/negative quick-add presets and manual inputs; Undo own latest increment; correct an erroneous increment through history; recalculate a day from complete to partial; correct above-target progress; concurrent shared contributions; unauthorized edit; stale retry; backdated entry; paused/ended date; accurate audit and accessible error/confirmation states.

### Confirmed progress-aware numeric habit reminders (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** At each **already-configured reminder time** for an eligible numeric daily or selected-weekday habit, Madeora checks the current committed quantity against that scheduled day's effective target. If incomplete, the reminder may show the current amount and target with their unit (for example, **750 / 2,000 mL**). Do not schedule extra or adaptive reminders based on progress; do not change reminder times automatically.
- Suppress the reminder if the day's target is already met or exceeded, the opportunity is unscheduled, paused, ended or otherwise ineligible, or the recipient has disabled notifications. Use the correct personal or Home timezone, active target/unit version and the recipient's notification preferences and quiet hours. Snoozes remain governed by the approved 15/30/60-minute policy and must be cancelled when the target is reached or the habit becomes ineligible.
- For shared Home numeric habits, calculate the aggregate eligible contribution total while sending recipient-specific notifications only to authorized, opted-in members. Never disclose private member contribution details in a notification or on an unauthorized/locked surface. Respect platform notification preview privacy settings and use a generic fallback when progress cannot be safely shown.
- Treat progress as a point-in-time snapshot; do not claim it is live after delivery. Revalidate the latest committed state at delivery/action time when supported; if another device/member completes the target between scheduling and delivery, suppress the stale reminder where possible. Tapping a reminder opens the current habit state; a notification completion action must not invent an increment or mark a numeric habit complete without a valid recorded quantity.
- Quantity edits, Undo, corrections, target changes, pause/resume and concurrent shared contributions update subsequent reminder eligibility without duplicate sends or retroactive notifications. Handle offline state, retry, unavailable progress and stale snapshots with clear non-misleading fallback behavior. The reminder remains optional and non-punitive.
- Acceptance criteria: incomplete 750/2000 mL reminder at configured time; 2000/2000 and 2250/2000 suppressed; no additional reminders; custom unit; selected-weekday off day; quiet hours and opt-out; paused/ended habit; same-day target edit; correction from complete to partial; concurrent shared completion; stale notification and retry; locked-screen privacy; accessible quantity/unit labels.

### Confirmed numeric habit daily totals without routine contribution timelines (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** The normal numeric-habit history and insights views show **one daily total** for each eligible scheduled day, with the effective target and unit (for example, 2,250 / 2,000 mL), completion status and relevant pause/unscheduled indicators. Do **not** add a default per-day contribution timeline, timestamp list or intra-day accumulation chart to ordinary habit history.
- **Correction and audit access remains:** Individual quantity contributions, original timestamps, contributor attribution, amendments, voids and reasons where required remain available in the **existing authorized correction/audit workflow**, not in the routine daily summary. Provide a clear entry point such as **Review contributions** from a selected day to users with appropriate correction or audit rights. A daily summary must never imply that the aggregate was one entry.
- **Personal and shared privacy:** Owners can access their own personal habit contribution records subject to existing policy. Shared Home habit members see the permitted aggregate daily total; detailed member-attributed contributions are shown only to users authorized by the existing Home history/correction permissions. Do not leak private contributor details in dashboards, notifications, exports or wall displays.
- **Accuracy and historical continuity:** Aggregate only valid, non-voided positive contributions for the selected date, using its effective target, unit and schedule versions. Above-target totals remain visible; excess does not carry forward. Preserve historical daily totals and corrections across target changes, unit conversions, pauses, end/reactivation periods, backdated entries and time-zone boundaries. Authorized corrections recalculate the relevant day, streaks, completion rates and insights without rewriting other days.
- **Usability and concurrency:** The daily totals view should distinguish zero progress from no scheduled opportunity, be accessible on mobile and desktop, and explain corrected totals when appropriate without exposing private audit detail. Loading, offline/stale data, retries and simultaneous shared contributions must not show false completion or duplicate quantities.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Daily history shows 750/2000 and 2250/2000 without a routine contribution list; authorized correction view can inspect individual entries; unauthorized member sees aggregate only; multiple shared contributions aggregate correctly; Undo/void/edit recomputes the total; backdated entry, historical target/unit changes, pause and reactivation boundaries; accessible status and error states.

### Confirmed one daily note for numeric habit progress (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Each eligible scheduled numeric-habit day supports **at most one optional short plain-text daily note** attached to that day's aggregate progress, not to any individual quantity contribution. Keep numeric check-ins fast and note-free; the note is independently editable through the day detail/history without adding or changing a quantity.
- Show the daily note alongside the day's aggregate total only to users authorized to view that note. For a personal habit, only its owner may create, edit or read the note. For a shared Home habit, follow the existing shared-habit visibility and editing permissions, record the author and last editor, and avoid leaking private member details on wall displays, notification previews or unauthorized surfaces. Do not infer permission to edit a note merely from permission to contribute quantity.
- Support creating, editing, clearing and viewing the daily note on a partial, complete or above-target day. Notes are optional and must never affect quantities, target achievement, streaks, reminders, milestones or statistics. Use bounded length, plain text, accessible editing controls, clear save/error feedback and safe handling of empty input.
- Maintain one canonical daily note under concurrent edits: protect against silent overwrites, detect stale versions, and provide a review/retry path. Record who changed the note and when in permission-aware audit history; do not expose a contribution timeline in the ordinary day view. Removing a quantity entry or reversing completion does not silently erase its day's note; follow the existing retention and deletion rules for the habit/day.
- Respect the effective Home/member calendar day, historical schedule, vacation/manual pauses, end/reactivation periods and seven-day quantity backdating rules. Editing a daily note does not itself backdate a quantity or create an otherwise missing scheduled occurrence. Past-note edits require authorized history access; do not treat the seven-day quantity backdating window as an automatic note-retention limit.
- Acceptance criteria: add a note at 250/2000 and retain it at 2000/2000; edit or clear without changing totals; multiple increments share one note; shared contributor without edit rights; concurrent edits; unscheduled/paused day; past scheduled day; Undo and correction leave note intact; private dashboard/wall display; offline retry and audit history.

### Confirmed numeric habit decimal precision and localized entry (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Numeric habit targets, manual contributions and quick-add presets support positive whole numbers and **up to three decimal places where appropriate to the selected unit**. Do not require fractional values for inherently discrete units such as pages, repetitions or glasses unless the unit explicitly supports fractions; permit values such as 2.5 km and 0.25 L for suitable measurable units. This is a fixed, limited precision policy, not per-habit configurable arbitrary precision.
- Validate input using the member's locale (including decimal comma or decimal point conventions) while storing and calculating exact canonical decimal quantities; avoid binary floating-point accumulation errors. Reject non-finite, zero/negative increments, malformed separators, excess precision and out-of-range amounts with specific, accessible guidance. Targets must remain strictly positive.
- Compatible unit conversions must be dimensionally valid and deterministic, using defined conversion factors and a documented precision/rounding policy. Never silently truncate an entered amount or invent conversions for custom units or variable measures (such as glasses to mL). Where conversion produces more precision than the canonical unit supports, preview the rounded result and require an explicit confirmation when it would materially change a target or contribution; otherwise require a more suitable unit.
- Format progress, quick-add labels, daily totals, reminders, history and insights consistently in the user's locale, with enough decimal places to avoid obscuring recorded values; do not imply extra precision. Sum valid entries exactly, including above-target totals, without carryover; compute completion from the exact effective target and total, not from rounded display text.
- Apply effective-dated unit/target changes prospectively and preserve the original historical target, unit, contribution values and conversion context for each period. Revalidate queued/offline entries against current configuration and avoid duplicate increments during retries or concurrent shared check-ins. Corrections and Undo recompute totals, completion and streaks accurately.
- Acceptance criteria: 2.5 km target; 0.25 L increments; 1.5 L to 1500 mL; comma-decimal locale; discrete-unit fractional validation; malformed and excessive precision; exact repeated addition (0.1 + 0.2); converted-value rounding preview; above-target total; shared concurrent contributions; historical unit/target changes; correction, Undo and backdating; accessible validation errors.

### Confirmed numeric habits excluded from calendar (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option A:** Numeric habits do **not** generate calendar events, all-day items, calendar badges or calendar-specific check-in controls. Track them in the dedicated habit experience and authorized dashboard widgets, including quantity progress, quick-add actions, daily notes and insights as previously approved.
- The calendar must not silently create entries when a numeric habit is created, scheduled, paused, resumed, backdated, completed, edited or ended. Habit scheduling remains authoritative for tracking eligibility, reminders, streaks and historical reporting independently of calendar visibility.
- Preserve existing calendar functionality for tasks, chores, vacations and other previously approved calendar content. Do not interpret this decision as removing the dedicated **monthly activity calendar inside habit insights**; that is a habit visualization, not the main Madeora calendar.
- Personal and shared Home habit privacy, permissions, wall-display restrictions, timezone rules and responsive accessibility continue to apply to habit views and widgets. Calendar search, filters, agenda feeds and exports must not imply numeric habits are calendar events.
- Acceptance criteria: creating a daily or selected-weekday numeric habit adds no main-calendar entry; dashboard quick-add updates habit progress but not the calendar; changing schedule/target or pausing a habit does not create calendar items; dedicated habit insights monthly activity view remains available; no private/shared habit leakage into calendar views or calendar feeds.

### Confirmed optional numeric habit-to-goal automatic progress synchronization (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C (latest decision, superseding the preceding option B read-only-link selection):** Eligible daily numeric habits may optionally connect to a measurable goal so recorded quantities automatically contribute to the goal's progress. This is an explicit exception to the earlier general Goals rule prohibiting automatic syncing or inferred progress; no syncing from tasks, finances, recipes or other modules is approved. Habit and goal creation remain independent by default, and linking is never automatic.
- Only authorized editors with access to both the source habit and destination goal may establish, change or remove a link. Require compatible numeric dimensions and deterministic unit conversion; reject incompatible, ambiguous or custom-unit conversions without an approved mapping. Respect personal versus Home boundaries and existing cross-Home isolation; never expose a private habit through a shared goal or reveal individual shared-habit contributors. Where a link cannot be displayed safely, deny it rather than leaking details.
- On setup, show an explicit preview of source habit, destination goal, unit conversion, effective starting date, historical inclusion choice and projected progress impact. Require confirmation. Define one authoritative contribution ledger per link/day with stable identifiers; synchronize actual accepted daily quantities, including above-target amounts, rather than awarding the daily target or counting a completion as an additional contribution. Prevent duplicate counting across retries, quick-add, imports, edits or repeated background updates. A numeric habit may synchronize with multiple compatible measurable goals under the later approved multi-goal linking rules; each goal independently aggregates eligible linked sources without counting the same source entry twice within that goal.
- A measurable goal has a clear progress breakdown distinguishing **manual adjustments** from **linked habit contributions**. Existing manual progress is retained as a separately identifiable baseline; never overwrite or silently add the same recorded activity twice. Authorized goal editors can adjust the manual portion, while the linked portion is read-only and derives from source entries. Display total progress, target and achievement status using existing goal rules; reaching the target does not silently mark a goal achieved where manual confirmation is required.
- Changes to valid habit quantities, Undo, seven-day backdating, authorized historical corrections, pauses, end/reactivation, schedule or unit changes, and target edits must deterministically recompute affected linked contributions without altering unrelated manual progress. Use historical source units and effective dates for conversion; do not retroactively revalue history because a current target or unit changes. Paused or unscheduled days contribute only where an authorized recorded quantity actually exists under the source rules; never synthesize quantities from streaks, completion flags or reminders.
- Linking, unlinking, changing the effective date, moving to a different goal, archiving or deleting either side must show the exact impact before confirmation. On unlink, stop future synchronization and offer an explicit, clearly explained choice to retain previously synchronized amounts as a fixed manual adjustment or remove linked amounts from the goal; do not silently double-count, lose or orphan contributions. If source access is revoked, freeze or disconnect safely under existing privacy/deletion policy without exposing restricted values; display a permission-safe explanation to authorized goal editors.
- Handle simultaneous edits, stale previews, offline actions, and partial failures transactionally or with idempotent reconciliation so the habit's canonical quantity and the goal's derived contribution eventually agree. Surface pending/stale/error states without claiming synced success early. Provide permission-aware audit history for link setup, changes, removals and resulting goal adjustments, and keep historical records consistent with existing retention and seven-day Trash rules.
- Acceptance criteria: opt-in link with compatible km goal; several daily contributions sum exactly; above-target amounts; two different compatible habits linked to one goal; manual baseline plus derived total; incompatible unit rejection; private-to-shared leak prevented; member loses source permission; same-day Undo and seven-day backdating; historical correction and unit change; target edit without quantity change; concurrent quick-add and offline retry; unlink retain/remove preview; deleted or archived habit; goal achieved threshold without automatic achievement; cross-Home isolation and accessible confirmation.

### Confirmed selectable historical start for habit-to-goal links (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When an authorized user links an eligible numeric habit to a measurable goal, offer three explicit inclusion choices: **from today**, **from a selected past calendar date**, or **from the beginning of the habit**. The chosen start is inclusive, defined in the effective Home/member calendar timezone, and stored as a stable link effective date; never silently assume that all past activity should count.
- **From today** includes eligible quantities dated today and subsequent eligible dates, even if entered before the link was created today; explain this distinction clearly. **Selected past date** cannot precede the habit's recorded start and must be within the accessible, retained history; **beginning of habit** includes all retained eligible recorded history from its first valid date. Do not invent records or count paused/unscheduled dates without valid recorded contributions. The existing seven-day backdating limit for *new quantity entries* is unchanged; selecting older history for inclusion is not permission to backdate new entries.
- Before confirmation show source habit and goal, chosen start date and date range, compatible units/conversion, quantity already eligible for inclusion, manual goal baseline, linked contribution before/after, projected total and percentage, and whether the goal target would be reached. Clearly label historical amounts versus future contributions, including above-target quantities; reaching a numeric threshold does not automatically mark a goal achieved if manual confirmation is required.
- Prevent double counting of any source entry across overlapping links, manual baseline and historical import; warn that manually recorded goal progress representing the same activity must be reviewed, never automatically delete or reinterpret it. Recompute the preview from authoritative data at confirmation and require renewed confirmation if entries, permissions, units, goal state or link availability changed.
- After linking, corrections or Undo to included historical quantities update derived goal progress deterministically, even if corrected later; entries before the selected start remain excluded. A future change to the link start date or destination requires the existing impact preview, explicit confirmation, permissions and audit record. Preserve historical source units, decimal precision, effective schedules and immutable entry identifiers; use idempotent recalculation across concurrent or offline changes.
- Show permission-safe explanations when older records are unavailable, deleted or restricted; do not expose a private source through a shared goal. Acceptance criteria: today with earlier same-day entries; chosen date between existing entries; habit beginning; older history outside seven-day entry window; partial/above-target daily values; converted km/m; manually entered goal baseline; stale preview; revoked permissions; historical correction; timezone boundary; overlapping sources and duplicate prevention.

### Confirmed continued habit-to-goal synchronization after achievement (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** When an eligible measurable goal is marked achieved, active numeric habit links **continue synchronizing actual eligible quantities** until the link is explicitly stopped or changed. Progress can exceed 100% (for example, 125/100 km); display the uncapped total and a clearly labeled over-target amount while keeping visual progress indicators accessible and readable.
- Goal achievement remains a distinct state governed by existing manual-confirmation rules. Reaching or exceeding the target, including through linked contributions, does not automatically mark the goal achieved; once achieved, further contributions do not create another completion, award duplicate rewards, restart a completion event or repeatedly trigger celebrations/notifications. Preserve the original achievement timestamp and actor unless an authorized user explicitly changes the achievement state.
- Continue applying the approved historical link start, compatible unit conversions, quantity corrections, Undo, backdating, privacy and idempotent ledger reconciliation after achievement. Authorized corrections may reduce the total below the target; show the discrepancy clearly, but do not silently revoke or rewrite the manually confirmed achieved state. Offer the authorized goal editor a deliberate review/reopen action with impact preview and audit trail when the achieved status no longer matches numeric progress.
- The achieved goal remains accessible in completed-goal views with its current synchronized quantity and an indication that tracking continues. If a goal is archived, deleted, unlinked or loses required permissions, follow the existing lifecycle and link-disconnection safeguards rather than continuing hidden updates. Respect goal and habit ownership and never expose private contribution details in shared views or wall displays.
- Acceptance criteria: reach 100/100 without automatic achievement; manually achieve then progress to 125/100; no duplicate completion/celebration; Undo to 95/100 after achievement without silently revoking status; correction of pre-link or excluded entries; multi-habit contributions; stale/offline retries; unlink after achievement; archived/deleted goal; restricted-source access; accessible over-target display and audit history.

### Confirmed multiple independent goal links per numeric habit (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** One eligible numeric habit may simultaneously synchronize with **multiple compatible measurable goals**. This supersedes the earlier one-automatic-goal-per-habit restriction. Each habit-to-goal relationship is an independently managed link with its own inclusive historical start date, target goal, compatible unit conversion, permissions, status and audit history. The same legitimate source quantity may contribute once to *each distinct goal*; this is not duplicate counting across goals.
- For each individual goal, deduplicate by immutable source habit, recorded quantity entry/day and link identity, and prevent the same source entry from being counted twice through duplicate links, overlapping effective periods, retries, imports or corrections. Reject or merge redundant links between the same habit and goal rather than stacking contributions. Multiple distinct habits may contribute to one goal only under the already approved compatibility and non-overlapping-source safeguards.
- Creating, editing, pausing or removing one link must not silently modify another goal's link, start date, historical contributions, manual baseline, achieved status or synchronization. Show a per-goal impact preview and require confirmation; when multiple links are affected, show the impact for **each** destination goal separately. Allow users to inspect the list of linked goals from the habit and the contributing habits from each goal, subject to visibility permissions.
- Require authorization to access and edit the source habit and each destination goal. A private habit must not be exposed through shared goals; cross-Home boundaries remain isolated. Recheck permissions independently per link, including after membership changes, archiving, deletion or revocation. Do not reveal names, quantities or link existence to viewers lacking access.
- Propagate valid source quantity changes, Undo, permitted backdating, corrections and historical unit conversions independently to every active eligible link, respecting each link's own effective start date. Use idempotent per-goal reconciliation so offline retries and partial failures cannot double-count or silently lose updates; display link-specific pending/error states and support safe recovery.
- Keep achievement behavior independent: one goal can remain in progress while another is achieved and continues to accumulate beyond 100%, without duplicate achievement events. Unlinking one goal uses its existing retain-as-manual-or-remove preview and does not change other links; warn about potential manual double-counting separately in each destination.
- Acceptance criteria: one running habit linked to monthly and annual distance goals with different start dates; one entry contributes exactly once to each; repeated link attempt to the same goal; independent unit conversions; one goal achieved and another incomplete; source correction after both links exist; unlink only monthly goal; concurrent quick-add and offline retry; permission revoked for one goal; cross-Home/privacy rejection; independent audit and accurate per-goal previews.

### Confirmed flexible habit tracking schedules (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Habits support daily schedules, selected weekdays, and weekly frequency targets (for example, three completions per week). Numeric daily targets are separately approved for daily and selected-weekday habits; advanced coaching remains out of scope.
- A habit has an owner/context (personal or an eligible Home), name, active schedule, optional description, start date, optional end date, and optional reminder settings. Require only fields necessary to create a valid habit; optional information can be added later. Follow existing personal/Home privacy and permission rules.
- Members can record and undo completion for eligible dates/occurrences, see completion history, current progress against the active schedule, and understandable streaks. Prevent duplicate completion credit for the same scheduled occurrence; support accessible correction of accidental check-ins with a clear audit/history policy consistent with global rules.
- For **daily and selected-weekday** habits, count consecutive scheduled opportunities completed, not calendar days on which the habit was not scheduled. For **weekly-frequency** habits, count consecutive completed target weeks; show progress as completions out of the target for the current week. Define the week boundary using the member's configured locale/timezone consistently and disclose it in the interface.
- Missing a scheduled opportunity is an incomplete occurrence, not an assumed completion. Future opportunities do not count as missed. Provide a clear distinction between pending, completed, missed, paused, and not scheduled; avoid punitive language. If schedule changes, preserve historical completion records and the schedule applicable to each period rather than retroactively rewriting history.
- Allow members to edit a schedule, pause/resume a habit, and archive it without erasing history. Clarify the effect on streaks and future reminders in an editable confirmation preview; paused dates should not create missed occurrences. Changes to an active weekly target must not silently re-score prior weeks.
- Reminders are optional and must respect existing notification preferences, quiet hours, permissions, timezone, and Home privacy. No default reminders or unrequested public leaderboard visibility. Habit completion does not automatically award reward points unless a separately approved reward rule explicitly allows it.
- Acceptance criteria: daily habit completed/missed; Monday-Wednesday-Friday habit with intervening unscheduled days; three-times-per-week target reached and not reached; week/timezone boundary; same-day duplicate check-in and undo; late backfill; paused/resumed habit; schedule edit midweek; archived habit; private vs Home habit; optional reminder disabled/quiet hours; inaccessible habit; responsive and accessible progress/streak states.

### Confirmed measurable and completion-based goals (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** The Goals module supports both **completion-based goals** (achieved/not achieved) and **measurable goals** with a numeric target and manually entered progress (for example, 12 of 20 books or €750 of €2,000). Milestones, subtasks, dependencies, and advanced analytics are not approved by this decision. Automatic progress syncing is allowed **only** for explicitly linked eligible numeric habits under the later approved numeric habit-to-goal synchronization rules.
- A goal has a personal or authorized Home context, required title and goal type, optional description and deadline. Measurable goals require a valid target greater than zero, a clear user-selected unit/label where applicable, and an initial progress value (default zero); completion goals require no numeric target. Keep other fields optional and permit later editing subject to permissions.
- Show an accessible progress view: clear achieved/not-achieved status for completion goals, and current value, target, unit and percentage for measurable goals. Clearly distinguish reaching a target from manually marking a goal completed; do not silently alter user-entered values or infer progress from finances, recipes or tasks. The separately approved optional numeric habit-to-goal link may supply an explicitly identified derived progress component without changing manually entered values.
- Allow authorized members to enter and correct progress manually, with validation for numeric values, decimal precision and units. Warn before a target/unit change that would reinterpret existing progress. Handle values beyond target transparently rather than hiding them or automatically capping recorded progress; cap only the visual progress bar at 100% where necessary.
- Deadlines are optional; a passed deadline does not erase progress or automatically delete a goal. Distinguish active, achieved, overdue, paused (if supported), and archived states without implying failure when no deadline exists. Changes and corrections must respect existing activity/history and recovery policies.
- Respect personal/Home isolation, view/edit permissions, privacy on widgets and wall displays, and notification preferences. No automatic rewards, public rankings, financial account connections or automatic deadline reminders are implied by this approval.
- Acceptance criteria: create/complete a binary goal; create and increment a measurable goal; zero and decimal progress; target reached and exceeded; correction/decrease; invalid/negative target; incompatible unit edit; deadline passed; deadline absent; goal edited/archived; personal versus Home access; unauthorized change; accessible mobile progress display.

### Confirmed collaborative Home goal contributions (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Home-owned goals support collaborative, permission-controlled progress contributions. Eligible Home members may record contributions to measurable shared goals; members with authorized editing rights may correct entries and change goal definitions or targets. Do not introduce leaderboards, rankings, personal contribution targets, or automatic module-derived progress.
- Keep personal goals private under existing personal-space rules. For Home goals, distinguish **view**, **contribute progress**, and **manage/edit goal** capabilities using the approved Home permission system. Viewing a shared goal does not automatically grant contribution or editing rights; a member cannot contribute after losing access.
- Every measurable-goal contribution records its signed or positive numeric change as appropriate, unit, contributing member, timestamp and optional note, while the goal displays its current aggregate value, target and progress. Members can see an appropriately permission-filtered contribution history identifying who contributed and when. Make it clear that these are manual declarations, not verified financial transactions or independently confirmed activities.
- Prefer append-only correction history or clearly linked correction records over silently rewriting previous contributions. Authorized corrections may adjust or reverse a mistaken contribution, including entries by other members when permitted, with an audit trail identifying the correcting member, time, original entry and reason when supplied. Preserve the integrity of the total; prevent duplicate submissions and reconcile simultaneous contributions safely.
- For completion-based shared goals, eligible contributors may mark completion only if the goal's permissions permit that action; changing a goal's completion state or target is treated as a goal-management action where the existing permission model requires it. Do not turn binary completion goals into contribution counters.
- Validate numeric precision, goal units, negative/zero values, current goal state, active Home membership, permission changes and goal availability before saving. Show a clear confirmation/undo or correction path, distinguish additions from replacement of the total, and prevent stale offline retries from applying a contribution twice. Respect archive, recovery, visibility, notification and wall-display privacy rules.
- Acceptance criteria: two members contributing concurrently; contributor identity and date; contributor without edit permission; viewer without contribution permission; correction of own and another member's entry by an authorized editor; removal of access after contributing; duplicated retry; decimal units; target reached/exceeded; archived or deleted goal; completion-only shared goal; privacy-safe widgets and accessible contribution history.

### Confirmed completed goal lifecycle and guided renewal (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option C:** Achieved goals appear in a distinct **Completed** section, with their achievement date, final progress and contribution/correction history preserved. Members with appropriate permissions may reopen or archive a completed goal and may use guided **Renew**, **Increase target**, or **Create recurring goal** actions. This is explicit user-driven continuation, not automatic renewal or a new automatic progress source.
- For completion-based goals, authorized members explicitly mark the goal achieved. For measurable goals, reaching or exceeding the target should visibly indicate the target is reached and offer an explicit **Mark achieved** action; do not silently change achievement state solely because a numeric threshold was reached. A completed goal is separated from active views by default but remains accessible through Completed/history filters and permission-safe widgets.
- **Renew / duplicate:** Present an editable proposal for a new goal based on the completed one, preserving the original goal and its history. Default the new goal's progress to zero and its status to active; allow the member to review title, type, target, unit, ownership/Home, optional deadline and recurrence before confirmation. Never copy old contributions as new progress or assume that a previously shared goal can be shared to a different Home.
- **Increase target:** Show a clear before-and-after target and current progress, with a choice to **extend the existing goal** or **create a new follow-up goal**. Extending preserves the contribution ledger and records the target change; it must not erase or silently rewrite the historical achievement milestone. If the new target means the goal is no longer achieved, show and confirm the resulting status change explicitly. Validate compatible units and positive targets.
- **Recurring goals:** Support a bounded, user-configured monthly or yearly repeat pattern for completion-based goals, and weekly, monthly or yearly repeat patterns for measurable goals under the later explicit approval. Each new period creates a distinct goal instance with its own zeroed progress, optional derived period deadline and independent achieved state; retain links to previous periods for history. Make the recurrence schedule, start period, timezone, next instance and stop/pause behavior visible before enabling. Do not backfill old periods, create duplicates on retries, or infer achievements from previous periods. Only authorized members may configure, pause or end a recurrence; completed past instances remain unchanged.
- Respect existing personal/Home ownership, view/contribute/manage permissions, privacy, notification rules, audit and recovery policies. Revalidate permissions and current goal state on every action; handle simultaneous edits, archived/deleted goals, revoked Home access, invalid dates and recurring month/year boundaries. Do not introduce leaderboards, milestone planning, automatic module-derived progress or automatic rewards.
- Acceptance criteria: complete binary goal; reach measurable target without automatic achievement; achieved goal appears in Completed; authorized reopen/archive; renew with zero progress; duplicate into permitted Home only; extend target with recorded prior achievement; extend target below/above existing progress; monthly and yearly recurring instances; pause/end recurrence; year/month boundary; shared goal contributor history; read-only member denied; concurrent renewal and idempotent retry; privacy-safe widgets; accessible mobile confirmation and history.

### Confirmed weekly, monthly and yearly recurring measurable goals (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Measurable goals may optionally recur **weekly, monthly or yearly**. This extends the earlier monthly/yearly recurring-goal lifecycle approval to weekly measurable goals and replaces any implication that each new period must be started manually. Recurrence is opt-in; one-time goals and the separately approved completion-based goal recurrence remain supported.
- Configure target, compatible unit, recurrence frequency, first period, timezone and applicable week-start convention, optional end/stop date and pause behavior. Preview the first and next periods before activation. Each period is a separate, traceable goal instance with its own start/end boundaries, progress ledger, target, achieved state, deadlines/reminders where enabled, audit history and stable link to the recurring series. Never roll excess quantity into the next period or copy prior-period progress.
- Automatically create each eligible period once, idempotently, even across retries, offline actions, concurrent edits, daylight-saving changes and month/year boundaries. Display current, upcoming and historical periods clearly; paused/stopped series create no new active periods, while previous instances and achievements remain accessible. Do not backfill historical instances without a separately reviewed, explicit operation.
- An eligible numeric habit may synchronize with multiple compatible recurring measurable goal series. For each destination instance, count only qualifying habit quantities on dates **within that instance's period** and within that habit-to-goal link's approved inclusive historical start date. A quantity can contribute once to each distinct compatible goal instance but never twice to the same instance. Respect source habit schedules, pauses, unit versions, corrections and permissions; avoid cross-period leakage and double counting.
- When configuring a recurring link, preview which goal instances are affected and any eligible historical quantities. Future instances inherit the approved link configuration but revalidate source/destination access and unit compatibility before syncing. If a past correction changes a past period, reconcile that instance alone, retain an auditable achievement history and use the existing explicit review path before revoking an achieved state. Continue permitted beyond-target synchronization within the corresponding instance without awarding repeat achievements.
- Recurrence edits to target, frequency, dates, unit or linkage apply prospectively by default, with an impact preview and explicit approval for any change to existing instances. Preserve historical instances under their original definitions. Ending or unlinking a series must explain effects on future synchronization and existing recorded progress; no silent deletion of history. Manual contributions remain separately identifiable from automatically derived quantities.
- Respect Home/personal privacy, contributor versus manager permissions, reminders, wall-display restrictions and accessibility. Errors, incompatible conversions, revoked access or failed period creation must be visible to authorized users and recoverable without duplicate instances or entries.
- Acceptance criteria: weekly running target with locale week start; monthly and yearly targets; weekly boundary across years; February and leap-year periods; one habit linked to monthly and annual series; link starts midway through a period; repeated synchronization retries; backdated correction across a period boundary; beyond-100% progress; past achievement review; pause/resume/stop series; changing target next period; unauthorized Home member; cross-Home rejection; accessible period history and preview.

### Confirmed recurring measurable goal period finalization and historical corrections (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** At the end of each weekly, monthly or yearly recurring measurable-goal period, automatically finalize that instance's result as **target reached** or **target not reached** based on its effective target and recorded eligible progress at the period boundary. Start the next eligible period independently at zero, even when the previous period missed its target; never carry forward shortages, excess or unreviewed contributions.
- Distinguish the **calculated period outcome** from the existing explicit **Mark achieved** action and preserved achievement milestones. Finalization must not silently fabricate a manually confirmed achievement or revoke one. Show the final calculated outcome, recorded quantity, target, end date and any separately confirmed achieved status clearly in period history and summaries.
- Authorized historical corrections, including audited manual contribution corrections and eligible source numeric-habit corrections/backdating, may update the finalized period's total and calculated outcome. Recalculate only affected instances using their original period boundaries, target and unit versions; never shift a past contribution into the current period or retroactively apply a later target definition. Record before/after values, reason when supplied, actor, timestamp and source, with permission-filtered history.
- When a correction changes an ended period from target not reached to target reached, update its calculated outcome and surface the change in history without granting duplicate celebrations, rewards or achievements. When a correction would invalidate a previously explicitly achieved period, preserve that achievement pending the existing explicit review/confirmation path rather than silently revoking it. Do not automatically reopen or extend the period.
- Finalization and recalculation must be idempotent across period boundaries, offline retries, concurrent writes, timezone/DST changes and delayed synchronization. Revalidate Home/personal permissions, source-link eligibility and retention/correction limits; reject unauthorized edits with useful error feedback. Clearly mark finalized results that were later corrected.
- Acceptance criteria: October 75/100 closes not reached and November starts 0/100; authorized late correction to October 105/100 changes October's calculated result only; reverse correction below target; confirmed achievement requires review before revocation; two linked monthly/yearly goal series; source habit correction; duplicate boundary job; simultaneous correction and rollover; paused/stopped recurrence; cross-Home access rejection; privacy-safe historical display.

### Confirmed recurring measurable goal target effective periods (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** An authorized editor can change the target of a recurring measurable-goal series effective for **the current period or a selected future period**. The default should be the next period; show which instances will use the new target and retain earlier period definitions.
- Before saving, preview the current target, proposed target and unit, effective period, current recorded progress, target-reached outcome and any effect on explicitly confirmed achievement, reminders, widgets, and linked numeric-habit synchronization. If a change to the **current** period would alter its calculated completion/target-reached status, require explicit confirmation after showing the before/after effect; never silently revoke a previously confirmed achievement.
- Apply target revisions atomically and prospectively from the chosen period. Completed historical periods retain their original targets, outcomes and audit history. Do not rewrite past instances or retroactively recalculate them under a later target; corrections to historical contributions still use the original period target.
- Validate positive target values, unit compatibility, recurrence status, role/ownership permissions and concurrent changes. Recheck progress at confirmation; if the preview becomes stale, show the updated impact and request confirmation again. Log editor, time, old/new target and effective period. Make failures recoverable without partially updating the series or creating duplicate instances.
- Acceptance criteria: edit next monthly period; edit current period without changing result; lower current target to reach it; raise current target to no longer reach it; already confirmed achievement review; edit future annual period; historical period remains unchanged; concurrent progress update; unauthorized editor; timezone boundary; retry and offline recovery.

### Confirmed configurable goal deadline and overdue reminders (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Goals with an optional deadline may have **opt-in, configurable reminders** before the deadline and an optional overdue reminder. This explicitly supersedes the earlier statement that the basic goal definition alone does not imply automatic deadline reminders; reminders exist only when deliberately enabled for a goal. No predictive pacing, inferred urgency, or automatic progress warnings.
- Show a clear reminder toggle and editable advance timing (for example, a chosen number of days before the deadline) and optional overdue timing. No reminders are scheduled by default. Do not require a deadline for goals without reminders; enabling deadline reminders requires a valid deadline. Show a preview of the next reminder date/time in the relevant personal or Home timezone, including when a requested lead time has already passed.
- Respect each recipient's existing notification-channel preferences, quiet hours, consent, access rights, and Home/personal boundaries. For shared Home goals, only eligible recipients with goal visibility and notification eligibility may receive notifications; contribution or management rights are not implicitly granted. Do not reveal private progress, contribution identities, or sensitive goal details in shared-screen previews or restricted notifications.
- Reminders stop when the goal is **achieved, archived, deleted, or otherwise inaccessible** to a recipient. Reopening a goal does not silently restore disabled reminders; if previously enabled, revalidate its deadline and settings before scheduling further notifications. Changing a deadline, goal ownership, recurrence, or reminder configuration cancels obsolete reminders and recomputes only eligible future ones. Never send a reminder retroactively just because a lead-time threshold was missed.
- For recurring monthly/yearly goals, reminder preferences may be included in the reviewed recurrence setup, but each instance's deadline and reminder eligibility are evaluated separately. A completed instance never continues reminding merely because the series remains active. Avoid duplicate delivery on retries and concurrent edits; expose delivery failure or unavailable-channel states without falsely claiming a notification was delivered.
- Acceptance criteria: no deadline/no reminder; deadline with reminder disabled; configurable advance reminder; optional overdue reminder; completed or archived goal suppresses delivery; late enablement; deadline change; goal reopened; shared Home permissions and member removal; per-member quiet hours; timezone and daylight-saving boundary; recurring goal instance; duplicate scheduling/retry; inaccessible goal; accessible mobile configuration and notification preview.

### Confirmed forward-only habit schedule changes (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Editing a personal or shared Home habit schedule preserves historical completion records, missed/paused opportunities, streak achievements and the schedule that applied to each historical period. The revised schedule applies only from a selected **effective date forward**; never retroactively re-score earlier periods or rewrite earned achievements.
- Authorized editors preview the old schedule, proposed daily/weekday/weekly-target schedule, effective date, affected upcoming opportunities, reminders, current streak display and any intersecting pauses before confirming. Require a valid effective date no earlier than the next unfinalized scheduling boundary; do not permit a backdated edit that changes finalized history. Apply Home permissions to shared-habit edits and protect personal habits from other members.
- Keep a dated, auditable schedule-version history. Evaluate each occurrence under the schedule version effective for its date; do not create duplicate occurrences, missed days or completions across a transition. Existing check-ins remain attributed to their original opportunities and retain their contributor and correction history.
- For weekly-frequency targets, avoid mixing incompatible targets inside a partially elapsed week: default the effective boundary to the start of the next habit-local week, or require an explicit impact-aware transition policy that preserves already-finalized results. For daily/weekday schedules, future eligible dates follow the new version. Display streak continuity intelligibly across schedule versions, with historical streak milestones unchanged; current progress uses only the applicable schedule.
- Preserve manual and vacation pauses across the change; paused periods remain excluded from missed-opportunity and catch-up calculations. Re-evaluate future reminders against the new schedule without emitting retroactive alerts. Handle timezone and DST boundaries, overlapping pauses, pending check-ins, archived habits, concurrent edits, offline retries and revoked permissions safely and idempotently.
- Acceptance criteria: daily-to-weekdays and weekdays-to-weekly transitions; effective date selection; midweek weekly-target change; historical missed and completed occurrences; streak before/after transition; overlapping manual/vacation pause; reminder rescheduling; simultaneous edits and check-ins; Home permissions; accessible impact preview and history.

### Confirmed opt-in shared Home habit pause notifications (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each eligible Home member may independently opt in to notifications when an individual shared Home habit is **manually paused or resumed** by an authorized manager. Default to no dedicated pause/resume notification until the member opts in; the habit's effective **Paused** or active status must always appear in authorized Home views regardless of notification choice.
- Offer a clear per-member Home-habit pause/resume notification preference in the relevant notification settings. An opt-in controls delivery, not whether the member may see the habit. Apply existing Home membership, habit visibility, channel consent, quiet hours, accessibility and device preferences; never disclose a restricted habit or manager-only details through notifications, lock screens or wall displays.
- Notify opted-in eligible members of confirmed manager-initiated manual pause, manager-initiated manual resume and automatic resumption at a configured resume date, when the effective status actually changes. Show the habit name only to recipients authorized to see it, and a neutral status and optional resume date. Do not send duplicate notifications for edits that leave the effective status unchanged, or for changes attributable solely to a Home vacation; existing silent vacation-change policy remains in force.
- Where a manual pause ends but an overlapping manual or vacation pause still applies, keep the habit visibly paused and avoid a misleading **resumed** notification. Re-evaluate effective state after each change; avoid exposing private vacation reasons. If an opted-in member loses Home/habit access, stop delivery immediately. Suppress obsolete scheduled notifications when pause dates, visibility or member preferences change.
- Record pause/resume changes in permission-aware history, but do not expose other members' notification preferences. Notification failure must not roll back the underlying habit state or imply successful delivery. Handle concurrent edits, retries, date boundaries and overlapping pauses idempotently.
- Acceptance criteria: default opt-out; member opts in/out; manager manually pauses; manager manually resumes; scheduled auto-resume; status remains visible with notifications off; overlapping vacation prevents false resumed alert; manual pause edited without effective state change; quiet hours; revoked permission; duplicate retry; private Home separation; accessible settings and in-app status.

### Confirmed independent manual shared Home habit pauses (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized Home managers may manually pause an individual **shared Home habit**, independently of vacations, starting immediately. Each pause may have a future resume date or remain open-ended until a manager explicitly resumes it. Ordinary members may view the status but cannot create, edit or end shared-habit pauses unless separately authorized by the Home permission model.
- Before confirmation, show the habit, Home, start date, optional resume date, affected scheduled occurrences, reminder suppression and streak impact. Use Home-local calendar dates and existing timezone and daylight-saving rules. A dated pause automatically ends at the specified resume boundary; open-ended pauses require manager action. Changes affect only that Home and that shared habit, not personal habits or other Homes.
- While paused, show a clear **Paused** state to permitted Home members on habit details, dashboards and relevant widgets, with the expected resume date or **Until resumed**. Suppress due/missed indicators and reminders for paused occurrences. Paused occurrences are neither completed nor failed, preserve the existing streak, award no completion credit and require no catch-up. Resume on the original cadence without backfilling or fabricating completions.
- Combine manual pauses with applicable Home vacation pauses: the shared habit remains paused while **any** valid pause applies. Ending a manual pause must not override an active vacation pause, and ending a vacation must not override a still-active manual pause. Explain the effective paused state without revealing information a viewer is not entitled to see.
- Preserve completed historical occurrences, legitimate prior rewards/contributions, audit history and existing habit definitions. Log manager, creation/edit/resume actions and effective dates. Revalidate permissions and occurrence state on edits, completion attempts, schedule changes, concurrent actions and retries; prevent duplicate or retroactive state transitions.
- Acceptance criteria: pause one shared habit immediately; future dated automatic resumption; open-ended manual resumption; manager edit/cancel; ordinary-member read-only status; overlapping vacation and manual pause; daily/weekday/weekly schedules; suppressed reminders and missed flags; streak preserved without false completions; no catch-up; existing completion protected; multi-Home isolation; permission revocation; concurrent edits; accessible mobile, desktop and wall-display representations.

### Confirmed independent manual personal habit pauses (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Members may manually pause any individual personal habit independently of vacations, beginning immediately, with an optional future resume date or an open-ended duration. Only the habit owner may create, edit, resume or cancel these pauses; no Home manager privilege grants access to another member's private habit settings.
- Offer a clear **Pause habit** action on personal habit details/settings, previewing the effective start, optional resume date, affected scheduled occurrences, reminder suppression and streak treatment before confirmation. An open-ended pause remains active until the member explicitly resumes; a dated pause resumes automatically at the chosen boundary in the habit's applicable timezone. Show **Paused** and an accessible resume/edit action rather than implying the habit was completed or missed.
- Suppress check-in obligations, missed-day penalties, streak-breaking effects and routine reminders only for opportunities covered by the effective pause. Preserve the streak accumulated before the pause without awarding artificial completions or points. Do not require catch-up or backfill after resumption; resume the existing cadence and normal streak rules for newly active opportunities. Keep historical completions and audit entries intact.
- Combine manual and vacation-based personal-habit pauses as a union of applicable intervals: a habit remains paused while **any** valid pause covers an occurrence. Ending one pause must not reactivate an occurrence still covered by another. Vacation settings remain separately editable and do not overwrite manual pauses.
- Handle daily, selected-weekday and weekly habits consistently; define occurrence-level pause applicability in the habit's configured timezone, with DST-safe inclusive calendar-day semantics and a clear preview for partially overlapping weekly periods. Editing a future resume date must re-evaluate only unfinalized future occurrences, without rewriting genuine past completions or converting already paused opportunities into retroactive misses.
- Prevent duplicate reminders, missed records or streak adjustments under retries, simultaneous check-in/pause edits and automatic resumption. Preserve permission-aware personal history; exclude private pause reasons or settings from Home dashboards, manager views and wall displays. A reason is not required.
- Acceptance criteria: pause immediately with date; pause indefinitely and resume manually; auto-resume; cancel/edit; multiple habits with independent pauses; overlap with individual vacation; daily/weekday/weekly schedules; reminders suppressed/resumed; streak continuity without artificial completion; no backfill; prior completion protected; DST/timezone; private access; concurrent actions; responsive accessible states.

### Confirmed optional personal habit pauses during individual vacations (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Each member may optionally pause **selected personal habits** for their own approved individual vacation date range. The default is no personal-habit pause; do not automatically pause all habits, and do not apply another member's vacation choices to the user's personal space. This is separate from manager-configured Home-wide shared-habit pauses.
- In vacation creation/editing and the member's own vacation participation settings, offer a permission-safe list of that member's active personal habits with independent pause switches and a preview of affected scheduled days/weeks, reminders and streaks. The member controls their own selection; Home managers cannot inspect or change private habit choices merely because they manage the vacation. Group-vacation participants choose independently after accepting their participation.
- During the member's actual inclusive individual vacation dates, suppress scheduled obligations and routine reminders for selected habits; show **Paused for vacation** rather than Missed or Completed. Paused opportunities do not break streaks, do not grant completion credit, and require no catch-up or retroactive backfill. Resume the original schedule automatically afterward, preserving pre-vacation history and streak continuity. Unselected personal habits continue normally.
- For weekly-frequency targets, exclude vacation-paused scheduled opportunities consistently when determining progress and streaks; display a clear vacation-adjusted weekly state rather than silently treating an incomplete paused week as failure or awarding artificial progress. Preserve genuine completions already recorded before the pause and permit deliberate member-initiated history corrections under existing rules without creating duplicates.
- Recalculate future pause intervals when the member approves, edits, shortens, withdraws from or cancels their individual vacation. Preview consequences, including newly active scheduled opportunities; never silently erase real completions, alter historical points, or backfill missed check-ins. Overlapping vacations that pause the same habit act as a union of dates; removing one pause does not resume a day still covered by another. Use the personal habit's applicable timezone and schedule boundaries, with clear handling of DST.
- Keep personal habit selections, history and reminders private from other Home members, shared dashboards and wall displays. Honor notification settings and accessibility; use idempotent transitions for concurrent vacation/habit edits, membership changes and retries.
- Acceptance criteria: default no pause; one of several personal habits paused; daily/weekday and weekly targets; streak preserved with no artificial completion; reminders suppressed and restored; participant in group vacation chooses independently; overlapping vacations; shortened/cancelled vacation; existing completion retained; private habit not disclosed to manager; timezone/DST; no backfill; accessible mobile settings and history.

### Confirmed personal and collaborative Home habits (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Habits may belong to a member's private personal space or to one Home. A Home habit has one shared schedule and **one Home-wide completion per scheduled occurrence**, regardless of which eligible member checks it off.
