Do not introduce per-member completion requirements, individual streaks for a Home habit, leaderboards or competitive rankings.
- Home members with the appropriate habit **view** permission may see the shared habit and its permitted history; members with **complete/check-in** permission may mark an eligible occurrence complete or submit a permitted correction; managing the habit's name, schedule, reminders, pause/archive status and permissions requires the relevant **manage** permission. Personal habits remain visible and editable only under personal-space access rules.
- Each shared occurrence records its completion state, the member who completed it, completion timestamp and relevant scheduled date/week. Show this contributor information in permission-filtered history. For weekly-frequency habits, count eligible check-ins toward the single Home-wide weekly target, with no duplicate credit for the same recorded check-in; make clear which check-ins count and how many remain.
- Prevent concurrent members from creating duplicate credit for the same daily/weekday occurrence; if another member already completed it, show the existing state and contributor instead of overwriting them. For weekly-frequency targets, ensure concurrency-safe counting and no double application of retries. Only authorized members may undo or correct a shared completion; retain the original contributor and correction audit information in accordance with existing history policies.
- Compute Home-habit progress and streaks from the shared schedule and Home-wide completion history, using a consistent disclosed Home timezone/week boundary where configured, without counting unscheduled or paused days as missed. Changes to Home membership or permission must take effect immediately for future access, while retaining historically accurate contributor attribution subject to privacy policy.
- Show personal and Home habits distinctly in their appropriate dashboards and widgets. Never expose personal check-ins or restricted contributor details on Home dashboards, wall displays, notifications or other shared surfaces. Optional reminders obey the approved notification and privacy rules; no automatic rewards are introduced.
- Acceptance criteria: personal habit private; Home habit visible to permitted members; member with view-only access; two members attempting same daily check-in concurrently; shared weekly target completed by multiple members; duplicate retry; contributor history; authorized correction/undo; member removed from Home; pause and schedule change; timezone/week boundary; privacy-safe widget and wall-display rendering; accessible mobile check-in states.

### Confirmed seven-day habit completion backdating (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Authorized members may explicitly record a genuinely completed personal or shared Home habit opportunity retrospectively for **up to seven calendar days**, measured in the habit's applicable personal/Home local timezone. The current date and seven preceding local dates are eligible; older opportunities are read-only for new completion backfills under this feature. This narrows the previously open-ended historical correction wording for **adding forgotten completions**, without erasing established audit records.
- Backdating is available only for an actual scheduled opportunity under the schedule version effective on that date, after the habit started, and not for an unscheduled, paused, future, deleted or otherwise ineligible occurrence. For weekly-frequency targets, backdated check-ins must represent legitimate distinct eligible contributions within the permitted date window, without duplicate credit or bypassing the weekly target. Never create artificial completions for paused periods or imply that a completion occurred at the time it was entered.
- Show the chosen occurrence/date, original state, recorded-at timestamp and claimed completion date before confirmation. After saving, recalculate affected weekly totals, current/historical streaks and progress using the historically applicable schedule and pauses; preserve previously earned streak milestones as historical facts while transparently showing any corrected calculations. Keep the actor, original contributor if any, claimed date, actual recording time and before/after values in permission-aware audit history.
- For personal habits, only the owner may backdate; for shared Home habits, require current membership and explicit completion/correction permission. Prevent concurrent duplicate check-ins, stale-state overwrites and repeated retries. Explain why an out-of-window, already-completed or ineligible date cannot be backfilled. Honor DST boundaries, schedule changes, archival and privacy in widgets, notifications and wall displays.
- This seven-day limit applies to **new retrospective completion entries**; separately authorized correction or removal of an inaccurate *existing* record remains subject to the established correction permissions and audit policy, and must not be used as a workaround to create an older missing completion.
- Acceptance criteria: same-day and seven-days-ago eligible backfill; eight-days-ago rejected; local midnight and DST; missed daily/weekday streak restored; weekly target reached after valid backfill; unscheduled/paused opportunity rejected; schedule version change; duplicate shared Home check-in; revoked permission; correcting existing erroneous history versus adding a new old completion; accessible preview and audit.

### Confirmed subtle habit streak milestone celebrations (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Recognize meaningful personal and shared Home habit streak milestones with a brief, tasteful, non-intrusive visual celebration. This is visual recognition only: no extra points, reward credits, collectible badges, leaderboard entries, or new Ora assistant capabilities.
- Use a small, consistent set of clearly labelled milestones (for example 7, 30 and 100 qualifying scheduled opportunities or completed target weeks, depending on habit frequency); show the actual streak unit and never present calendar days as completed habit days for a non-daily schedule. Apply the historically effective schedule and pause rules.
- Trigger once when a persisted check-in or authorized correction newly establishes a milestone; group or suppress simultaneous/repeated effects, and never replay on refresh, retries, background synchronization or ordinary navigation. A backdated correction may update milestone history without an intrusive retroactive animation. Do not celebrate provisional, unsaved or duplicate completions.
- Respect reduced-motion preferences by replacing motion with static, accessible confirmation; keep animations short, dismissible and non-blocking, without sound or forced focus. Respect quiet hours, notification preferences and muted celebration settings; no push notification is required by this decision.
- Personal milestone recognition stays private to the habit owner. Shared Home milestone recognition appears only in permission-authorized Home habit surfaces and must not reveal restricted contributors, private member habits or information on shared wall displays. Avoid celebrations for paused or ineligible occurrences.
- If a completion is undone or corrected and the qualifying streak changes, recalculate current progress accurately while retaining an auditable record of what was previously shown; do not issue duplicate milestone celebrations when the same threshold is regained through correction/retry. Preserve existing streak-history and schedule-version rules.
- Acceptance criteria: daily and weekly milestone; selected-weekday schedule; manual/vacation pause; successful completion; reduced motion; muted celebrations; concurrent shared completion; repeated save/retry; backdated correction; Undo; privacy on Home dashboard and wall display; no bonus points or badges.

### Confirmed simple habit insights (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Provide a dedicated, straightforward habit insights view for personal habits and permission-accessible shared Home habits. Show completion rate, longest achieved streak, a monthly activity calendar, and simple progress trends. Do not introduce advanced analytics, competitive rankings, automated coaching, or additional rewards.
- Calculate completion rate as completed eligible scheduled opportunities divided by eligible scheduled opportunities in the selected period. Exclude paused, unscheduled, not-yet-due, and otherwise ineligible opportunities from the denominator; distinguish zero eligible opportunities from a 0% completion rate. Weekly-frequency habits use their historical weekly targets and applicable partial-week rules rather than assuming a daily target.
- Preserve schedule history: use the schedule effective on each historical date, never retroactively impose the current schedule. Manual and vacation pauses are neutral, preserve streaks, and create no catch-up debt. Longest streak uses the approved streak rules for each frequency and historical schedule, with paused intervals neutral rather than achievements.
- Monthly calendar clearly differentiates completed, missed, paused, not scheduled, future, and pending/eligible opportunities; weekly-target habits display their week-level fulfillment without inventing daily obligations. Simple trend charts show consistent time periods, readable labels, and sufficient context for schedule changes and pauses so rate changes are not misleading.
- Backdated completions, authorized corrections, Undo, changed effective schedules and pause changes refresh affected statistics deterministically without duplicating milestones or modifying immutable audit records. Explain when a statistic changes because of a correction.
- Personal insights are visible only to their owner; shared Home habit insights follow existing Home access and contribution privacy rules. Never expose another member's private habit activity or vacation details through charts, tooltips, exports, or wall displays. Provide accessible text equivalents for calendars and trends, responsive layouts, empty states, and clear insufficient-data messaging.
- Acceptance criteria: daily/weekday/weekly schedules; historical schedule transition; overlapping pauses; no eligible opportunities; mid-period habit creation; late/backdated completion; correction and Undo; shared habit permissions; cross-Home isolation; responsive and reduced-motion accessible rendering.

### Confirmed optional habit end dates (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Personal and shared Home habits may have an optional **inclusive end date**, in addition to their existing start date and schedule. No end date means the habit continues until explicitly ended or archived. The end date stops generating eligible scheduled opportunities **after that date**; it does not delete the habit or its historical records.
- The personal habit owner or an authorized Home habit manager may set, edit or clear the end date, subject to existing permissions. Validate that the end date is not earlier than the habit start date; show the applicable personal/Home timezone, a preview of the last eligible scheduled occurrence and the effect on future reminders, widgets and progress. An end date may fall on an unscheduled or paused day without creating a completion obligation.
- Once ended, show a distinct **Ended** status and preserve the habit in accessible history and insights. Stop future check-in opportunities, reminders, streak obligations and milestone celebrations; do not treat days after the end date as missed, paused or completed. Preserve historical completions, contributor attribution, previously attained streaks, historical schedule versions, pauses, milestones and audit trails. Insights denominators exclude dates after the end date and distinguish ended habits from habits with no recent activity.
- Editing or removing a future end date updates only future opportunities. Moving an end date into the past requires an explicit impact preview and confirmation, particularly when existing completions, corrections or shared contributions would lie beyond the proposed end; do not silently erase or invalidate recorded history. Reject conflicting changes or require a separate authorized resolution before applying them. Reopening an ended habit by clearing/extending the end date must not automatically backfill elapsed opportunities or retroactively break preserved streak achievements; show a clear preview of how the renewed schedule will start.
- Manual and vacation pauses remain independent; an end date does not cancel or change their audit history. Respect the seven-day backdating window for opportunities on or before the end date, existing history-correction permissions, concurrency protections and Home isolation. Archive/delete/recovery are separate actions and must not be conflated with ending a habit.
- Acceptance criteria: no end date; future end date; end on scheduled versus unscheduled day; end while paused; reminders and widgets stop; insights and longest streak retained; past-end check-in rejected; valid seven-day backfill before end; invalid end before start; changing end date with conflicting existing completion; extending/reopening ended habit without silent catch-up; shared Home permissions and audit; local midnight/DST; mobile, wall and accessibility states.

### Confirmed separate ended habit section (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** The primary personal and shared Home habit lists focus on active and currently paused habits. Habits whose inclusive end dates have passed appear in a clearly labelled, separately accessible **Ended habits** section, not intermingled with daily check-ins. Ended is a lifecycle status, **not** automatic archival or deletion.
- Provide an easy-to-find entry to Ended habits from the habit list, with an optional count and useful search/filter behavior. Each ended habit retains its name, original end date, historical schedule, completions, achieved streaks, milestones and dedicated insights; selecting it opens a read-only historical overview with authorized end-date editing or reactivation controls.
- Ended habits generate no new check-ins, missed opportunities, reminders or celebrations. Existing dashboard habit check-in widgets should exclude ended habits from active queues; configured insights widgets may still display authorized historical insights with an explicit **Ended** label and no misleading current obligation.
- Reopening an ended habit through the approved end-date editing workflow returns it to the active list only after valid confirmation, without silently backfilling elapsed dates or altering preserved history. Archived and deleted habits remain governed by their separate archive/Trash and recovery rules; never merge those states into Ended.
- Personal owners and authorized Home members see only habits and histories permitted in their context. Respect Home isolation, shared habit manager permissions, concurrent end-date edits, accessibility, mobile layouts, empty sections and wall-display privacy.
- Acceptance criteria: active versus ended list placement; paused-but-not-ended habit; ended history and insights; empty Ended section; search; optional dashboard widgets; extending/removing end date; archive/delete distinction; permission loss; Home isolation; timezone boundary at end date.

### Confirmed ended habit reactivation tracking periods (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Reactivating an **Ended** personal or shared Home habit starts a distinct **new tracking period** for the same habit, with a **fresh current streak**. Preserve the original tracking period, all historical completions, streak achievements, milestones, statistics, schedule versions and audit history. Do not duplicate the habit or merge the inactive gap into an ongoing streak.
- Only the personal habit owner or an authorized Home habit manager may reactivate. Offer an explicit **Reactivate** workflow from Ended habits, with a preview of the proposed new start/effective date, schedule, reminders, pauses, active-list placement and streak reset. Reactivation begins no earlier than the current applicable local date; never create or backfill opportunities for the gap between the former inclusive end date and the new tracking period. Require a future end date or no end date for the new period; do not silently remove historical end-date boundaries.
- Each period has its own effective start and inclusive end boundary (when ended), progress, current streak and completion-rate denominator. The new period starts with a zero current streak until eligible completions occur; historical longest-streak achievements remain visible, clearly distinguished from current-period streak and insights. Overall historical insights may span periods if clearly segmented and exclude inactive gaps from eligible-opportunity denominators.
- Retain historical end-date records even if a new period has no end date. Extending or clearing an ended habit's date through the existing editing controls must route through this same reactivation workflow, not reinterpret the inactive gap or retroactively join streaks. Editing a future end date on an already active period remains a normal edit. Respect pre-existing manual and vacation pauses where their intervals overlap the new period; no pause creates catch-up debt.
- Reactivation is not permitted for archived, deleted, inaccessible or unauthorized habits until their separate restoration/access workflows apply. Handle simultaneous reactivation attempts idempotently to create exactly one new period; reject stale updates, prevent duplicate reminders, milestones or scheduled opportunities, and keep member-visible activity and audit entries accurate. Shared Home habit changes respect Home isolation and manager permissions.
- Acceptance criteria: ended-to-active transition; fresh current streak; prior longest streak retained; no gap backfill; reactivation while a manual/vacation pause remains active; multiple prior periods; new end date; no end date; backdated check-ins limited to valid eligible periods; historical insights segmented; concurrent reactivation; archive/delete restrictions; timezone boundaries; accessible mobile and wall-display states.

### Confirmed reminder review during habit reactivation (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Reactivating an ended personal or shared Home habit requires an explicit **Review reminders** step before confirmation. Present the previous reminder configuration, clearly marked as historical/inactive, and allow the authorized actor to **keep**, **modify**, or **disable** it for the new tracking period. Never silently restore notifications or send a reminder before the reactivation is confirmed.
- Show reminder times, applicable timezone, delivery channels, eligible scheduled days, and current notification/quiet-hour settings. For shared Home habits, respect the existing distinction between manager-controlled habit settings and each member's individual notification preferences; managers must not override members' opt-outs or expose private notification settings.
- Validate kept or modified reminders against the new effective schedule, start/end dates, active pauses, permissions and supported notification channels. Flag obsolete times or schedules and require an explicit valid choice; disabling reminders must be possible without blocking habit reactivation. A previously disabled reminder remains disabled unless deliberately enabled.
- On confirmation, atomically create the new tracking period and its chosen reminder configuration. Schedule only future eligible reminders, never backfill reminders from the ended interval, duplicate queued deliveries, or notify on paused/unscheduled days. If reactivation fails or is cancelled, leave the habit ended and previous reminders inactive. Handle concurrent edits and repeated confirmation idempotently.
- Provide a clear accessible review and confirmation summary, including reminders disabled by platform permission or member preference. Preserve prior reminder configuration and the reactivation decision in permission-aware history without recording sensitive device settings or changing historical habit records.
- Acceptance criteria: keep unchanged valid reminders; modify time/channel; disable; previously disabled reminders; changed schedule invalidating old reminder; shared Home member opt-out; active manual/vacation pause; quiet hours; platform permission denied; cancelled or failed reactivation; concurrent reactivation; timezone/DST; no premature, historical or duplicate delivery.

### Confirmed optional compact habit insights dashboard widget (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Offer an optional, compact **Habit insights** dashboard widget for eligible personal and shared Home dashboards. It is not added by default and does not replace existing habit check-in, progress or streak widgets. Dashboard owners add, remove, position and size it through established widget customization.
- The widget displays a clearly labelled completion rate for a defined recent period, current streak with its correct scheduled-opportunity or qualifying-week unit, and a restrained miniature activity trend. Provide a clear **View insights** action opening the dedicated habit insights view in the same authorized personal or Home context; do not duplicate the full monthly calendar or analytics controls inside the widget.
- Allow selecting an accessible habit for the widget using existing dashboard configuration conventions. Only show personal habits to their owner and shared Home habits to authorized Home viewers; do not mix personal data into shared dashboards or leak another member's individual contributions, private pauses, or vacation details. Wall-display exposure must follow its separate approved privacy and interaction rules.
- Reuse the dedicated insights definitions for eligible opportunities, historical schedule versions, partial weeks, pauses, backdated check-ins, corrections and Undo. Rates and trends must not count paused or unscheduled periods as failures. Keep the widget and detailed view consistent after updates, with graceful stale/loading/error and empty or insufficient-data states.
- Support compact/mobile layouts, resizing, keyboard and screen-reader navigation, readable numeric summaries for the trend, reduced motion, and sufficient contrast. If space is tight, prioritize completion rate and current streak, then simplify the trend rather than truncating essential context or exposing extra detail.
- Acceptance criteria: add/remove/rearrange widget; personal versus Home context; habit selection and deleted/archived habit; daily/weekday/weekly schedules; overlapping pauses; no eligible opportunities; schedule change; backdating and Undo; cross-Home isolation; wall display; mobile narrow layout; accessibility; navigation to detailed insights.

### Confirmed immediate habit completion Undo (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Immediately after a successful personal or shared Home habit check-in, offer a short-lived, clearly labelled **Undo** action in the completion confirmation. After this action disappears, authorized members can still reverse erroneous completions through the established dated history correction workflow. Do not introduce an always-on tap-to-toggle interaction or weaken historical correction permissions.
- Undo reverses only the specific newly recorded check-in and requires the actor to retain the relevant permission and the record to remain eligible for reversal. For shared Home habits, never undo another member's later contribution or overwrite an intervening correction; if the check-in has changed, show a clear stale-state explanation and direct the member to authorized history correction instead.
- On successful Undo, update the occurrence or weekly contribution, progress, streak and reminder eligibility consistently with the applicable historical schedule and pauses. Preserve the original check-in, actor, timestamp, Undo actor and reversal time in permission-aware audit history. Undo must not generate or revoke unrelated chore points or rewards.
- Show Undo only after confirmed persistence, not while a save is pending or failed. Support keyboard, touch and screen readers; announce the completion and reversible action without relying on color or animation. Expiration of the brief action must not block the normal correction route.
- Revalidate at execution time for changed permissions, Home membership, concurrent check-ins, archived/deleted habits, offline/retry conditions and repeated Undo attempts; ensure exactly-once reversal and truthful feedback. Honor personal privacy, shared contributor visibility, wall-display limitations and applicable timezone/week boundaries.
- Acceptance criteria: successful personal check-in then immediate Undo; shared daily check-in Undo; shared weekly contribution Undo; brief Undo expires then history correction; revoked permission; second member changes record; repeated taps/retries; save failure; offline recovery; progress/streak/reminder recalculation; accessible mobile and keyboard interaction.

### Confirmed historical habit corrections and streak recalculation (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Missing a scheduled habit occurrence breaks the relevant streak, but members with the appropriate permissions can record a genuinely completed habit retrospectively or correct an inaccurate past check-in. Do not introduce streak freezes, forgiveness credits, or automatic grace periods.
- For daily and selected-weekday habits, a missed scheduled opportunity breaks the run of consecutive completed scheduled opportunities; unscheduled, paused and future dates do not count as missed. For weekly-frequency habits, a week counts toward a streak only when its target was reached; an incomplete elapsed target week breaks the streak. Show the active timezone and week boundaries consistently with the habit's personal/Home context.
- Offer a clear dated history with **Mark completed**, **Undo completion**, and **Correct** actions only for eligible past occurrences. Identify the selected occurrence and existing status before saving; do not allow a check-in outside the habit's active schedule or silently modify the schedule to accommodate a backfill. Historical corrections must be explicitly initiated by a member, never inferred from reminders or other modules.
- Recalculate affected past and current streaks, weekly totals and progress after each accepted correction using the schedule applicable to each historical period. Display the updated result and indicate when a past correction changed a previously displayed streak. Preserve previous completion timestamps and original contributor attribution in an auditable change history, including who corrected what and when.
- Personal-habit corrections follow personal access rules. Home-habit corrections require current Home membership and the relevant complete/correct permission; show original and correcting contributors only to permitted viewers. A correction cannot bypass a revoked permission or disclose private records in notifications, widgets or wall displays.
- Prevent duplicate backfills and repeated correction writes, including concurrent Home-member actions and retries. Handle deleted/archived habits, stale schedule changes, timezone boundaries and unavailable history safely, explaining why a correction cannot be applied where relevant. Respect the existing history-retention and recovery policies.
- Acceptance criteria: missed Monday-Wednesday-Friday occurrence breaks streak; late legitimate Friday check-in restores it; an erroneous completion is undone and streak falls; weekly three-times target reached after backfill; past target week still incomplete; paused and unscheduled dates ignored; changed schedule preserves original period; two Home members correcting the same occurrence; contributor audit; read-only member denied; timezone/week boundary; inaccessible or archived habit; accessible mobile history and corrected progress.

### Confirmed optional notes on habit completions (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Allow a short, optional plain-text note on an individual completed personal or shared Home habit occurrence. A note is never a prerequisite for completion, streak progress or weekly-target credit. Do not add rich text, photo attachments or a habit journal as part of this decision.
- Provide a concise note field in the in-app completion flow and an **Add/Edit note** action in authorized completion history. A member using a one-tap check-in, dashboard control or supported notification action can complete immediately without a note, then optionally add one later in Madeora. Notes belong to the specific occurrence/contribution, not the habit definition or all future occurrences.
- Show notes only to viewers authorized for the corresponding habit completion. Personal-habit notes remain private to their owner; shared Home habit notes follow Home membership and existing habit-history visibility/correction permissions. Clearly attribute a note to its author without revealing other members' private data in notifications, dashboards or wall displays.
- Permit the original contributor to add or edit their note while they retain the appropriate access; any additional manager correction authority must follow existing shared-habit correction permissions. Do not permit a member to silently alter another contributor's note. Offer an explicit save/cancel flow, reasonable length validation, empty-note removal, and accessible read/edit states.
- Record creation and edit times and the actor in permission-aware audit history, preserving prior text as required by existing audit/recovery policy. Editing or removing note text must **not** change whether the occurrence was completed, its actual completion timestamp, streaks, points, weekly targets, milestones or approval state. If the completion is undone or corrected, retain its note in restricted history rather than attaching it to a different completion.
- Handle simultaneous edits, stale history, revoked permissions, archived/ended habits, deleted/recovered records and offline retries without losing other members' contributions or creating duplicate completions. Provide clear save errors and prevent unsaved draft notes from appearing as committed history.
- Acceptance criteria: completion with/without note; add/edit/remove note afterward; notification-origin completion followed by note; shared Home author versus another member; manager correction permission; long/empty text; Undo and historical correction; ended/archived habit; stale edit, permission revocation and retry; privacy in wall display and notifications; no changes to streaks or rewards.

### Confirmed direct habit completion from notifications (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Where the notification platform supports secure actionable notifications, offer **Complete habit** directly on eligible personal and shared Home habit reminders, alongside already approved Open, Dismiss and Snooze actions where supported. This is a one-step check-in for the specific reminder's scheduled occurrence or eligible weekly contribution, not blanket permission to edit habit settings, past history, or another member's records. Where actionable notifications are unsupported, open the authenticated habit check-in view instead; never claim completion merely because the notification was tapped.
- At action time authenticate the acting member as required, recheck their current personal/Home permissions and membership, the exact habit and occurrence identity, schedule version, timezone and date/week boundary, current pause/end/archive state, and whether the relevant daily/weekday opportunity or weekly target is still eligible. Shared Home habits retain their existing one-completion-per-occurrence rules; weekly contributions must respect the approved target and attribution rules. Never complete a paused, expired, unscheduled, already completed or inaccessible opportunity.
- Commit atomically with idempotency protection so duplicate taps, offline delivery, retries, delayed notifications, concurrent Home check-ins and cross-device actions cannot double-complete an occurrence, inflate progress, award points or duplicate streak celebrations. Recompute progress, streaks, insights, widgets and future reminder/snooze eligibility only after a confirmed successful commit. Existing no-extra-points rules remain unchanged.
- Give accurate success, already-completed, no-longer-eligible, authentication-required and retryable-error feedback through the supported notification surface; otherwise direct the member to Madeora with the current state. A notification action must never silently fail or falsely report success. Provide the existing brief **Undo** when a supported authenticated surface can safely offer it; otherwise the standard in-app history correction remains available, with the same authorization and audit rules.
- Preserve a permission-aware audit record identifying the actor, actual submission timestamp, intended scheduled opportunity and notification-origin action without implying the habit was completed at reminder delivery time. Keep personal habit data private, never reveal other shared members' responses, and respect wall-display restrictions and notification settings. Action buttons must have clear accessible labels and be usable without relying on motion or color.
- Acceptance criteria: personal daily and weekday completion; shared Home first-completer race; weekly target partially fulfilled/already reached; reminder snoozed then completed; stale notification after pause/end/archive, schedule edit or permission revocation; quiet hours and timezone rollover; unsupported platform fallback; offline/retry and duplicate taps; accurate feedback; Undo/history correction; audit and privacy isolation.

### Confirmed simple habit reminder snoozing (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** A member receiving an eligible personal or shared Home habit reminder may snooze that **individual reminder** for **15, 30, or 60 minutes**. Snoozing postpones only that reminder delivery; it never changes the habit's recurring schedule, reminder configuration, target, completion status, streak, or historical check-ins. Dismiss and Open habit remain available where supported.
- Show the three explicit snooze durations in the reminder action surface, with clear feedback indicating the new local reminder time. Snoozing is a member-specific preference/action: for shared Home habits it never snoozes another member's reminder, and it never changes Home-wide habit settings. Respect notification opt-outs, platform capabilities, Home isolation, and current permissions.
- Before delivering the snoozed reminder, revalidate that the habit and member remain eligible, the relevant opportunity or weekly target remains incomplete, the habit has not ended, been archived or paused, and the reminder is still permitted. Cancel obsolete snoozes after completion, correction, permission loss, reminder disabling, relevant schedule changes or removal from the Home. Do not emit reminders for completed or ineligible opportunities.
- Respect quiet hours and the applicable timezone/DST rules. If the snoozed time falls within quiet hours or after the eligible opportunity/reminder window, suppress the delivery rather than violating preferences or carrying a stale reminder into a new day/week. Show a clear explanation when a snooze cannot be scheduled.
- Snooze is a single deferred delivery, not a new recurring reminder or escalation policy. Prevent duplicate delivery under retries, repeated taps and concurrent check-ins; failure to schedule or deliver must not modify completion records. Do not create catch-up obligations, points, milestone celebrations or extra notifications.
- Acceptance criteria: snooze 15/30/60 minutes; dismiss/open; personal and shared member-specific delivery; completion before snooze fires; weekly target reached; pause/end/archive; notification opt-out and permission loss; quiet-hour boundary; timezone/DST; schedule/reminder edit; concurrent updates; offline/retry; no duplicate delivery or habit-state changes; accessible action feedback.

### Confirmed flexible habit reminders (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Habit reminders are optional and can use member-selected reminder days and times. For weekly-frequency habits, members may additionally enable reminders while the current week's target remains unfinished; these stop once the target is reached. Do not add adaptive timing, repeated escalation, or reminders enabled by default.
- For daily and selected-weekday habits, offer eligible reminder days and configurable local clock times consistent with the active schedule. For weekly-frequency habits, allow selected reminder days/times within the current target week, with a clear indication of progress remaining. Keep the configuration understandable on mobile and accessible to keyboard and assistive technology users.
- Stop or suppress reminders for an already completed scheduled occurrence, a reached weekly target, paused or archived habits, dates outside the active schedule, and inaccessible habits. Recheck current completion state and permissions at delivery time so late check-ins, corrections and simultaneous Home completions do not produce misleading reminders.
- Personal reminders go only to the relevant member. For shared Home habits, each eligible member's reminder preferences and permitted notification channels are respected independently; no notification reveals private information or restricted contributor details. Reminders must honor existing notification preferences, quiet hours, timezone, Home boundaries, and permission revocation.
- Editing the schedule, target, reminder days/times, or pause state must update future reminders without silently resending old ones. Use the habit's configured personal/Home timezone and clearly handle daylight-saving changes and week boundaries. Reminder delivery failures must not alter completion or streak history.
- Acceptance criteria: daily reminder before completion; selected weekdays; multiple chosen reminder days/times; weekly target unfinished versus achieved; completion just before reminder; historical correction; paused/archived habit; shared Home habit completed by another member; one member opted out; quiet hours; permission revoked; timezone and daylight-saving boundary; notification delivery failure; accessible reminder settings.

### Confirmed interactive habit and goal dashboard widgets (13 October 2026)
- **Approved option B:** Personal and Home dashboard widgets for Habits and Goals are interactive, with permission-controlled quick habit check-ins, undo of eligible check-ins, and manual goal-progress updates. Creation, schedule changes, target changes, archive/pause, permission configuration, and complex history management stay in their full modules. Do not expand this approval into full widget-based management.
- Habit widgets show the member's permitted scheduled occurrences, completion state, relevant weekly-target progress and concise streak/progress indicators. Offer **Complete** and **Undo** only when the underlying occurrence is eligible and the current member has the appropriate rights. An undo must visibly identify the occurrence affected and use the approved historical-correction and audit rules; shared Home habits count a completed occurrence only once.
- Goal widgets show accessible status and progress for completion-based and measurable goals. For eligible measurable goals, **Add progress** opens a compact editor displaying the current value, target, unit, entered adjustment, resulting total and relevant Home context. Do not confuse adding a contribution with replacing the aggregate total. Completion-based goal actions follow the approved separate manage/contribute permissions; do not infer automatic progress from other modules.
- Apply existing personal/Home ownership, Home member permissions, privacy, contributor attribution, audit history, correction, concurrency and idempotency rules. A Home widget must never expose private habits or goals. On wall displays, interactive controls must respect the separately approved device security and privacy restrictions; do not assume a wall display may write as a member.
- Provide immediate pending/success/error feedback and accessible focus, keyboard, touch and screen-reader states. Revalidate permissions and current values on action; if another member has already completed a habit or changed goal progress, refresh and explain the new state instead of overwriting or double-counting. Show a safe retry path for failed writes without replaying successful actions.
- Keep advanced configuration and detailed contribution/check-in history one clear navigation step away in the full module. Dashboard widget configuration follows the existing widget-editing rules; do not introduce additional module-management interfaces within widgets.
- Acceptance criteria: personal daily habit check-in/undo; shared Home habit simultaneous check-in; weekly target completion; read-only member; correction audit; measurable goal contribution with decimal unit and resulting total; completion goal permission; concurrent Home goal contributions; stale widget state; failed retry; revoked access; private content excluded from shared dashboard; wall-display read-only state; keyboard and mobile quick actions.

## 12. Open decisions

**Already decided — do not reopen without an explicit change request:** multi-Home membership; three roles; configurable MVP permissions; privacy-first checkbox sharing to multiple Homes; synchronized single-source shared items; personal/Home dashboard ownership; wall-display-specific layout; Ora's fixed visual identity; dedicated edit mode and contextual widget side panel; all three phases included in MVP; explicit excluded modules.


- Recover the original item-by-item contents of the three agreed MVP phases and map each to acceptance criteria; all three phases are already committed to MVP. Delivery milestones and dates remain to be specified.
- Technical stack, deployment, database, storage, and authentication implementation.
- Expired/revoked invitation reissue details and email invitation expiration options, account deletion, member-removal and ownership-transfer edge cases, precise permission matrix, and member color semantics; both invitation channels, required sign-in, revocation, and expiration are already agreed.
- Pricing, free/paid boundaries, monetization, and go-to-market plan.
- Widget API details and template permission matrix; personal vs Home layout ownership and persisted sidebar preference are already agreed.
- Provider-specific integration scope and supported import formats.
- Wall-display security model and device support.
- Notification/reminder delivery and user preferences.
- Data retention, export, deletion, and operational policies.

---

# UI / UX Style Guide

## Visual principles
1. Calm, modern, welcoming, useful, and approachable rather than corporate.
2. Clear hierarchy and generous breathing room; avoid dense dashboard grids.
3. Light and dark modes have the same structure and interaction behavior.
4. **Only the page background may use an atmospheric gradient/glow.** No gradient buttons, borders, icons, cards, charts, progress indicators, or accent fills.
5. **No decorative background lines, grids, waves, or geometric patterns.**
6. Softly rounded surfaces, restrained depth, subtle neumorphic influence; maintain clear boundaries and contrast.
7. Users can rearrange, resize, add, and hide dashboard widgets.

## Color foundations
Suggested initial tokens (verify against accessibility tests and real screens):

| Token | Light | Dark |
|---|---|---|
| Page base | `#F7F8FC` | `#10141D` |
| Sidebar | `#FFFFFF` | `#11151E` |
| Surface | `#FFFFFF` | `#1A1F2A` |
| Elevated surface | `#FFFFFF` | `#222836` |
| Primary text | `#151A29` | `#F7F8FC` |
| Secondary text | `#667085` | `#AAB3C5` |
| Border | `#E6E9F1` | `#303746` |
| Primary action | `#8057F5` | `#936BFF` |
| Focus ring | `#8057F5` | `#A98AFF` |

Use solid accent colors. Users may select curated palettes or custom accent colors; validate contrast and derive accessible hover/pressed states. Semantic colors (success, warning, danger, information) must remain distinguishable without color alone.

### Atmospheric background
A very low-opacity, blurred purple/blue radial wash may sit behind the page content. It must fade into the base color, never obscure content, and never appear as an element-specific gradient. The background is decorative only; the interface must work with it disabled and under reduced-transparency settings.

## Typography and spacing
- Use a clean, highly legible sans-serif; choose and license a production font separately.
- Suggested type scale: page title 28–32px; section title 18–20px; body 14–16px; helper text 12–14px.
- Suggested 4px spacing grid; typical gaps 16–24px; card padding 20–24px.
- Card radius 14–18px; control radius 9–12px. Avoid overly pill-shaped controls everywhere.
- Maintain readable line heights, keyboard focus visibility, and accessible contrast.

## Application shell and navigation
- Persistent sidebar on desktop; expanded approximately 240px, collapsed approximately 72px.
- Expanded: Madeora mark + wordmark, icons with labels, active-item styling, and secondary/help controls.
- Collapsed: mark and centered icons only; accessible tooltips and labels, including for keyboard and assistive technology users.
- Explicit collapse/expand control near the sidebar header; smooth ~200–250ms transition respecting reduced-motion preference.
- **Remember the sidebar preference** between sessions.
- Collapsing the sidebar **increases available content width**; it must not narrow or replace the main dashboard with a mobile layout.
- Responsive mobile navigation should be designed independently rather than squeezing desktop sidebar patterns.
- Provide an obvious switch between personal and shared household contexts; indicate the active context and permissions.

## Dashboard widgets
- Composable cards with a clear title, optional context, concise primary content, and a relevant action.
- Representative modules: Ora greeting, today's schedule, tasks, household chores, meal plan, shopping list, habits, weather (when available), pets, and rewards.
- Widget content should prioritize actionable information over decorative metrics.
- Provide edit-layout mode with drag/reorder, resizing where appropriate, hide/show, reset layout, and responsive fallbacks.
- Avoid cramming all modules into the initial dashboard; users choose what matters.
- Design loading, empty, error, permission-restricted, and disconnected-integration states.
- Do not present invented personal events or statistics as real data.

## Ora assistant
Ora should feel friendly and helpful, with a recognizable but restrained visual identity. It may offer contextual suggestions and quick actions without dominating the dashboard. Make dismissing or minimizing its widget easy. Ask for confirmation before consequential changes; respect household permissions and privacy.

## Component rules
- Buttons: solid fills, clear hierarchy (primary, secondary, ghost, destructive); no gradient treatments.
- Cards: flat or very subtle elevation; restrained borders/shadows; no heavy glassmorphism.
- Icons: consistent stroke weight and size; pair ambiguous icons with labels or tooltips.
- Inputs: visible labels, helpful validation, accessible focus/error states.
- Lists and checkboxes: easy scanning, generous targets, clear completed states.
- Charts and progress: solid semantic colors, accessible labels, no decorative gradient fills.
- Motion: purposeful and subtle; respect `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- Target WCAG 2.2 AA where applicable; test contrast in both themes.

## Design implementation checklist
- [ ] Both themes use matching layouts and behavior.
- [ ] Only the page background contains any gradient-like treatment.
- [ ] No background lines, grid motifs, or waves.
- [ ] Sidebar expands/collapses and persists preference.
- [ ] Main content reflows to use reclaimed sidebar width.
- [ ] Personal/household context is clear.
- [ ] Widgets can be configured and handle all data states.
- [ ] Ora is helpful but unobtrusive.
- [ ] Responsive, keyboard, screen-reader, and reduced-motion behavior tested.

## Scope of visual approval
Approved: overall calm dashboard aesthetic, light/dark direction, soft background glow, absence of decorative background lines, solid-color UI accents, and collapsible sidebar behavior.

Still flexible: exact hex values, font family, breakpoints, component dimensions, individual module layouts, and final accessibility adjustments.

## Reference direction
Use the latest Madeora lifestyle/household dashboard mockups as **visual inspiration**, not as literal data or final pixel-perfect specifications. Do not reuse the unrelated website-builder dashboard mockups.
